The model consists of the time domain Maxwell's equations coupled to two ordinary differential equations; the nonlinear Kerr-Debye equation and the linear dispersive Lorentz equation, in one space dimension. The proposed methods combine staggered spatial discretizations of $2M$th-order, for integer $M>0$, with second-order explicit leapfrog or implicit trapezoidal time integrators. To handle the stiff relaxation in the nonlinear Kerr-Debye equation, we adopt a modified exponential integrator, introduced in \cite{PengJCP2020}, which ensures asymptotic-preserving (AP) behavior in the limit of vanishing relaxation time. We prove that the resulting schemes satisfy a discrete analogue of the continuous energy identity and preserve the positivity (PP) of the nonlinear susceptibility. For the schemes based on the leapfrog time integrator, we establish energy stability under a CFL condition, while the schemes based on the trapezoidal time integrator are unconditionally energy stable. High-order temporal accuracy in the trapezoidal time integrator based schemes is further achieved via Richardson extrapolation yielding $2M$th-order accurate methods in both space and time. Numerical experiments demonstrate the theoretical convergence rates and the ability of the proposed schemes to capture nonlinear wave phenomena such as soliton propagation, self-steepening, and odd harmonic generation. This work extends previous AP-PP and energy stable approaches in a discontinuous Galerkin framework to the FDTD setting, offering a computationally efficient, flexible and robust tool for simulating electromagnetic wave propagation in nonlinear dispersive optical media.

</details>


### [56] [Global error estimators for parametric monotone nonlinearities and neural approximations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05767)
*Pablo Cortés Castillo,Wolfgang Dahmen,Jay Gopalakrishnan*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper constructs computable error estimators that double as loss functions for neural networks solving a class of parametric nonlinear PDEs with a monotonicity property, proving they are globally reliable and efficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Estimating error in parametric nonlinear PDEs is hard, especially globally (not just near the solution). The authors want loss functions that are variationally correct and computable for arbitrary inputs, enabling neural network approximations of parameter-to-solution maps.

Method: Split the nonlinear operator into a linear part and a strongly monotone closure; measure the linear residual in a discrete dual norm. Since the closure's dual norm has no closed form when the trial norm is stronger than L2, use a computable surrogate satisfying a pairing bound and a Lipschitz bound. Verify assumptions for a model class of nonlinear fluxes with explicit constants in terms of the parameter range.

Result: Main theorem yields two-sided bounds with explicit constants, covering two instances: (1) a first-order system least-squares estimator on conforming trial spaces, valid on the whole trial space for arbitrary approximations; (2) a discontinuous Petrov-Galerkin estimator on finite element trial spaces per parameter, built with broken test spaces, requiring no conformity, with the dual norm computed by independent element-local problems.

Conclusion: Both estimators are computable for arbitrary inputs and thus serve as variationally correct loss functions for neural network approximations of parameter-to-solution maps.

Abstract: We construct computable error estimators, which double as loss functions for neural networks, for a class of parametric nonlinear partial differential equations with a monotonicity property, and prove that they are globally reliable and efficient. The value of such a loss function is bounded above and below by the squared error in the natural trial norm, for every trial function, not merely for those near the exact solution; this global property rests on monotonicity. The construction rests on splitting the nonlinear operator into a linear part and a strongly monotone closure, and on measuring the linear residual in a discrete dual norm. Since the closure contributes a dual norm that admits no closed form when the trial norm is stronger than an $L_2$ norm, the estimator is built around a computable surrogate for it, required only to satisfy a pairing bound and a Lipschitz bound. The main theorem then yields two-sided bounds with explicit constants and covers two instances. A first-order system least-squares estimator on conforming trial spaces is the first instance studied: its two-sided bound holds on the whole trial space and therefore applies to arbitrary approximations, including those not in a discrete finite element space. The second instance is a discontinuous Petrov-Galerkin estimator on trial spaces of finite element functions for each parameter value, built with broken test spaces, for which no conformity is required and the dual norm is computed by independent element-local problems. All assumptions are verified for a model class of nonlinear fluxes, with the constants tracked explicitly in terms of the parameter range. Being computable for an arbitrary input, both estimators serve as variationally correct loss functions for neural network approximations of parameter-to-solution maps.

</details>


### [57] [Entropy-Stable and Well-Balanced Discontinuous Galerkin Methods for the Compressible Euler Equations in Vector-Invariant Form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05820)
*Marco Artiano,Kieran Ricardo,Oswald Knoth,Peter Spichtinger,Hendrik Ranocha*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: Structure-preserving DGSEM methods for compressible Euler equations with gravity conserve thermodynamic entropy and total energy, and are entropy-stable and well-balanced for isothermal/isentropic states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Atmospheric dynamical cores need numerical schemes that respect key physical invariants (entropy, energy) and remain well-balanced for background states to ensure robustness and accuracy on general meshes.

Method: Within the flux-differencing DGSEM framework, the Euler equations with gravity are written in vector-invariant form with potential temperature as prognostic variable. Nonconservative terms are discretized as symmetric/antisymmetric products to derive two-point numerical fluxes; an entropy-stable flux well-balanced for isothermal and isentropic states is designed, with properties proven to extend to high-order DGSEM on curvilinear meshes.

Result: Two-point fluxes that conserve both thermodynamic entropy and total energy are derived; an entropy-stable, well-balanced flux is constructed. All properties carry over to high-order DGSEM on general curvilinear meshes, confirmed by numerical examples.

Conclusion: The scheme provides a robust, accurate structure-preserving method suitable for modern atmospheric flow dynamical cores, as validated by numerical experiments.

Abstract: We develop structure-preserving methods for the compressible Euler equations with gravity in vector-invariant form and potential temperature as a prognostic variable within the flux-differencing discontinuous Galerkin spectral element method (DGSEM) framework. By discretizing the nonconservative terms as symmetric and antisymmetric products, we derive two-point numerical fluxes that conserve both the thermodynamic entropy and the total energy. Moreover, we design an entropy-stable numerical flux that is well-balanced for both isothermal and isentropic background states. All properties are shown to carry over to the high-order DGSEM on general curvilinear meshes. Several numerical examples confirm the theoretical findings and show the robustness and accuracy of the scheme for use in modern dynamical cores for atmospheric flows.

</details>


### [58] [A TVD Active Flux method for scalar conservation laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05920)
*Wasilij Barsukow*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A TVD theory is developed for the fully discrete, single-stage, third-order Active Flux method applied to scalar conservation laws, extending total variation and Harten's lemma to this scheme.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Active Flux methods are high-order schemes for conservation laws, but a total variation diminishing (TVD) guarantee—important for suppressing spurious oscillations near discontinuities—was missing for the fully discrete single-stage formulation.

Method: The authors adapt the notion of discrete total variation to the Active Flux setting and derive a corresponding version of Harten's lemma. They then introduce tunable parameters into the high-order method, chosen so they can be computed efficiently to satisfy the lemma's conditions.

Result: An adapted discrete total variation and a matching Harten's lemma for Active Flux are established, and parameters ensuring TVD are obtained with efficient computation, giving a third-order method under the classical degrees of freedom.

Conclusion: The work provides a rigorous TVD framework for the fully discrete, single-stage Active Flux method on scalar conservation laws, enabling oscillation control while retaining third-order accuracy.

Abstract: Focusing on scalar conservation laws, a TVD theory is developed for the fully discrete, single-stage Active Flux method. The classical choice of degrees of freedom is considered, yielding a third-order method. Starting from an adaptation of the concept of (discrete) total variation to Active Flux, a corresponding adaptation of Harten's lemma is achieved. Finally, the high-order method is endowed with parameters chosen such that they can be efficiently computed to fulfill the conditions of the lemma.

</details>


### [59] [Quasi-Trefftz spaces for the first-order time-harmonic Maxwell's equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05975)
*Ilaria Fontana,Lise-Marie Imbert-Gérard*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper identifies optimal high-order Taylor-based polynomial quasi-Trefftz spaces for first-order time-harmonic Maxwell's equations, constructs their bases, and proves best approximation properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard polynomial spaces achieve high-order approximation but are inefficient (large dimension). Quasi-Trefftz spaces retain high-order best approximation while being far smaller in dimension by approximating only smooth solutions of the governing equation. For vector-valued equations like Maxwell's, naive quasi-Trefftz definitions fail to systematically yield the smallest space with the desired approximation property.

Method: The article identifies the optimal quasi-Trefftz spaces for first-order time-harmonic Maxwell's equations, proposes two approaches to construct quasi-Trefftz bases, and proves the best approximation property using polynomial Helmholtz decompositions and exactness of polynomial de Rham sequences. Numerical experiments validate the theory.

Result: Two construction approaches for quasi-Trefftz bases are proposed, and the best approximation property of the optimal spaces is proved. Theoretical results are validated with numerical experiments.

Conclusion: Optimal high-order Taylor-based polynomial quasi-Trefftz spaces exist for Maxwell's equations, offering smaller dimension while preserving high-order best approximation, supported by theory and numerics.

Abstract: The goal of this article is to study optimal high-order Taylor-based polynomial quasi-Trefftz spaces for first-order time-harmonic Maxwell's equations. Compared with standard polynomial spaces, the purpose of quasi-Trefftz spaces is to retain the same high-order best approximation property but more efficiently: their dimension is considerably smaller. To achieve this goal, they approximate only smooth solutions to the governing equation rather than to approximate general smooth functions. While there is a natural definition of Taylor-based polynomial quasi-Trefftz spaces for scalar equations, the situation is fundamentally different for vector-valued equations, as the naive quasi-Trefftz property does not systematically define the smallest space with the desired best approximation property. This article identifies the optimal quasi-Trefftz spaces for first-order time-harmonic Maxwell's equations, proposes two approaches to construct quasi-Trefftz bases, and proves the spaces' best approximation property. The theoretical results, which strongly leverage polynomial Helmholtz decompositions and the exactness of the polynomial de Rham sequences, are validated with numerical experiments.

</details>


### [60] [Nonparametric face-based finite element methods on complex hybrid three-dimensional meshes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06208)
*Aubin Brunel,J. -C. Latch{é}*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper proposes and analyzes low-order nonconforming face-based finite element methods for elliptic problems on complex hybrid 3D meshes with arbitrary polytopal cells.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard parametric mapping approaches degrade approximation properties on distorted meshes, and handling very general polytopal cells (including curved faces) stably remains challenging.

Method: A nonparametric construction of local approximation spaces is adopted, defined over face-based finite elements for general cells. A DG-inspired technique is used to remove consistency errors on strongly degraded meshes, even with curved faces. Constructions are exemplified for prismatic (triangular basis) and pyramidal (quadrilateral basis) cells, extendable tensor-wise to Crouzeix-Raviart and Rannacher-Turek generalizations.

Result: The nonparametric approach avoids degradation of approximation properties caused by parametric mappings; schemes are shown theoretically and numerically to be stable and convergent with optimal convergence rate.

Conclusion: The proposed face-based nonconforming methods provide a robust framework for elliptic problems on complex hybrid polytopal meshes, with optimal accuracy and general applicability.

Abstract: In this work, we propose and analyze low-order nonconforming face-based finite element methods for elliptic problems on complex hybrid three-dimensional meshes, composed of arbitrary polytopal cells. The focus is on scalar problems, but the approach can be extended in a tensor manner to design generalizations of the classical Crouzeix--Raviart and Rannacher--Turek methods for mixed formulations. We adopt a nonparametric construction of the local approximation spaces. We demonstrate that it avoids the degradation of approximation properties commonly induced by parametric mappings on distorted meshes. Using these approximation spaces, we define finite element schemes for very general cells. We further demonstrate that, on strongly degraded meshes, an approach inspired by discontinuous Galerkin methods enables removing consistency errors, even in the presence of potentially curved faces. Explicit examples of the construction of the polynomial space are given for prismatic (of triangular basis) and pyramidal (of quadrilateral basis) cells. The resulting schemes are shown theoretically and numerically to be stable and convergent, with an optimal convergence rate.

</details>


### [61] [IGA-KAN: Isogeometric Analysis with Physics-Informed Closed-Form Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks for Forward and Inverse PDEs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06348)
*Sima Naraghi,Kourosh Parand,Amirhossein Sadr,Dara Rahmati*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: IGA-KAN improves an existing isogeometric Galerkin solution by fitting local Kolmogorov-Arnold ridge models in closed form on each patch, achieving large accuracy gains without extra Galerkin unknowns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Isogeometric analysis (IGA) is accurate on exact NURBS geometry, but neural solvers are mesh-free yet far less accurate and rely on non-convex optimization without error control. The goal is to combine IGA's accuracy with neural/local model flexibility.

Method: Run an IGA Galerkin solve to get u_h. On each knot-vertex patch, fit a Kolmogorov-Arnold ridge model to the strong form, exact boundary data, and u_h; blend models via IGA hat functions. With fixed inner functions, fitting is a batched linear least-squares problem (no optimizer, learning rate, or initialization). An a posteriori safeguard based on a maximum-principle bound selects where local models are used.

Result: On eight benchmarks with exact solutions (including one on a brain-slice MRI-fitted domain), the method reduces IGA error at unchanged Galerkin unknowns by 4.2-90x in L^2 and 4.1-220x in H^1 on reference meshes. Its L^2 error is 6 to 6x10^4 times smaller than the best from-scratch KAN. In an inverse problem, it recovers an unknown constant source 167x more accurately than IGA.

Conclusion: IGA-KAN enhances rather than replaces IGA, delivering substantial accuracy improvements with no extra Galerkin unknowns and no optimizer. Gains are attributed to superconvergence of local averages of the Galerkin solution.

Abstract: Isogeometric analysis (IGA) solves partial differential equations accurately on exact NURBS geometry, whereas neural solvers are mesh-free but often orders of magnitude less accurate and typically trained by non-convex optimization without error control. We propose IGA-KAN, which uses local Kolmogorov-Arnold networks, fitted in closed form, to improve the IGA solution instead of replacing it. An IGA Galerkin solve produces u_h; on every knot-vertex patch a Kolmogorov-Arnold ridge model is fitted to the strong form of the equation, the exact boundary data and u_h, and the models are blended by IGA hat functions. With fixed inner functions the fit is one batched linear least-squares problem, without optimizer, learning rate or initialization. An a posteriori safeguard, motivated by a maximum-principle bound, decides where local models are used, keeping the IGA solution elsewhere. On eight benchmarks with exact solutions, five from the literature and one also posed on a domain fitted to a brain slice from MRI, the method reduces the error of IGA, at an unchanged number of Galerkin unknowns, by factors of 4.2 to 90 in L^2 and 4.1 to 220 in H^1 on the reference meshes, and its L^2 error is 6 to 6x10^4 times smaller than that of the best Kolmogorov-Arnold network trained from scratch on the same equations with a fixed budget. In an inverse problem it recovers an unknown constant source from one noise-free observation 167 times more accurately than IGA. The gain is attributed to the superconvergence of local averages of the Galerkin solution.

</details>


### [62] [A Mixed-Precision Model for Matrix-Splitting Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06414)
*Neil Lindquist,David Appelhans,Chales W. Jackson,Joseph M. Derlaga*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A mixed-precision technique cuts data movement in stationary iterative methods, preserving convergence and reaching full double accuracy via iterative refinement, with measured speedups up to 1.60x.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Stationary iterative methods are memory-bound due to low arithmetic intensity, so data movement dominates runtime.

Method: Use mixed precision (e.g., half + double) in matrix-splitting iterative methods, with theoretical and experimental convergence analysis depending on precision and conditioning of the M matrix, and apply iterative refinement to recover full double-precision accuracy.

Result: Average speedup over uniform double precision: 1.60x for scalar/block Jacobi, 1.25x for scalar/block Gauss-Seidel. Reducing precision in SSOR used by NASA's OVERFLOW gave 1.14x overall application speedup.

Conclusion: Mixed precision effectively reduces memory traffic while maintaining convergence, and with iterative refinement achieves full double accuracy with practical speedups, including real-world HPC applications.

Abstract: The performance of stationary iterative methods is memory-bound, due to the low arithmetic intensity. So, we propose a mixed-precision technique to reduce the amount of data movement in matrix-splitting iterative methods. We demonstrate both theoretically and experimentally that this technique will generally converge at a similar rate as an exact-precision iteration, depending on the precision used and the conditioning of the $M$ matrix. Furthermore, we demonstrate that iterative refinement can be used to refine the solution to full double-precision accuracy. Using this approach, we mixed half and double precision to achieve an average speedup over uniform double precision of $1.60\times$ for scalar and block Jacobi and $1.25\times$ for scalar and block Gauss-Seidel. Furthermore, we reduced the precision in the SSOR iteration used by OVERFLOW, a NASA code for compressible fluid dynamics simulations, and achieved a $1.14\times$ speedup to the overall application.

</details>


### [63] [Delta Redistribution: a variation of State Redistribution for steady-state embedded boundary computations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06459)
*Marsha Berger,Andrew Giuliani*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A new algorithm addresses the small cell problem in Cartesian cut cell grids while exactly preserving the base scheme's steady state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cartesian cut cell grids suffer from the small cell problem, which can destabilize finite volume schemes. Existing remedies often fail to preserve the base scheme's steady state, motivating a variation that maintains this property.

Method: The authors propose an algorithm for handling the small cell problem in Cartesian cut cell grids, designed so that it preserves the base scheme's steady state exactly. They demonstrate it using finite volume schemes in two space dimensions.

Result: Several computational examples in two space dimensions validate the approach and confirm exact steady-state preservation.

Conclusion: The proposed algorithm resolves the small cell problem while retaining the base scheme's steady state exactly, as shown in 2D finite volume examples.

Abstract: We present an algorithm for handling the small cell problem in Cartesian cut cell grids. The advantage of this variation is that it preserves the base scheme's steady state exactly. We show several computational examples using finite volume schemes in two space dimensions.

</details>


### [64] [One Extra Dimension Suffices for the Complete-Graph Squared-Stress](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06531)
*Lilin Yan,Hongwei Zhao*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: Proves that every second-order stationary point of the complete-graph squared-stress objective in R^k has zero residual whenever k > l, resolving the one-extra-dimension conjecture for complete uniformly weighted data, with a Lean 4 formalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The one-extra-dimension conjecture for complete, uniformly weighted distance data was open. Prior results likely needed genericity assumptions or restricted configurations; this work aims for full generality including repeated and degenerate points.

Method: Combines positivity of the residual stress, polar normalization of the candidate configuration, a row-wise rank-nullity argument, and propagation of zero curvature through the full Hessian; passes from the quartic objective to its matrix formulation.

Result: Every second-order stationary point of the complete-graph squared-stress objective in R^k has zero residual whenever k > l, for any number of points, without genericity assumptions, allowing repeated points and degenerate ground-truth configurations.

Conclusion: The one-extra-dimension conjecture is resolved for complete, uniformly weighted data. The theorem and the quartic-to-matrix derivative passage are formalized in Lean 4, verified at zero trust level with a transitive axiom audit.

Abstract: Let all pairwise distances of an arbitrary configuration of points in $\mathbb{R}^{\ell}$ be known exactly. We prove that every second-order stationary point of the complete-graph squared-stress objective over configurations in $\mathbb{R}^{k}$ has zero residual whenever $k > \ell$. The result holds for every number of points, without a genericity assumption, and allows repeated points and degenerate ground-truth configurations. It resolves the one-extra-dimension conjecture for complete, uniformly weighted data. The proof combines positivity of the residual stress, polar normalization of the candidate configuration, a row-wise rank-nullity argument, and propagation of zero curvature through the full Hessian. The theorem, including the passage from derivatives of the original quartic objective to its matrix formulation, has been formalized in Lean 4. The accompanying verification records include a kernel check with zero trust level and a transitive axiom audit.

</details>


### [65] [Singular parameters and missing limits in neural PDE solvers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06770)
*Daniel Fernández*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: Neural PDE solvers may have parameters that grow without bound, so the limiting solution can lack a finite representation and the best loss is never attained. The authors link these "missing limits" in tanh networks to unbounded hidden parameters or redundant neurons, characterize the missing functions for translated-kernel models, and recover them by adding kernel derivatives to the model, making the optimum attainable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neural solvers for PDEs can approach accurate solutions while their parameters grow without bound. In such cases the limiting solution may have no finite representation in the chosen model, leaving the best loss unattainable. The paper aims to understand and fix this representational gap.

Method: The analysis connects missing limits in deep neural tanh-networks to unbounded hidden parameters or increasingly redundant neurons. For a class of models built from translated kernels, the authors characterize the missing functions and recover them by adding kernel derivatives to the model, completing the function space so the best approximation becomes attainable under standard assumptions. Numerical studies follow the parameter growth and examine how this completion affects PDE optimization.

Result: Unbounded parameters and redundant neurons explain the missing limits in tanh-networks. Adding kernel derivatives recovers the missing functions, making the best approximation attainable under standard assumptions. Numerical experiments track the associated parameter growth and show the effect of completion on PDE optimization.

Conclusion: Missing limits in neural PDE solvers arise from unbounded parameters or redundant neurons; augmenting translated-kernel models with kernel derivatives completes the model and restores attainability of the optimal loss, with numerical studies illustrating the impact on PDE optimization.

Abstract: Neural solvers for partial differential equations (PDEs) can approach an accurate solution while their parameters grow without bound. In such cases, the limiting solution may have no finite representation in the chosen model, leaving the best loss unattained. Our analysis connects missing limits in deep neural tanh- networks to unbounded hidden parameters or increasingly redundant neurons. For a class of models built from translated kernels, we describe the missing functions and recover them by adding kernel derivatives to the model. This completion makes the best approximation attainable under standard assumptions. Numerical studies follow the associated parameter growth and explore how completion affects PDE optimization.

</details>


### [66] [On the Growth Factor of Random Matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06785)
*John Urschel*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper proves new results on Gaussian elimination's growth factor for random matrices, most notably resolving Trefethen's conjecture that the growth factor of an n×n Gaussian matrix under partial pivoting is rarely much larger than √n.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gaussian elimination is the oldest and most popular linear solver, and its numerical stability is governed by the growth factor. Understanding the growth factor of random matrices, especially under partial pivoting, has been a longstanding open problem—including Trefethen's conjecture.

Method: The authors derive tight growth factor estimates for Haar-orthogonal preconditioned matrices without pivoting, characterize the asymptotic distribution of the growth factor for Gaussian matrices without pivoting, and develop techniques to analyze the partially pivoted Gaussian case.

Result: They provide tight growth factor estimates for Haar-orthogonal preconditioned matrices (no pivoting), characterize the asymptotic growth factor distribution for Gaussian matrices (no pivoting), prove the growth factor of an n×n Gaussian matrix under partial pivoting is rarely much larger than √n, and obtain an improved smoothed analysis of the growth factor under partial pivoting.

Conclusion: The work confirms and resolves Trefethen's conjecture and advances understanding of numerical stability of Gaussian elimination for random matrices, with improved smoothed analysis results.

Abstract: Gaussian elimination is the oldest and most popular method for solving a linear system. Its numerical stability for a given matrix is controlled by the growth factor, a measure of how large entries can become during elimination. In this work, we prove a number of new results regarding the growth factor of random matrices. First, we provide tight estimates for the growth factor of a matrix preconditioned by a Haar orthogonal matrix without pivoting and characterize the asymptotic distribution of the growth factor of Gaussian matrices without pivoting. Second, and most notably, we prove that the growth factor of an $n \times n$ Gaussian matrix under partial pivoting is rarely much larger than $\sqrt{n}$, resolving an old conjecture of Nick Trefethen. The same techniques used to prove this conjecture also provide an improved smoothed analysis of the growth factor under partial pivoting.

</details>


<div id='math.AP'></div>

# math.AP [[Back]](#toc)

### [67] [Lower bound on Dirac resonances with mass-type potentials in the semi-classical limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02356)
*Zhuo Chen,Michael Melgaard*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves lower bounds of order Cℏ⁻³ on the number of resonances for 3D semiclassical Dirac operators with mass-type potentials near boundary points of the relative spectral measure, matching Khochman's upper bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish sharp resonance counting lower bounds for semiclassical Dirac operators with mass-type potentials, complementing known upper bounds and connecting resonances to trapping energies.

Method: Associates a relative distribution μ to two mass functions, transfers an analytic wave-front singularity of μ to the relative classical density of states, and combines Khochman's local trace formula with a relative semiclassical trace formula (proved here for general Hermitian matrix-valued perturbations).

Result: If E₀>1 is a boundary point of supp μ, every complex neighbourhood of E₀ and of −E₀ contains at least Cℏ⁻³ resonances of the two operators (counted with multiplicity). For a single mass-type potential, D has at least Cℏ⁻³ resonances near 1+sup v and its negative for small ℏ.

Conclusion: The exponent matches Khochman's upper bound, the two resonance families are linked by spectral symmetry, and E₀ is necessarily a trapping energy; a relative semiclassical trace formula is established for general Hermitian matrix-valued perturbations.

Abstract: We establish lower bounds on the number of resonances for three-dimensional semiclassical Dirac operators with mass-type potentials. For a pair $\Dl_j=\Dl_0+\mbfβv_j$, $j=1,2$, satisfying the exterior analyticity, decay and non-degeneracy assumptions of the paper, we associate a relative distribution $μ$ to the corresponding mass functions. We prove that if $E_0>1$ is a boundary point of $\suppμ$, then every complex neighbourhood of $E_0$, and likewise of $-E_0$, contains in total at least $C\hbar^{-3}$ resonances of the two operators, counted with multiplicity. The proof transfers an analytic wave-front singularity of $μ$ to the relative classical density of states and combines this with Khochman's local trace formula and a relative semiclassical trace formula. As a consequence, if $v$ is a single bounded mass-type potential, analytic with power decay in an exterior sector, with $\inf(1+v)>0$ and $\sup v>0$, then $\Dl=\Dl_0+\mbfβv$ has at least $C\hbar^{-3}$ resonances in every complex neighbourhood of $E_0=1+\sup v$ and of $-E_0$, for all sufficiently small $\hbar$. The exponent agrees with Khochman's upper bound, the two resonance families are related by spectral symmetry, and $E_0$ is necessarily a trapping energy. We also prove the relative semiclassical trace formula needed in the argument for general Hermitian matrix-valued perturbations.

</details>


### [68] [Blow-up for the integrable defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02358)
*Dirk Hennig,Nikos I. Karachalios,Dionyssios Mantzavinos,Dimitrios Mitsotakis,Barbara Prinari*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves finite-time blow-up for the defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik lattice on infinite and finite lattices, showing the unit circle acts as a separatrix and that blow-up dominates dynamics for data outside the closed unit ball.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik equation is completely integrable, yet its global-in-time versus finite-time blow-up behavior for arbitrary (non-decaying) initial data is not fully understood. The paper aims to characterize which initial data lead to global solutions versus singular ones.

Method: Establishes local well-posedness in $\ell^\infty$ for arbitrary data with general boundary conditions. Proves the unit circle is a separatrix invariant for site moduli. Constructs explicit singular solutions with exact blow-up times and global solutions via unit-circle convergence, and uses singular solutions as subsolutions to prove blow-up dominance.

Result: Data in the closure of the $\ell^\infty$ unit ball yield global solutions; blow-up is restricted to sites with initial modulus outside the unit circle. Explicit blow-up times are constructed, and finite-time blow-up is shown to dominate the dynamics for general data outside the closed unit ball.

Conclusion: For the completely integrable defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik lattice, the dominant dynamics for general initial data outside the closed $\ell^\infty$ unit ball is finite-time blow-up, supported by analytical subsolution arguments and numerical examples.

Abstract: This work establishes finite-time blow-up for the completely integrable defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik equation on both infinite and finite lattices. We first establish local well-posedness for arbitrary initial data in the space $\ell^\infty$ of uniformly bounded sequences, with no decay assumptions and under general boundary conditions. We then show that the unit circle acts as a separatrix for the lattice dynamics: sites with an initial modulus inside, on, or outside the unit circle maintain their modulus within their respective region for as long as the solution exists. As a consequence, initial data in the closure of the $\ell^\infty$ unit ball generate global-in-time solutions. On the other hand, the unit circle separatrix opens up the possibility for finite-time blow-up, which is restricted to sites with initial modulus outside the unit circle. We establish this scenario by constructing a class of initial data whose corresponding solutions are singular, with explicitly given exact blow-up times. We also construct solutions emanating from initial data with an arbitrarily large single-site excitation outside the unit circle that exist globally in time, a behavior driven by the convergence of the corresponding modulus to the unit circle equilibrium. In the presence of such solutions, along with other important classes of analytical, global-in-time solutions that remain outside the closed $\ell^\infty$ unit ball for all times, we examine the fundamental question of the dominant dynamics for general initial data outside the closed unit ball. By utilizing the constructed class of singular solutions as subsolutions for the general problem, we prove that the dynamics for the completely integrable defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik lattice is dominated by finite-time blow-up. We conclude with numerical studies of illustrative examples of general initial data

</details>


### [69] [Strictly positive optimal Hardy weights on manifolds and graphs via Green potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02365)
*Ujjal Das,Matthias Keller,Yehuda Pinchover*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper constructs a family of strictly positive critical Hardy weights for a subcritical functional Q associated with a p-Schrödinger operator on domains in noncompact Riemannian manifolds, via supersolution construction using the Green potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study critical Hardy weights and the best Hardy constant for p-Schrödinger operators, exploring the transition between positive- and null-criticality.

Method: Supersolution construction based on the Green potential with a strictly positive charge vanishing at infinity, parametrized by ε in [0,1].

Result: A family of strictly positive critical Hardy weights is obtained, exhibiting a transition from positive-criticality to null-criticality at ε=0. The best Hardy constant is c=[(p-1)/p]^{p-1}, and for ε=0 the Hardy weight is optimal. An analogous result holds on graphs.

Conclusion: The construction yields optimal critical Hardy weights with a sharp constant, extending to graphs.

Abstract: Consider a subcritical functional $Q$ associated with a $p$-Schrödinger operator defined on a domain $Ω$ in noncompact Riemannian manifold. Employing the supersolution construction, we obtain a family of strictly positive critical Hardy weights for $Q$. The construction is based on the Green potential, with a strictly positive charge, which is assumed to vanish at infinity. An interesting new feature of this family, depending on a parameter $0\leq \varepsilon\leq 1$, is the transition from positive-criticality to null-criticality at $\varepsilon=0$. Moreover, we show that the best Hardy constant for this family is given by $c=[(p-1)/p]^{p-1}$, and that for $\varepsilon=0$ the corresponding Hardy weight is optimal. We furthermore, prove an analogous result on graphs.

</details>


### [70] [Finite-time blowup of the Euler equation without forcing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02509)
*Fan Zheng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: These notes reconstruct the finite-time blowup construction for the 3D incompressible unforced Euler equations by iteratively adding oscillatory layers to smooth parent solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To present the construction of finite-time blowup for the three-dimensional incompressible, unforced Euler equations, a long-standing open problem in fluid dynamics.

Method: Successively add oscillatory layers to smooth parent solutions; each layer is built via a finite expansion plus an exact correction, ensuring the resulting child remains an exact solution of Euler.

Result: An explicit iterative scheme producing solutions that develop finite-time blowup through accumulating oscillatory (high-frequency) layers.

Conclusion: The notes give a self-contained account of the finite-time blowup construction, with each nested layer preserving exactness of the Euler equations.

Abstract: These notes present the construction in \cite{euler} of finite-time blowup for the three-dimensional incompressible, unforced Euler equations on $\mathbb R^3$: \begin{equation}
  \partial_tu+(u\cdot\nabla)u+\nabla p=0,\qquad \nabla\cdot u=0. \end{equation} The construction successively adds oscillatory layers to smooth parent solutions. Each layer is constructed by a finite expansion and an exact correction, so that the resulting child solves Euler.

</details>


### [71] [Asymptotic behavior of the one-dimensional Vlasov--Poisson system with a repulsive harmonic potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02551)
*Huang,Wenrui*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes modified scattering and constructs modified wave operators for the 1D Vlasov–Poisson system with a repulsive harmonic potential and repulsive self-interactions, for arbitrary-size data in weighted Sobolev spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing higher-dimensional theory on this system leaves the one-dimensional case open; the authors aim to extend modified scattering results to 1D without restrictive assumptions, complementing prior work in higher dimensions.

Method: A Hamilton–Jacobi momentum correction combined with exploiting the sign of the repulsive interaction to derive large-data bounds, avoiding symmetry, compact support, or spatial moment assumptions.

Result: Modified scattering is proved for arbitrary-size initial data in weighted Sobolev spaces, and modified wave operators are constructed.

Conclusion: The results complement the higher-dimensional theory and the methods are expected to apply to a broader class of kinetic equations with very long-range interactions.

Abstract: We consider the one-dimensional Vlasov--Poisson system with a repulsive harmonic potential. For repulsive self-interactions, we establish modified scattering for initial data of arbitrary size in some weighted Sobolev spaces without symmetry, compact support, or spatial moment assumptions. The proof uses a Hamilton-Jacobi momentum correction and the sign of the repulsive interaction to obtain large-data bounds. We also construct modified wave operators. These results complement the higher-dimensional theory in \cite{HK26,VR24,BVR25}. We expect our methods to be useful in the study of a broader class of kinetic equations with very long-range interactions.

</details>


### [72] [Stability and Instability of the Unique Positive Steady State for a Nonlocal Population Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02620)
*Yuan Lou,Ming-Zhen Xin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies how spatial heterogeneity affects linear stability of unique positive steady states in a nonlocal population equation, showing uniqueness does not guarantee stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The nonlocal term makes the linearized operator non-self-adjoint and potentially violates the maximum principle, so standard principal-eigenvalue stability arguments fail, requiring new approaches.

Method: Establish linear stability for sufficiently large diffusion, and under conditions on the maximum set of the local growth ratio, for sufficiently small diffusion. In one dimension, construct unstable positive steady states and obtain Hopf bifurcations.

Result: Stability holds for large diffusion and, under suitable conditions, for small diffusion. In 1D, unstable steady states and Hopf bifurcations exist; by varying the nonlocal integral weight (with fixed local coefficients), either supercritical or subcritical Hopf bifurcation occurs.

Conclusion: Uniqueness of a positive steady state does not imply its linear stability in nonlocal population equations.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with the effect of spatial heterogeneity on the linear stability of unique positive steady states for a nonlocal population equation. The main difficulty is that, because of the nonlocal term, the linearized operator need not be self-adjoint or satisfy the maximum principle, so the usual stability arguments based on the principal eigenvalue do not apply directly. We establish linear stability for sufficiently large diffusion and, under suitable conditions on the maximum set of the local growth ratio, for sufficiently small diffusion. In one space dimension, we also construct unstable positive steady states and obtain Hopf bifurcations for sufficiently small diffusion. Keeping the local coefficients fixed, we obtain either a supercritical or a subcritical Hopf bifurcation by changing the weight in the nonlocal integral. Our results show that uniqueness of a positive steady state does not imply its linear stability.

</details>


### [73] [A Compressible Miles Instability for Wind-Wave Generation in a Neutral Atmosphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02628)
*Tianyi Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes a compressible analog of Miles' critical-layer instability for an air-water interface, proving a limiting absorption principle and deriving a residue formula that yields an unstable root under a sign condition on the gradient of specific vorticity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The incompressible theory of critical-layer instability at an air-water interface (Bühler, Shatah, Walsh, Zeng) lacks a rigorous compressible counterpart. This work aims to extend that theory to compressible Euler flow in air over incompressible water, including gravity and surface tension, for small density ratios and subsonic flow.

Method: The authors study normal-mode stability of a flat interface with quiescent water beneath a hydrostatic, constant-entropy shear flow U(z) (neutrally stratified atmosphere). They assume a small air-to-water density ratio ε and uniformly subsonic wave-relative flow. They prove a limiting absorption principle at regular, non-resonant phase speeds and a residue formula for the imaginary part of the air-side pressure response boundary value Z_a^+(k,c_r). The critical-layer singular coefficient has a residue proportional to Q = U'' + gU'/a^2 = ρ̄_0 (U'/ρ̄_0)', replacing the incompressible curvature U''.

Result: If Im Z_a^+(k,c_k) > 0 at the free gravity-capillary wave speed c_k, then for small ε the dispersion relation has exactly one unstable root c_ε near c_k, independent of ε. A sufficient condition is Q ≤ 0 at every critical level with Q < 0 at the highest. As Mach number M → 0, Z_a^+ converges to its incompressible counterpart at rate O(M^2); the unstable root satisfies |c_ε(M) − c_ε(0)| ≤ CεM^2.

Conclusion: The paper provides a rigorous compressible extension of Miles' critical-layer instability at an air-water interface, showing that the gradient of specific vorticity replaces the incompressible curvature, and that the compressible results converge to the incompressible ones as M → 0 at rate O(M^2).

Abstract: We study the normal-mode stability of a flat interface between compressible Euler flow in the air and incompressible Euler flow in the water, including gravity and surface tension. The base state is quiescent water beneath a hydrostatic, constant-entropy shear flow $U(z)$ with $U(0)=0$, so the atmosphere is neutrally stratified. For a small ratio $\varepsilon$ of the air density at the interface to the water density, and for uniformly subsonic wave-relative flow, we establish a compressible analog of the critical-layer instability of Miles, extending the rigorous incompressible theory of Bühler, Shatah, Walsh, and Zeng. For a real phase speed $c_r$, the singular coefficient at a regular critical level $U(z_c)=c_r$ has a residue proportional to $Q=U''+gU'/a^2=\barρ_0(U'/\barρ_0)'$, with gravitational acceleration $g$, sound speed $a$, and normalized base-state air density $\barρ_0$. Thus the gradient of specific vorticity replaces the curvature $U''$ of the incompressible theory.
  We prove a limiting absorption principle at regular, non-resonant phase speeds and a residue formula for the imaginary part of the boundary value $Z_{\mathrm a}^+(k,c_r)$ of the air-side pressure response at wavenumber $k>0$. Let $c_k>0$ be the phase speed of the free gravity-capillary wave. If $\mathrm{Im}\,Z_{\mathrm a}^+(k,c_k)>0$, then, for sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, the dispersion relation has exactly one unstable root $c_\varepsilon$ in a neighborhood of $c_k$ that does not depend on $\varepsilon$. A sufficient condition is $Q\le0$ at every critical level and $Q<0$ at the highest one.
  As the Mach number $M\to0$, $Z_{\mathrm a}^+$ converges to its incompressible counterpart at rate $O(M^2)$. Under the corresponding incompressible sign condition, the unstable root satisfies $|c_\varepsilon(M)-c_\varepsilon(0)|\le C\varepsilon M^2$ with $C$ independent of $\varepsilon$ and $M$.

</details>


### [74] [Hopf bifurcation in a predator-prey model with diffusion and spatial heterogeneity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02630)
*Yuan Lou,Ming-Zhen Xin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Spatial heterogeneity can destabilize a unique positive equilibrium in 1D predator-prey reaction-diffusion systems with Neumann boundary conditions, producing Hopf bifurcations into positive time-periodic solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses how spatial heterogeneity influences stability of positive equilibria in predator-prey reaction-diffusion systems, showing instability can arise even when the equilibrium is unique.

Method: Constructs smooth, spatially heterogeneous coefficient families in one-dimensional predator-prey reaction-diffusion systems with Neumann boundary conditions, and analyzes linear stability and eigenvalue behavior as a reaction parameter varies.

Result: At a critical parameter value, a simple conjugate eigenvalue pair crosses the imaginary axis transversely, and a Hopf bifurcation yields a local branch of strictly positive, nonstationary time-periodic classical solutions. Three example families are given: two with concentrated prey (one positive, one negative predator growth rate) and one with concentrated predator and negative growth rate.

Conclusion: Spatial heterogeneity alone can cause loss of stability of a unique positive equilibrium and generate nonstationary periodic solutions via Hopf bifurcation in these systems.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate how spatial heterogeneity affects the stability of positive equilibria in one-dimensional predator--prey reaction--diffusion systems with Neumann boundary conditions. Our examples show that spatial heterogeneity can lead to instability even when the positive equilibrium is unique. For the smooth, spatially heterogeneous coefficient families constructed here, this equilibrium loses linear stability as a parameter in the reaction coefficients varies. At the critical parameter value, a simple conjugate pair of eigenvalues crosses the imaginary axis transversely, and a Hopf bifurcation produces a local branch of strictly positive, nonstationary classical time-periodic solutions. The examples include two families with spatially concentrated prey, one with an everywhere positive and the other with an everywhere negative predator intrinsic growth rate. A third family has a spatially concentrated predator population and an everywhere negative predator intrinsic growth rate.

</details>


### [75] [A common structure for modified scattering: Vlasov--Riesz, Hartree, and their coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02643)
*Wenrui Huang,Mengyi Xie*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes small-data global existence and modified scattering in 3D for the Hartree equation, Vlasov--Riesz equation, and coupled Vlasov--Hartree system with potential λ|x|^{-α} (0<α≤1/2), using a unified "action-density" structure along free rays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Modified scattering for strongly long-range interactions (α≤1/2) is challenging and previously unaddressed for these models; the authors seek a common framework handling Hartree, Vlasov--Riesz, and their coupling across this whole regime.

Method: Identify a common action-density structure along free rays, where feedback between nonlinear actions and rescaled densities controls wave phase modulations and kinetic momentum translations; show the structure persists under source exchange, yielding convergent modified profiles and an explicit recursive construction of asymptotic corrections.

Result: Small-data global existence and modified scattering in three spatial dimensions for all three models; the asymptotic action contains finitely many successive corrections beyond its leading term in the strongly long-range regime.

Conclusion: These are, to the authors' knowledge, the first forward modified-scattering results for the Hartree, Vlasov--Riesz, and coupled Vlasov--Hartree models throughout the range 0<α≤1/2.

Abstract: We identify a common action--density structure for the Hartree and Vlasov--Riesz equations and their coupling. Along free rays, the feedback between nonlinear actions and rescaled densities governs wave phase modulations and kinetic momentum translations. This structure persists under the exchange of sources, providing convergent modified profiles and an explicit recursive construction of the asymptotic corrections. We thereby establish small-data global existence and modified scattering in three spatial dimensions for the Hartree equation, the Vlasov--Riesz equation, and the coupled Vlasov--Hartree system, with interaction potential $λ|x|^{-α}$, $λ\in\mathbb R$ and $0<α\leq 1/2$. In this strongly long-range regime, the asymptotic action contains finitely many successive corrections beyond its leading term. To our knowledge, these are the first forward modified-scattering results for all three models throughout this range.

</details>


### [76] [Quantitative Parabolic Decay With Bounded Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02685)
*Igor Kukavica,Qi Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Proves matching quantitative two-time lower bounds for double exponential decay of energy solutions to a drift-diffusion equation with potential, and shows this decay scale is optimal for the given coefficient classes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A bounded time-dependent drift plus an arbitrarily small real potential can force double exponential decay of the full spatial L² norm; the authors aim to establish matching lower bounds and prove this decay scale is optimal.

Method: For energy solutions of ∂_t u − Δu = v u + w·∇u on R^n (n≥3), with w ∈ L^∞_{t,x} and either v small in L^∞_t L^{n/2}_x or v ∈ L^∞_t L^p_x (n/2<p≤∞) without smallness, the authors prove quantitative two-time lower bounds. Optimality is shown by constructing, in every n≥3, a real bounded drift, an arbitrarily small real potential, and a nonzero real energy solution with double exponential decay, localizing Rowan's torus example and absorbing the localization error.

Result: A nonzero global solution satisfies a double exponential lower bound at large times. The double exponential decay scale is optimal for these coefficient classes, as demonstrated by explicit constructions in all dimensions n≥3.

Conclusion: Double exponential decay is both forced (lower bounds) and achievable (sharpness), establishing it as the optimal decay scale for these drift and potential classes.

Abstract: A bounded time-dependent drift together with an arbitrarily small real potential can produce double exponential decay of the full spatial $L^2$ norm. We prove matching quantitative two-time lower bounds for energy solutions of $\partial_tu-Δu=vu+w\cdot\nabla u$ on $\mathbb{R}^n$, for $n\geq3$, when $w\in L^\infty_{t,x}$ and either $v$ is sufficiently small in $L^\infty_tL^{n/2}_x$ or $v\in L^\infty_tL^p_x$ for some $n/2<p\leq\infty$, without a smallness assumption. In particular, a nonzero global solution satisfies a double exponential lower bound at large times. This decay scale is optimal for these coefficient classes: In every dimension $n\geq3$, we construct a real bounded drift, a real potential arbitrarily small in the critical space and in any prescribed subcritical $L^p$ space, and a nonzero real energy solution with double exponential decay. The construction localizes Rowan's torus example by absorbing the localization error.

</details>


### [77] [Continuation and threshold global strong solutions for the two-dimensional Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02714)
*Pang-hung Chung,Dan Han*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes finite-terminal continuation criteria and subthreshold global existence for the 2D Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert equation in the $H_Q^2$ strong-solution class.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding when strong solutions to the 2D Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert equation can be continued past finite time and identifying energy thresholds for global existence.

Method: Analysis of the $H_Q^2$ strong-solution class using full-frequency $L^4_{t,x}$ norm of $\nabla u$ on a terminal time window, with an ultraviolet-tail condition above a fixed dyadic cutoff.

Result: If the full-frequency $L^4_{t,x}$ norm of $\nabla u$ is finite on a terminal window, the strong solution continues; it also continues if the ultraviolet tail above one dyadic cutoff lies in $L^4_{t,x}$. Global strong solutions exist when $E(u_0)<4\pi$, raised to $8\pi$ in the degree-zero sector.

Conclusion: Provides continuation criteria and improved energy thresholds ($4\pi$, and $8\pi$ for degree-zero) for global strong solutions of the 2D Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert equation.

Abstract: This paper studies finite-terminal continuation and subthreshold global existence for the two-dimensional Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert equation in the $H_Q^2$ strong-solution class. The proof shows that if a maximal strong solution terminates at a finite time and the full-frequency $L^4_{t,x}$ norm of $\nabla u$ is finite on a terminal time window, then the strong solution can be continued. Furthermore, the solution can still be continued as long as the ultraviolet tail above one fixed dyadic cutoff belongs to $L^4_{t,x}$. As applications, we obtain global strong solutions when $E(u_0)<4π$; in the degree-zero sector, this energy threshold can be raised to $8π$.

</details>


### [78] [Large-Data Global Criteria for Two-Dimensional Critical-Energy Schrödinger Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02721)
*Pang-Hung Chung,Dan Han*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global well-posedness (large-data continuation) for the 2D sphere-valued Schrödinger map below the critical energy E(u0)<4π, showing blow-up requires persistent high-frequency concentration on late critical parabolic windows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Below-critical-energy large-data behavior for the 2D sphere-valued Schrödinger map is not fully understood. The paper aims to characterize the mechanism forcing finite-time blow-up and identify critical-norm criteria guaranteeing continuation.

Method: Works directly with the original equation using complex tangential fields W_m = ∂_m u + i u×∂_m u and ambient connections Γ_α, establishing frequency-envelope bounds governed by the critical norm ||∇u||_{ℓ²L⁴_{t,x}}.

Result: T_+ = ∞ if ||∇u||_{ℓ²L⁴([0,T_+)×ℝ²)} < ∞; also if a terminal one-window high-frequency tail A_win(t) satisfies liminf_{t↑T_+} A_win(t) < ε_*.

Conclusion: Any finite-time failure must force persistent high-frequency concentration on all sufficiently late critical parabolic windows.

Abstract: We study large-data continuation below the critical energy for the two-dimensional sphere-valued Schrödinger map \[ \partial_t u=u\timesΔu, \qquad u:[0,T_+)\times\mathbb{R}^2\to\mathbb{S}^2, \qquad E(u_0)<4π. \] Working directly with the original equation through the complex tangential fields $W_m=\partial_m u+\mathrm{i} u\times\partial_m u$ and the ambient connections $Γ_α$, we prove frequency-envelope bounds governed by the critical norm $\|\nabla u\|_{\ell^2L^4_{t,x}}$. As consequences, $T_+=\infty$ if $\|\nabla u\|_{\ell^2L^4([0,T_+)\times\mathbb{R}^2)}<\infty$, and also if a terminal one-window high-frequency tail $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{win}}(t)$ satisfies $\liminf_{t\uparrow T_+}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{win}}(t)<\varepsilon_*$. Thus any finite-time failure must force persistent high-frequency concentration on all sufficiently late critical parabolic windows.

</details>


### [79] [Lower regularity well-posedness for a higher-order Schrödinger equation with cubic nonlinearities on the half-line](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02747)
*Yuhao Xi,Shenghao Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves optimal-regularity well-posedness for the nonlinear Schrödinger equation with dispersion of order 2m and cubic nonlinearities on the half-line, via new Strichartz-based trilinear estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work by Himonas and Yan established well-posedness only for regularity s > -(m-1)/2, leaving open the question of whether the optimal regularity s = -(m-1)/2 could be reached. This paper aims to resolve that open question and handle the different nonlinearity types k.

Method: The authors derive trilinear estimates at lower regularity using a variety of Strichartz estimates, rather than the [k;Z]-multiplier norm method used in the previous work. These estimates are applied to prove well-posedness on the half-line via the standard contraction/energy framework.

Result: For general k, well-posedness is proven at the optimal regularity s = -(m-1)/2, improving the earlier s > -(m-1)/2. For k = 0 and k = 2, well-posedness holds at s = -(m-1)/2 as well. For k = 3, a stronger resonance relation yields well-posedness for s > -(2m-1)/3.

Conclusion: The paper resolves the open problem of Himonas–Yan by achieving optimal-regularity well-posedness for cNLS-2m on the half-line, with special cases k = 0, 2, 3 treated separately. The Strichartz-based approach offers an alternative to multiplier norm methods.

Abstract: In this paper, we continue the study of Himonas and Yan\cite{himonas2024schrodinger,himonas2026higher,himonas2024higher} on the nonlinear Schrödinger equation with a dispersion of order $2m$ and cubic nonlinearities,
  \[ iu_t+\left( -1 \right) ^{m+1}\partial _{x}^{2m}u=N_k\left( u,u,u \right), \]
  where, $m\ge1$ being an integer,
  \[N_0\left( u,u,u \right) =uuu,\quad N_1\left( u,u,u \right) =\bar{u}uu,\quad N_2\left( u,u,u \right) =\bar{u}\bar{u}u,\quad N_3\left( u,u,u \right) =\bar{u}\bar{u}\bar{u},\]
  We derive trilinear estimates at lower regularity and thereby prove that the cNLS-2m on the half-line is well-posed at the optimal regularity $s=-\fr{m-1}{2}$. This improves the previous result, $s>-\fr{m-1}{2}$, and answers an open question left in \cite{himonas2026higher}. In addition, for $k=0,2$, we establish well-posedness at $s=-\fr{m-1}{2}$ as well. Moreover, for $k=3$, the nonlinearity exhibits a stronger resonance relation, which implies well-posedness for $s>-\fr{2m-1}{3}$. Our derivation of the trilinear estimates relies on varieties of Strichartz estimates, which differs from the $[k;Z]$-multiplier norm method employed in \cite{himonas2026higher}.

</details>


### [80] [Initial and initial-boundary value problems for a cubic sixth-order Boussinesq equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02748)
*Yuhao Xie,Shenghao Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper improves local well-posedness for the sixth-order Boussinesq equation with cubic nonlinearity on R+ and R, in Bourgain-type spaces X^{s,b} for s > -1/2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior local well-posedness results (esfahani2025, zhong2024) needed improvement; the trilinear estimate in Bourgain-type spaces is the bottleneck requiring refinement.

Method: Refines the trilinear estimate on Bourgain-type spaces X^{s,b} by adapting Tao's multiplier method, interpolation theory, and prior work on IBVPs of dispersive equations.

Result: Local well-posedness is improved in X^{s,b} for s > -1/2 with b = 1/2 and b > 1/2 accordingly, on both R+ and R.

Conclusion: The refined trilinear estimate yields improved local well-posedness for the sixth-order Boussinesq equation with cubic nonlinearity.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider the sixth-order Boussinesq equation with a cubic nonlinearity,
  \[ u_{tt}-u_{xx}+k u_{xxxx}-u_{xxxxxx}+\left( u^3 \right) _{xx}=0, \ \ \mbox{with }k=\pm 1,\]
  posed on both $\mathbb{R}^+$ and $\mathbb{R}$. The local wellposedness are improved in related Bourgain-type spaces, $X^{s,b}$, for $s>-\frac12$ with $b=\frac12$ and $b>\frac12$ accordingly (See. \cite{esfahani2025well,zhong2024local}). The key ingredient is to refine the trilinear estimate on Burgain type space by adapting ideas from Tao's multiplier method, interpolation theory and our early work on IBVPs of dispersive equations \cite{li2020low,tao2001multilinear}.

</details>


### [81] [Sharp Liouville thresholds and endpoint rigidity for finite Morse index solutions of the $p$-Laplace Lane--Emden equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02751)
*Phuong Le*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper removes the radial symmetry assumption in the classification of stable-outside-a-compact-set solutions to the p-Laplace equation -Δ_p u = |u|^{q-1}u, settling the whole range p-1<q<q_c(N,p), q≠p*-1, via an iterated comparison against an explicit supersolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Damascelli-Farina-Sciunzi-Valdinoci settled the subcritical range for p>2 and the supercritical range p*-1<q<q_c only for radial solutions; for p=2 the arguments rely on a monotonicity formula that has no analogue when p≠2, so the non-radial case was left open.

Method: Iterate the comparison principle against an explicit two-parameter supersolution, upgrading the decay forced by stability to that of the p-harmonic fundamental solution; this separate step uses no stability, only smallness of the self-similar decay.

Result: Every C^1 weak solution stable outside a compact set is trivial for p*-1<q<q_c(N,p); the whole range p-1<q<q_c with q≠p*-1 is settled (as for p=2 by Farina), and every finite Morse index solution is trivial. Sharp upper threshold: for q>q_c positive bounded radial stable solutions exist. At the Sobolev endpoint every C^1 stable solution has finite energy; for p>2 this removes the a priori D^{1,p} assumption and shows Aubin-Talenti extremals have Morse index one. At the upper endpoint q_c, exactly two homogeneous weak solutions on R^N\{0} are stable outside a ball.

Conclusion: The radial hypothesis is removed and the full picture for p-1<q<q_c (q≠p*-1) is completed, matching Farina's theorem for p=2, with sharp thresholds confirmed by explicit examples and consequences for Morse index and the Aubin-Talenti extremals.

Abstract: We study $-Δ_p u = |u|^{q-1}u$ in $\mathbb{R}^N$ with $N>p\ge 2$, for solutions that are stable outside a compact set, with no assumption of sign, boundedness or symmetry. Damascelli, Farina, Sciunzi and Valdinoci settled the subcritical range for $p>2$, and treated the supercritical range $p^*-1<q<q_c(N,p)$ only for radial solutions, stating that without radial symmetry they were not able to conclude. The obstruction is that the arguments available for $p=2$ rest on a monotonicity formula with no monotone analogue when $p\ne2$.
  We remove the radial hypothesis: every $C^1$ weak solution stable outside a compact set is trivial when $p^*-1<q<q_c(N,p)$. The whole range $p-1<q<q_c(N,p)$ with $q\ne p^*-1$ is thereby settled, as it is for $p=2$ by Farina's theorem, and every finite Morse index solution in it is trivial. In its place we iterate the comparison principle against an explicit two-parameter supersolution, upgrading the decay forced by stability to that of the $p$-harmonic fundamental solution; this step uses no stability, only smallness of the self-similar decay, and is stated on its own. The upper threshold is sharp: for every $q>q_c(N,p)$ we exhibit positive bounded radial solutions stable outside a compact set. At the Sobolev endpoint, every $C^1$ solution stable outside a compact set has finite energy; for $p>2$ this removes the a priori $\mathcal D^{1,p}$ assumption from the low-Morse-index classification of Farina, Mercuri and Willem and answers their question on the Morse index of the Aubin--Talenti extremals: it is one. At the upper endpoint, $q_c(N,p)$ is the exact threshold for a nonzero homogeneous weak solution on $\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\}$ to be stable outside a ball, and at the threshold there are exactly two.

</details>


### [82] [The critical boundary value for a nonlinear Schrödinger equation in a half-space: rigidity and dimensional transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02790)
*Phuong Le*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies bounded positive solutions of a threshold boundary value problem for -Δv+v=v^p on the half-space with boundary value c_p. It establishes a dimension-dependent rigidity/nonuniqueness picture: uniqueness for 2≤N≤5, nonuniqueness for N≥8, and nonuniqueness in N=7 whenever a reduced cubic coefficient κ(p) is positive (in particular for p≥2).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: This is the threshold boundary value left open by Fernández and Weth (Math. Ann. 383 (2022), 361-397). The problem concerns the borderline case where solutions are expected to possibly exhibit non-one-dimensional behavior, and understanding rigidity versus nonuniqueness is the central open question.

Method: The proof combines a boundary-adapted Modica estimate, projection onto the kernel of the one-dimensional linearized operator, an automatic tail non-concentration estimate, and a nonlinear cell problem yielding the exact reduced nonlinearity. For N≥8 the reduced linearization is invertible in weighted spaces; in N=7 the critical resonance is bypassed by solving the projected radial equation nonlinearly and closing the transverse equation via a Schauder-Tychonoff fixed point.

Result: For every p>1, the profile w_0(x_N) is the unique bounded positive solution when 2≤N≤5. For N≥8 there is a one-parameter family of bounded positive non-one-dimensional solutions. In N=7, nonuniqueness holds whenever the cubic coefficient κ(p) of the exact reduced nonlinearity is positive, in particular for all p≥2. The amplitudes have Fowler ends, with limiting energy, neck size, bulge size, and logarithmic period determined to leading order. The cases N=6 and the complementary range in N=7 remain open.

Conclusion: The paper resolves most of the open threshold problem, giving a sharp dimension-dependent rigidity/nonuniqueness dichotomy: rigidity in low dimensions (N≤5) and nonuniqueness in high dimensions (N≥8) and in the critical dimension N=7 (conditionally on κ(p)>0). The cases N=6 and part of N=7 are left open as remaining challenges.

Abstract: Let $w_0$ be the positive homoclinic solution of $-w''+w=w^p$ and set $c_p=w_0(0)$. We study bounded positive solutions of \[ -Δv+v=v^p\quad\text{in }\mathbb R^N_+, \qquad v=c_p\quad\text{on }\partial\mathbb R^N_+, \qquad v(x',x_N)\to0 \] uniformly as $x_N\to\infty$. This is the threshold boundary value left open by Fernández and Weth [Math.\ Ann.\ \textbf{383} (2022), 361--397]. We prove a dimension-dependent rigidity/nonuniqueness picture. For every $p>1$, the profile $w_0(x_N)$ is the unique bounded positive solution when $2\le N\le5$, whereas for $N\ge8$ there is a one-parameter family of bounded positive non-one-dimensional solutions. In the critical dimension $N=7$, nonuniqueness holds whenever a cubic coefficient $κ(p)$ of the exact reduced nonlinearity is positive; in particular $κ(p)>0$ for all $p\ge2$, and continuity extends this range below $2$. The corresponding amplitudes have Fowler ends, with their limiting energy, neck size, bulge size, and logarithmic period determined to leading order. The case $N=6$, as well as the complementary range in $N=7$, remain open.
  The proof combines a boundary-adapted Modica estimate, projection onto the kernel of the one-dimensional linearized operator, an automatic tail non-concentration estimate, and a nonlinear cell problem yielding the exact reduced nonlinearity. For $N\ge8$ the reduced linearization is invertible in weighted spaces. In $N=7$ the critical resonance is bypassed by solving the projected radial equation nonlinearly and closing the transverse equation by a Schauder--Tychonoff fixed point.

</details>


### [83] [Boundedness of Solutions to Nonlocal Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02810)
*Serena Dipierro,João Gonçalves da Silva,Giorgio Poggesi,Enrico Valdinoci*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes uniform L∞ bounds for all distributional solutions of multi-dimensional nonlocal semilinear systems (Ginzburg-Landau and Allen-Cahn type), including new results for scalar equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Distributional (weak) solutions of such nonlocal semilinear systems may a priori be unbounded; obtaining uniform L∞ bounds under mild mass/energy assumptions is a nontrivial regularity question, and existing results do not cover this multi-dimensional nonlocal setting.

Method: Under mild mass or energy assumptions, the authors derive uniform L∞ bounds for all distributional solutions of a multi-dimensional system of nonlocal semilinear equations, covering Ginzburg-Landau systems and Allen-Cahn equations.

Result: Uniform L∞ bounds are proved for all distributional solutions in the considered nonlocal setting; the results are new even in the scalar case.

Conclusion: The work provides new boundedness (regularity) results for nonlocal semilinear systems and scalar equations, with implications for Ginzburg-Landau and Allen-Cahn models.

Abstract: Under mild mass or energy assumptions, we prove uniform $L^\infty$ bounds for all distributional solutions of a multi-dimensional system of nonlocal semilinear equations.
  The setting comprises Ginzburg-Landau systems and Allen-Cahn equations, and the results obtained are new even for scalar equations.

</details>


### [84] [Sign-changing solutions for the critical Neumann problem in the three-dimensional unit ball](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02857)
*Fei Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence of infinitely many distinct nonradial sign-changing solutions to the Neumann problem Δu−μu+|u|^4u=0 in the unit ball for every μ>0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish nonradial sign-changing solutions for a critical Neumann problem with arbitrary parameter μ>0, extending understanding beyond known radial or positive solutions.

Method: Inner–outer gluing scheme combined with finite-dimensional reduction; gluing K symmetrically arranged transformed copies of a sign-changing entire solution of the critical equation in R^3, using a detailed expansion of the reduced energy to locate an interior critical point.

Result: For every μ>0, the problem admits infinitely many distinct nonradial sign-changing solutions; for each sufficiently large even integer K, a solution is constructed.

Conclusion: Infinitely many nonradial sign-changing solutions exist for all μ>0, achieved via gluing and energy expansion techniques.

Abstract: We consider the existence of nonradial sign-changing solutions to the problem $Δu-μu+|u|^4u=0$ with zero Neumann boundary conditions in the unit ball for an arbitrary fixed parameter $μ>0$. We prove that, for every $μ>0$, this problem admits infinitely many distinct nonradial sign-changing solutions. More precisely, for every sufficiently large even integer $K$, we construct a solution by gluing $K$ symmetrically arranged, suitably transformed copies of a sign-changing entire solution of the critical equation in $\mathbb{R}^3$. The construction combines an inner--outer gluing scheme with a finite-dimensional reduction. A detailed expansion of the reduced energy allows us to locate an interior critical point in the admissible parameter region and thereby complete the construction.

</details>


### [85] [On the derivative loss for a class of abstract evolution equations with time dependent oscillating coefficients vanishing at initial time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02915)
*Marina Ghisi,Massimo Gobbino,Michael Reissig*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies derivative loss for an abstract wave-type evolution equation whose time-dependent propagation speed is a product of a possibly degenerate monotone shape function and a bounded oscillating factor, quantifying how degeneracy and oscillations jointly determine regularity loss.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Propagation speeds in wave-type equations can both degenerate and oscillate in time, and it is unclear how these two effects interact in causing solutions to lose derivatives. The paper aims to characterize this interaction precisely.

Method: The problem is reduced via spectral analysis to a family of ODEs, yielding frequency-dependent energy estimates. The proof decomposes the time interval into frequency-dependent regions and uses different adapted energies tailored to each region.

Result: In the purely degenerate case, monotonicity of the shape function prevents excessive loss, and solutions lose at most one derivative. With oscillations, the derivative loss is given by a minimization problem in which degenerate and oscillatory effects genuinely interact.

Conclusion: Degeneracy alone causes at most one derivative of loss, while oscillations can couple with degeneracy to produce a more complex, minimization-determined loss, captured through frequency-adapted energy methods.

Abstract: We consider an abstract wave-type evolution equation with a propagation speed depending only on time. We assume that this propagation speed is the product of a monotone, but possibly degenerate, shape function and an oscillating factor bounded from above and from below by positive constants. We investigate how degeneracy and oscillations interact in determining the derivative loss of solutions.
  After reducing the problem to a family of ordinary differential equations through spectral analysis, we derive frequency-dependent energy estimates. In the purely degenerate case, monotonicity of the shape function prevents excessive loss, and solutions lose at most one derivative. In the presence of oscillations, the resulting derivative loss is described by a minimization problem in which the degenerate and oscillatory effects genuinely interact.
  The proof is based on a decomposition of the time interval into frequency-dependent regions, where different adapted energies are used.

</details>


### [86] [Normalized ground states and a mass-constrained scattering threshold for the inhomogeneous NLS with an inverse-square potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02933)
*Mohamed Majdoub,Tarek Saanouni*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Connects scattering theory below the ground-state threshold with variational analysis of prescribed-mass solutions for the focusing inhomogeneous NLS with inverse-square potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Two research lines on this INLS model—scattering below the ground-state threshold and variational analysis with prescribed mass—have developed largely independently, and this work aims to bridge them.

Method: Studies the focusing inhomogeneous NLS with inverse-square potential in the mass-supercritical, energy-subcritical regime via both scattering-theoretic and variational (prescribed-mass) approaches.

Result: Establishes a connection between the scattering theory below the ground-state threshold and the variational analysis of solutions with prescribed mass.

Conclusion: The two approaches for this model are unified, linking scattering below the ground state with prescribed-mass variational analysis.
