Abstract: We study the focusing inhomogeneous nonlinear Schrödinger equation with an inverse-square potential $$ i\partial_t u+Δu-a|x|^{-2}u+|x|^{-b}|u|^αu=0, \qquad (t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}, $$ in the mass-supercritical and energy-subcritical regime $$ \frac{4-2b}{N}<α<\frac{4-2b}{N-2}, $$ where $N\ge3$ and $0<b<2$. Two main lines of research have developed largely independently for this model: scattering theory below the ground-state threshold and the variational analysis of solutions with prescribed mass. In this work, we establish a connection between these two approaches.

</details>


### [87] [Global existence for viscous systems of conservation laws in $L^p$ framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03011)
*Jean-Paul Adogbo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies global well-posedness of partially diffusive hyperbolic systems in critical homogeneous Besov spaces with L^p-based high-frequency control (p>2), extending prior work and applying results to the compressible Fourier-Navier-Stokes system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend the authors' previous analysis to a functional framework where high-frequency components are controlled in L^p-based spaces with p>2. This broader setting enables global existence under weaker smallness assumptions on initial data and provides finer qualitative and regularity information.

Method: Analysis conducted in critical homogeneous Besov spaces, exploiting L^p-based (p>2) control of high-frequency components, building on the approach from the authors' earlier work.

Result: Establishes global existence under weaker smallness conditions on initial data, yields refined regularity, and provides more precise qualitative behavior of solutions.

Conclusion: The global existence theorem applies to the multidimensional compressible Fourier-Navier-Stokes system, demonstrating the broader applicability of the framework.

Abstract: In this paper, which originates from Chapter 4 of the author's PhD thesis [1, Chap. 4], we investigate the global well-posedness of a class of partially diffusive hyperbolic systems in critical homogeneous Besov spaces. Our main objective is to extend the analysis developed in our previous work [2] to a functional framework in which the high-frequency com- ponents of the solution are controlled in $L^p$-based spaces with $p > 2$. This broader framework allows us to establish global existence under weaker smallness assumptions on the initial data and provides more precise information on the qualitative behavior of the resulting solutions. In particular, our analysis yields refined regularity. As an application, our global existence theorem applies to the multidimensional compressible Fourier-Navier-Stokes system

</details>


### [88] [Obstructions to exact regularity of sublaplacians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03037)
*Gian Maria Dall'Ara*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper examines when exact regularity holds for a sublaplacian equation on a manifold with a characteristic submanifold, proving failure via "worm sublaplacians" modeled on Kohn Laplacians.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study is motivated by the role of characteristic submanifolds—tangent to all vector fields—in causing breakdowns of regularity, drawing on real analogues of phenomena from several complex variables.

Method: The main tool is a scaling lemma inspired by Barrett and Christ's work on the ∂̄-Neumann problem, applied to worm-like sublaplacians.

Result: The authors prove that exact regularity fails for a class of worm sublaplacians.

Conclusion: Characteristic submanifolds can indeed cause failure of exact regularity for sublaplacian equations.

Abstract: Let $M$ be a closed manifold equipped with a collection of smooth real vector fields $X_1,\ldots, X_k$ and a smooth measure $μ$. Let $P:=\sum_j X_j^\dagger X_j $ be the associated sublaplacian. Under the assumption that each pair of points of $M$ can be connected by a path obtained by joining integral curves of the vector fields, the equation \[ Pu=f \] admits unique zero-average solutions $u$ in the natural energy space, for all zero average data $f\in L^2$. We say that exact regularity holds for the above equation if $u\in H^k(M)$ whenever $f\in H^k(M)$, where $H^k(M)$ is the $L^2$ based Sobolev space of any order $k\in \mathbb{N}$. We investigate how the presence of a characteristic submanifold, namely a submanifold tangent to all vector fields $X_j$, may cause a failure of exact regularity. We prove that this indeed happens for a class of "worm sublaplacians", which are real analogues of Kohn Laplacians on Diederich--Fornaess worm domains, of interest in several complex variables. Our main tool is a scaling lemma inspired by work of Barrett and Christ on the $\bar\partial$-Neumann problem.

</details>


### [89] [A Comparison Pogorelov Estimate for Graphical $σ_k$-Curvature Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03062)
*Jundong Zhou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes comparison Pogorelov estimates for admissible solutions of graphical σ_k-curvature equations, generalizing prior two-surface estimates for scalar curvature to the σ_k setting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work extends the two-surface estimate of Qiu and Yan for the graphical scalar curvature equation to the broader class of graphical σ_k-curvature equations, addressing more general curvature settings and data types.

Method: The proof combines the two-surface localization and mixed Gårding comparison of Qiu and Yan with Yan's spectral concavity inequality and an angle-function weight. The comparison graph is assumed only to be k-admissible, with bounded slope and a positive interior gap vanishing on the boundary.

Result: Comparison Pogorelov estimates are obtained covering 2 ≤ k < n for prescribed data f(x,u), and n/2 ≤ k < n for general data f(x,u,Du).

Conclusion: The estimates extend two-surface comparison results to graphical σ_k-curvature equations under mild admissibility assumptions on the comparison graph.

Abstract: We establish comparison Pogorelov estimates for admissible solutions of graphical $σ_k$-curvature equations, extending the two-surface estimate of Qiu and Yan for the graphical scalar curvature equation. The comparison graph is assumed only to be $k$-admissible, with a bounded slope and a positive interior gap that vanishes on the boundary. The estimates cover $2\le k<n$ for prescribed data $f(x,u)$, and $n/2\le k<n$ for general data $f(x,u,Du)$. The proof combines the two-surface localization and mixed Gårding comparison of Qiu and Yan with Yan's spectral concavity inequality and an angle-function weight.

</details>


### [90] [Döblin--Fourier cancellation and kinetic Aleksandrov estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03083)
*Amélie Loher,Connor Mooney,Clément Mouhot*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a cancellation mechanism for linear second-order kinetic equations with rough (measurable, uniformly elliptic) coefficients, yielding exponential Fourier decay with enhanced dissipation, and applies it to kinetic Aleksandrov estimates and enhanced dissipation on torus/sphere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish decay and regularity for kinetic equations with rough (non-smooth) coefficients, where classical approaches via smooth coefficients fail, providing first decay results for such equations.

Method: Decompose dynamics into spatial waves and follow two velocity paths acquiring nearly opposite phases; use parabolic Krylov–Safonov theory for a common lower bound on velocity marginals; cancel contributions up to small phase error to get a Döblin-type contraction; iterate to obtain exponential Fourier decay with enhanced dissipation.

Result: Kinetic Aleksandrov estimate (max principle with L^p source) for p>2n+1 (rough A(t,v)); for autonomous 1D coefficients, p>4 via Harnack comparison. Both thresholds optimal. Second application: enhanced dissipation with optimal square-root frequency power, Gevrey regularity in position, and for autonomous A(v): exponential L^2 convergence to equilibrium and quantitative spectral gap.

Conclusion: The cancellation mechanism yields exponential Fourier decay and enables optimal kinetic Aleksandrov estimates and enhanced dissipation; these are the first decay results for laws of kinetic equations with rough coefficients.

Abstract: We develop a cancellation mechanism for linear second-order kinetic equations in non-divergence form with measurable uniformly elliptic coefficients. We decompose the dynamics into spatial waves and follow two families of velocity paths along which the waves acquire nearly opposite phases. The parabolic Krylov--Safonov theory gives a common lower bound for the two velocity marginals. The corresponding contributions cancel up to a small phase error, producing a contraction \textit{à la Döblin}. Iterating this contraction yields exponential Fourier decay estimates with enhanced dissipation.
  We then present two applications.
  The first contribution is a kinetic Aleksandrov estimate: a maximum principle in which the source is measured in an $L^p$ norm. For rough coefficients $A(t,v)$ independent of position, we obtain the estimate for every $p>2n+1$, where $n$ is the dimension of position and velocity. The Fourier decay also gives spatial smoothness, and parabolic regularity gives Hölder continuity in time and velocity. For autonomous coefficients $a(x,v)$ in dimension one, position serves as time away from zero velocity. A Harnack comparison controls returns to small velocity intervals and yields the estimate for every $p>4$. Both thresholds are optimal among those valid for all ellipticity ratios.
  The second contribution concerns position on the torus and velocity on the sphere, with measurable uniformly elliptic diffusion coefficients $A(t,v)$ independent of position. We prove enhanced dissipation with the optimal square-root frequency power and Gevrey regularity in position. For autonomous coefficients $A=A(v)$, we also obtain exponential convergence to equilibrium in $L^2$ weighted by the invariant velocity measure, and a quantitative spectral gap. To our knowledge, these are the first results on decay established for laws of kinetic equations with rough coefficients.

</details>


### [91] [Singular Contour Dynamics and Paradifferential Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03091)
*Xingyu Li,Emeric Roulley,Stefano Scrobogna*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A structure-preserving paralinearization theorem for nonlinear graph integral operators is developed and applied to 3D two-phase free-boundary Euler equations, yielding local well-posedness in the stable KRT regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Contour dynamics gives rise to nonlinear graph integral operators, but existing analyses often rely on a Dirichlet–Neumann reduction. A direct paradifferential treatment is needed to capture the full symbolic structure and preserve key structural properties (e.g., skew-adjointness) uniformly across the critical case.

Method: Abstract structure-preserving paralinearization theorem for homogeneous kernels with nonlinear graph dependence via normalized finite differences, valid in arbitrary dimension and covering critical principal-value and subcritical locally integrable kernels; explicit principal symbols; separation of finite-order symbolic, order-zero far-field, and smoothing terms; intermediate symbolic order cancellation; applied to Birkhoff–Rott singular integrals via canonical surface variables, then Alinhac good unknown and diagonalization.

Result: Explicit principal symbol formulas and a clean decomposition for graph integral operators; exact skew-adjoint preservation at the critical endpoint; complete paradifferential structure of the 3D two-phase free-boundary Euler system with constant interfacial background vorticity without using Dirichlet–Neumann; local well-posedness for small Sobolev perturbations in the stable Kelvin–Rayleigh–Taylor regime, uniform on compact subsets of the stable parameter set.

Conclusion: A direct, dimension-free paradifferential framework provides a robust route to analyzing contour-dynamics integral operators and establishes uniform local well-posedness for the 3D two-phase free-boundary Euler equations in the stable KRT regime.

Abstract: We develop a direct paradifferential approach to nonlinear graph integral operators arising in contour dynamics. The main methodological result is an abstract structure-preserving paralinearization theorem, valid in arbitrary dimension, for a class of homogeneous kernels whose nonlinear dependence on the graph is expressed through normalized finite differences. The theorem covers both critical principal-value kernels and subcritical locally integrable kernels, gives explicit formulas for the principal symbols, and separates the finite-order symbolic contribution generated by the homogeneous singularity from order-zero far-field terms and arbitrarily smoothing remainders. A structural feature of the reduction is the cancellation of the intermediate symbolic order; at the critical endpoint the construction also preserves the exact skew-adjoint structure of the original singular integral.
  As a main application, we consider the three-dimensional two-phase free-boundary Euler equations with constant interfacial background vorticity. We first derive an autonomous contour-dynamics equation in canonical surface variables and then apply the abstract theorem directly to the singular integral operators generated by the Birkhoff--Rott formulation. This yields the complete paradifferential structure of the system without taking the Dirichlet--Neumann reduction as a starting point. After introducing an Alinhac good unknown and diagonalizing the resulting system, we obtain local well-posedness for small Sobolev perturbations in the stable Kelvin--Rayleigh--Taylor regime, uniformly on compact subsets of the stable parameter set.

</details>


### [92] [Weak-BV stability of inflow and outflow problems for the isentropic Euler system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03236)
*Moon-Jin Kang,Jiayun Meng,HyeonSeop Oh,Alexis F. Vasseur*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves well-posedness, uniqueness, and weak-BV stability of small BV subsonic solutions to the 1D isentropic Euler system on a half-line with inflow and outflow boundary conditions defined via Navier-Stokes boundary layers and zero-speed shocks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Boundary conditions for the isentropic Euler system are subtle: admissible trace sets arise from viscosity (Navier-Stokes boundary layers) and zero-speed shocks. Prior stability/uniqueness theory for inflow/outflow initial-boundary value problems in BV was lacking, especially a weak-BV framework that does not rely on front tracking or bounded variation of the comparison solution.

Method: Construct small BV subsonic solutions for both inflow and outflow problems. Formulate boundary conditions via admissible trace sets. Use the a-contraction framework, verifying compatibility of the boundary set with it, and show the front-tracking limit satisfies the prescribed inflow/outflow conditions.

Result: Existence and uniqueness of small BV subsonic solutions for inflow and outflow problems, with a quantitative stability estimate \|U(·,t)-V(·,t)\|_{L^2} ≲ sqrt(\|U(·,0)-V(·,0)\|_{L^2}) holding for any small BV solution V and any L^∞ entropy solution U satisfying the strong trace property and the boundary conditions.

Conclusion: Establishes the first weak-BV stability and uniqueness theory for inflow and outflow initial-boundary value problems. The comparison solution U may lie outside the subsonic region and need not have bounded variation or be constructed via front tracking. Key difficulties: boundary-set compatibility with a-contraction and verifying the front-tracking limit meets inflow/outflow conditions.

Abstract: We study the well-posedness of small BV solutions to the one-dimensional isentropic Euler system on the half-line under inflow and outflow boundary conditions. These boundary conditions are formulated in terms of admissible trace sets determined by Navier--Stokes boundary layers and zero-speed shocks. For both the inflow and outflow problems, we construct small BV solutions taking values in the subsonic region. These solutions are unique and satisfy the quantitative stability estimate \begin{align*}
  \|U(\cdot, t)-V(\cdot, t)\|_{L^2} \lesssim \sqrt{\|U(\cdot, 0) - V(\cdot, 0)\|_{L^2}} \, , \end{align*} which holds for any such small BV solution $V$ and any $L^\infty$ entropy solution $U$ satisfying the strong trace property and the inflow/outflow boundary conditions. In particular, we emphasize that $U$ may take values outside the subsonic region and need not have bounded variation or be constructed by the front tracking scheme. This establishes the first weak-BV stability and uniqueness theory for inflow and outflow initial-boundary value problems. A key difficulty lies in verifying that the boundary set is compatible with the $a$-contraction framework, and in proving that the front-tracking limit satisfies the prescribed inflow/outflow conditions.

</details>


### [93] [Quantitative homogenization of Monge-Ampère equations in periodic media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03269)
*Tianling Jin,YanYan Li,Hung V. Tran,Xushan Tu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves optimal homogenization error rates for the Monge-Ampère equation with oscillating coefficients, showing $\|u^\varepsilon-u\|_{L^\infty}\leq C\varepsilon^{m+\alpha}$ for $m=0,1$, along with locally weighted estimates for periodic Borel measures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quantitative homogenization rates for fully nonlinear equations like the Monge-Ampère equation are less understood than for linear elliptic problems, motivating sharp error estimates in appropriate regularity scales.

Method: Compare convex solutions of $\det D^2u^\varepsilon=F(x,x/\varepsilon)$ with the homogenized equation $\det D^2u=\overline F(x)$ under identical Dirichlet data, estimating $L^\infty$ differences using regularity assumptions on $u$ ($C^{m+2,\alpha}$) and $F$ ($C_x^{m,\alpha}\times C_y^{0,\gamma}$).

Result: For $m=0,1$ and $0<\alpha\leq1$, $\|u^\varepsilon-u\|_{L^\infty(\Omega)}\leq C\varepsilon^{m+\alpha}$ for all $0<\varepsilon\leq1$; both exponents are optimal in their regularity scales. Also obtains an $O(\varepsilon^\alpha)$ rate for locally weighted periodic Borel measures with constants independent of the microscopic measure's distribution.

Conclusion: The results establish sharp homogenization rates for the Monge-Ampère equation and extend them to weighted periodic Borel measures, providing optimal error control in the relevant regularity scales.

Abstract: Let $u^\varepsilon$ and $u$ be the convex solutions of \[ \det D^2u^\varepsilon=F(x,x/\varepsilon),\qquad \det D^2u=\overline F(x) \] on a bounded convex domain $Ω$, with the same Dirichlet data. Here, $F$ is uniformly positive and periodic in its second variable, and $\overline F(x)=\int_{\mathbb T^n}F(x,y)\,dy$. For $m=0,1$ and $0<α\leq1$, we prove \[ \|u^\varepsilon-u\|_{L^\infty(Ω)}\leq C\varepsilon^{m+α},\qquad \forall\,0<\varepsilon\leq1, \] provided that $u\in C^{m+2,α}(\overlineΩ)$ and $F\in C_x^{m,α}(\overlineΩ;C_y^{0,γ}(\mathbb T^n))$ for some $0<γ<1$. Both exponents are optimal in their respective regularity scales. We also establish the $O(\varepsilon^α)$ rate for locally weighted periodic Borel measures, with constants independent of the distribution of the microscopic measure.

</details>


### [94] [On the instability of De Gregorio excited states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03287)
*Miguel M. G. Pascual-Caballo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: All excited states $-\sin(nx)$ ($n\geq2$) of the De Gregorio equation on the torus are nonlinearly unstable (Hadamard sense); each linearized operator has a real eigenvalue $\lambda_n\in(0.56,0.6)$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish nonlinear (Hadamard) instability of the excited states of the De Gregorio equation, extending beyond the trivial state to all $n\geq2$.

Method: Transform the eigenvalue problem into a Fuchsian equation on the complex disk to obtain a matching condition for the eigenvalue, verified rigorously via interval arithmetic; prove nonlinear instability through a bootstrap argument in the spirit of Guo, Hallstrom, and Spirn.

Result: For every $n\geq2$, the linearized operator admits a real eigenvalue $\lambda_n\in(0.56,0.6)$, and arbitrarily small $H^2$ perturbations leave a fixed $H^1$ neighborhood of the state.

Conclusion: The excited states $-\sin(nx)$ of the De Gregorio equation on the torus are nonlinearly unstable in the sense of Hadamard for all $n\geq2$.

Abstract: We prove that all excited states $-\sin(nx)$, $n\geq2$, of the De Gregorio equation on the torus are nonlinearly unstable in the sense of Hadamard. More precisely, perturbations arbitrarily small in $H^2$ leave a fixed $H^1$ neighborhood of the state. For every $n\geq2$, the linearized operator has a real eigenvalue $λ_n\in(0.56,0.6)$. To prove this, we transform the eigenvalue problem into a Fuchsian equation on the complex disk and obtain a matching condition for the eigenvalue. This condition is verified rigorously using interval arithmetic. The nonlinear instability follows from a bootstrap argument in the spirit of Guo, Hallstrom, and Spirn.

</details>


### [95] [Well-posedness for the generalised fractional BBM equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03288)
*Jackson Barratt*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes improved low-regularity local/global well-posedness for generalized fractional BBM equations on the torus, proves endpoint global well-posedness for the cubic regularized Benjamin-Ono equation, and shows strong ill-posedness for negative Sobolev indices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior well-posedness results for fractional BBM equations (Bona–Chen 2003; Kim–Kwak 2026) were limited to higher regularity regimes, leaving low-regularity behavior on H^s(T^d) and negative-index ill-posedness largely unexplored.

Method: Analysis of a generalized family of fractional BBM equations on the d-dimensional torus T^d (d≥1), using low-regularity techniques on the H^s(T^d) scale and L^{k+1}(T^d) spaces (k the polynomial nonlinearity order), combined with randomization of initial data.

Result: Improved low-regularity local and global well-posedness on H^s(T^d); endpoint global well-posedness for the cubic regularized Benjamin-Ono equation in H^{1/2}(T); well-posedness in L^{k+1}(T^d); almost sure global well-posedness in H^s(T^d) for s≥0 under suitable randomization; and strong ill-posedness (infinite loss of regularity at all initial data) for s<0.

Conclusion: The generalized fractional BBM family is well-posed in low regularity (and almost surely for randomized data in H^s, s≥0), while exhibiting sharp negative-regularity ill-posedness; the endpoint BO result further sharpens the known theory.

Abstract: In this paper, we study a generalised family of fractional BBM equations on the $d$ dimensional torus $\mathbb{T}^d$ for $d\geq 1$. We establish low regularity local and global well-posedness results on the $H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$ scale that improve upon the works of Bona and Chen (2003) and Kim and Kwak (2026). We also prove endpoint global well-posedness for the cubic regularised Benjamin-Ono equation in $H^{1/2}(\mathbb{T})$. Furthermore, we obtain a well-posedness result for the generalised fractional BBM in the spaces $L^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^d)$ where $k$ is order of the polynomial nonlinearity. As an application of this result, we show that for a certain range of values and for a suitable randomisation of the initial data, the model is almost surely globally well-posed in $H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$ for $s\geq 0$. Lastly, we show that the family exhibits a strong form of ill-posedness in $H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$ for $s<0$ in the form of an infinite loss of regularity at all initial datum.

</details>


### [96] [Geometrically distinct unbounded continua of solutions for Yamabe-type equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03292)
*Piotr Stefaniak*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence of unbounded continua of sign-changing solutions for symmetric Yamabe-type equations on closed manifolds, with applications to spheres and projective spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Global bifurcation theory for Yamabe-type equations on manifolds with symmetries, especially in critical and supercritical regimes, is not fully understood. The authors seek to establish unbounded continua of solutions and lower bounds on their number.

Method: Global bifurcation analysis for Yamabe-type equations on closed Riemannian manifolds with isometric group actions having interval orbit space; bifurcation from positive eigenvalues of the Laplace-Beltrami operator admitting invariant eigenfunctions, over a suitable range of exponents.

Result: Pairwise disjoint unbounded continua bifurcate from positive Laplace-Beltrami eigenvalues; all nontrivial solutions in these continua change sign. Results cover critical and some supercritical exponents. Lower bounds on geometrically distinct continua from the same eigenvalue are obtained on spheres and complex and quaternionic projective spaces.

Conclusion: Symmetry yields many unbounded sign-changing solution continua for Yamabe-type equations, with quantitative multiplicity on important model spaces.

Abstract: We study global bifurcation for Yamabe-type equations on closed Riemannian manifolds with symmetries. For isometric group actions with interval orbit space and a suitable range of exponents, we prove the existence of pairwise disjoint unbounded continua bifurcating from the positive eigenvalues of the Laplace--Beltrami operator that admit invariant eigenfunctions. All nontrivial solutions in these continua change sign. The results cover critical and some supercritical exponents. On spheres and complex and quaternionic projective spaces, we obtain lower bounds on the number of geometrically distinct continua bifurcating from the same eigenvalue.

</details>


### [97] [Local Well-posedness of a Diffuse Interface Two-phase Flow Model with Surfactants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03342)
*Helmut Abels,Songzhuang Chen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves local well-posedness of strong solutions for the Garcke-Lam-Stinner diffuse interface model of two-phase flows with surfactants (Model C) by developing a tailored resolvent analysis that cancels highest-order cross-couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Model C has a non-standard, mixed-order strongly coupled structure, so classical Agmon-Douglis-Nirenberg theory and Lopatinskii-Shapiro boundary conditions fail due to divergent scaling behaviors.

Method: A tailored resolvent analysis adapted to the temporal regularity of the source term in a suitable Hilbert space, achieving exact cancellation of highest-order cross-couplings; decoupling enables application of the operator-valued Mikhlin multiplier theorem for resolvent estimates on R. A structural variant (Model D) is also analyzed.

Result: Resolvent estimates on R are obtained, establishing local well-posedness of strong solutions for Model C; Model D transfers interfacial surfactant energy from the gradient part to the potential part, yielding structural decoupling and local well-posedness.

Conclusion: Tailored resolvent analysis overcomes the limitations of classical theory for mixed-order coupled surfactant models, and structural decoupling (Model D) provides an alternative route to well-posedness.

Abstract: We establish the local well-posedness of strong solutions for a thermodynamically consistent diffuse interface model describing two-phase flows with surfactants, specifically the so-called Model C introduced by Garcke, Lam, and Stinner. Because of the non-standard, mixed-order strongly coupled structure of Model C, the classical Agmon-Douglis-Nirenberg theory and conventional Lopatinskii-Shapiro boundary conditions are not applicable due to divergent scaling behaviors. To overcome this, we develop a tailored resolvent analysis, adapted to the temporal regularity of the source term, in a suitable Hilbert space, which yields an exact cancellation of the highest-order cross-couplings in the estimates. This crucial decoupling enables the rigorous application of the operator-valued Mikhlin multiplier theorem to obtain resolvent estimates on $\mathbb{R}$. Furthermore, we analyze a structural variant, denoted as Model D, which achieves a crucial structural decoupling by transferring the interfacial surfactant energy entirely from the gradient part to the potential part, and we readily establish local well-posedness for strong solutions.

</details>


### [98] [Mathematical study of a new Navier-Stokes-alpha model with nonlinear filter equation - Regularity theory and refined time asymptotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03386)
*Manuel Fernando Cortez,Oscar Jarrín*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies higher-order regularity and long-time dynamics for a Navier–Stokes-α model with a nonlinear filter equation, a doubly nonlinear parabolic–elliptic coupled system, proving uniform higher-order regularity of the global attractor and an exact determining-modes property.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Following Part I, which established global well-posedness of weak Leray-type solutions and a global attractor, this work addresses the challenge that the nonlinear filter structure and its coupling obstruct standard regularity arguments used for linearly filtered Navier–Stokes-α models, requiring new estimates.

Method: Under natural assumptions on the filter operator A(·), the authors develop new higher-order estimates for the nonlinear elliptic filter equation and combine them with regularity analysis of the coupled evolution system to derive uniform higher-order regularity of the attractor.

Result: The paper establishes (i) uniform higher-order regularity for the global attractor and (ii) an exact determining-modes property: if projections of two complete trajectories on the attractor onto a sufficiently large finite-dimensional space of Stokes modes coincide at every time, then the trajectories coincide identically.

Conclusion: Higher-order regularity of the attractor serves as a fundamental tool for proving the determining-modes property, and the new elliptic-filter estimates may be of independent interest, extending the analysis of nonlinear-filtered Navier–Stokes-α models.

Abstract: This article is devoted to the mathematical analysis of a new Navier--Stokes-$α$ model involving a nonlinear filter equation. The resulting model is governed by a doubly nonlinear parabolic--elliptic coupled system. In our previous work [M.~F.~Cortez and O.~Jarrín, \emph{Mathematical study of a new Navier--Stokes-alpha model with nonlinear filter equation -- Part I}, J. Math. Fluid Mech. 28, no.~12 (2026)], we established the global well-posedness of weak Leray-type solutions and the existence of a global attractor. In the present work, under natural assumptions on $A(\cdot)$, we investigate several additional properties of this model, with particular emphasis on higher-order regularity and its consequences for the long-time dynamics. The regularity analysis constitutes a central and delicate issue, since the nonlinear structure of the elliptic filter and its coupling with the evolution equation preclude a direct application of the standard regularity arguments available for linearly filtered Navier--Stokes-$α$ models. Overcoming this difficulty requires new higher-order estimates for the nonlinear elliptic filter equation, which may also be of independent interest. Among the results obtained, two constitute the main contributions of the article. First, we establish uniform higher-order regularity for the global attractor. This result is then used as a fundamental ingredient in proving an exact determining-modes property for complete trajectories on the attractor: if the projections of two complete trajectories onto a sufficiently large finite-dimensional space of Stokes modes coincide at every time, then the two trajectories coincide identically.

</details>


### [99] [Quantitative uniqueness for bi-Laplace equations with $C^2$ lower-order coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03614)
*Zhiwei Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves quantitative unique continuation (three-ball inequality and vanishing-order bound) for bi-Laplace equations with C^2 lower-order coefficients, using the weighted-frequency function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quantitative unique continuation is a fundamental property for PDEs, and it is worth extending such estimates to higher-order operators like the bi-Laplace equation with variable coefficients.

Method: The authors use the weighted-frequency function to handle the fourth-order bi-Laplace operator with C^2 lower-order coefficients W and V.

Result: They establish an L^\infty three-ball inequality whose exponential factor depends on \|W\|_{C^2}^{1/2}+\|V\|_{C^2}^{1/3}, and, under normalization, a vanishing-order bound of size C(\|W\|_{C^2}^{1/2}+\|V\|_{C^2}^{1/3}).

Conclusion: The bi-Laplace equation admits quantitative unique continuation estimates with explicit dependence on the C^2 norms of the lower-order coefficients.

Abstract: We study quantitative unique continuation for bi-Laplace equations
  \[
  Δ^2u+W(x)Δu+V(x)\cdot\nabla u=0
  \] with $C^2$ lower-order coefficients. Using the weighted-frequency function, we establish an $L^\infty$ three-ball inequality whose exponential factor has coefficient dependence $\|W\|_{C^2}^{1/2}+\|V\|_{C^2}^{1/3}$. Under some normalization, we obtain a vanishing-order bound of size $C(\|W\|_{C^2}^{1/2}+\|V\|_{C^2}^{1/3})$.

</details>


### [100] [On improved Hardy-type inequalities in Log-Weighted Sobolev Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03668)
*Sourav Ghosh*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves sharp Hardy-type inequalities in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces and improves them with remainder terms, yielding infinite series improvements and an optimal Leray-Trudinger-type improvement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sharp Hardy-type inequalities have non-attainable optimal constants, motivating the need for refined versions with remainder terms that capture the deficit.

Method: The authors add suitable remainder terms composed of optimal weights and sharp constants to the sharp Hardy-type inequalities, obtaining an infinite series improvement in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces. These improved inequalities are then applied to derive an optimal Leray-Trudinger-type improvement.

Result: Sharp Hardy-type inequalities are established and improved via remainder terms, giving infinite series improvements. An optimal Leray-Trudinger-type improvement is proved, and interpolation between Hardy-type and Leray-Trudinger-type inequalities is derived.

Conclusion: The work refines Hardy-type inequalities in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces, connects them to Leray-Trudinger-type improvements, and provides interpolation results linking the two families of inequalities.

Abstract: We prove sharp Hardy-type inequalities in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces. Due to the non-attainability of the sharp constant, we improve these inequalities by adding suitable remainder terms consisting of optimal weights and sharp constants. Consequently, this gives an infinite series improvement in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces. Next, by using these improved Hardy-type inequalities, we also prove an optimal Leray-Trudinger-type improvement. Furthermore, we derive interpolation between Hardy-type inequalities and Leray-Trudinger-type inequalities.

</details>


### [101] [Lower bound on Dirac resonances with mass-type potentials in the semi-classical limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02356)
*Zhuo Chen,Michael Melgaard*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Establishes sharp lower bounds of order ℏ^{-3} on the number of resonances for 3D semiclassical Dirac operators with mass-type potentials, matching Khochman's upper bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Khochman gave an upper bound Cℏ^{-3} on resonances for these Dirac operators, but the corresponding lower bound was unknown. This paper fills that gap, showing the exponent is sharp and connecting resonance counting to spectral/trapping properties.

Method: For two operators Dl_j = Dl_0 + β v_j satisfying exterior analyticity, decay, and non-degeneracy, a relative distribution μ is associated to the mass functions. The proof transfers an analytic wave-front singularity of μ to the relative classical density of states, combined with Khochman's local trace formula and a relative semiclassical trace formula. A relative semiclassical trace formula for general Hermitian matrix-valued perturbations is also proven.

Result: If E_0 > 1 is a boundary point of supp μ, then every complex neighbourhood of E_0 (and of -E_0) contains at least Cℏ^{-3} resonances of the two operators, counted with multiplicity. For a single bounded mass-type potential v (analytic with power decay in an exterior sector, inf(1+v)>0, sup v>0), Dl = Dl_0 + βv has at least Cℏ^{-3} resonances near ±E_0 = ±(1+sup v) for small ℏ.

Conclusion: The lower bound matches Khochman's upper bound, confirming optimality. The two resonance families are related by spectral symmetry, and E_0 is necessarily a trapping energy. The relative semiclassical trace formula is established for general Hermitian matrix-valued perturbations.

Abstract: We establish lower bounds on the number of resonances for three-dimensional semiclassical Dirac operators with mass-type potentials. For a pair $\Dl_j=\Dl_0+\mbfβv_j$, $j=1,2$, satisfying the exterior analyticity, decay and non-degeneracy assumptions of the paper, we associate a relative distribution $μ$ to the corresponding mass functions. We prove that if $E_0>1$ is a boundary point of $\suppμ$, then every complex neighbourhood of $E_0$, and likewise of $-E_0$, contains in total at least $C\hbar^{-3}$ resonances of the two operators, counted with multiplicity. The proof transfers an analytic wave-front singularity of $μ$ to the relative classical density of states and combines this with Khochman's local trace formula and a relative semiclassical trace formula. As a consequence, if $v$ is a single bounded mass-type potential, analytic with power decay in an exterior sector, with $\inf(1+v)>0$ and $\sup v>0$, then $\Dl=\Dl_0+\mbfβv$ has at least $C\hbar^{-3}$ resonances in every complex neighbourhood of $E_0=1+\sup v$ and of $-E_0$, for all sufficiently small $\hbar$. The exponent agrees with Khochman's upper bound, the two resonance families are related by spectral symmetry, and $E_0$ is necessarily a trapping energy. We also prove the relative semiclassical trace formula needed in the argument for general Hermitian matrix-valued perturbations.

</details>


### [102] [Blow-up for the integrable defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02358)
*Dirk Hennig,Nikos I. Karachalios,Dionyssios Mantzavinos,Dimitrios Mitsotakis,Barbara Prinari*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This work proves finite-time blow-up for the defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik lattice in ℓ∞, showing the unit circle acts as a separatrix and that blow-up dominates the dynamics for data outside the closed unit ball.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The dynamics of the completely integrable defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik equation in low-regularity (ℓ∞) regimes with no decay and general boundary conditions were not well understood, particularly regarding global existence versus blow-up.

Method: Establish local well-posedness in ℓ∞ for arbitrary data; prove the unit circle is invariant/separatrix for site moduli; construct explicit singular solutions with exact blow-up times; build global solutions from large single-site excitations; use singular solutions as subsolutions to show blow-up dominance. Add numerical studies.

Result: Data in the closure of the ℓ∞ unit ball yield global solutions; blow-up is restricted to sites with initial modulus outside the unit circle. Explicit blow-up times are constructed, global solutions with large excitation exist, and finite-time blow-up dominates general dynamics outside the closed unit ball.

Conclusion: The defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik lattice exhibits a unit-circle separatrix, with finite-time blow-up dominating the dynamics for general initial data outside the closed ℓ∞ unit ball, despite the integrability of the system.

Abstract: This work establishes finite-time blow-up for the completely integrable defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik equation on both infinite and finite lattices. We first establish local well-posedness for arbitrary initial data in the space $\ell^\infty$ of uniformly bounded sequences, with no decay assumptions and under general boundary conditions. We then show that the unit circle acts as a separatrix for the lattice dynamics: sites with an initial modulus inside, on, or outside the unit circle maintain their modulus within their respective region for as long as the solution exists. As a consequence, initial data in the closure of the $\ell^\infty$ unit ball generate global-in-time solutions. On the other hand, the unit circle separatrix opens up the possibility for finite-time blow-up, which is restricted to sites with initial modulus outside the unit circle. We establish this scenario by constructing a class of initial data whose corresponding solutions are singular, with explicitly given exact blow-up times. We also construct solutions emanating from initial data with an arbitrarily large single-site excitation outside the unit circle that exist globally in time, a behavior driven by the convergence of the corresponding modulus to the unit circle equilibrium. In the presence of such solutions, along with other important classes of analytical, global-in-time solutions that remain outside the closed $\ell^\infty$ unit ball for all times, we examine the fundamental question of the dominant dynamics for general initial data outside the closed unit ball. By utilizing the constructed class of singular solutions as subsolutions for the general problem, we prove that the dynamics for the completely integrable defocusing Ablowitz-Ladik lattice is dominated by finite-time blow-up. We conclude with numerical studies of illustrative examples of general initial data

</details>


### [103] [Strictly positive optimal Hardy weights on manifolds and graphs via Green potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02365)
*Ujjal Das,Matthias Keller,Yehuda Pinchover*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A family of strictly positive critical Hardy weights is constructed for a subcritical p-Schrödinger functional Q on domains of noncompact Riemannian manifolds (and analogously on graphs), using supersolution constructions based on the Green potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hardy-type inequalities and criticality theory for p-Schrödinger operators are central in analysis on manifolds and graphs; the paper aims to produce explicit critical Hardy weights and identify optimality, extending such results to a general noncompact setting.

Method: Supersolution construction based on the Green potential with a strictly positive charge assumed to vanish at infinity, parametrized by 0 ≤ ε ≤ 1.

Result: A family of strictly positive critical Hardy weights is obtained, exhibiting a transition from positive-criticality to null-criticality at ε = 0. The best Hardy constant is c = [(p−1)/p]^{p−1}, and at ε = 0 the Hardy weight is optimal. An analogous result holds on graphs.

Conclusion: The construction yields critical Hardy weights with sharp constant and optimality at ε = 0, valid both on noncompact Riemannian manifolds and on graphs.

Abstract: Consider a subcritical functional $Q$ associated with a $p$-Schrödinger operator defined on a domain $Ω$ in noncompact Riemannian manifold. Employing the supersolution construction, we obtain a family of strictly positive critical Hardy weights for $Q$. The construction is based on the Green potential, with a strictly positive charge, which is assumed to vanish at infinity. An interesting new feature of this family, depending on a parameter $0\leq \varepsilon\leq 1$, is the transition from positive-criticality to null-criticality at $\varepsilon=0$. Moreover, we show that the best Hardy constant for this family is given by $c=[(p-1)/p]^{p-1}$, and that for $\varepsilon=0$ the corresponding Hardy weight is optimal. We furthermore, prove an analogous result on graphs.

</details>


### [104] [Finite-time blowup of the Euler equation without forcing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02509)
*Fan Zheng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: These notes reconstruct the Elgindi-style construction of finite-time blowup for the 3D incompressible, unforced Euler equations on R^3, building singular solutions by iteratively adding oscillatory layers to smooth parent solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an exposition of a finite-time blowup construction for the 3D incompressible Euler equations, a striking result showing smooth solutions can lose regularity in finite time.

Method: The construction successively adds oscillatory layers to smooth "parent" solutions. Each new layer is built via a finite expansion combined with an exact correction, ensuring the resulting "child" solution still solves the Euler equations exactly.

Result: A finite-time blowup solution for the 3D incompressible, unforced Euler equations on R^3 is constructed, with each iterative layer preserving exactness of the Euler solution.

Conclusion: The iterative oscillatory-layer method provides a concrete route to finite-time blowup for the 3D Euler equations, illustrating how singularity formation arises from successively refined oscillations.

Abstract: These notes present the construction in \cite{euler} of finite-time blowup for the three-dimensional incompressible, unforced Euler equations on $\mathbb R^3$: \begin{equation}
  \partial_tu+(u\cdot\nabla)u+\nabla p=0,\qquad \nabla\cdot u=0. \end{equation} The construction successively adds oscillatory layers to smooth parent solutions. Each layer is constructed by a finite expansion and an exact correction, so that the resulting child solves Euler.

</details>


### [105] [Asymptotic behavior of the one-dimensional Vlasov--Poisson system with a repulsive harmonic potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02551)
*Huang,Wenrui*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes modified scattering for the 1D Vlasov–Poisson system with a repulsive harmonic potential and repulsive self-interactions, for arbitrary-size data in weighted Sobolev spaces without symmetry, compact support, or spatial moment assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior higher-dimensional results (HK26, VR24, BVR25) motivate extending the theory to the one-dimensional setting with a repulsive harmonic potential. The authors aim to handle large data and remove restrictive assumptions like symmetry, compact support, and spatial moments, leveraging the repulsive sign structure.

Method: The proof uses a Hamilton–Jacobi momentum correction combined with the sign of the repulsive interaction to derive large-data bounds. They also construct modified wave operators.

Result: Modified scattering is established for initial data of arbitrary size in weighted Sobolev spaces, without symmetry, compact support, or spatial moment assumptions. Modified wave operators are constructed.

Conclusion: The results complement the higher-dimensional theory and the methods are expected to be useful for a broader class of kinetic equations with very long-range interactions.

Abstract: We consider the one-dimensional Vlasov--Poisson system with a repulsive harmonic potential. For repulsive self-interactions, we establish modified scattering for initial data of arbitrary size in some weighted Sobolev spaces without symmetry, compact support, or spatial moment assumptions. The proof uses a Hamilton-Jacobi momentum correction and the sign of the repulsive interaction to obtain large-data bounds. We also construct modified wave operators. These results complement the higher-dimensional theory in \cite{HK26,VR24,BVR25}. We expect our methods to be useful in the study of a broader class of kinetic equations with very long-range interactions.

</details>


### [106] [Stability and Instability of the Unique Positive Steady State for a Nonlocal Population Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02620)
*Yuan Lou,Ming-Zhen Xin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies how spatial heterogeneity affects linear stability of the unique positive steady state in a nonlocal population equation, showing that uniqueness does not imply stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The linearized operator for nonlocal population equations need not be self-adjoint or satisfy the maximum principle, so standard principal-eigenvalue stability arguments fail. The paper aims to develop stability results despite this obstacle.

Method: The authors analyze linear stability of the unique positive steady state at large and small diffusion regimes. They use conditions on the maximum set of the local growth ratio, and in one dimension construct unstable positive steady states and derive Hopf bifurcations by varying the nonlocal integral weight.

Result: Linear stability is established for sufficiently large diffusion and, under suitable conditions, for sufficiently small diffusion. In one dimension, unstable positive steady states and Hopf bifurcations (supercritical or subcritical, depending on the nonlocal weight) are obtained.

Conclusion: Uniqueness of a positive steady state does not imply its linear stability in nonlocal population equations.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with the effect of spatial heterogeneity on the linear stability of unique positive steady states for a nonlocal population equation. The main difficulty is that, because of the nonlocal term, the linearized operator need not be self-adjoint or satisfy the maximum principle, so the usual stability arguments based on the principal eigenvalue do not apply directly. We establish linear stability for sufficiently large diffusion and, under suitable conditions on the maximum set of the local growth ratio, for sufficiently small diffusion. In one space dimension, we also construct unstable positive steady states and obtain Hopf bifurcations for sufficiently small diffusion. Keeping the local coefficients fixed, we obtain either a supercritical or a subcritical Hopf bifurcation by changing the weight in the nonlocal integral. Our results show that uniqueness of a positive steady state does not imply its linear stability.

</details>


### [107] [A Compressible Miles Instability for Wind-Wave Generation in a Neutral Atmosphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02628)
*Tianyi Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves a compressible analog of Miles' critical-layer instability for an air-water interface with gravity and surface tension, showing instability arises when the gradient of specific vorticity Q satisfies a sign condition at critical levels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend the rigorous incompressible critical-layer instability theory of Bühler, Shatah, Walsh, and Zeng to compressible air flow over incompressible water, where the singular coefficient involves specific vorticity rather than just curvature.

Method: Analyze normal-mode stability of a flat interface between compressible Euler (air) and incompressible Euler (water), with gravity and surface tension, small density ratio ε, and uniformly subsonic wave-relative flow. Prove a limiting absorption principle at regular non-resonant phase speeds and derive a residue formula for Im Z_a^+(k,c_r).

Result: The critical-level residue is proportional to Q = U'' + gU'/a^2 = ρ̄_0(U'/ρ̄_0)', replacing the curvature U'' of the incompressible theory. If Im Z_a^+(k,c_k)>0, the dispersion relation has exactly one unstable root near c_k for small ε. A sufficient condition is Q≤0 at all critical levels and Q<0 at the highest. As M→0, Z_a^+ converges to its incompressible counterpart at rate O(M^2), with |c_ε(M)−c_ε(0)|≤CεM^2.

Conclusion: The compressible theory generalizes Miles' instability, with specific vorticity gradient governing the singular coefficient, and recovers the incompressible result in the zero-Mach-number limit.

Abstract: We study the normal-mode stability of a flat interface between compressible Euler flow in the air and incompressible Euler flow in the water, including gravity and surface tension. The base state is quiescent water beneath a hydrostatic, constant-entropy shear flow $U(z)$ with $U(0)=0$, so the atmosphere is neutrally stratified. For a small ratio $\varepsilon$ of the air density at the interface to the water density, and for uniformly subsonic wave-relative flow, we establish a compressible analog of the critical-layer instability of Miles, extending the rigorous incompressible theory of Bühler, Shatah, Walsh, and Zeng. For a real phase speed $c_r$, the singular coefficient at a regular critical level $U(z_c)=c_r$ has a residue proportional to $Q=U''+gU'/a^2=\barρ_0(U'/\barρ_0)'$, with gravitational acceleration $g$, sound speed $a$, and normalized base-state air density $\barρ_0$. Thus the gradient of specific vorticity replaces the curvature $U''$ of the incompressible theory.
  We prove a limiting absorption principle at regular, non-resonant phase speeds and a residue formula for the imaginary part of the boundary value $Z_{\mathrm a}^+(k,c_r)$ of the air-side pressure response at wavenumber $k>0$. Let $c_k>0$ be the phase speed of the free gravity-capillary wave. If $\mathrm{Im}\,Z_{\mathrm a}^+(k,c_k)>0$, then, for sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, the dispersion relation has exactly one unstable root $c_\varepsilon$ in a neighborhood of $c_k$ that does not depend on $\varepsilon$. A sufficient condition is $Q\le0$ at every critical level and $Q<0$ at the highest one.
  As the Mach number $M\to0$, $Z_{\mathrm a}^+$ converges to its incompressible counterpart at rate $O(M^2)$. Under the corresponding incompressible sign condition, the unstable root satisfies $|c_\varepsilon(M)-c_\varepsilon(0)|\le C\varepsilon M^2$ with $C$ independent of $\varepsilon$ and $M$.

</details>


### [108] [Hopf bifurcation in a predator-prey model with diffusion and spatial heterogeneity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02630)
*Yuan Lou,Ming-Zhen Xin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Spatial heterogeneity can destabilize the unique positive equilibrium in 1D predator-prey reaction-diffusion systems with Neumann boundary conditions, leading to Hopf bifurcation and nonstationary periodic solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior intuition often assumes a unique positive equilibrium is stable; the paper examines how spatial heterogeneity in coefficients can break this assumption and induce instability in predator-prey diffusive systems.

Method: Construct smooth spatially heterogeneous coefficient families (prey-concentrated and predator-concentrated) in 1D reaction-diffusion systems with Neumann conditions; analyze linear stability and eigenvalue behavior; apply Hopf bifurcation theory as a parameter varies.

Result: Spatial heterogeneity causes loss of linear stability even when the positive equilibrium is unique. At the critical parameter, a simple conjugate eigenvalue pair crosses the imaginary axis transversely, producing a local branch of strictly positive, nonstationary classical time-periodic solutions via Hopf bifurcation.

Conclusion: Spatial heterogeneity fundamentally alters stability in predator-prey reaction-diffusion systems, enabling Hopf bifurcations and time-periodic patterns from a unique positive equilibrium.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate how spatial heterogeneity affects the stability of positive equilibria in one-dimensional predator--prey reaction--diffusion systems with Neumann boundary conditions. Our examples show that spatial heterogeneity can lead to instability even when the positive equilibrium is unique. For the smooth, spatially heterogeneous coefficient families constructed here, this equilibrium loses linear stability as a parameter in the reaction coefficients varies. At the critical parameter value, a simple conjugate pair of eigenvalues crosses the imaginary axis transversely, and a Hopf bifurcation produces a local branch of strictly positive, nonstationary classical time-periodic solutions. The examples include two families with spatially concentrated prey, one with an everywhere positive and the other with an everywhere negative predator intrinsic growth rate. A third family has a spatially concentrated predator population and an everywhere negative predator intrinsic growth rate.

</details>


### [109] [A common structure for modified scattering: Vlasov--Riesz, Hartree, and their coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02643)
*Wenrui Huang,Mengyi Xie*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes small-data global existence and modified scattering in 3D for the Hartree, Vlasov-Riesz, and coupled Vlasov-Hartree equations with strongly long-range potentials λ|x|^{-α}, 0<α≤1/2, via a unified action-density structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The long-range regime 0<α≤1/2 is challenging because standard scattering fails; no forward modified-scattering results existed for all three models across this full range.

Method: Identify a common action-density structure along free rays, where feedback between nonlinear actions and rescaled densities produces wave phase modulations and kinetic momentum translations. This structure is stable under source exchange, yielding convergent modified profiles and explicit recursive asymptotic corrections.

Result: Small-data global existence and modified scattering in three spatial dimensions for the Hartree equation, the Vlasov-Riesz equation, and the coupled Vlasov-Hartree system, with λ∈R and 0<α≤1/2. The asymptotic action contains finitely many successive corrections beyond its leading term.

Conclusion: These are the first forward modified-scattering results for all three models throughout the strongly long-range range 0<α≤1/2.

Abstract: We identify a common action--density structure for the Hartree and Vlasov--Riesz equations and their coupling. Along free rays, the feedback between nonlinear actions and rescaled densities governs wave phase modulations and kinetic momentum translations. This structure persists under the exchange of sources, providing convergent modified profiles and an explicit recursive construction of the asymptotic corrections. We thereby establish small-data global existence and modified scattering in three spatial dimensions for the Hartree equation, the Vlasov--Riesz equation, and the coupled Vlasov--Hartree system, with interaction potential $λ|x|^{-α}$, $λ\in\mathbb R$ and $0<α\leq 1/2$. In this strongly long-range regime, the asymptotic action contains finitely many successive corrections beyond its leading term. To our knowledge, these are the first forward modified-scattering results for all three models throughout this range.

</details>


### [110] [Quantitative Parabolic Decay With Bounded Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02685)
*Igor Kukavica,Qi Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes matching quantitative two-time lower bounds for double exponential decay of energy solutions to a parabolic equation with bounded drift and arbitrary small potential, proving the decay scale is optimal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work showed that a bounded time-dependent drift plus an arbitrarily small real potential can cause double exponential decay of the spatial L^2 norm, but the optimality and quantitative lower bounds of this decay scale were not established.

Method: The authors prove two-time lower bounds for energy solutions of ∂_t u - Δu = vu + w·∇u on R^n (n≥3), with w in L^∞_{t,x} and v either small in L^∞_t L^{n/2}_x or in L^∞_t L^p_x (n/2 < p ≤ ∞) without smallness. They construct explicit counterexamples—a bounded drift, arbitrarily small potential in critical/subcritical spaces, and a nonzero solution—by localizing Rowan's torus example while absorbing localization error.

Result: Nonzero global solutions satisfy a double exponential lower bound at large times, and this decay scale is shown to be optimal for the given coefficient classes in every dimension n≥3.

Conclusion: Double exponential decay is the sharp, optimal decay rate achievable for these coefficient classes, confirmed by matching upper and lower bounds and explicit constructions.

Abstract: A bounded time-dependent drift together with an arbitrarily small real potential can produce double exponential decay of the full spatial $L^2$ norm. We prove matching quantitative two-time lower bounds for energy solutions of $\partial_tu-Δu=vu+w\cdot\nabla u$ on $\mathbb{R}^n$, for $n\geq3$, when $w\in L^\infty_{t,x}$ and either $v$ is sufficiently small in $L^\infty_tL^{n/2}_x$ or $v\in L^\infty_tL^p_x$ for some $n/2<p\leq\infty$, without a smallness assumption. In particular, a nonzero global solution satisfies a double exponential lower bound at large times. This decay scale is optimal for these coefficient classes: In every dimension $n\geq3$, we construct a real bounded drift, a real potential arbitrarily small in the critical space and in any prescribed subcritical $L^p$ space, and a nonzero real energy solution with double exponential decay. The construction localizes Rowan's torus example by absorbing the localization error.

</details>


### [111] [Continuation and threshold global strong solutions for the two-dimensional Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02714)
*Pang-hung Chung,Dan Han*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Studies finite-time continuation and subthreshold global existence for the 2D Landau–Lifshitz–Gilbert equation in the H_Q^2 strong-solution class, with energy thresholds of 4π and 8π (degree-zero sector).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish continuation criteria and global existence below critical energy thresholds for the 2D Landau–Lifshitz–Gilbert equation.

Method: Analytic proof in the H_Q^2 strong-solution class, using full-frequency L^4_{t,x} control of ∇u and an ultraviolet-tail condition above a fixed dyadic cutoff.

Result: If a maximal strong solution terminates and the full-frequency L^4_{t,x} norm of ∇u is finite on a terminal window, it continues; continuation also holds when the UV tail above one dyadic cutoff is in L^4_{t,x}. Global solutions exist for E(u_0)<4π, raised to 8π in the degree-zero sector.

Conclusion: Provides finite-time continuation criteria and improved subthreshold global existence results for the 2D Landau–Lifshitz–Gilbert equation.

Abstract: This paper studies finite-terminal continuation and subthreshold global existence for the two-dimensional Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert equation in the $H_Q^2$ strong-solution class. The proof shows that if a maximal strong solution terminates at a finite time and the full-frequency $L^4_{t,x}$ norm of $\nabla u$ is finite on a terminal time window, then the strong solution can be continued. Furthermore, the solution can still be continued as long as the ultraviolet tail above one fixed dyadic cutoff belongs to $L^4_{t,x}$. As applications, we obtain global strong solutions when $E(u_0)<4π$; in the degree-zero sector, this energy threshold can be raised to $8π$.

</details>


### [112] [Large-Data Global Criteria for Two-Dimensional Critical-Energy Schrödinger Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02721)
*Pang-Hung Chung,Dan Han*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For the 2D sphere-valued Schrödinger map below the critical energy 4π, frequency-envelope bounds controlled by the critical norm give global well-posedness unless persistent high-frequency concentration occurs on late parabolic windows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish large-data continuation (global existence) below the critical energy for the 2D sphere-valued Schrödinger map, extending understanding of blow-up mechanisms via critical-norm control.

Method: Work directly with the original equation using complex tangential fields W_m = ∂_m u + i u×∂_m u and ambient connections Γ_α; prove frequency-envelope bounds governed by the critical norm ‖∇u‖_{ℓ²L⁴_{t,x}}.

Result: T_+ = ∞ if ‖∇u‖_{ℓ²L⁴} < ∞, and also if a terminal one-window high-frequency tail satisfies liminf_{t↑T_+} A_win(t) < ε_*.

Conclusion: Any finite-time failure must force persistent high-frequency concentration on all sufficiently late critical parabolic windows.

Abstract: We study large-data continuation below the critical energy for the two-dimensional sphere-valued Schrödinger map \[ \partial_t u=u\timesΔu, \qquad u:[0,T_+)\times\mathbb{R}^2\to\mathbb{S}^2, \qquad E(u_0)<4π. \] Working directly with the original equation through the complex tangential fields $W_m=\partial_m u+\mathrm{i} u\times\partial_m u$ and the ambient connections $Γ_α$, we prove frequency-envelope bounds governed by the critical norm $\|\nabla u\|_{\ell^2L^4_{t,x}}$. As consequences, $T_+=\infty$ if $\|\nabla u\|_{\ell^2L^4([0,T_+)\times\mathbb{R}^2)}<\infty$, and also if a terminal one-window high-frequency tail $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{win}}(t)$ satisfies $\liminf_{t\uparrow T_+}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{win}}(t)<\varepsilon_*$. Thus any finite-time failure must force persistent high-frequency concentration on all sufficiently late critical parabolic windows.

</details>


### [113] [Lower regularity well-posedness for a higher-order Schrödinger equation with cubic nonlinearities on the half-line](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02747)
*Yuhao Xi,Shenghao Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Proves well-posedness of the cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation with order 2m dispersion on the half-line at the optimal regularity s = -(m-1)/2 for k=1 (and k=0,2), and s > -(2m-1)/3 for k=3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Continues prior work by Himonas and Yan, aiming to lower the regularity threshold for well-posedness of cNLS-2m on the half-line and to answer an open question from earlier literature.

Method: Derives trilinear estimates at lower regularity using various Strichartz estimates, rather than the [k;Z]-multiplier norm method used previously.

Result: Establishes well-posedness at the optimal regularity s = -(m-1)/2, improving the previous s > -(m-1)/2; also well-posedness at s = -(m-1)/2 for k=0,2, and s > -(2m-1)/3 for k=3 due to stronger resonance.

Conclusion: The optimal regularity well-posedness for cNLS-2m on the half-line is achieved, resolving an open question, with distinct behavior for k=3.

Abstract: In this paper, we continue the study of Himonas and Yan\cite{himonas2024schrodinger,himonas2026higher,himonas2024higher} on the nonlinear Schrödinger equation with a dispersion of order $2m$ and cubic nonlinearities,
  \[ iu_t+\left( -1 \right) ^{m+1}\partial _{x}^{2m}u=N_k\left( u,u,u \right), \]
  where, $m\ge1$ being an integer,
  \[N_0\left( u,u,u \right) =uuu,\quad N_1\left( u,u,u \right) =\bar{u}uu,\quad N_2\left( u,u,u \right) =\bar{u}\bar{u}u,\quad N_3\left( u,u,u \right) =\bar{u}\bar{u}\bar{u},\]
  We derive trilinear estimates at lower regularity and thereby prove that the cNLS-2m on the half-line is well-posed at the optimal regularity $s=-\fr{m-1}{2}$. This improves the previous result, $s>-\fr{m-1}{2}$, and answers an open question left in \cite{himonas2026higher}. In addition, for $k=0,2$, we establish well-posedness at $s=-\fr{m-1}{2}$ as well. Moreover, for $k=3$, the nonlinearity exhibits a stronger resonance relation, which implies well-posedness for $s>-\fr{2m-1}{3}$. Our derivation of the trilinear estimates relies on varieties of Strichartz estimates, which differs from the $[k;Z]$-multiplier norm method employed in \cite{himonas2026higher}.

</details>


### [114] [Initial and initial-boundary value problems for a cubic sixth-order Boussinesq equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02748)
*Yuhao Xie,Shenghao Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper improves local well-posedness for the sixth-order Boussinesq equation with cubic nonlinearity on ℝ⁺ and ℝ in Bourgain-type spaces X^{s,b} for s > -1/2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior local well-posedness results (cited works) are improved, motivating a refined trilinear estimate to lower the regularity threshold to s > -1/2.

Method: Refines the trilinear estimate on Bourgain-type spaces X^{s,b} using Tao's multiplier method, interpolation theory, and prior work on IBVPs of dispersive equations; treats b = 1/2 and b > 1/2 cases.

Result: Local well-posedness is established for s > -1/2 with b = 1/2 and b > 1/2 on both ℝ⁺ and ℝ.

Conclusion: The refined trilinear estimate yields improved well-posedness for the sixth-order Boussinesq equation, extending the range of admissible Sobolev regularity.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider the sixth-order Boussinesq equation with a cubic nonlinearity,
  \[ u_{tt}-u_{xx}+k u_{xxxx}-u_{xxxxxx}+\left( u^3 \right) _{xx}=0, \ \ \mbox{with }k=\pm 1,\]
  posed on both $\mathbb{R}^+$ and $\mathbb{R}$. The local wellposedness are improved in related Bourgain-type spaces, $X^{s,b}$, for $s>-\frac12$ with $b=\frac12$ and $b>\frac12$ accordingly (See. \cite{esfahani2025well,zhong2024local}). The key ingredient is to refine the trilinear estimate on Burgain type space by adapting ideas from Tao's multiplier method, interpolation theory and our early work on IBVPs of dispersive equations \cite{li2020low,tao2001multilinear}.

</details>


### [115] [Sharp Liouville thresholds and endpoint rigidity for finite Morse index solutions of the $p$-Laplace Lane--Emden equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02751)
*Phuong Le*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper removes the radial symmetry assumption for stable-outside-a-compact-set solutions of the $p$-Laplace equation $-\Delta_p u = |u|^{q-1}u$ in $\mathbb{R}^N$, settling the full subcritical-to-critical range $p-1<q<q_c(N,p)$ with $q\neq p^*-1$ for arbitrary sign, non-bounded, non-symmetric solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Damascelli, Farina, Sciunzi and Valdinoci settled the subcritical range for $p>2$ and treated the supercritical range $p^*-1<q<q_c(N,p)$ only for radial solutions, unable to conclude without radial symmetry. The obstruction is that the $p=2$ arguments rest on a monotonicity formula with no monotone analogue for $p\neq2$.

Method: The authors iterate the comparison principle against an explicit two-parameter supersolution, upgrading the decay forced by stability to that of the $p$-harmonic fundamental solution. This step uses no stability, only smallness of the self-similar decay, and is stated independently.

Result: Every $C^1$ weak solution stable outside a compact set is trivial when $p^*-1<q<q_c(N,p)$. The whole range $p-1<q<q_c(N,p)$, $q\neq p^*-1$, is settled, and every finite Morse index solution is trivial. The upper threshold is sharp: for every $q>q_c(N,p)$ there exist positive bounded radial solutions stable outside a compact set. At the Sobolev endpoint, every $C^1$ solution stable outside a compact set has finite energy. At the upper endpoint, $q_c(N,p)$ is the exact threshold for a nonzero homogeneous weak solution on $\mathbb{R}^N\setminus\{0\}$ to be stable outside a ball, with exactly two solutions at the threshold.

Conclusion: The radial hypothesis is fully removed, completing the classification program for stable and finite Morse index solutions. For $p>2$ this also removes the a priori $\mathcal{D}^{1,p}$ assumption from the low-Morse-index classification of Farina, Mercuri and Willem and answers their question on the Morse index of the Aubin--Talenti extremals, showing it is one.

Abstract: We study $-Δ_p u = |u|^{q-1}u$ in $\mathbb{R}^N$ with $N>p\ge 2$, for solutions that are stable outside a compact set, with no assumption of sign, boundedness or symmetry. Damascelli, Farina, Sciunzi and Valdinoci settled the subcritical range for $p>2$, and treated the supercritical range $p^*-1<q<q_c(N,p)$ only for radial solutions, stating that without radial symmetry they were not able to conclude. The obstruction is that the arguments available for $p=2$ rest on a monotonicity formula with no monotone analogue when $p\ne2$.
  We remove the radial hypothesis: every $C^1$ weak solution stable outside a compact set is trivial when $p^*-1<q<q_c(N,p)$. The whole range $p-1<q<q_c(N,p)$ with $q\ne p^*-1$ is thereby settled, as it is for $p=2$ by Farina's theorem, and every finite Morse index solution in it is trivial. In its place we iterate the comparison principle against an explicit two-parameter supersolution, upgrading the decay forced by stability to that of the $p$-harmonic fundamental solution; this step uses no stability, only smallness of the self-similar decay, and is stated on its own. The upper threshold is sharp: for every $q>q_c(N,p)$ we exhibit positive bounded radial solutions stable outside a compact set. At the Sobolev endpoint, every $C^1$ solution stable outside a compact set has finite energy; for $p>2$ this removes the a priori $\mathcal D^{1,p}$ assumption from the low-Morse-index classification of Farina, Mercuri and Willem and answers their question on the Morse index of the Aubin--Talenti extremals: it is one. At the upper endpoint, $q_c(N,p)$ is the exact threshold for a nonzero homogeneous weak solution on $\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\}$ to be stable outside a ball, and at the threshold there are exactly two.

</details>


### [116] [The critical boundary value for a nonlinear Schrödinger equation in a half-space: rigidity and dimensional transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02790)
*Phuong Le*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies bounded positive solutions of a threshold boundary value problem for $-Δv+v=v^p$ in the half-space, establishing a dimension-dependent rigidity/nonuniqueness result (uniqueness for $2≤N≤5$, nonuniqueness for $N≥8$, and conditional nonuniqueness in $N=7$).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: This threshold boundary value was left open by Fernández and Weth (Math. Ann. 383, 2022). The aim is to resolve the rigidity versus nonuniqueness behavior of bounded positive solutions depending on the spatial dimension $N$.

Method: Combines a boundary-adapted Modica estimate, projection onto the kernel of the one-dimensional linearized operator, an automatic tail non-concentration estimate, and a nonlinear cell problem yielding the exact reduced nonlinearity. For $N≥8$ the reduced linearization is invertible in weighted spaces; in $N=7$ the critical resonance is handled by solving the projected radial equation nonlinearly and closing the transverse equation via a Schauder–Tychonoff fixed point.

Result: For every $p>1$, the profile $w_0(x_N)$ is the unique bounded positive solution when $2≤N≤5$, while for $N≥8$ there exists a one-parameter family of non-one-dimensional bounded positive solutions. In the critical dimension $N=7$, nonuniqueness holds whenever the cubic coefficient $κ(p)$ of the reduced nonlinearity is positive, which includes all $p≥2$ by continuity extending below 2. The amplitudes have Fowler ends with energy, neck size, bulge size, and logarithmic period determined to leading order.

Conclusion: The paper identifies a dimension-dependent rigidity/nonuniqueness picture for the threshold boundary value. The cases $N=6$ and the complementary range in $N=7$ remain open.

Abstract: Let $w_0$ be the positive homoclinic solution of $-w''+w=w^p$ and set $c_p=w_0(0)$. We study bounded positive solutions of \[ -Δv+v=v^p\quad\text{in }\mathbb R^N_+, \qquad v=c_p\quad\text{on }\partial\mathbb R^N_+, \qquad v(x',x_N)\to0 \] uniformly as $x_N\to\infty$. This is the threshold boundary value left open by Fernández and Weth [Math.\ Ann.\ \textbf{383} (2022), 361--397]. We prove a dimension-dependent rigidity/nonuniqueness picture. For every $p>1$, the profile $w_0(x_N)$ is the unique bounded positive solution when $2\le N\le5$, whereas for $N\ge8$ there is a one-parameter family of bounded positive non-one-dimensional solutions. In the critical dimension $N=7$, nonuniqueness holds whenever a cubic coefficient $κ(p)$ of the exact reduced nonlinearity is positive; in particular $κ(p)>0$ for all $p\ge2$, and continuity extends this range below $2$. The corresponding amplitudes have Fowler ends, with their limiting energy, neck size, bulge size, and logarithmic period determined to leading order. The case $N=6$, as well as the complementary range in $N=7$, remain open.
  The proof combines a boundary-adapted Modica estimate, projection onto the kernel of the one-dimensional linearized operator, an automatic tail non-concentration estimate, and a nonlinear cell problem yielding the exact reduced nonlinearity. For $N\ge8$ the reduced linearization is invertible in weighted spaces. In $N=7$ the critical resonance is bypassed by solving the projected radial equation nonlinearly and closing the transverse equation by a Schauder--Tychonoff fixed point.

</details>


### [117] [Boundedness of Solutions to Nonlocal Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02810)
*Serena Dipierro,João Gonçalves da Silva,Giorgio Poggesi,Enrico Valdinoci*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves uniform L∞ bounds for all distributional solutions of a multi-dimensional system of nonlocal semilinear equations (Ginzburg-Landau systems and Allen-Cahn equations), under mild mass or energy assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establishing a priori bounds for solutions of nonlocal semilinear PDE systems is important for regularity and well-posedness, yet uniform bounds are not generally known; the results are new even for the scalar case.

Method: Analytical PDE techniques; exploit mild mass or energy assumptions to derive uniform L∞ estimates valid for all distributional solutions.

Result: Uniform L∞ bounds hold for all distributional solutions of the considered nonlocal semilinear systems, including Ginzburg-Landau systems and Allen-Cahn equations, in multi-dimensional settings.

Conclusion: The findings provide new uniform boundedness results for nonlocal semilinear systems and even for scalar equations, under weak structural hypotheses.

Abstract: Under mild mass or energy assumptions, we prove uniform $L^\infty$ bounds for all distributional solutions of a multi-dimensional system of nonlocal semilinear equations.
  The setting comprises Ginzburg-Landau systems and Allen-Cahn equations, and the results obtained are new even for scalar equations.

</details>


### [118] [Sign-changing solutions for the critical Neumann problem in the three-dimensional unit ball](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02857)
*Fei Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence of infinitely many nonradial sign-changing solutions to a critical Neumann problem in the unit ball, using gluing techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding nonradial sign-changing solutions for critical elliptic problems with Neumann boundary conditions.
