Method: Combines an inner-outer gluing scheme with a finite-dimensional reduction, using a detailed expansion of the reduced energy to find interior critical points.

Result: For every μ>0, the problem admits infinitely many distinct nonradial sign-changing solutions; for each large even integer K, a solution is built by gluing K transformed copies of a sign-changing entire solution of the critical equation in R^3.

Conclusion: The problem possesses infinitely many nonradial sign-changing solutions for all μ>0, constructed via energy-expansion and gluing methods.

Abstract: We consider the existence of nonradial sign-changing solutions to the problem $Δu-μu+|u|^4u=0$ with zero Neumann boundary conditions in the unit ball for an arbitrary fixed parameter $μ>0$. We prove that, for every $μ>0$, this problem admits infinitely many distinct nonradial sign-changing solutions. More precisely, for every sufficiently large even integer $K$, we construct a solution by gluing $K$ symmetrically arranged, suitably transformed copies of a sign-changing entire solution of the critical equation in $\mathbb{R}^3$. The construction combines an inner--outer gluing scheme with a finite-dimensional reduction. A detailed expansion of the reduced energy allows us to locate an interior critical point in the admissible parameter region and thereby complete the construction.

</details>


### [119] [On the derivative loss for a class of abstract evolution equations with time dependent oscillating coefficients vanishing at initial time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02915)
*Marina Ghisi,Massimo Gobbino,Michael Reissig*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Study of derivative loss for an abstract wave-type equation with a time-dependent speed that is a degenerate monotone shape times a bounded oscillation; degeneracy and oscillations interact, with loss at most one derivative in the purely degenerate case and a minimization problem characterizing the combined case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Propagation speed varying only in time, possibly degenerate, is a setting where one wants to understand how degeneracy and oscillations jointly affect regularity (derivative loss) of solutions.

Method: Reduce the PDE to a family of ODEs via spectral analysis; derive frequency-dependent energy estimates; decompose the time interval into frequency-dependent regions using adapted energies.

Result: Frequency-dependent energy estimates showing at most one derivative loss in the purely degenerate case (monotonicity prevents excessive loss), while with oscillations the loss is given by a minimization problem where degenerate and oscillatory effects genuinely interact.

Conclusion: Degeneracy alone limits loss to one derivative; oscillations interplay with degeneracy to determine the exact derivative loss via a minimization problem.

Abstract: We consider an abstract wave-type evolution equation with a propagation speed depending only on time. We assume that this propagation speed is the product of a monotone, but possibly degenerate, shape function and an oscillating factor bounded from above and from below by positive constants. We investigate how degeneracy and oscillations interact in determining the derivative loss of solutions.
  After reducing the problem to a family of ordinary differential equations through spectral analysis, we derive frequency-dependent energy estimates. In the purely degenerate case, monotonicity of the shape function prevents excessive loss, and solutions lose at most one derivative. In the presence of oscillations, the resulting derivative loss is described by a minimization problem in which the degenerate and oscillatory effects genuinely interact.
  The proof is based on a decomposition of the time interval into frequency-dependent regions, where different adapted energies are used.

</details>


### [120] [Normalized ground states and a mass-constrained scattering threshold for the inhomogeneous NLS with an inverse-square potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02933)
*Mohamed Majdoub,Tarek Saanouni*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper connects scattering theory below the ground-state threshold with variational analysis of prescribed-mass solutions for the focusing inhomogeneous NLS with inverse-square potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Two research lines for this model—scattering below the ground-state threshold and variational analysis with prescribed mass—have developed largely independently. The paper aims to bridge them.

Method: Studies the focusing inhomogeneous nonlinear Schrödinger equation with inverse-square potential in the mass-supercritical, energy-subcritical regime, linking scattering theory and prescribed-mass variational analysis.

Result: Establishes a connection between the two previously separate approaches.

Conclusion: Provides a unified perspective linking scattering theory and variational analysis for this model.

Abstract: We study the focusing inhomogeneous nonlinear Schrödinger equation with an inverse-square potential $$ i\partial_t u+Δu-a|x|^{-2}u+|x|^{-b}|u|^αu=0, \qquad (t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}, $$ in the mass-supercritical and energy-subcritical regime $$ \frac{4-2b}{N}<α<\frac{4-2b}{N-2}, $$ where $N\ge3$ and $0<b<2$. Two main lines of research have developed largely independently for this model: scattering theory below the ground-state threshold and the variational analysis of solutions with prescribed mass. In this work, we establish a connection between these two approaches.

</details>


### [121] [Global existence for viscous systems of conservation laws in $L^p$ framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03011)
*Jean-Paul Adogbo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies global well-posedness of partially diffusive hyperbolic systems in critical homogeneous Besov spaces with high-frequency control in L^p-based spaces (p>2), extending prior work and applying the results to the multidimensional compressible Fourier-Navier-Stokes system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous analysis was limited to a functional framework that did not allow high-frequency components to be controlled in L^p-based spaces with p>2. Extending to this broader framework enables global existence under weaker smallness assumptions and provides more precise qualitative behavior and refined regularity of solutions.

Method: The authors extend their earlier analysis to critical homogeneous Besov spaces where high-frequency components of the solution are controlled in L^p-based spaces with p>2. This generalized functional framework is used to establish global existence and refined regularity for a class of partially diffusive hyperbolic systems.

Result: Global existence is established under weaker smallness assumptions on the initial data, with refined regularity and more precise qualitative information on the resulting solutions. The global existence theorem applies to the multidimensional compressible Fourier-Navier-Stokes system.

Conclusion: The broader L^p-based (p>2) framework successfully extends the prior analysis, yielding stronger global well-posedness results and providing an application to the compressible Fourier-Navier-Stokes system.

Abstract: In this paper, which originates from Chapter 4 of the author's PhD thesis [1, Chap. 4], we investigate the global well-posedness of a class of partially diffusive hyperbolic systems in critical homogeneous Besov spaces. Our main objective is to extend the analysis developed in our previous work [2] to a functional framework in which the high-frequency com- ponents of the solution are controlled in $L^p$-based spaces with $p > 2$. This broader framework allows us to establish global existence under weaker smallness assumptions on the initial data and provides more precise information on the qualitative behavior of the resulting solutions. In particular, our analysis yields refined regularity. As an application, our global existence theorem applies to the multidimensional compressible Fourier-Navier-Stokes system

</details>


### [122] [Obstructions to exact regularity of sublaplacians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03037)
*Gian Maria Dall'Ara*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies exact Sobolev regularity for sublaplacians $P=\sum_j X_j^\dagger X_j$ on a closed manifold and shows that a characteristic submanifold (tangent to all $X_j$) can cause exact regularity to fail, proving this for "worm sublaplacians" analogous to Kohn Laplacians on worm domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Exact regularity ($u\in H^k$ when $f\in H^k$) is a basic well-posedness property for the sublaplacian equation $Pu=f$. The paper aims to understand how geometry—specifically a characteristic submanifold tangent to all the vector fields—can obstruct this regularity, motivated by analogous phenomena in several complex variables (Kohn Laplacians on Diederich–Fornaess worm domains).

Method: The main tool is a scaling lemma inspired by work of Barrett and Christ on the $\bar\partial$-Neumann problem. The authors construct a class of "worm sublaplacians" as real analogues of Kohn Laplacians on worm domains and use the scaling argument to detect failure of exact regularity.

Result: Assuming Hörmander-type connectivity (any two points joined by integral curves of the $X_j$), $Pu=f$ has unique zero-average solutions in the natural energy space for all zero-average $f\in L^2$. However, exact regularity fails for the class of worm sublaplacians: the presence of a characteristic submanifold breaks the $H^k\to H^k$ regularity.

Conclusion: Characteristic submanifolds can genuinely destroy exact Sobolev regularity for sublaplacians, as demonstrated by the worm sublaplacian examples. This provides a real-variable analogue of regularity failure for Kohn Laplacians on Diederich–Fornaess worm domains.

Abstract: Let $M$ be a closed manifold equipped with a collection of smooth real vector fields $X_1,\ldots, X_k$ and a smooth measure $μ$. Let $P:=\sum_j X_j^\dagger X_j $ be the associated sublaplacian. Under the assumption that each pair of points of $M$ can be connected by a path obtained by joining integral curves of the vector fields, the equation \[ Pu=f \] admits unique zero-average solutions $u$ in the natural energy space, for all zero average data $f\in L^2$. We say that exact regularity holds for the above equation if $u\in H^k(M)$ whenever $f\in H^k(M)$, where $H^k(M)$ is the $L^2$ based Sobolev space of any order $k\in \mathbb{N}$. We investigate how the presence of a characteristic submanifold, namely a submanifold tangent to all vector fields $X_j$, may cause a failure of exact regularity. We prove that this indeed happens for a class of "worm sublaplacians", which are real analogues of Kohn Laplacians on Diederich--Fornaess worm domains, of interest in several complex variables. Our main tool is a scaling lemma inspired by work of Barrett and Christ on the $\bar\partial$-Neumann problem.

</details>


### [123] [A Comparison Pogorelov Estimate for Graphical $σ_k$-Curvature Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03062)
*Jundong Zhou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes comparison Pogorelov estimates for admissible solutions of graphical σ_k-curvature equations, generalizing the two-surface estimate of Qiu and Yan for the graphical scalar curvature equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the two-surface estimate of Qiu and Yan (previously for the graphical scalar curvature equation) to the more general graphical σ_k-curvature equations.

Method: Combines the two-surface localization and mixed Gårding comparison of Qiu and Yan with Yan's spectral concavity inequality and an angle-function weight. The comparison graph is assumed k-admissible with bounded slope and a positive interior gap vanishing on the boundary.

Result: Comparison Pogorelov estimates are obtained for 2≤k<n for prescribed data f(x,u), and n/2≤k<n for general data f(x,u,Du).

Conclusion: Comparison Pogorelov estimates hold for admissible solutions of graphical σ_k-curvature equations under the stated admissibility and boundary conditions.

Abstract: We establish comparison Pogorelov estimates for admissible solutions of graphical $σ_k$-curvature equations, extending the two-surface estimate of Qiu and Yan for the graphical scalar curvature equation. The comparison graph is assumed only to be $k$-admissible, with a bounded slope and a positive interior gap that vanishes on the boundary. The estimates cover $2\le k<n$ for prescribed data $f(x,u)$, and $n/2\le k<n$ for general data $f(x,u,Du)$. The proof combines the two-surface localization and mixed Gårding comparison of Qiu and Yan with Yan's spectral concavity inequality and an angle-function weight.

</details>


### [124] [Döblin--Fourier cancellation and kinetic Aleksandrov estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03083)
*Amélie Loher,Connor Mooney,Clément Mouhot*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a cancellation mechanism for linear second-order kinetic equations with rough (measurable, uniformly elliptic) coefficients, yielding exponential Fourier decay with enhanced dissipation and applications to kinetic Aleksandrov estimates and enhanced dissipation on the torus/sphere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Kinetic equations with rough coefficients lack decay/regularity results; the goal is to establish Fourier decay, maximum principles, and enhanced dissipation for non-divergence form operators with measurable uniformly elliptic coefficients.

Method: Decompose dynamics into spatial waves and follow two families of velocity paths where waves acquire nearly opposite phases. Parabolic Krylov–Safonov theory gives a common lower bound for the two velocity marginals; contributions cancel up to a small phase error, producing a Döblin-type contraction, which is iterated to yield exponential Fourier decay. Applications use this decay plus parabolic regularity and Harnack comparisons.

Result: Kinetic Aleksandrov estimate (maximum principle with L^p source): p>2n+1 for coefficients A(t,v) independent of position, and p>4 for autonomous a(x,v) in dimension one; both thresholds optimal across ellipticity ratios. For torus position and sphere velocity with A(t,v), enhanced dissipation with optimal square-root frequency power and Gevrey regularity in position; for A=A(v), exponential L^2 convergence to equilibrium (weighted by invariant measure) and a quantitative spectral gap.

Conclusion: The cancellation/contraction framework yields exponential Fourier decay and enhanced dissipation for kinetic equations with rough coefficients, providing the first decay results for such laws, along with sharp Aleksandrov estimates and optimal enhanced dissipation rates.

Abstract: We develop a cancellation mechanism for linear second-order kinetic equations in non-divergence form with measurable uniformly elliptic coefficients. We decompose the dynamics into spatial waves and follow two families of velocity paths along which the waves acquire nearly opposite phases. The parabolic Krylov--Safonov theory gives a common lower bound for the two velocity marginals. The corresponding contributions cancel up to a small phase error, producing a contraction \textit{à la Döblin}. Iterating this contraction yields exponential Fourier decay estimates with enhanced dissipation.
  We then present two applications.
  The first contribution is a kinetic Aleksandrov estimate: a maximum principle in which the source is measured in an $L^p$ norm. For rough coefficients $A(t,v)$ independent of position, we obtain the estimate for every $p>2n+1$, where $n$ is the dimension of position and velocity. The Fourier decay also gives spatial smoothness, and parabolic regularity gives Hölder continuity in time and velocity. For autonomous coefficients $a(x,v)$ in dimension one, position serves as time away from zero velocity. A Harnack comparison controls returns to small velocity intervals and yields the estimate for every $p>4$. Both thresholds are optimal among those valid for all ellipticity ratios.
  The second contribution concerns position on the torus and velocity on the sphere, with measurable uniformly elliptic diffusion coefficients $A(t,v)$ independent of position. We prove enhanced dissipation with the optimal square-root frequency power and Gevrey regularity in position. For autonomous coefficients $A=A(v)$, we also obtain exponential convergence to equilibrium in $L^2$ weighted by the invariant velocity measure, and a quantitative spectral gap. To our knowledge, these are the first results on decay established for laws of kinetic equations with rough coefficients.

</details>


### [125] [Singular Contour Dynamics and Paradifferential Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03091)
*Xingyu Li,Emeric Roulley,Stefano Scrobogna*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a direct paradifferential approach for nonlinear graph integral operators, proving a structure-preserving paralinearization theorem in arbitrary dimension, and applies it to prove local well-posedness of 3D two-phase free-boundary Euler equations in the stable KRT regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model and analyze nonlinear singular integral operators from contour dynamics (such as the Birkhoff-Rott formulation) directly, without relying on the Dirichlet-Neumann reduction, and to establish well-posedness for the 3D two-phase Euler system with interfacial vorticity.

Method: An abstract structure-preserving paralinearization theorem for homogeneous kernels with nonlinear dependence via normalized finite differences, covering critical principal-value and subcritical locally integrable kernels; applied to the 3D Euler contour-dynamics equation, with an Alinhac good unknown and diagonalization.

Result: Explicit principal symbols, separation of symbolic/far-field/smoothing contributions, cancellation of intermediate symbolic order, preservation of exact skew-adjoint structure at the critical endpoint; local well-posedness for small Sobolev perturbations in the stable KRT regime, uniformly on compact subsets of the stable parameter set.

Conclusion: The direct paradifferential framework captures the complete structure of nonlinear graph integral operators and yields well-posedness for the 3D two-phase free-boundary Euler equations in the stable KRT regime, bypassing the Dirichlet-Neumann reduction.

Abstract: We develop a direct paradifferential approach to nonlinear graph integral operators arising in contour dynamics. The main methodological result is an abstract structure-preserving paralinearization theorem, valid in arbitrary dimension, for a class of homogeneous kernels whose nonlinear dependence on the graph is expressed through normalized finite differences. The theorem covers both critical principal-value kernels and subcritical locally integrable kernels, gives explicit formulas for the principal symbols, and separates the finite-order symbolic contribution generated by the homogeneous singularity from order-zero far-field terms and arbitrarily smoothing remainders. A structural feature of the reduction is the cancellation of the intermediate symbolic order; at the critical endpoint the construction also preserves the exact skew-adjoint structure of the original singular integral.
  As a main application, we consider the three-dimensional two-phase free-boundary Euler equations with constant interfacial background vorticity. We first derive an autonomous contour-dynamics equation in canonical surface variables and then apply the abstract theorem directly to the singular integral operators generated by the Birkhoff--Rott formulation. This yields the complete paradifferential structure of the system without taking the Dirichlet--Neumann reduction as a starting point. After introducing an Alinhac good unknown and diagonalizing the resulting system, we obtain local well-posedness for small Sobolev perturbations in the stable Kelvin--Rayleigh--Taylor regime, uniformly on compact subsets of the stable parameter set.

</details>


### [126] [Weak-BV stability of inflow and outflow problems for the isentropic Euler system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03236)
*Moon-Jin Kang,Jiayun Meng,HyeonSeop Oh,Alexis F. Vasseur*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes the first weak-BV well-posedness and stability theory for small BV subsonic solutions of the 1D isentropic Euler system on a half-line with inflow/outflow boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inflow and outflow initial-boundary value problems for the isentropic Euler system lack a weak-BV stability and uniqueness theory; existing results do not handle boundary conditions determined by Navier-Stokes layers and zero-speed shocks, nor allow general L∞ entropy solutions outside the subsonic region.

Method: Boundary conditions are given via admissible trace sets derived from Navier-Stokes boundary layers and zero-speed shocks. Small BV subsonic solutions are constructed, and a quantitative L2 stability estimate is proven using the a-contraction framework, showing compatibility of the boundary set and that the front-tracking limit satisfies the prescribed inflow/outflow conditions.

Result: For both inflow and outflow problems, unique small BV subsonic solutions are constructed. They satisfy the stability estimate ||U(·,t)-V(·,t)||_{L2} ≲ sqrt(||U(·,0)-V(·,0)||_{L2}) for any small BV solution V and any L∞ entropy solution U with the strong trace property and inflow/outflow conditions.

Conclusion: This yields the first weak-BV stability and uniqueness theory for inflow and outflow initial-boundary value problems, notably allowing U to take values outside the subsonic region and need not have bounded variation or be built by front tracking.

Abstract: We study the well-posedness of small BV solutions to the one-dimensional isentropic Euler system on the half-line under inflow and outflow boundary conditions. These boundary conditions are formulated in terms of admissible trace sets determined by Navier--Stokes boundary layers and zero-speed shocks. For both the inflow and outflow problems, we construct small BV solutions taking values in the subsonic region. These solutions are unique and satisfy the quantitative stability estimate \begin{align*}
  \|U(\cdot, t)-V(\cdot, t)\|_{L^2} \lesssim \sqrt{\|U(\cdot, 0) - V(\cdot, 0)\|_{L^2}} \, , \end{align*} which holds for any such small BV solution $V$ and any $L^\infty$ entropy solution $U$ satisfying the strong trace property and the inflow/outflow boundary conditions. In particular, we emphasize that $U$ may take values outside the subsonic region and need not have bounded variation or be constructed by the front tracking scheme. This establishes the first weak-BV stability and uniqueness theory for inflow and outflow initial-boundary value problems. A key difficulty lies in verifying that the boundary set is compatible with the $a$-contraction framework, and in proving that the front-tracking limit satisfies the prescribed inflow/outflow conditions.

</details>


### [127] [Quantitative homogenization of Monge-Ampère equations in periodic media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03269)
*Tianling Jin,YanYan Li,Hung V. Tran,Xushan Tu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves optimal homogenization rates for the Monge-Ampère equation with periodic oscillating right-hand side, showing that convex solutions converge at rate O(ε^{m+α}) depending on the regularity of the solution and the coefficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Homogenization of fully nonlinear elliptic equations, particularly the Monge-Ampère equation, requires sharp convergence rates. Prior results lacked optimality of exponents in respective regularity scales, and constants often depended on microscopic measure distribution.

Method: Quantitative homogenization for det D²u^ε = F(x, x/ε) with periodic F, comparing u^ε to the homogenized solution u solving det D²u = F̄(x), using regularity theory and elliptic estimates for Monge-Ampère.

Result: For m=0,1 and 0<α≤1, ‖u^ε − u‖_{L∞(Ω)} ≤ Cε^{m+α}, given u ∈ C^{m+2,α} and F ∈ C_x^{m,α}(Ω̄; C_y^{0,γ}(T^n)). Both exponents are optimal. Also established O(ε^α) rate for locally weighted periodic Borel measures with distribution-independent constants.

Conclusion: Optimal homogenization rates for Monge-Ampère are obtained, matching regularity scales, and extended to weighted periodic measures with uniform constants.

Abstract: Let $u^\varepsilon$ and $u$ be the convex solutions of \[ \det D^2u^\varepsilon=F(x,x/\varepsilon),\qquad \det D^2u=\overline F(x) \] on a bounded convex domain $Ω$, with the same Dirichlet data. Here, $F$ is uniformly positive and periodic in its second variable, and $\overline F(x)=\int_{\mathbb T^n}F(x,y)\,dy$. For $m=0,1$ and $0<α\leq1$, we prove \[ \|u^\varepsilon-u\|_{L^\infty(Ω)}\leq C\varepsilon^{m+α},\qquad \forall\,0<\varepsilon\leq1, \] provided that $u\in C^{m+2,α}(\overlineΩ)$ and $F\in C_x^{m,α}(\overlineΩ;C_y^{0,γ}(\mathbb T^n))$ for some $0<γ<1$. Both exponents are optimal in their respective regularity scales. We also establish the $O(\varepsilon^α)$ rate for locally weighted periodic Borel measures, with constants independent of the distribution of the microscopic measure.

</details>


### [128] [On the instability of De Gregorio excited states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03287)
*Miguel M. G. Pascual-Caballo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: All excited states of the De Gregorio equation on the torus are nonlinearly unstable in the Hadamard sense.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish nonlinear instability of the excited states -sin(nx), n>=2, of the De Gregorio equation, which was an open question.

Method: Transform the linearized eigenvalue problem into a Fuchsian equation on the complex disk, obtain a matching condition, verify it rigorously via interval arithmetic, then apply a bootstrap argument (Guo-Hallstrom-Spirn).

Result: Every n>=2 admits a real eigenvalue λ_n in (0.56, 0.6); arbitrarily small H^2 perturbations escape a fixed H^1 neighborhood of the state.

Conclusion: The excited states are nonlinearly unstable, confirmed by rigorous eigenvalue verification and the nonlinear bootstrap.

Abstract: We prove that all excited states $-\sin(nx)$, $n\geq2$, of the De Gregorio equation on the torus are nonlinearly unstable in the sense of Hadamard. More precisely, perturbations arbitrarily small in $H^2$ leave a fixed $H^1$ neighborhood of the state. For every $n\geq2$, the linearized operator has a real eigenvalue $λ_n\in(0.56,0.6)$. To prove this, we transform the eigenvalue problem into a Fuchsian equation on the complex disk and obtain a matching condition for the eigenvalue. This condition is verified rigorously using interval arithmetic. The nonlinear instability follows from a bootstrap argument in the spirit of Guo, Hallstrom, and Spirn.

</details>


### [129] [Well-posedness for the generalised fractional BBM equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03288)
*Jackson Barratt*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes sharp low-regularity local/global well-posedness for generalized fractional BBM equations on the torus, including an endpoint result for the cubic regularized Benjamin-Ono equation, plus probabilistic well-posedness and strong ill-posedness below regularity zero.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior well-posedness results for fractional BBM equations (Bona–Chen 2003; Kim–Kwak 2026) were not sharp; the authors aim to lower the required regularity on the H^s(T^d) scale and understand the threshold behavior, including endpoints and the supercritical regime.

Method: They work on the d-dimensional torus and combine low-regularity (dispersive) analysis with fixed-point arguments, also exploiting L^{k+1} spaces adapted to the degree-k polynomial nonlinearity. Probabilistic (randomization) techniques on the initial data are used for the almost-sure global result, and counterexample/normal-form style constructions yield the ill-posedness.

Result: Low-regularity local and global well-posedness on H^s(T^d) improving Bona–Chen (2003) and Kim–Kwak (2026); endpoint global well-posedness for the cubic regularized Benjamin-Ono equation in H^{1/2}(T); well-posedness in L^{k+1}(T^d); almost-sure global well-posedness in H^s(T^d), s>=0, under suitable randomization; and strong ill-posedness (infinite loss of regularity at every initial datum) in H^s for s<0.

Conclusion: The results are essentially sharp: well-posedness holds down to s>=0 (probabilistically for the general case and deterministically at the endpoint), while for s<0 the equation is strongly ill-posed, exhibiting infinite loss of regularity at all initial data.

Abstract: In this paper, we study a generalised family of fractional BBM equations on the $d$ dimensional torus $\mathbb{T}^d$ for $d\geq 1$. We establish low regularity local and global well-posedness results on the $H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$ scale that improve upon the works of Bona and Chen (2003) and Kim and Kwak (2026). We also prove endpoint global well-posedness for the cubic regularised Benjamin-Ono equation in $H^{1/2}(\mathbb{T})$. Furthermore, we obtain a well-posedness result for the generalised fractional BBM in the spaces $L^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^d)$ where $k$ is order of the polynomial nonlinearity. As an application of this result, we show that for a certain range of values and for a suitable randomisation of the initial data, the model is almost surely globally well-posed in $H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$ for $s\geq 0$. Lastly, we show that the family exhibits a strong form of ill-posedness in $H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$ for $s<0$ in the form of an infinite loss of regularity at all initial datum.

</details>


### [130] [Geometrically distinct unbounded continua of solutions for Yamabe-type equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03292)
*Piotr Stefaniak*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies global bifurcation for Yamabe-type equations on closed Riemannian manifolds with symmetries, proving existence of unbounded continua of sign-changing solutions and giving multiplicity lower bounds on spheres and projective spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Yamabe-type equations are central in geometry and analysis, and understanding solution structures (especially global bifurcation phenomena) under symmetry assumptions is of significant interest. Prior work often lacks global descriptions of solution branches or multiplicity results in symmetric settings.

Method: Global bifurcation theory applied to Yamabe-type equations on closed Riemannian manifolds, restricted to isometric group actions with interval orbit space and a suitable range of exponents, including critical and some supercritical exponents.

Result: Existence of pairwise disjoint unbounded continua bifurcating from positive eigenvalues of the Laplace–Beltrami operator admitting invariant eigenfunctions; all nontrivial solutions in these continua change sign. Lower bounds on the number of geometrically distinct continua bifurcating from the same eigenvalue are obtained on spheres and complex/quaternionic projective spaces.

Conclusion: The results extend bifurcation theory for Yamabe-type problems to symmetric manifolds and critical/supercritical exponents, providing new multiplicity bounds on important model spaces.

Abstract: We study global bifurcation for Yamabe-type equations on closed Riemannian manifolds with symmetries. For isometric group actions with interval orbit space and a suitable range of exponents, we prove the existence of pairwise disjoint unbounded continua bifurcating from the positive eigenvalues of the Laplace--Beltrami operator that admit invariant eigenfunctions. All nontrivial solutions in these continua change sign. The results cover critical and some supercritical exponents. On spheres and complex and quaternionic projective spaces, we obtain lower bounds on the number of geometrically distinct continua bifurcating from the same eigenvalue.

</details>


### [131] [Local Well-posedness of a Diffuse Interface Two-phase Flow Model with Surfactants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03342)
*Helmut Abels,Songzhuang Chen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves local well-posedness of strong solutions for a thermodynamically consistent diffuse interface model of two-phase surfactant flows (Model C), using a tailored resolvent analysis to decouple high-order cross-couplings, and also establishes local well-posedness for a variant (Model D).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Model C (Garcke-Lam-Stinner) for two-phase flows with surfactants has a non-standard, mixed-order strongly coupled structure, so classical Agmon-Douglis-Nirenberg theory and standard Lopatinskii-Shapiro boundary conditions fail due to divergent scaling behaviors. A new analytical framework is needed.

Method: A tailored resolvent analysis adapted to the temporal regularity of the source term in a suitable Hilbert space, yielding exact cancellation of the highest-order cross-couplings; this decoupling enables application of the operator-valued Mikhlin multiplier theorem to obtain resolvent estimates on R. A structural variant, Model D, is also analyzed.

Result: Local well-posedness of strong solutions is established for Model C. Model D achieves a crucial structural decoupling by transferring interfacial surfactant energy entirely from the gradient part to the potential part, and local well-posedness for strong solutions is readily established for it.

Conclusion: The tailored resolvent framework overcomes the limitations of classical theory for mixed-order strongly coupled surfactant flow models, and the structural decoupling in Model D yields a simpler well-posedness proof.

Abstract: We establish the local well-posedness of strong solutions for a thermodynamically consistent diffuse interface model describing two-phase flows with surfactants, specifically the so-called Model C introduced by Garcke, Lam, and Stinner. Because of the non-standard, mixed-order strongly coupled structure of Model C, the classical Agmon-Douglis-Nirenberg theory and conventional Lopatinskii-Shapiro boundary conditions are not applicable due to divergent scaling behaviors. To overcome this, we develop a tailored resolvent analysis, adapted to the temporal regularity of the source term, in a suitable Hilbert space, which yields an exact cancellation of the highest-order cross-couplings in the estimates. This crucial decoupling enables the rigorous application of the operator-valued Mikhlin multiplier theorem to obtain resolvent estimates on $\mathbb{R}$. Furthermore, we analyze a structural variant, denoted as Model D, which achieves a crucial structural decoupling by transferring the interfacial surfactant energy entirely from the gradient part to the potential part, and we readily establish local well-posedness for strong solutions.

</details>


### [132] [Mathematical study of a new Navier-Stokes-alpha model with nonlinear filter equation - Regularity theory and refined time asymptotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03386)
*Manuel Fernando Cortez,Oscar Jarrín*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a new Navier-Stokes-α model with a nonlinear filter equation, governed by a doubly nonlinear parabolic-elliptic coupled system, and establishes higher-order regularity of the global attractor plus an exact determining-modes property for complete trajectories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Building on prior work that proved global well-posedness of weak Leray-type solutions and existence of a global attractor, the authors aim to understand deeper properties of the model, especially higher-order regularity and its impact on long-time dynamics, since standard regularity arguments for linearly filtered Navier-Stokes-α models do not apply due to the nonlinear structure of the elliptic filter and its coupling with the evolution equation.

Method: The authors perform a rigorous mathematical analysis of the doubly nonlinear parabolic-elliptic coupled system, under natural assumptions on A(·). They develop new higher-order estimates for the nonlinear elliptic filter equation to address the delicate regularity issue.

Result: Two main results are obtained: (1) uniform higher-order regularity for the global attractor; (2) an exact determining-modes property for complete trajectories on the attractor, namely, if projections of two complete trajectories onto a sufficiently large finite-dimensional space of Stokes modes coincide at every time, then the trajectories coincide identically.

Conclusion: The new higher-order estimates for the nonlinear elliptic filter equation overcome the obstacles posed by the nonlinear filter and its coupling, and may be of independent interest. The uniform higher-order regularity of the attractor serves as the key ingredient for proving the exact determining-modes property.

Abstract: This article is devoted to the mathematical analysis of a new Navier--Stokes-$α$ model involving a nonlinear filter equation. The resulting model is governed by a doubly nonlinear parabolic--elliptic coupled system. In our previous work [M.~F.~Cortez and O.~Jarrín, \emph{Mathematical study of a new Navier--Stokes-alpha model with nonlinear filter equation -- Part I}, J. Math. Fluid Mech. 28, no.~12 (2026)], we established the global well-posedness of weak Leray-type solutions and the existence of a global attractor. In the present work, under natural assumptions on $A(\cdot)$, we investigate several additional properties of this model, with particular emphasis on higher-order regularity and its consequences for the long-time dynamics. The regularity analysis constitutes a central and delicate issue, since the nonlinear structure of the elliptic filter and its coupling with the evolution equation preclude a direct application of the standard regularity arguments available for linearly filtered Navier--Stokes-$α$ models. Overcoming this difficulty requires new higher-order estimates for the nonlinear elliptic filter equation, which may also be of independent interest. Among the results obtained, two constitute the main contributions of the article. First, we establish uniform higher-order regularity for the global attractor. This result is then used as a fundamental ingredient in proving an exact determining-modes property for complete trajectories on the attractor: if the projections of two complete trajectories onto a sufficiently large finite-dimensional space of Stokes modes coincide at every time, then the two trajectories coincide identically.

</details>


### [133] [Quantitative uniqueness for bi-Laplace equations with $C^2$ lower-order coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03614)
*Zhiwei Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves a quantitative unique continuation result for bi-Laplace equations with C² lower-order coefficients, deriving an L∞ three-ball inequality and a vanishing-order bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quantitative unique continuation for bi-Laplace equations with variable lower-order coefficients is less understood than the classical Laplace case; establishing sharp coefficient dependence is the goal.

Method: Uses the weighted-frequency function technique to analyze Δ²u + W(x)Δu + V(x)·∇u = 0 with C² coefficients.

Result: An L∞ three-ball inequality whose exponential coefficient scales as ‖W‖_{C²}^{1/2} + ‖V‖_{C²}^{1/3}; under normalization, a vanishing-order bound of size C(‖W‖_{C²}^{1/2} + ‖V‖_{C²}^{1/3}).

Conclusion: The weighted-frequency approach yields quantitative unique continuation for bi-Laplace equations with explicit, presumably optimal coefficient dependence on the lower-order terms.

Abstract: We study quantitative unique continuation for bi-Laplace equations
  \[
  Δ^2u+W(x)Δu+V(x)\cdot\nabla u=0
  \] with $C^2$ lower-order coefficients. Using the weighted-frequency function, we establish an $L^\infty$ three-ball inequality whose exponential factor has coefficient dependence $\|W\|_{C^2}^{1/2}+\|V\|_{C^2}^{1/3}$. Under some normalization, we obtain a vanishing-order bound of size $C(\|W\|_{C^2}^{1/2}+\|V\|_{C^2}^{1/3})$.

</details>


### [134] [On improved Hardy-type inequalities in Log-Weighted Sobolev Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03668)
*Sourav Ghosh*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Sharp Hardy-type inequalities in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces are proven, improved via optimal remainder terms yielding an infinite series improvement, and used to establish an optimal Leray-Trudinger-type improvement and interpolation results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sharp Hardy-type inequalities in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces suffer from non-attainability of the sharp constant, leaving room for strengthened forms.

Method: Add suitable remainder terms with optimal weights and sharp constants to improve the sharp inequalities, then apply these improved inequalities to Leray-Trudinger-type settings.

Result: Sharp Hardy-type inequalities in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces, an infinite series improvement, an optimal Leray-Trudinger-type improvement, and interpolation between Hardy-type and Leray-Trudinger-type inequalities.

Conclusion: The approach unifies and strengthens Hardy-type and Leray-Trudinger-type inequalities through improved remainder terms and interpolation.

Abstract: We prove sharp Hardy-type inequalities in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces. Due to the non-attainability of the sharp constant, we improve these inequalities by adding suitable remainder terms consisting of optimal weights and sharp constants. Consequently, this gives an infinite series improvement in Log-weighted Sobolev spaces. Next, by using these improved Hardy-type inequalities, we also prove an optimal Leray-Trudinger-type improvement. Furthermore, we derive interpolation between Hardy-type inequalities and Leray-Trudinger-type inequalities.

</details>


### [135] [Aleksandrov-Bakelman-Pucci maximum principle for $L^n$-viscosity solutions of uniformly elliptic equations with unbounded terms: The critical case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03918)
*Andrzej Święch*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Proves an Aleksandrov-Bakelman-Pucci (ABP) maximum principle for L^n-viscosity sub/super-solutions of fully nonlinear uniformly elliptic equations with a drift term γ satisfying γ(ln(1+γ))^{(n-1)/n} ∈ L^n(Ω), using convex duality and Trudinger-Moser inequalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend the classical ABP maximum principle to fully nonlinear uniformly elliptic equations with a drift coefficient that lies beyond L^n, addressing the borderline integrability regime where standard results may fail.

Method: Uses L^n-viscosity sub/super-solutions; relies on convex duality and Trudinger-Moser type inequalities to handle the weakened integrability condition on the drift γ.

Result: Establishes the ABP maximum principle under the drift condition γ(ln(1+γ))^{(n-1)/n} ∈ L^n(Ω), which is a log-refined borderline integrability assumption.

Conclusion: The ABP maximum principle holds for such equations under the stated Orlicz-type drift condition, providing a sharpened framework via duality and Trudinger-Moser techniques.

Abstract: We prove the Aleksandrov-Bakelman-Pucci maximum principle for $L^n$-viscosity sub/super-solutions of fully nonlinear uniformly elliptic equations in $Ω\subset\mathbb R^n$ with the drift coefficient $γ$ such that $γ(\ln(1+γ))^{\frac{n-1}{n}}\in L^n(Ω)$. The approach is based on convex duality and Trudinger-Moser type inequalities.

</details>


### [136] [Distance Geometry and a Hydrodynamical Hopf-Rinow Criterion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03926)
*Theodore D. Drivas,Patrick Heslin,Gerard Misiołek*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the hydrodynamical Hopf-Rinow problem on the flat torus, characterizing when a fluid reconfiguration is achieved by an energy-minimizing Euler flow via metric betweenness, and proves local geodesic convexity and regularity of the kinetic energy metric on the volume-preserving diffeomorphism group.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The hydrodynamical Hopf-Rinow problem asks whether a given reconfiguration of an ideal fluid can be attained by an Euler flow, and its strong version requires that this flow minimizes total kinetic energy. Understanding this requires geometric properties of the volume-preserving diffeomorphism group equipped with the kinetic energy metric, which were previously unclear.

Method: The authors work on the flat n-dimensional torus (n ≥ 2), proving that sufficiently small normal neighborhoods in the volume-preserving diffeomorphism group with the kinetic energy metric are geodesically convex. They establish local bi-Lipschitz equivalence between the intrinsic geodesic distance and the extrinsic L² distance, and show the squared geodesic distance from the identity admits a C^{1,1} extension.

Result: An equivalent criterion for the strong hydrodynamical Hopf-Rinow problem is given in terms of metric betweenness on the flat 2D torus. Small normal neighborhoods are shown to be geodesically convex, resolving the strong problem locally in the H^s topology. Local bi-Lipschitz equivalence of geodesic and L² distances is established, and the squared geodesic distance extends as C^{1,1} to the ambient L² space.

Conclusion: The paper resolves the strong hydrodynamical Hopf-Rinow problem locally in the H^s topology and provides key geometric and regularity properties of the kinetic energy metric on the volume-preserving diffeomorphism group of the flat torus.

Abstract: The hydrodynamical Hopf-Rinow problem asks whether a given reconfiguration of an ideal fluid can be attained by an Euler flow. On the flat two-dimensional torus, we give an equivalent criterion for the strong version of this problem (which requires that the fluid motion minimizes the total kinetic energy required) in terms of metric betweenness. Toward this, we prove that sufficiently small normal neighborhoods in the volume-preserving diffeomorphism group of the flat $n$-dimensional torus, $n \geq 2$, equipped with the kinetic energy metric, are geodesically convex; resolving the strong problem locally in the $H^s$ topology. We further establish local bi-Lipschitz equivalence of the intrinsic geodesic distance and the extrinsic $L^2$ distance. Finally, we prove that the squared geodesic distance from the identity admits a $C^{1,1}$ extension from a sufficiently small normal neighborhood to the ambient $L^2$ space.

</details>


### [137] [Vanishing discount selection for constant-sign local perturbations of periodic Hamiltonians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04149)
*Andrea Davini*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the vanishing discount selection problem for critical Hamilton-Jacobi equations on R^d with Hamiltonian G=H-V, proving local uniform convergence of discounted solutions to a distinguished critical solution under sign conditions on V.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The vanishing discount problem asks which critical solution is selected as the discount factor vanishes. Little is known on the whole space R^d with a compactly supported potential, where mass can escape to infinity, complicating the selection.

Method: Decompose limiting discounted measures into a retained component and a periodic component capturing mass lost at infinity. For V<=0, prove local uniform convergence and characterize the limit via Mather measures and the unperturbed periodic selected solution. For V>=0, add a zero-average condition w.r.t. every periodic Mather measure.

Result: For V<=0, local uniform convergence to a distinguished critical solution characterized by Mather measures. For V>=0, convergence holds under the extra zero-average condition. Explicit examples show convergence but failure of natural candidate formulas.

Conclusion: The selection depends on whether V<=0 or V>=0, and on mass lost at infinity. Natural formulas for the candidate limit can fail to identify the selected solution even when convergence holds.

Abstract: We study the vanishing discount selection problem for critical Hamil\-ton--Jacobi equations posed on ${\mathbb R}^d$ with Hamiltonian $G=H-V$, where $H$ is continuous, periodic in space, convex and superlinear in the momentum, and $V$ is a compactly supported potential of constant sign. For $V\leqslant0$, we prove local uniform convergence of the discounted solutions to a distinguished critical solution, which we characterize in terms of Mather measures and of the solution selected by the unperturbed periodic problem. The proof relies on a decomposition of limiting discounted measures into a retained component and a periodic component accounting for the mass lost at infinity. For $V\geqslant0$, we establish convergence under the additional assumption that the unperturbed selected solution has zero average with respect to every periodic Mather measure. We also give explicit examples in which the discounted solutions converge, but natural formulas for the candidate limit fail to identify the selected solution.

</details>


### [138] [Slow motion for the time-fractional Allen-Cahn equation with linear and nonlinear diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04208)
*Davide Cusseddu,Raffaele Folino,Luis Fernando López Ríos*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies slow motion of phase transition layers in a time-fractional Allen-Cahn equation with Caputo derivative of order α∈(0,1), showing fractional memory preserves the exponential/algebraic slow-motion distinction and extends to nonlinear diffusion operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how fractional memory in a time-fractional Allen-Cahn equation affects the slow dynamics of phase transition layers, and whether it distinguishes non-degenerate and degenerate potentials as in the classical case.

Method: Analyze the time-fractional Allen-Cahn equation with Caputo time derivative of order α∈(0,1); examine layered solutions for non-degenerate and degenerate potentials; extend analysis to nonlinear diffusion operators, including the p-Laplacian and Perona-Malik type diffusion.

Result: Fractional memory preserves the distinction between exponentially and algebraically slow dynamics. In the non-degenerate case, layered solutions persist on an exponentially long time scale of order exp(A/(αε)), A>0; in the degenerate case, the algebraic time scale is modified by factor 1/α. The same fractional slow-motion mechanism persists for p-Laplacian and Perona-Malik diffusion.

Conclusion: The fractional slow-motion mechanism is robust, preserving the exponential/algebraic dichotomy and generalizing to nonlinear diffusion settings.

Abstract: We study the slow motion of phase transition layers for a time-fractional Allen--Cahn equation with a Caputo time derivative of order $α\in(0,1)$. We show that the fractional memory preserves the distinction between exponentially and algebraically slow dynamics associated with non-degenerate and degenerate potentials, respectively. In the non-degenerate case, layered solutions persist on an exponentially long time scale of order $\exp(A/(α\varepsilon))$, $A>0$, while in the degenerate case the corresponding algebraic time scale is modified by the factor $1/α$. Finally, we extend the analysis to nonlinear diffusion operators, considering both the $p$-Laplacian and a Perona--Malik type diffusion, showing that the same fractional slow-motion mechanism persists in these more general settings.

</details>


### [139] [Existence and stability of shock profiles for a one-dimensional full compressible Navier--Stokes--Poisson equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04257)
*Yeping Li,Yuan Yuan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Studies existence and asymptotic stability of traveling shock waves for the 1D full compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson equations modeling viscous heat-conducting ions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The 1D full compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson system describes viscous heat-conducting ions in a self-consistent electrostatic field; understanding shock wave existence and stability is important for these physical models.

Method: Proves existence/uniqueness of a small-amplitude smooth shock profile via the center manifold theorem, then establishes large-time asymptotic stability under small smooth perturbations using energy estimates on a specially constructed integrated system with anti-derivative variables incorporating electrostatic potential and heat conduction effects.

Result: Existence and uniqueness of a smooth small-amplitude shock profile is proven, and its large-time asymptotic stability is established under suitably small smooth perturbations.

Conclusion: The dissipative integrated reformulation, combined with careful handling of induced couplings, yields the desired energy estimates and stability results.

Abstract: This paper studies the existence and asymptotic stability of traveling shock waves for the one-dimensional full compressible Navier--Stokes--Poisson equations, which model viscous heat-conducting ions in a self-consistent electrostatic field. We first prove the existence and uniqueness of a smooth shock profile of small amplitude by the center manifold theorem, and then establish its large-time asymptotic stability under suitably small smooth perturbations. The proof is based on an energy estimate for a specially constructed integrated system derived from the full compressible Navier--Stokes--Poisson equations. To carry out this estimate, we introduce suitable anti-derivative variables that incorporate the effects of the self-consistent electrostatic potential and heat conduction, thereby reformulating the original system as a dissipative integrated one. The subsequent energy estimates are completed by carefully handling the induced couplings.

</details>


### [140] [Sharp Poincaré Inequalities for Families of Nonlocal Nonconvex Functionals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04259)
*Feng Dai,Dachun Yang,Wen Yuan,Yirui Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes sharp Poincaré inequalities for a family of nonlocal nonconvex functionals Φ^γ_{λ,p}, showing the inequality holds if and only if γ ≤ -1, extending Nguyen's result and resolving open questions on nonlocal Poincaré inequalities, Sobolev/BV characterizations, and Rellich–Kondrachov/VMO regularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Nguyen posed questions on nonlocal Poincaré inequalities, and earlier works (Nguyen C. R. Math. 2025; Brezis et al. Anal. PDE 2024) raised questions on Sobolev and BV characterizations. This paper aims to answer these and provide tools for studying regularity and compactness via these functionals.

Method: The authors analyze the nonlocal nonconvex functional Φ^γ_{λ,p}(g;Q_0), which measures (weighted) the measure of pairs where the difference quotient exceeds a threshold λ|x-y|^{1+γ/p}, and prove the sharp Poincaré-type estimate ∫∫|g(x)-g(y)|^p dx dy ≲ λ^p + Φ^γ_{λ,p}(g;Q_0).

Result: The estimate holds for all measurable g and λ if and only if γ ∈ (-∞,-1], with the implicit constant depending only on N, p, γ. This extends Nguyen's Poincaré inequality (case γ = -p). As applications: a lower bound for Γ-convergence of Φ^γ_{λ,p}, asymptotic characterizations for Sobolev and BV spaces, and identification of the optimal γ range for the Rellich–Kondrachov theorem and a VMO regularity criterion at fixed threshold.

Conclusion: The results answer Nguyen's question on nonlocal Poincaré inequalities and, for γ ∈ (-∞,-1], also answer questions on Sobolev and BV characterizations posed by Nguyen and by Brezis et al.

Abstract: Let $N\in\mathbb N$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, and $Q_0:=(0,1)^N$. In this article, we establish sharp Poincaré inequalities for the nonlocal nonconvex functionals $$ Φ_{λ,p}^γ(g;Q_0) :=λ^p\iint_{\{(x,y)\in Q_0^2:\,x\ne y,\,|g(x)-g(y)|>λ|x-y|^{1+\fracγ{p}}\}} |x-y|^{γ-N}\,dx\,dy, $$ where $g:Q_0\to\mathbb R$ is measurable, $λ\in(0,\infty)$, and $γ\in\mathbb R$. Specifically, we prove that the estimate $$ \int_{Q_0}\int_{Q_0}|g(x)-g(y)|^p\,dx\,dy \lesssimλ^p+Φ_{λ,p}^γ(g;Q_0) $$ holds for any such $g$ and $λ$ if and only if $γ\in(-\infty,-1]$, where the implicit positive constant depends only on $N$, $p$, and $γ$. This extends Nguyen's Poincaré inequality for $γ=-p$ and provides a useful tool for studying regularity and compactness through these functionals. As applications, we obtain a lower bound for the $Γ$-convergence of $Φ^γ_{λ,p}$ and related asymptotic characterizations for Sobolev and BV spaces. Moreover, we identify the optimal range of $γ$ for which both the Rellich--Kondrachov theorem and a criterion for VMO regularity involving $Φ^γ_{λ,p}$ at a fixed threshold hold. These results also answer the question on nonlocal Poincaré inequalities posed by H.-M. Nguyen in [C. R. Math. Acad. Sci. Paris 363 (2025)]. For $γ\in(-\infty,-1]$, they further answer questions on Sobolev and BV characterizations posed in the same article and by H. Brezis et al. in [Anal. PDE 17 (2024)].

</details>


### [141] [Complete classification of solutions for spin-1 Bose-Einstein condensates in R](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04411)
*Xiao Luo,Cunxin Wei,Juncheng Wei,Maoding Zhen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper presents a complete classification of explicit solutions for spin-1 Bose-Einstein condensates in R using a non-standard Hirota bilinear method, determinant formula, and general left-uniqueness strategy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spin-1 BECs are a key platform for quantum magnetism and coherent spin dynamics, with three Zeeman components coupled by spin-exchange interactions under conserved particle number and magnetization. A rigorous classification of stationary states clarifies possible spin configurations and their physical origins.

Method: The authors apply the non-standard Hirota's bilinear method together with a determinant formula and a general left-uniqueness strategy. Unlike the classical Hirota method, the non-standard version introduces auxiliary functions to construct the bilinear form, adding constraints on wave functions and enabling treatment of more complex equations.

Result: Novel explicit solutions are obtained and a complete classification of solutions for spin-1 BECs in R is given. This is claimed to be the first systematic complete classification of solutions for spin-1 BECs.

Conclusion: The classification provides experimentally testable predictions for spin populations, relative phases, and magnetic textures, offering insight into the physical origins of spin configurations.

Abstract: Spin-1 Bose-Einstein condensates provide a fundamental platform for exploring quantum magnetism and coherent spin dynamics. Their three Zeeman components are coupled by spin-exchange interactions under conserved particle number and magnetization, giving rise to rich magnetic phases and nonlinear spin structures; a rigorous classification of stationary states therefore clarifies the possible spin configurations and their physical origins. In this paper, as a continuation of our previous work \cite{LLWZ23,LLWZ24,LLWZ26}, we study the classification of solution for the spin-1 Bose-Einstein condensates in $\mathbb{R}$, by applying the non-standard Hirota's bilinear method, the determinant formula and the general left-uniqueness strategy, we present some novel explicit solution and give the complete classification of the solutions. In comparison with the classical Hirota's method, the non-standard Hirota's method needs to introduce auxiliary functions to construct Hirota's bilinear form, which essentially adds additional constraint on the wave functions, so that it possible to deal with more complex equations. As far as we know, this is the first systematic study the complete classification of solutions to the spin-1 Bose-Einstein condensates. Our classification results yields experimentally testable predictions for spin populations, relative phases, and magnetic textures

</details>


### [142] [On some concepts of convergence and their connections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04478)
*Tatiana Danielsson,Liselott Flodén,Anders Holmbom,Pernilla Johnsen,Marianne Olsson Lindberg,Jens Persson*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper traces classical two-scale convergence and periodic unfolding back to the broader concept of general two-scale convergence, compares very weak two-scale convergence with periodic unfolding for amplified Sobolev-bounded sequences, and shows these approaches are not immediately equivalent in that setting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical two-scale convergence and periodic unfolding both rely on an added scale to represent periodic micro-oscillations, yet their relationship to general two-scale convergence and to each other remains unclear. The paper aims to unify these notions and clarify their differences.

Method: The authors relate classical two-scale convergence and periodic unfolding to the general two-scale convergence framework, then compare very weak two-scale convergence with periodic unfolding for sequences bounded in Sobolev spaces amplified by the micro-oscillation scale. Examples and illustrations of the concepts of convergence are provided.

Result: For amplified sequences bounded in Sobolev spaces, very weak two-scale convergence and periodic unfolding turn out not to be equivalent in an immediate sense.

Conclusion: The paper serves both as a literature overview, with examples and illustrations of convergence concepts, and as a source of new results linking and distinguishing the various two-scale notions.

Abstract: The notions of classical two-scale convergence and the periodic unfolding method, which both rely on an additional scale for the representation of periodic micro-oscillations, are in this paper traced back to the wider concept of general two-scale convergence. Moreover, a comparison is made between very weak two-scale convergence and periodic unfolding with respect to sequences bounded in Sobolev spaces, amplified by the scale of the micro-oscillations. It turns out that, for amplified sequences, these approaches are not equivalent in an immediate sense. The paper both serves as a literature overview, with examples and illustrations of the concepts of convergence, and contains new results.

</details>


### [143] [Global and nonglobal solutions for an abstract semilinear heat equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04550)
*Ricardo Castillo,Ricardo Freire,Miguel Loayza*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies global and non-global nonnegative solutions of an abstract semilinear heat equation in L^p spaces, deriving Fujita-type blow-up results applicable to sub-Riemannian manifolds and graphs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work extends recent results of Lenz, Schmidt, and Zimmermann on blow-up of nonnegative solutions of abstract semilinear heat equations, generalizing to more general settings involving sub-Markovian semigroups and multiple nonlinear terms.

Method: Existence of global and non-global nonnegative solutions is analyzed for u_t - Lu = sum of l_i(t) f_i(u) in L^p(X,mu), where L generates a sub-Markovian strongly continuous semigroup, using the framework of sigma-finite measure spaces and continuous coefficient/nonlinearity functions.

Result: The findings yield Fujita-type results applied to the Laplacian operator on sub-Riemannian manifolds and locally finite graphs.

Conclusion: The results generalize and extend the prior work of Lenz, Schmidt, and Zimmermann, broadening the scope of abstract semilinear heat equation blow-up theory.

Abstract: In this paper, we focus on the existence of both global and non-global nonnegative solutions for the abstract semilinear heat equation $u_t - Lu = \sum_{i=1}^k \ell_i(t) f_i(u)$ in $L^p(X, μ)$. Here $(X,μ)$ represents a $σ$-finite measure space, $L$ is the infinitesimal generator of a sub-Markovian strongly continuous semigroup of bounded linear operators in $L^p(X,μ)$, and for each $i = 1, \ldots, k$, the functions $\ell_i: [0, \infty) \rightarrow [0, \infty)$ and $f_i: [0, \infty) \rightarrow [0, \infty)$ are continuous. We utilize our findings to apply to the Laplacian operator on sub-Riemannian manifolds and locally finite graphs, obtaining Fujita-type results. Our results extend the recent work of Lenz, Schmidt, and Zimmermann, "Blow-up of nonnegative solutions of an abstract semilinear heat equation" (Calc. Var. Partial Differential Equations, 2023).

</details>


### [144] [Remarks on a multi-dimensional transport equation with nonlocal velocity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04632)
*Wanwan Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a multidimensional nonlocal transport equation involving the transform R_a, generalizing the Kiselev-Sarsam equation, and establishes improved inequalities yielding finite-time blow-up results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The equation was recently introduced as a natural generalization of the Kiselev-Sarsam equation. The authors aim to improve upon an existing nonlinear integral inequality and to better understand blow-up behavior.

Method: An improved pointwise inequality for the transform R_a is used to derive a better, more natural version of a prior nonlinear integral inequality. A new weighted nonlinear inequality for R_a is also established.

Result: The improved pointwise inequality leads to a sharper nonlinear integral inequality. The new weighted inequality implies finite-time blow-up for a class of smooth radial initial data.

Conclusion: The transform R_a admits refined inequalities that yield stronger analytical results, including finite-time blow-up for certain smooth radial data.

Abstract: In this paper, we revisit a multi-dimensional nonlocal transport equation of the form \begin{eqnarray*} \partial_tρ+g\mathcal{R}_aρ\cdot \nablaρ= 0,~ρ(\cdot,0)=ρ_{0}, \end{eqnarray*} where the transform $\mathcal{R}_a$ is defined by \begin{eqnarray*} \mathcal{R}_aρ(x,t)=\frac{Γ(\frac{n+1}{2})}{π^{\frac{n+1}{2}}}P.V.\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^n}\Big(\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{n+1}}-\frac{x-y}{(|x-y|^2+a^2)^{\frac{n+1}{2}}}\Big)ρ(y,t)dy. \end{eqnarray*} This model was first introduced in [W. Zhang, J. Differential Equations 467 (2026) 114275] as a natural generalization of the well-known Kiselev-Sarsam equation proposed in [A. Kiselve and N.A. Sarsam, Nonlinearity 38 (2025) 055018]. By using an improved pointwise inequality for the transform $\mathcal{R}_a$, we obtain a better and more natural version of a nonlinear integral inequality proved in Zhang [31] for the transform $\mathcal{R}_a$. In addition, we also establish a new weighted nonlinear inequality for the transform $\mathcal{R}_a$ implying the finite-time blow-up for a class of smooth radial initial data.

</details>


### [145] [Global well-posedness for the 2D primitive equations with subcritical horizontal dissipation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04840)
*Quyuan Lin,Changhui Tan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global well-posedness of classical solutions to the 2D primitive equations with fractional horizontal dissipation for large data in the subcritical range 1<α≤2, establishing a sharp dissipation threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the sharp dissipation threshold separating global well-posedness from ill-posedness for the 2D primitive equations with fractional horizontal dissipation.

Method: A hydrostatic energy estimate is derived by splitting the nonlinear energy into symmetric and antisymmetric parts, exploiting the commutator structure of the antisymmetric part; anisotropy and incompressibility bound the nonlinear energy by the L∞ norm of hydrostatic vorticity times a quadratic velocity norm with one-half extra horizontal derivative; the vorticity maximum principle yields enhanced velocity bounds closing the estimates.

Result: Global well-posedness of classical solutions for arbitrarily large initial data in 1<α≤2, complementing known ill-posedness for 0≤α<1, thus giving a sharp threshold.

Conclusion: The subcritical range 1<α≤2 is exactly the sharp regime for large-data global well-posedness in the corresponding solution framework.

Abstract: We establish global well-posedness of classical solutions to the two-dimensional primitive equations with fractional horizontal dissipation for arbitrarily large initial data in the full subcritical range $1<α\leq2$. Together with the known ill-posedness results for $0\leqα<1$, this establishes the sharp dissipation threshold for large-data global well-posedness in the corresponding solution framework.
  The key ingredient is a hydrostatic energy estimate obtained by splitting the nonlinear energy into symmetric and antisymmetric parts and exploiting the commutator structure of the latter. Using anisotropy and incompressibility, we bound the nonlinear energy by the $L^\infty$ norm of the hydrostatic vorticity times a quadratic velocity norm with only one-half additional horizontal derivative. The vorticity maximum principle then yields enhanced velocity bounds, which close the vorticity estimates and verify the continuation criterion throughout the subcritical regime.

</details>


### [146] [Compactly Supported Strong Ill-Posedness of the Two-Dimensional Euler Flow in $W^{3,1}(\mathbb R^2)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04872)
*Yingzhe Ban,Maotuo Guo,Qi Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves strong ill-posedness of the 2D incompressible Euler equation in the endpoint velocity space W^{3,1}_σ(R^2), constructing divergence-free data arbitrarily close to any smooth background whose unique global solutions blow up instantaneously in W^{3,1} norm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The 2D Euler equation is known to be well-posed in many Sobolev spaces, but the endpoint space W^{3,1} (where the norm barely fails to control the Lipschitz norm of vorticity) is a borderline case. The authors aim to determine whether well-posedness holds at this endpoint or whether the equation is strongly ill-posed there.

Method: The proof combines localized hyperbolic insertion, the Bonami–Poornima nonmultiplier mechanism, and many-packet dilution. Moment cancellation and positive material separation are used to control the nonlocal Biot–Savart interactions, enabling a nested construction within a fixed compact region. A complementary far-field argument handles the non-compact-support case.

Result: For any compactly supported smooth divergence-free background and any δ>0, the authors build a divergence-free datum within δ of it in W^{3,1}, localized in a prescribed open neighborhood of (and disjoint from) the background support. The unique global finite-energy solution has curl u ∈ C([0,∞); W^{2,1}), yet ess sup_{0<t<T} ||u(t)||_{W^{3,1}} = ∞ for every T>0. Without compact support, a dense G_δ set of data loses W^{3,1} regularity at every positive rational time and on a residual set of times.

Conclusion: The 2D incompressible Euler equation is strongly ill-posed in the endpoint space W^{3,1}_σ(R^2), with instantaneous unbounded growth of the W^{3,1} norm from arbitrarily small perturbations of any smooth data, both in compactly supported and general settings.

Abstract: We prove strong ill-posedness for the two-dimensional incompressible Euler equation in the endpoint velocity space $W^{3,1}_σ(\mathbb R^2)$. Given any compactly supported smooth divergence-free background and any $δ>0$, we construct a single compactly supported divergence-free datum within $δ$ of the background in $W^{3,1}$. The perturbation can be localized inside any prescribed nonempty open neighborhood of the background support while remaining disjoint from that support. The associated unique global finite-energy solution $u$ satisfies ${\rm curl} u\in C([0,\infty);W^{2,1}(\mathbb R^2))$, but \[
  \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0<t<T}\|u(t)\|_{W^{3,1}}=\infty
  \qquad\text{for every }T>0. \] The proof combines localized hyperbolic insertion, the Bonami--Poornima nonmultiplier mechanism, and many-packet dilution. Moment cancellation and positive material separation control the nonlocal Biot--Savart interactions, allowing a nested construction inside one fixed compact region. Without the compact-support requirement on the limiting datum, a complementary far-field argument yields a dense $G_δ$ set of data whose solutions leave $W^{3,1}$ at every positive rational time and on a residual set of times, with unbounded essential supremum on every nonempty open time interval.

</details>


### [147] [Hardy Space and Exponential Decay of Inhomogeneous Navier-Stokes System in the 2D Global domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04884)
*Xinyu Fan,Boqiang Lv,Xue Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global well-posedness and uniform exponential decay of velocity for 2D inhomogeneous incompressible Navier-Stokes with vacuum and non-compact densities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Study 2D inhomogeneous incompressible Navier-Stokes on global domain with zero total momentum, allowing vacuum and densities with finite moments but not compact support—cases not covered by previous work.

Method: Exploit cancellation structure in 2D equations and spatial moment bounds on density; show inertial force lies in Hardy space; derive weighted Stokes estimates via endpoint Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequalities; identify far-field velocity and control remainder.

Result: Uniform exponential decay of velocity on global domains; global well-posedness of strong solutions for density-dependent viscosity; uniform regularity of flow maps for measurable density patches in constant viscosity model.

Conclusion: The approach yields strong global results for 2D inhomogeneous Navier-Stokes under weak density assumptions.

Abstract: We investigate the inhomogeneous incompressible Navier-Stokes system in the 2D global domain with zero initial total momentum, allowing vacuum and densities with finite moments but not necessarily compact support. Exploiting the cancellation structure inherent in the 2D equations along with spatial moment bounds on the density, we derive that the inertial force belongs to the Hardy space and establish weighted Stokes estimates through endpoint Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequalities. These estimates identify the far-field velocity and control the remainder, leading to uniform exponential decay of the velocity throughout global domains. Combined with higher order regularity bounds, we establish the global well-posedness of strong solutions for the density-dependent viscosity case, and prove the uniform regularity of flow maps for measurable density patches in the constant viscosity model.

</details>


### [148] [Global strong solutions to the 1D full compressible Navier--Stokes equations with temperature-dependent coefficients and vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04894)
*Yachun Li,Peng Lu,Zhaoyang Shang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global strong solutions are proven for the 1D full compressible Navier-Stokes system with degenerate viscosity/heat conductivity, allowing vacuum, for large data when boundary temperature is high.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses the 1D full compressible Navier-Stokes system with temperature-dependent viscosity and heat conductivity that vanish with temperature, and nonnegative density that may vanish (vacuum). The goal is to establish global existence of strong solutions for arbitrarily large data without requiring velocity or temperature perturbations to shrink as boundary temperature grows.

Method: For α>2 and β>2α+4, the authors prove global existence of strong solutions for large compatible H² data with sufficiently high boundary temperature. Techniques include a shifted initial-layer weight, exact symmetrization separating fast thermal and momentum modes, uniform estimates surviving the vacuum limit, a relative-entropy identity centered at the conserved mean density, physical effective viscous flux, a Zlotnik-type estimate along particle trajectories, and finite-time transport estimates for density derivatives.

Result: The density remains uniformly bounded and the temperature stays uniformly comparable to the prescribed boundary temperature. After the initial layer, vacuum-compatible exponential relaxation is established: density converges to its conserved mean in every finite L^p, velocity and temperature perturbations decay in H¹, and weighted material-derivative energy decays at rate cθ̄^{1-α}.

Conclusion: The system admits global strong solutions for large data with high boundary temperature and vacuum, exhibiting exponential relaxation toward equilibrium with quantified decay rates, without requiring smallness of perturbations.

Abstract: We study the initial--boundary value problem for the one-dimensional full compressible Navier--Stokes system whose viscosity and heat conductivity are given by $μ(θ)=μθ^α$ and $κ(θ)=κθ^β$, respectively. The initial density is nonnegative and is allowed to vanish. For $α>2$ and $β>2α+4$, we prove the existence of a global strong solution for arbitrarily large compatible $H^2$ data, provided that the prescribed boundary temperature is sufficiently high relative to their size. No velocity or temperature perturbation is required to shrink as the boundary temperature tends to infinity. A shifted initial-layer weight and an exact symmetrization of the two material-derivative equations separate the fast thermal mode from the momentum mode. The resulting estimates are uniform with respect to the positive lower bound used in the approximation and therefore survive the limit to genuine vacuum. The proof further combines a relative-entropy identity centered at the conserved mean density, the physical effective viscous flux, a Zlotnik-type estimate along particle trajectories, and finite-time transport estimates for the density derivatives. The density remains uniformly bounded and the temperature stays uniformly comparable with the prescribed boundary temperature. After the initial layer we establish vacuum-compatible exponential relaxation: the density converges to its conserved mean in every finite $L^p$, the velocity and temperature perturbation decay in $H^1$, and the weighted material-derivative energy decays at rate $c\barθ^{1-α}$.

</details>


### [149] [Long-Time Nonlinear Stability of Large-Amplitude Radial Rarefactive Flows for the Multidimensional Compressible Euler Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04901)
*Faris El-Katri*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies multidimensional perturbations of radially symmetric rarefactive flows for the compressible Euler equations without smallness assumptions on the background, deriving lower bounds on the classical lifespan and stability timespan.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior stability/lifespan results typically require smallness of the background. The authors aim to remove this restriction and to exploit the special rarefactive structure to obtain improved (longer) estimates compared to general classical backgrounds.

Method: A perturbation analysis of radially symmetric rarefactive flows for the compressible Euler equations is performed, deriving lower bounds on both the classical lifespan and stability timespan of the perturbed solution. No smallness assumption on the background is imposed.
