Result: The derived lower bounds tend to infinity as the perturbation size approaches zero. Moreover, due to the rarefactive nature of the background, the estimate is substantially longer than the corresponding one for a general classical background with bounded first derivatives.

Conclusion: Rarefactive backgrounds allow significantly longer lifespans/stability timespans for multidimensional perturbations, even without background smallness, highlighting the stabilizing role of rarefaction.

Abstract: In this paper, we study multidimensional perturbations of radially symmetric rarefactive flows for the compressible Euler equations. No assumptions on the smallness of the background are imposed. We derive lower bounds on both the classical lifespan and stability timespan of the perturbed solution. These lower bounds are demonstrated to tend to infinity as the size of the perturbation approaches zero. Furthermore, owing to the rarefactive nature of the background, the resulting estimate is shown to be substantially longer than the corresponding estimate for a general classical background with bounded first derivatives.

</details>


### [150] [Normalized solutions to critical and supercritical Schrödinger equations in a ball](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04935)
*Jun Wang,Zhaoyang Yin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For radial normalized solutions of a focusing NLS in the unit ball with fixed L2 mass, the set of attainable masses is a bounded interval for p >= p_c, disproving mass attainment for all masses in the critical/supercritical radial class.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Soave proved that for 1 < p < p_c every positive mass admits infinitely many radial normalized solutions and asked whether every mass is attained in the Sobolev critical and supercritical regimes. This paper answers that question in the radial class.

Method: Study radial normalized (fixed L2 mass) solutions to -Δu + λu = |u|^{p-1}u on the unit ball B1, with unknown λ and constraint ∫_{B1}|u|^2 = ρ, for p >= p_c = (N+2)/(N-2).

Result: For every p >= p_c, the set of masses attained by nontrivial radial solutions is an interval with finite positive supremum. At p = p_c the attainable masses form (0, ρ*_rad) or (0, ρ*_rad], with 0 < ρ*_rad < ∞; the same finite-threshold alternative holds for p > p_c.

Conclusion: In the radial class, the subcritical "all masses" phenomenon fails throughout the entire Sobolev critical and supercritical range.

Abstract: In this paper, we study radial normalized solutions to nonlinear Schrödinger equations in the unit ball \[
  -Δu+λu=|u|^{p-1}u,
  \qquad \int_{B_1}|u|^2\,dx=ρ, \] where $λ$ is unknown and $p\ge p_c:=\frac{N+2}{N-2}$. Soave \cite{Soave} proved that for every $1<p<p_c$ every positive mass is attained by infinitely many radial solutions and explicitly raised the question of whether every mass is attained in the Sobolev critical and supercritical regimes. We answer this question negatively in the radial class: for every $p\ge p_c$, the set of masses attained by nontrivial radial solutions is an interval with finite positive supremum. In particular, at $p=p_c$ the set of attainable masses is $(0,ρ^*_{\rm rad})$ or $(0,ρ^*_{\rm rad}]$, with $0<ρ^*_{\rm rad}<\infty$, and the same finite threshold alternative holds for every $p>p_c$. Thus, in the radial class, the subcritical all masses phenomenon fails throughout the full Sobolev critical and supercritical range.

</details>


### [151] [A Spacetime-Diffeomorphic Approach to Magnetohydrodynamics around Black Holes: Dilation Covariance, Linearization,and the No-Hair Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04983)
*Yuanya Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper introduces a uniform 4D spacetime dilation and shows the ideal MHD system is form-invariant under it, deriving that the L→∞ limit yields stationary electrovacuum uniquely given by Kerr–Newman—offering a conservation-law perspective on the no-hair theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The ideal MHD system describing a conducting plasma shares the same local balance structure as hot accretion flows around black holes. The authors seek a local, conservation-law-based route to the black hole no-hair theorem, complementary to classical Israel–Carter–Robinson theorems and modern GW/EHT tests.

Method: Define a uniform 4D spacetime dilation Φ_L scaling (t,x,y,z) by L>1, a smooth orientation-preserving C∞ diffeomorphism. Pull the MHD conservation laws back through Φ_L to test form-invariance, tracking rescalings of current density (j=j'/L), magnetic diffusivity (η'=η/L), and external sources (×L). Then take the formal limit L→∞ to localize dynamics near a horizon.

Result: The closed ideal-MHD system is proven covariant under Φ_L. The transformation realizes three effects: linearization to linearised MHD, slow-motion dilation of dissipative/dynamical timescales, and electron-microscope magnification of small-scale structure. The L→∞ limit makes resistivity vanish, freezes the asymptotic time derivative, and redshifts away transient multipolar structure, leaving the unique Kerr–Newman geometry characterized solely by M, J, Q.

Conclusion: The exterior of the compact object is fully characterized by mass M, angular momentum J, and charge Q. This provides a local, conservation-law-based perspective on the black hole no-hair theorem, complementing classical theorems and contemporary gravitational-wave and Event Horizon Telescope observations.

Abstract: The ideal magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) system that governs a conducting plasma---the Maxwell equations together with the conservation laws of mass, momentum and energy---is the same local balance structure that describes hot accretion flows around black holes. We introduce a uniform four-dimensional spacetime dilation \[ Φ_L:\;(t',x',y',z')\mapsto (t,x,y,z)=(Lt',Lx',Ly',Lz'),\qquad L>1, \] and show that it is a smooth, orientation-preserving $C^\infty$ diffeomorphism of flat spacetime. Pulling the MHD conservation laws back through $Φ_L$, we prove that the closed ideal-MHD system is form-invariant (covariant), with the current density rescaling as $\bm j=\bm j'/L$, the magnetic diffusivity as $η'=η/L$, and external sources rescaled by $L$. The transformation realises three physically distinct effects on the perturbed flow: a \emph{linearization} to the tangent-space (linearised MHD) system, a \emph{movie slow-motion} dilation of dissipative and dynamical timescales, and an \emph{electron-microscope} magnification of small-scale structure. Taking the formal limit $L\to\infty$ localises the dynamics on a neighbourhood of a horizon: resistivity vanishes, the asymptotic time derivative freezes, and all transient multipolar structure of the infalling plasma redshifts away. The remaining stationary, axisymmetric, asymptotically flat electrovacuum is uniquely the Kerr--Newman geometry, so that the exterior of the compact object is characterised only by its mass $M$, angular momentum $J$ and charge $Q$. The result provides a local, conservation-law-based perspective on the black hole no-hair theorem that is complementary to the classical Israel--Carter-- Robinson theorems and to contemporary gravitational-wave and Event Horizon Telescope tests.

</details>


### [152] [Construction of infinite time bubble tower solutions for energy critical equivariant Schrödinger maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05075)
*Yuchen Yin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Proves existence of infinite-time bubble tower solutions for energy-critical k-equivariant Schrödinger maps from R^2 to S^2 with k>=3, decomposing asymptotically into N bubbles with specific power-law scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the long-time asymptotic behavior and formation of multi-bubble (tower) structures for energy-critical equivariant Schrödinger maps, which is a natural setting for soliton/bubble dynamics.

Method: Backward construction combined with modulation analysis and high-order energy estimates. Uses Darboux coordinates adapted to the multi-bubble profile to write the remainder in complex scalar form, plus a correction term that cancels the leading nonlinear defect.

Result: For any integers k>=3 and N>=1, constructs a k-equivariant solution global in one time direction, asymptotically a superposition of N bubbles with scales λ_j(t) ~ |t|^{-β_j}, where β_j = (1/2)((k/(k-2))^{N-j} - 1).

Conclusion: Infinite-time bubble tower solutions exist for energy-critical k-equivariant Schrödinger maps with k>=3, exhibiting quantized power-law bubble scales determined by k and the tower index.

Abstract: We prove the existence of infinite time bubble tower solutions for energy critical $k$-equivariant Schrödinger maps $\mathbb{R}^2\to \mathbb{S}^2$ with $k\geq 3$. More precisely, for any integers $k\geq 3$ and $N\geq 1$, we construct a $k$-equivariant solution that is global in one time direction and decomposes asymptotically into a superposition of $N$ bubbles with scales $λ_j(t)\sim|t|^{-β_j}$, where $β_j=\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\frac{k}{k-2}\right)^{N-j}-1\right)$. Our approach is a backward construction combined with modulation analysis and high order energy estimates. We use Darboux coordinates adapted to the multi bubble profile to express the remainder in a complex scalar form. A key ingredient is the construction of a correction that cancels the leading effect of the nonlinear defect.

</details>


### [153] [Fourier Energy Strata for the Cubic Schrödinger Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05165)
*Yulin Xie*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves a geometric hierarchy of Fourier layer energies for the weakly nonlinear cubic Schrödinger equation on T_π × T_1, showing energy decays exponentially across nested frequency rectangles on long time scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding how energy distributes across Fourier modes in weakly nonlinear dispersive equations with resonant dynamics on tori is important, especially for the cubic Schrödinger equation where nontrivial resonances persist. The authors seek quantitative control of energy transfer in frequency space.

Method: A filtered Poincaré–Dulac normal form: input layers determine which nonresonant interactions are removed, while the same layer weights control remaining interactions. Finite coordinate changes and stability estimates transfer the hierarchy to the exact solution, using nested rectangles with half-widths (2j+1) times the initial ones.

Result: On 0≤t≤Tδ^{-1}, the H^1-squared energy in layer j is at most C_N δ^{2j} for 1≤j≤N, and energy outside the Nth rectangle is at most C_N δ^{2N+2}. Bounds hold at each fixed depth N under a uniform H^s bound (s>2), with constants depending on N. Also obtained: layer estimates for support bounded in one coordinate and geometrically decaying initial Fourier tails.

Conclusion: The Sobolev bound holds for every fixed T in the defocusing case. The results establish a geometric hierarchy of Fourier layer energies transferring to exact solutions, controlling energy concentration and decay across frequency scales.

Abstract: We prove a geometric hierarchy of Fourier layer energies for the weakly nonlinear cubic Schrodinger equation on $\mathbb T_π\times\mathbb T_1$, retaining its nontrivial resonant dynamics. For initial Fourier support in a rectangle, form nested rectangles with half-widths $(2j+1)$ times the initial ones; layers are the differences between consecutive rectangles. On $0\le t\le Tδ^{-1}$, the energy in layer $j$, measured by its squared $H^1$ norm, is at most $C_Nδ^{2j}$ for $1\le j\le N$; the energy outside the $N$th rectangle is at most $C_Nδ^{2N+2}$. These bounds hold at every fixed depth $N$ under a uniform $H^s$ bound, $s>2$, with constants depending on $N$. The Sobolev bound holds for every fixed $T$ in the defocusing case. We also obtain layer estimates for support bounded in one coordinate and for geometrically decaying initial Fourier tails. The proof uses a filtered Poincare-Dulac normal form: input layers select which nonresonant interactions are removed, while the same layer weights control the interactions left in the equation. Finite coordinate changes and stability estimates transfer this hierarchy to the exact solution.

</details>


### [154] [A separation threshold for ground states of two-component attractive Bose--Einstein condensates in displaced harmonic traps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05183)
*Shubin Yu,Chun-Lei Tang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper classifies existence of ground states for two-component attractive Bose-Einstein condensates with displaced harmonic traps at the critical interspecies coupling, finding a separation threshold d_c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extends Guo et al. (2019) and completes the existence classification of ground states for two-component attractive BECs with harmonic trapping potentials.

Method: Analyze the constrained minimization problem for V_1=|x-x_1|^2, V_2=|x-x_2|^2 with intraspecies a_1,a_2 in (0,a^*) at critical interspecies coupling β=β^*; characterize the threshold via an auxiliary variational problem giving d_c=Λ_*^{-1/4}.

Result: There exists a finite separation threshold d_c>0: no minimizer when 0<|x_1-x_2|<d_c, and a minimizer exists when |x_1-x_2|≥d_c; Λ_* is attained.

Conclusion: Ground-state existence depends sharply on trap displacement distance, completing the classification for two-component attractive BECs.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the existence of ground states for two-component attractive Bose--Einstein condensates with displaced harmonic trapping potentials \[ V_1(x)=|x-x_1|^2 \ \text{and}\ V_2(x)=|x-x_2|^2, \] where $x_1\neq x_2\in\mathbb R^2$. For intraspecies interactions $a_1,a_2\in (0,a^*)$, we focus on the critical interspecies coupling \[ β=β^*:=a^*+\sqrt{(a^*-a_1)(a^*-a_2)} \] and prove that there exists a separation threshold $d_c\in(0,\infty)$ such that the corresponding constrained minimization problem admits no minimizer when $0<|x_1-x_2|<d_c$, whereas it admits a minimizer when $|x_1-x_2|\geq d_c$. The threshold is given by $d_c=Λ_*^{-1/4}$, where $Λ_*$ is characterized by an auxiliary variational problem and attained. Our results extend the previous work of Guo et al. [J. Funct. Anal. 276 (2019)] and complete the existence classification of ground states for two-component attractive Bose--Einstein condensates with harmonic trapping potentials.

</details>


### [155] [On prediction from expert advice with more than five experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05186)
*Jeff Calder,Nadejda Drenska*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that no single rank-ordered adversary strategy is globally optimal for prediction with expert advice when there are six or more experts, in both geometric stopping and finite horizon settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding optimal adversary strategies in prediction with expert advice; prior exact optimality results exist for the five-expert problem, motivating the question of whether a single globally optimal rank-ordered strategy exists for larger n.

Method: Establish a leading-order correction when one expert moves far ahead of the others, linking optimal strategies between n and j<n experts, and leverage recent exact optimality-set results for the five-expert problem.

Result: No single rank-ordered adversary strategy is globally optimal for six or more experts, in both geometric stopping and finite time horizon settings.

Conclusion: The structure of optimal adversary strategies becomes richer with more experts; the five-expert case is a transition point, and no single strategy dominates for n≥6.

Abstract: We prove that no single rank ordered adversary strategy is globally optimal for the prediction with expert advice problem with six or more experts, in both the geometric stopping and finite time horizon settings. The proof is based on establishing a leading order correction when one expert moves far ahead of the others. This allows us to connect optimal strategies between $n$ and $j<n$ experts and utilize recent results on the exact optimality set for the five expert problem.

</details>


### [156] [Nontrivial periodic solutions to the one-phase free boundary problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05257)
*José Basulto,Nikola Kamburov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs new periodic (undulating) classical global solutions to the one-phase Bernoulli free boundary problem in R^3 and higher dimensions, bifurcating from product solutions built on a noncompact free boundary base (the HHP double hairpin times a line), and proves a strong distance nondegeneracy estimate for finite Morse index solutions with noncompact free boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous constructions of undulating cylinders relied on base solutions whose free boundary is compact modulo translations. This paper aims to extend such constructions to the noncompact case, thereby producing genuinely new global solutions and broadening the bifurcation scheme to more general base solutions.

Method: A bifurcation argument is applied to product solutions U(x,t)=u(x) in R^{n+1}, n>=3, where the base u has nontrivial finite Morse index, is nondegenerate within a symmetry class, and satisfies geometric conditions at infinity. To ensure positivity of perturbed solutions, a strong distance nondegeneracy estimate is proved for finite Morse index solutions with noncompact free boundary. In dimensions n=3,4 the classification of finite Morse index solutions (Fernandez-Real, Florit-Simon, Serra) is used to verify the geometric conditions.

Result: New classical global periodic solutions of the one-phase Bernoulli problem are constructed in R^3 from the HHP double hairpin times a line. More generally, the scheme gives periodic solutions in R^{n+1}, n>=3, bifurcating from product solutions, and in R^4 and R^5 existence reduces to a nondegeneracy property of the base solution.

Conclusion: The construction extends undulating-solution theory beyond compact-modulo-translations bases, and for n=3,4 the classification of finite Morse index solutions reduces the existence problem to nondegeneracy of the base solution.

Abstract: We construct new classical global solutions of the one-phase Bernoulli free boundary problem in $\mathbb{R}^3$ whose positive phases are periodic perturbations of the product of the Hauswirth--Hélein--Pacard double hairpin domain with a line. In contrast with previous constructions of undulating cylinders, the free boundary of the base solution is not compact modulo translations. More generally, we show that the same scheme yields periodic solutions in $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$, $n \geq 3$, bifurcating from product solutions $U(x,t)=u(x)$, when the base $u:\mathbb{R}^n\to [0,\infty)$ has nontrivial finite Morse index, is nondegenerate within a symmetry class, and satisfies certain natural geometric conditions at infinity. In dimensions $n\in\{3,4\}$, where the stable Bernstein theorem is available, the recent classification of finite Morse index solutions by Fernández-Real, Florit-Simon and Serra shows that every such solution satisfies these geometric conditions. Thus, the existence of undulating solutions in $\mathbb{R}^{4}$ and $\mathbb{R}^{5}$ bifurcating from their respective products reduces to a nondegeneracy property of the base solution. In order to confirm that the perturbed solutions are positive, we prove that finite Morse index solutions with noncompact free boundary satisfy a strong distance nondegeneracy estimate.

</details>


### [157] [The Global Existence and Energy Decay of a Partially Logarithmically Damped Wave System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05268)
*Salim A. Messaoudi,Suleyman Ulusoy*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a weakly coupled system of two wave equations with logarithmic damping acting only on the first equation, proving global existence of strong solutions and asymptotic energy decay under constraints on the cross-coupling parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The system has a structural asymmetry: only the first wave equation has nonlinear logarithmic damping, while the second is completely undamped and relies on linear cross-coupling for energy dissipation. Understanding stabilization mechanisms under such degenerate nonlinear damping is the key motivation.

Method: Faedo-Galerkin approximation scheme combined with structural monotonicity properties of the non-Lipschitz logarithmic nonlinearity to prove global existence of strong solutions; rigorous analysis under suitable constraints on the cross-coupling parameter for stability and energy decay.

Result: Rigorous global existence result for strong solutions is established, along with a proof of stability and asymptotic energy decay of solutions under appropriate cross-coupling parameter constraints.

Conclusion: The results provide new insights into the stabilization mechanisms of coupled hyperbolic systems under degenerate nonlinear damping, showing that cross-coupling can compensate for the absence of damping in one equation.

Abstract: We investigate the global existence and energy decay properties of a weakly coupled evolution system consisting of two wave equations in a bounded domain. The system exhibits a unique structural asymmetry: a nonlinear logarithmic damping term acts exclusively as a friction-like feedback mechanism on the first wave equation, while the second equation remains completely undamped and relies solely on linear cross-coupling to transfer and dissipate its energy.
  We establish two primary objectives. First, by utilizing the Faedo-Galerkin approximation scheme in conjunction with the structural monotonicity properties of the non-Lipschitz logarithmic nonlinearity, we prove a rigorous global existence result for strong solutions. Second, we provide a rigorous mathematical proof demonstrating the stability and asymptotic energy decay of the solutions under suitable constraints on the cross-coupling parameter. These results provide new insights into the stabilization mechanisms of coupled hyperbolic systems under degenerate nonlinear damping.

</details>


### [158] [Existence of a separating norm for the semilinear heat equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05286)
*Jin Takahashi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For the semilinear heat equation in the Fujita supercritical range, the authors construct a "separating norm" whose smallness on nonnegative initial data guarantees global existence while its largeness forces finite-time blow-up.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In the Fujita supercritical range global-in-time solutions may exist, so a sharp criterion distinguishing global existence from blow-up is needed. The paper aims to identify a norm that cleanly separates these two behaviors.

Method: Study the initial value problem for the semilinear heat equation with nonnegative initial data u0 in L-infinity(R^n); introduce and analyze a norm ||·||_X and relate its size on u0 to the global existence versus finite-time blow-up dichotomy.

Result: There exists a separating norm ||·||_X such that smallness of ||u0||_X implies global-in-time existence, while largeness of ||u0||_X implies finite-time blow-up. This separating norm is unique up to equivalence on nonnegative data.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a sharp smallness/largeness dichotomy via a separating norm for the supercritical semilinear heat equation, with the norm being essentially unique on nonnegative data.

Abstract: We consider the initial value problem of the semilinear heat equation in the Fujita supercritical range for which global-in-time solutions may exist. The main result shows the existence of a separating norm $\|\cdot\|_X$ in the sense that, for nonnegative initial data $u_0\in L^\infty(\mathbf{R}^n)$, the smallness of $\|u_0\|_X$ implies global-in-time existence, whereas the largeness of $\|u_0\|_X$ implies finite-time blow-up. The separating norm is unique up to equivalence on nonnegative data.

</details>


### [159] [Global solutions to the two-dimensional compressible MHD equations with density-dependent bulk viscosity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05312)
*Yachun Li,Peng Lu,Zhaoyang Shang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global weak and strong solutions are constructed for 2D periodic compressible MHD with constant shear viscosity/magnetic diffusivity, bulk viscosity ρ^β, and pressure ρ^γ, for all β>1, γ>1 (weak) and large-background strong solutions when γ>max{5−2β,β+1}, with admissible exponents infimum 7/3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Compressible MHD with degenerate (density-dependent) bulk viscosity and pressure laws poses major challenges for global well-posedness. Prior results typically impose restrictions on β, γ, or smallness of data. The goal is to establish global weak solutions for the full parameter range β>1, γ>1 and strong solutions for a wide pressure regime without requiring small velocity/magnetic data.

Method: Approximation-independent finite density moments; generalized effective-flux identities; joint logarithmic renormalization of density and its entropy defect; augmented relative energy with β-power density potential for weak–strong uniqueness. For strong solutions: frequency-dependent acoustic dissipation, staged vorticity weights, separate initial-layer flux estimate, simultaneous bootstrap; density perturbation bounded in scaled norm m^((γ−3)/2)‖ρ0−m‖_{H2}.

Result: Global finite-energy renormalized weak solutions exist for every β>1, γ>1 from positive bounded W^{1,q} densities (q>2) and arbitrary H^1 velocity/magnetic data. Weak–strong uniqueness holds against positive H^2 strong solutions across the full pressure range. Global strong existence for a large-background family when γ>max{5−2β,β+1}. Admissible pressure exponents have infimum 7/3 (endpoint excluded). For β>2 the damping rate is the minimum of viscous and pressure-relaxation rates, extending the argument without upper restriction on β.

Conclusion: The work provides a unified global theory for 2D compressible MHD with degenerate bulk viscosity and general pressure laws, covering weak solutions for the entire β,γ>1 range and strong solutions for broad pressure exponents with large backgrounds, sharpening the threshold to infimum 7/3 and removing upper restrictions on β (and γ under the scaled density hypothesis).

Abstract: We study the two-dimensional periodic resistive compressible magnetohydrodynamic equations with constant shear viscosity and magnetic diffusivity, bulk viscosity $ρ^β$, and pressure $ρ^γ$. For every $β>1$ and $γ>1$, we construct global finite-energy renormalized weak solutions from strictly positive bounded $W^{1,q}$ densities, $q>2$, and arbitrary $H^1$ velocity and magnetic data. The construction combines approximation-independent finite density moments, generalized effective-flux identities, and a joint logarithmic renormalization of the density and its entropy defect. An augmented relative energy containing the $β$-power density potential yields weak--strong uniqueness against positive $H^2$ strong solutions for the same full pressure range. We also establish global strong existence for a large-background family when $γ>\max\{5-2β,β+1\}$. Writing $m=\intρ_0$, the density perturbation is bounded in the scaled norm $m^{(γ-3)/2}\|ρ_0-m\|_{H^2}$, while the fixed $H^2$ velocity and magnetic norms need not be small. For sufficiently large $m$, frequency-dependent acoustic dissipation, staged vorticity weights, and a separate initial-layer flux estimate close a simultaneous bootstrap. The admissible pressure exponents have infimum $7/3$, with the endpoint excluded. For $β>2$ the relevant damping rate is the minimum of the viscous and pressure-relaxation rates; keeping both rates extends the argument without an upper restriction on $β$. The strong theorem has no upper restriction on $γ$ under this scaled density hypothesis; for fixed additive density perturbations it applies in the stated lower range up to $γ=3$.

</details>


### [160] [Sharp uniform stability of the logarithmic Sobolev inequality on finite cycles and stability of the cubic Sobolev inequality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05347)
*Lu Chen,Nguyen Lam,Guozhen Lu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes the sharp, dimension-independent quartic stability constant (1/3) in the logarithmic Sobolev inequality on finite cycles, the circle, and related models, and provides stability estimates for the cubic Sobolev inequality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Frank and Ivanisvili proved the sharp log-Sobolev inequality 2E_n(u) >= Ent(u^2) on cycles. Xie and Zhang gave a quartic stability estimate with constant 1/12. This paper seeks the sharp uniform constant, and the quantitative version of the cubic Sobolev inequality expected by Frank and Ivanisvili.

Method: Uses a bound on the first Fourier coefficient of u in terms of its mean (valid since u is nonnegative), and a sharp stability estimate for Gross's two-point log-Sobolev inequality with sharp constants in fourth and sixth powers of distance.

Result: Proves 2E_n(u) - Ent(u^2) >= (1/3)||u-1||_2^4 for all n >= 4 and nonnegative u with ||u||_2 = 1. This is sharp uniformly in n. The four-cycle is the only extremal model (constant 1/3 sharp but not attained); for n >= 5 and the circle the sharp constant is at least 1/3 + c. Explicit upper bounds are also given. A stability estimate for the cubic Sobolev inequality is obtained with optimal powers: four on C_n (n>=5) and the circle, six on C_4.

Conclusion: The sharp uniform quartic stability constant for the log-Sobolev inequality on finite cycles is 1/3, improving Xie and Zhang's 1/12. The four-cycle is uniquely extremal, and improved constants hold for larger cycles and the circle. The cubic Sobolev stability estimate attains the optimal powers expected by Frank and Ivanisvili.

Abstract: We determine the sharp uniform constant in the quartic stability estimate for the logarithmic Sobolev inequality on finite cycles. We equip $C_{n}=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}$ with the uniform probability measure, and we let $\mathcal{E}_{n}$ be the Dirichlet form of the simple random walk on $C_{n}$, normalized so that its spectral gap is one. Frank and Ivanisvili recently proved the sharp logarithmic Sobolev inequality $2\mathcal{E}_{n}(u)\geq \mathrm{Ent}(u^{2})$ for all $n\geq 4$. We prove that $2\mathcal{E}_{n}(u)-\mathrm{Ent}(u^{2})\geq \frac{1}{3}\Vert u-1\Vert _{2}^{4}$ for all $n\geq 4$ and all nonnegative $u$ with $\Vert u\Vert _{2}=1$. This is the sharp form, uniformly in $n$, of the quartic stability estimate of Xie and Zhang, who obtained the constant $\frac{1}{12}$. The same estimate holds on the circle and for the word-length energy on $C_{n}$. Among these models, the four-cycle is the only extremal one: on $C_{4}$ the constant $\frac{1}{3}$ is sharp but not attained, while on $C_{n}$ with $n\geq 5$ and on the circle the sharp constant is at least $\frac{1}{3}+c$, where $c>0$ does not depend on $n$. We also give explicit upper bounds for these sharp constants. Our second main result is a stability estimate for the cubic Sobolev inequality of Frank and Ivanisvili, which is the main step in their proof. It gives the quantitative version of this inequality expected by Frank and Ivanisvili, with the optimal powers of $\Vert u-1\Vert _{2}$: four on $C_{n}$ with $n\geq 5$ and on the circle, and six on $C_{4}$. Two key new ingredients of the proofs are a bound for the first Fourier coefficient of $u$ in terms of its mean, which holds because $u$ is nonnegative, and a sharp stability estimate for Gross's two-point logarithmic Sobolev inequality, with sharp constants in both the fourth and the sixth powers of the distance.

</details>


### [161] [A constrained log-HLS inequality and its associated Wasserstein flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05356)
*Monideep Ghosh,Debabrata Karmakar,Reetaj Sinha,Kartikey Verma*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a constrained log-Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality and its induced Wasserstein gradient flow, yielding a parabolic-elliptic Patlak-Keller-Segel system whose blow-up threshold doubles compared to the unconstrained case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how constraints affect log-HLS inequalities and the associated gradient-flow/PKS dynamics, and to identify a mechanism that suppresses blow-up distinct from existing approaches.

Method: Variational duality linking the constrained log-HLS inequality to the constrained Trudinger-Moser-Onofri-Aubin inequality, combined with the Wasserstein gradient-flow formulation leading to a parabolic-elliptic PKS system.

Result: The flow exhibits a global existence vs. finite-time blow-up dichotomy depending on initial mass, with the critical mass threshold raised by a factor of two relative to the unconstrained setting.

Conclusion: The constraint provides a distinct blow-up suppression mechanism, differing from those relying on forcing terms, fluid coupling, or harmonic perturbations of the Newtonian potential.

Abstract: We study a log-Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev (log-HLS) inequality under constraints and a naturally induced Wasserstein flow. The constrained log-HLS inequality is dual, in a variational sense, to the constrained Trudinger-Moser-Onofri-Aubin inequality. The corresponding gradient-flow formulation leads to a parabolic-elliptic Patlak-Keller-Segel (PKS) system whose global dynamics exhibit a dichotomy between global existence and finite-time blow-up depending on the initial mass. Remarkably, the constraint raises the critical mass threshold of the flow by a factor of two compared with the unconstrained setting. This provides a distinct mechanism for suppressing blow-up from those arising in related formulations of the PKS-system, where blow-up is mitigated through additional effects such as a forcing term, coupling with a fluid equation, or the ambient flow generated by harmonic perturbations of the Newtonian potential.

</details>


### [162] [Affine variational functionals, isoperimetric problems and stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05403)
*Francisco Marín Sola*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies three affine variational quantities based on the affine $L^p$ energy: affine Poincaré–Sobolev constants, affine torsional rigidity, and affine $p$-capacity. It shows ellipsoids are the unique extremals, proves an affine Kohler–Jobin inequality, and derives quantitative stability results via a stability version of the $L_p$ Busemann–Petty centroid inequality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Affine variational quantities built on the affine $L^p$ energy (Lutwak–Yang–Zhang) are natural affine-invariant counterparts of classical Sobolev-type functionals, but their isoperimetric extremals, existence of extremal potentials, and quantitative stability were not established.

Method: Following Haddad–Jiménez–Montenegro's approach to affine Sobolev inequalities, the authors build on the $L_p$ Busemann–Petty centroid inequality, proving a stability version of it. This yields quantitative forms of the relevant isoperimetric inequalities and of the affine Kohler–Jobin inequality.

Result: Ellipsoids are the only extremal sets in the isoperimetric inequalities for the affine Poincaré–Sobolev constants, affine torsional rigidity, and affine $p$-capacity. The affine capacity of a compact set of positive volume is attained by a potential solving the affine $p$-Laplace equation. An affine Kohler–Jobin inequality is proved: among sets with prescribed affine torsional rigidity, only ellipsoids minimize $Λ^{\mathcal{A}}_{p,q}$. Quantitative stability versions are obtained, including stability of the affine Kohler–Jobin inequality on convex sets.

Conclusion: The affine $L^p$ variational framework admits ellipsoids as unique extremals, a well-posed affine $p$-Laplace potential, and an affine Kohler–Jobin principle, all with quantitative stability driven by a stability form of the $L_p$ Busemann–Petty centroid inequality.

Abstract: We study three affine variational quantities built on the affine $L^p$ energy of Lutwak, Yang and Zhang: the affine Poincaré--Sobolev constants $Λ^{\mathcal{A}}_{p,q}(Ω)$ of a bounded open set, the associated affine torsional rigidity, and the affine $p$-capacity of a compact set. We show that ellipsoids are the only extremal sets in their corresponding isoperimetric inequalities, and that the affine capacity of a compact set of positive volume is attained by a potential solving the affine $p$-Laplace equation. We also prove an affine Kohler-Jobin inequality: among sets with prescribed affine torsional rigidity, only ellipsoids minimize $Λ^{\mathcal{A}}_{p,q}$. Following the approach of Haddad, Jiménez and Montenegro to affine Sobolev inequalities, our methods rest on the $L_p$ Busemann--Petty centroid inequality. Proving a stability version of it, we obtain quantitative forms of the isoperimetric inequalities for $Λ^{\mathcal{A}}_{p,q}$, the affine torsional rigidity and the affine $p$-capacity, together with a stability result for the affine Kohler-Jobin inequality on convex sets.

</details>


### [163] [Homogenization of some viscous Hamilton--Jacobi equations using variational arguments on probability spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05423)
*Andrea Braides,Gianni Dal Maso,Claude Le Bris*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the asymptotic behavior of viscous Hamilton–Jacobi equations with oscillatory periodic potentials via a novel stochastic variational approach, identifying a homogenized Lagrangian through a Γ-convergence procedure on random processes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The equations are already studied in the PDE framework and described via an effective Hamiltonian. The authors seek a probabilistic/variational reformulation that could yield a complete characterization of the homogenized limit and additional stability properties.

Method: A variational approach based on the classical stochastic control representation involving Brownian motion, reformulating the problem via functionals on probability spaces. The core tool is a Γ-convergence procedure adapted to random processes, treating filtrations and Brownian motions as variables. Blow-up arguments give lower/upper bounds; stochastic correctors are constructed.

Result: Identification of a homogenized Lagrangian; the effective Hamiltonian is recovered as its Legendre transform. Both lower and upper bounds are established, yielding a complete characterization of the homogenized limit, and a stability result with respect to localized defects embedded in the periodic background.

Conclusion: The stochastic Γ-convergence variational framework fully characterizes the homogenized limit of these viscous Hamilton–Jacobi equations and provides stability under localized perturbations.

Abstract: This work studies the asymptotic behavior of a class of viscous Hamilton--Jacobi equations with oscillatory periodic potentials, already studied by other authors in the PDE framework, and described in terms of an effective Hamiltonian. A novel variational approach is introduced, based on the classical stochastic control representation involving a Brownian motion, which allows the problem to be reformulated in terms of functionals defined on probability spaces. The core result is the identification of a homogenized Lagrangian obtained via a $Γ$-convergence procedure adapted to random processes, where filtrations and Brownian motions are treated as variables. The effective Hamiltonian is then recovered as the Legendre transform of this homogenized Lagrangian. The analysis provides both lower and upper bounds by means of blow-up arguments and the construction of stochastic correctors, yielding a complete characterization of the homogenized limit. This approach allows for a stability result with respect to the addition of some localized defects embedded in the periodic background.

</details>


### [164] [A complete classification of singularities for radial solutions of the Stefan problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05434)
*Dennis Kriventsov,Georg S. Weiss*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves a conjecture of Herrero and Velazquez on the precise asymptotic behavior of the free boundary near a singularity for radial solutions of the two-phase and one-phase Stefan problem, establishing complete asymptotic rigidity in the radial class.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Herrero and Velazquez showed existence of a solution whose free boundary satisfies g(t)=c_n sqrt(t)|log t|^{-1/(n-2)}(1+o(1)) in dimension n>=3 and conjectured these asymptotics hold in general. This work aims to prove that conjecture for all radial solutions.

Method: The authors apply the "Potential Reduction to ODE" method (introduced in arXiv:2608.18913 for the Neumann Bernoulli problem), where the asymptotic shape of the free surface is governed by a precise ODE. Notably, this is applied to an equation of a different type with quasilinear principal part, which in their regime is not a perturbation of a linear operator.

Result: The conjecture is proven for all radial solutions, yielding complete asymptotic rigidity in that class for both the two-phase and one-phase Stefan problems.

Conclusion: The results confirm the versatility of the Potential Reduction to ODE method, extending it to quasilinear equations where frequency and monotonicity formulas are unknown.

Abstract: We investigate singularities of radial solutions of the classical two-phase and one-phase Stefan problem. In a remarkable result, M.A. Herrero and J.J.L. Velazquez show existence of a solution such that the free boundary described by $g(t)$ satisfies in dimension $n\geq 3$ $$g(t)=c_n \sqrt{t} |\log t|^{\frac{-1}{n - 2}} (1 + o(1)), t\to 0,$$ and they conjecture that these asymptotics hold in general. Here we prove the conjecture for all radial solutions, which means complete asymptotic rigidity in that class.
  In order to obtain these precise asymptotics for the two-phase Stefan problem, for which frequency formulas and monotonicity formulas are unknown, we apply the Potential Reduction to ODE method introduced in arXiv:2608.18913 for the Neumann Bernoulli problem. The philosophy of that method is that the asymptotic shape of the free surface is governed by a precise ordinary differential equation. The fact that we can apply it in this paper to an equation of different type with quasilinear principal part, which is in our regime not the perturbation of a linear operator, confirms the versatility of the method.

</details>


### [165] [Bulk-surface spectral homogenization with indefinite mass in periodic perforated domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05487)
*Hermann DOUANLA*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies homogenization of an elliptic eigenvalue problem in a periodically perforated domain with an indefinite bulk-surface spectral measure, showing the spectrum's asymptotic behavior depends on the sign of an effective mean.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The eigenvalue problem has the spectral parameter appearing both in the equation (bulk density q) and in a Steklov condition on hole boundaries (surface density ρ), producing an indefinite bulk-surface spectral measure. Understanding how this indefinite coupling affects spectral asymptotics under homogenization motivates the study.

Method: Asymptotic/homogenization analysis in a periodically perforated domain. The approach classifies cases via the combined mean m_eff = ∫_{Y*} q dy + ∫_Γ ρ dS_y (positive, negative, or zero), and proves convergence of eigenvalue clusters (with multiplicity), eigenspaces, and—under extra regularity—O(ε) estimates for shifted/rescaled eigenvalues, spectral projectors, and aligned eigenspaces.

Result: The spectral asymptotic behavior depends essentially on whether m_eff is positive, negative, or zero. In all three cases, eigenvalue clusters converge with multiplicity and eigenspaces converge; under additional regularity, O(ε) estimates hold for shifted/rescaled eigenvalues, spectral projectors, and aligned eigenspaces.

Conclusion: Homogenization of this indefinite bulk-surface eigenvalue problem is governed by the sign of the effective mean m_eff, with rigorous convergence and O(ε) quantitative estimates across all three regimes.

Abstract: We study the homogenization of an elliptic eigenvalue problem in a periodically perforated domain, where the spectral parameter appears both in the equation, through a bulk density $q$, and in a Steklov condition on the boundaries of the holes, through a surface density $ρ$, and where the resulting bulk--surface spectral measure $q\,dy+ρ\,dS_y$ is indefinite. We show that the asymptotic behavior of the spectrum depends essentially on whether the combined mean $m_{\rm eff}=\int_{Y^*}q\,dy+\int_Γρ\,dS_y$ is positive, negative, or zero. In all three cases, we prove convergence of eigenvalue clusters with multiplicity and of eigenspaces and, under additional regularity, $O(\varepsilon)$ estimates for the shifted or rescaled eigenvalues, spectral projectors, and aligned eigenspaces.

</details>


### [166] [Rearrangements and the Maz'ya-Shaposhnikova limit: $Γ$-convergence for truncated interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05499)
*Andrea Braides,Anneliese Defranceschi,Enrico Vitali*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves the Gamma-liminf inequality for the Maz'ya-Shaposhnikova limit (s->0+) of the fractional Sobolev seminorm via spherical rearrangements, and applies it to truncated energies yielding a limit of (1-lambda)N*omega_N*||u||_{L^2}^2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The fractional Sobolev seminorm's behavior as s->0+ (Maz'ya-Shaposhnikova limit) needs a rigorous Gamma-liminf proof, particularly for truncated energies with restricted interactions, to identify the contribution of long-range interactions and interpolate between known regimes.

Method: Spherical rearrangements: rearrangements of a weakly convergent sequence are monotone radial profiles converging strongly by Helly's theorem. Since energy does not increase under rearrangement in the limit, the liminf inequality reduces to a pointwise convergence statement for radially decreasing functions. Applied to truncated energies restricted to a ball of radius r_s->infinity.

Result: Assuming r_s^{-2s}->lambda in [0,1], the scaled energies Gamma-converge with respect to weak L^2-convergence to (1-lambda)N*omega_N*||u||_{L^2}^2, where omega_N is the measure of the unit ball in R^N.

Conclusion: The result identifies the contribution of long-range interactions and gives a continuous interpolation between the classical Maz'ya-Shaposhnikova limit (lambda=0) and the vanishing-energy regime (lambda=1).

Abstract: We give a proof of the $Γ$-liminf inequality for the Maz'ya--Shaposhni\-kova limit, as $s\to0^+$, of the fractional Sobolev seminorm, based on spherical rearrangements. The key point is that the rearrangements of a weakly convergent sequence are monotone radial profiles, which converge strongly by Helly's theorem; since the energy does not increase under rearrangement (in the limit), this reduces the liminf inequality to a pointwise convergence statement for radially decreasing functions. As an application, we treat truncated energies, in which the interaction is restricted to a ball of radius $r_s\to\infty$. Assuming $r_s^{-2s}\toλ\in[0,1]$, we prove that the scaled energies $Γ$-converge, with respect to weak $L^2$-convergence, to $$ (1-λ)Nω_N\|u\|_{L^2}^2, $$ where $ω_N$ is the measure of the unit ball of $\mathbb R^N$. This identifies the contribution of the long-range interactions and gives a continuous interpolation between the classical Maz'ya--Shaposhnikova limit ($λ=0$) and the vanishing-energy regime ($λ=1$).

</details>


### [167] [Overdetermined problems for sum Hessian equations in the space forms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05631)
*Cheng Cheng,Yingbo Han*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies overdetermined problems for normalized sum Hessian equations and their quotient forms on space forms, proving domain rigidity results under certain geometric or boundary constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overdetermined problems connect PDE solutions to geometric rigidity, and Hessian-type equations (and their quotients) on space forms are natural yet not fully understood settings for such symmetry results.

Method: The equations and boundary conditions are used to establish admissibility and ellipticity. The proofs employ two auxiliary functions together with a Rellich–Pohožaev identity.

Result: For quotient equations, the domain is shown to be a geodesic ball if it is strictly star-shaped, or if the Dirichlet value satisfies an upper bound. The analogous result holds for sum Hessian equations without requiring star-shapedness.

Conclusion: The work extends overdetermined symmetry/rigidity results to normalized sum Hessian and quotient equations on space forms, with the star-shapedness assumption removable in the sum Hessian case.

Abstract: We study overdetermined problems for normalized sum Hessian equations and their quotients in the space forms. The equations and boundary conditions imply admissibility and ellipticity. For quotient equations, we prove that the domain is a geodesic ball if it is strictly star-shaped, or if the Dirichlet value satisfies an upper bound. We also prove the corresponding result for sum Hessian equations without star-shapedness. The proofs use two auxiliary functions and a Rellich--Pohožaev identity.

</details>


### [168] [Local well-posedness for the biharmonic nonlinear Schrödinger equation in low dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05655)
*Iryna Petrenko,Oscar Riaño,Svetlana Roudenko*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the 4th order (biharmonic) nonlinear Schrödinger equation with combined fourth and second order dispersion and a power nonlinearity in dimensions 1, 2, and 3, establishing local well-posedness both on a weighted Sobolev subset and in H^s spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quartic solitons have recently been experimentally observed in optics, making the mathematical theory of the 4th order NLS equation in physical dimensions N=1,2,3 timely to develop.

Method: The authors analyze the biharmonic NLS equation whose dispersion operator combines fourth order with lower second order terms, with the potential given by a power nonlinearity for any positive power, working in dimensions N=1,2,3.

Result: Local well-posedness of the 4th order NLS equation is shown on a weighted subset of a Sobolev space and in H^s spaces.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a local well-posedness theory for the biharmonic NLS equation in physical dimensions, contributing to the mathematical foundations driven by recent optical experiments on quartic solitons.

Abstract: In this paper we consider the 4th order (or biharmonic) nonlinear Schrödinger (NLS) equation in dimensions 1, 2, and 3, where the potential term is expressed as a power non-linearity (for any positive power) and the dispersion operator has the {\it fourth} order combined with the lower {\it second} order. The fourth order NLS equation has recently attracted attention of researchers since quartic solitons have been experimentally obtained in optics, and thus, mathematical theory of solutions to the 4th order NLS equation in physical dimensions $N=1,2,3$ is timely to develop. In this work we show the local well-posedness of the 4th order or bi-harmonic NLS equation on a weighted subset of a Sobolev space as well as in $H^s$ spaces.

</details>


### [169] [The conductivity problem with imperfect bonding interfaces and finite internal conductivities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05708)
*Hongjie Dong,Zhuolun Yang,Hanye Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Study of field concentration between two closely spaced finite-conductivity inclusions with imperfect (low-conductivity) bonding interfaces. Gradient may blow up as distance ε→0 when bonding parameter γ is large, but stays bounded when γ is small; threshold γ and optimal blow-up rates are identified, revealing a novel logarithmic blow-up at the critical value.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Finite-conductivity inclusions with ideal interfaces give bounded gradients, but real interfaces are imperfect. The effect of low-conductivity (Robin-type) bonding on field concentration between two nearby inclusions is unclear.

Method: Analyze an elliptic system with Robin-type boundary conditions coupling two finite-conductivity inclusions separated by distance ε; derive the threshold value of the bonding parameter γ separating bounded and blow-up regimes and compute optimal blow-up rates under symmetry assumptions.

Result: When γ is large, the gradient blows up as ε→0; when γ is small, it is uniformly bounded independently of ε. A critical threshold γ and optimal blow-up rates are identified, with a novel logarithmic blow-up at the critical value, differing from the perfect-conductor case.

Conclusion: Imperfect bonding qualitatively changes field concentration: a γ-dependent threshold governs boundedness versus blow-up, and a new logarithmic blow-up emerges at criticality—distinct from perfect-conductor inclusions.

Abstract: We study the field concentration phenomenon between two closely spaced inclusions with imperfect bonding interfaces of low conductivity type. The inclusions are assumed to have finite conductivities. The problem is governed by a system of elliptic equations coupled with Robin-type boundary conditions. While it is known that finite-conductivity inclusions with ideal interfaces yield bounded gradients, in this paper we show that with imperfect bonding interfaces, the gradient of the solution may blow up as $\varepsilon$ (the distance between two inclusions) tends to zero when the bonding parameter $γ$ is large, while it is uniformly bounded independently of $\varepsilon$ when the bonding parameter $γ$ is small. Moreover, we identify the threshold of $γ$ and the optimal blow-up rates under certain symmetry assumptions. Compared to the case when the inclusions are perfect conductors, we find a novel logarithmic blow-up phenomenon at the critical value of $γ$.

</details>


### [170] [Pathological viscosity solutions of Hamilton--Jacobi equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05736)
*Hung V. Tran,Yifeng Yu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A single continuous function u and a fixed smooth Hamiltonian H are constructed on the unit ball in R^4 such that u is a viscosity solution of H(Du)=c for every c in [1,2], showing the right-hand side is not determined by (u, H).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Viscosity solutions typically are expected to determine the equation's right-hand side given a fixed function and Hamiltonian. The authors seek to expose a pathology where this uniqueness of the right-hand side fails.

Method: Constructive: build a continuous function u on the closed unit ball in R^4 and a smooth autonomous Hamiltonian H: R^4 -> [1,2], using viscosity solution theory to verify H(Du)=c in the unit ball for all c in [1,2].

Result: There exists u and H such that u solves H(Du)=c in the viscosity sense for every c in [1,2], demonstrating that a fixed (u, H) does not determine the right-hand side.

Conclusion: This reveals a striking and distinctive pathology of viscosity solutions: the right-hand side of the equation need not be determined by the fixed function and fixed Hamiltonian.

Abstract: We construct a continuous function $u$ on the closed unit ball in $\mathbb{R}^4$ and a smooth autonomous Hamiltonian $H:\mathbb{R}^4\to[1,2]$ for which $u$ solves $H(Du)=c$ in the unit ball in the viscosity sense for every $c\in[1,2]$. This reveals a striking and distinctive pathology of viscosity solutions: a fixed function and a fixed Hamiltonian need not determine the right-hand side of the equation.

</details>


### [171] [Optimal Global Sobolev Estimates for Fractional Dirichlet Problems in Bounded Lipschitz Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05753)
*Wenxian Ma,Sibei Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes optimal global Sobolev (W^{t,p}) estimates for the fractional Dirichlet problem (-Δ)^s u = f on bounded Lipschitz domains, with sharp target/baseline exponent ranges and matching counterexamples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Global Sobolev regularity for the fractional Dirichlet problem on Lipschitz domains is not fully understood, and the optimal (Dahlberg-sense) exponents were unknown. The authors aim to determine sharp global W^{t,p} estimates.

Method: For t ∈ [s, min{2s, s+1/2}), prove sup-norm/global estimates controlled by ||f||_{L^q(Ω)}. Handle cases: (i) n=2, s∈(1/2,1) with q∈(1,4/(1+2s)+η); (ii) n=2, s∈(0,1/2] or n≥3, s∈(0,1) with q∈(1, n(2s+1)/(nt+(2s-t)(2s+1))+η) and p≤nq/(n-(2s-t)q). Counterexamples via regular Cantor sets on the boundary and, in the planar exceptional case, homogeneous solutions in sectors.

Result: Optimal global Sobolev estimates ||u||_{W^{t,p}(R^n)} ≤ C||f||_{L^q(Ω)} for the fractional Dirichlet problem, with explicit sharp exponent ranges depending on n,s,t, and C depending only on n,s,t,p,q,Ω.

Conclusion: The stated baselines are optimal in Dahlberg's sense: any larger target exponent p fails on some bounded Lipschitz domain with f≡1, so the estimates are sharp.

Abstract: Let $n\geq2$, $s\in(0,1)$, and let $Ω\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ be a bounded Lipschitz domain. In this paper, we establish optimal global Sobolev estimates for the fractional Dirichlet problem \begin{equation*} \left\{\begin{aligned} (-Δ)^su&=f & & \text{in}\ \ Ω,
  u&=0 & & \text{in}\ \ \mathbb{R}^n\setminusΩ. \end{aligned}\right. \end{equation*} More precisely, for any given $t\in[s,\min\{2s,\,s+1/2\})$, we prove that there exists a small positive constant $η=η(n,s,t,Ω)$ such that the following holds. If $n=2$ and $s\in(1/2,1)$, then, for any $q\in(1,\frac{4}{1+2s}+η)$ and $p\in(1,\frac{nq}{n-(2s-t)q}]$, \begin{equation*} \|u\|_{W^{t,p}(\mathbb{R}^n)}\le C\|f\|_{L^q(Ω)}, \end{equation*} and, if either $n=2$ and $s\in(0,1/2]$ or $n\ge 3$ and $s\in(0,1)$, then, for any $q\in(1,\frac{n(2s+1)}{nt+(2s-t)(2s+1)}+η)$ and $p\in(1,\frac{nq}{n-(2s-t)q}]$, \begin{equation*} \|u\|_{W^{t,p}(\mathbb{R}^n)}\le C\|f\|_{L^q(Ω)}. \end{equation*} Here the positive constant $C$ depends only on $n$, $s$, $t$, $p$, $q$, and $Ω$. The stated universal baselines are optimal in Dahlberg's sense: for any larger target exponent $p$ there is a bounded Lipschitz domain whose solution with right-hand side identically $1$ is not in $W^{t,p}(\mathbb{R}^n)$. The counterexamples are constructed using regular Cantor sets on the boundary and, in the planar exceptional case, homogeneous solutions in sectors.

</details>


### [172] [Path-Space Optimal Transport with Interactions: Kinetic Equations and Eikonal Structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05754)
*Rene Cabrera*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper connects dynamical optimal transport on path-space (with kinetic cost and nonlocal Gaussian interaction) to mean-field kinetic dynamics, linking endpoint geometry, eikonal behavior, and a measure-valued Liouville/kinetic equation via a superposition principle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a variational connection between endpoint geometry, eikonal behavior of the Gaussian interaction potential, and kinetic mean-field dynamics through path-space optimal transport.

Method: Uses cyclical monotonicity and regularity of the effective endpoint cost to obtain a conservative vector field from initial momentum; derives Euler-Lagrange dynamics via endpoint-preserving perturbations of the optimal path measure; establishes gradient estimates for W_{π_0} and shows it is a viscosity subsolution of an eikonal equation; proves a superposition principle lifting measure-valued solutions to path-space measures.

Result: For a Gaussian kernel, the effective endpoint cost yields a conservative vector field linked to initial momentum; W_{π_0} satisfies a uniform gradient estimate and is a viscosity subsolution of an eikonal equation under a quantitative interaction strength condition; phase-space marginals satisfy a nonlocal kinetic (Liouville-type) equation driven by a smooth Gaussian self-consistent force.

Conclusion: Path-space optimal transport offers a variational bridge connecting endpoint geometry, eikonal behavior of the Gaussian interaction potential, and kinetic mean-field dynamics.

Abstract: We study a dynamical optimal transport problem on path-space with kinetic cost and nonlocal interaction. For a Gaussian interaction kernel, cyclical monotonicity and the regularity of the effective endpoint cost yield a conservative vector field associated with the initial momentum of the optimal trajectories. Separately, the Gaussian interaction potential $W_{π_0}$ satisfies a uniform gradient estimate and, under a quantitative condition on the interaction strength, is a classical and hence viscosity subsolution of an eikonal equation. We also derive the Euler--Lagrange dynamics directly from path-space optimality by means of endpoint-preserving perturbations of the optimal path measure. The associated phase-space marginals satisfy a measure-valued Liouville-type, or nonlocal kinetic, equation driven by the smooth Gaussian self-consistent force. Conversely, a superposition principle lifts suitable measure-valued solutions of the phase-space continuity equation to measures on phase-space trajectories. Thus path-space optimal transport provides a variational connection between endpoint geometry, eikonal behavior of the Gaussian interaction potential, and kinetic mean-field dynamics.

</details>


### [173] [Scattering of non-radial solutions for the fourth-order Schrödinger equation with $\dot{H}^{1/2}$-critical and supercritical nonlinearity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05802)
*Takahisa Inui,Koichi Komada,Kuranosuke Nishimura*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves scattering of non-radial solutions below the ground state for the focusing fourth-order NLS with $\mu\ge0$, for $d\ge2$ and $1+8/(d-1)\le p<1+8/(d-4)_+$, extending prior radial-only results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior scattering results below the ground state (Guo 2016, Dinh 2021) only covered radial solutions. Removing the radial symmetry is a natural and challenging open problem, requiring new techniques to handle the loss of compactness.

Method: Kenig–Merle concentration compactness argument. To handle the non-radial case, the authors use a virial identity in the direction orthogonal to the momentum vector; the condition $p\ge1+8/(d-1)$ ensures positivity of the relevant functional.

Result: Scattering of non-radial solutions below the ground state for $d\ge2$ and $1+8/(d-1)\le p<1+8/(d-4)_+$.

Conclusion: The radial assumption can be dropped for the scattering of fourth-order NLS below the ground state, using a directional virial identity; the condition $p\ge1+8/(d-1)$ guarantees positivity.

Abstract: We consider the focusing nonlinear fourth-order Schrödinger equation \begin{equation*}
  i \partial_t u + μΔu - Δ^2 u
  = - |u|^{p-1} u, \ \ \ (t,x) \in \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{R}^d, \end{equation*} where $μ\ge 0$. The scattering of radial solutions below the ground state is shown by Guo (2016) and Dinh (2021) in the case where $d \ge 2$ and $1 + 8/d < p < 1 + 8/(d-4)_{+}$. In the present paper, we prove the scattering of non-radial solutions below the ground state in the case where $d \ge 2$ and $1 + 8/(d-1) \le p < 1 + 8/(d-4)_{+}$. Our proof is based on the concentration compactness argument by Kenig--Merle (2006). To overcome difficulties in the non-radial case, we use the virial identity in the direction orthogonal to the momentum vector. The condition $p \ge 1 + 8/(d-1)$ is required to show the positivity of the functional in that virial identity.

</details>


### [174] [The influence of a Generalized Hardy-Type Potential on Finite Morse Index Solutions of the Hénon Problem: Local and Nonlocal Cases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05841)
*Habib Fourti,Abdelbaki Selmi,Cherif Zaidi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies nonexistence of stable (and stable-outside-a-compact-set) solutions to a fractional semilinear elliptic equation with Hardy-type potential and Hénon weight, in the whole space and half-space, using energy estimates, a Pohozaev identity, and a monotonicity formula.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analyze how a Hardy-type power potential interacts with a Hénon weight in governing existence/classification of stable solutions, including unbounded and sign-changing ones.

Method: Energy estimates combined with a Pohozaev identity; in the supercritical regime, a monotonicity formula following Dávila et al. (2017).

Result: Nonexistence results for nontrivial stable solutions and, more generally, solutions stable outside a compact set—covering critical and supercritical regimes without extra assumptions, and the subcritical regime under an L∞-smallness condition. Applies to both local (s=1) and nonlocal (0<s<1) cases in whole space and half-space.

Conclusion: The Hardy-type potential and Hénon weight jointly preclude nontrivial stable solutions across regimes; the framework unifies local and nonlocal, whole-space and half-space settings.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate nonexistence results for stable solutions of the semilinear elliptic problem $$(-Δ)^s u+λ|x|^αu=|x|^β|u|^{p-1}u \; \mbox{in}\; \mathcal{H}, $$ where $\mathcal{H}=\mathbb{R}^n$ or $\mathcal{H}=\mathbb{R}^n_+=\{x=(x',x_n),\, x'\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1},x_n>0\}$ , with Dirichlet conditions in the half-space case. We assume that $n\geq2s$, $p>1$, $λ>0,$ $α, β>-2s$ and $0<s\leq1$.
  Our aim is to analyze the influence of the Hardy-type power potential $λ|x|^αu$ in the presence of the Hénon weight $|x|^β$, with particular emphasis on the existence and classification of weak stable solutions, including solutions that may be unbounded or sign-changing. We establish nonexistence results for nontrivial stable solutions and, more generally, for solutions that are stable outside a compact set, covering critical and supercritical regimes without additional assumptions, and the subcritical regime under a suitable $L ^\infty$-smallness condition on a weighted version of the solution. Our results apply to both the local case $s = 1$ and the nonlocal case $0 < s < 1 $, in the whole space and in the half-space.
  The proofs are based on energy estimates combined with a Pohozaev identity. In the supercritical regime, we further establish a monotonicity formula, following the approach of Dávila et al. (Trans. Amer. Math. Soc. 369, 6087-6104, 2017).

</details>


### [175] [A rigidity result for the weighted p-Laplace equation in higher dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05866)
*Ching-Lung Lin,Yi-Hsuan Lin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves rigidity for the weighted p-Laplace equation: a smooth positive weight close to 1 must be identically 1 if its Dirichlet-to-Neumann derivative at an affine boundary value matches that of the unit weight, and establishes local rigidity at every smooth positive reference weight constant in one direction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to extend rigidity results for the weighted p-Laplace equation, removing restrictive assumptions such as convexity, analyticity, closeness to 1, or directional restrictions on the unknown weight.

Method: The proof combines linearization at a solution without critical points with recent rigidity results for the anisotropic Calderón problem. An identity for the linearized conductivity tensors is used to remove the remaining diffeomorphism.

Result: A smooth positive weight sufficiently close to 1 is identically 1 if the derivative of its Dirichlet-to-Neumann map at one affine boundary value agrees with that for the unit weight. Local rigidity holds at every smooth positive reference weight constant in one direction, without requiring closeness to 1 or a directional restriction on the unknown weight.

Conclusion: The results establish broad rigidity for the weighted p-Laplace equation in dimensions three and higher, with the final diffeomorphism-removal step requiring no smallness of either weight.

Abstract: We study rigidity for the weighted $p$-Laplace equation on smooth bounded domains in dimensions three and higher. We show that a smooth positive weight sufficiently close to $1$ is identically $1$ if the derivative of its Dirichlet-to-Neumann map at one affine boundary value agrees with that for the unit weight. We also prove local rigidity at every smooth positive reference weight which is constant in one direction. The reference weight need not be close to $1$, and the unknown weight is only required to be sufficiently close to it, without a directional restriction. No convexity, analyticity, or agreement near the boundary is assumed. The proof combines linearization at a solution without critical points with recent rigidity results for the anisotropic Calderón problem. An identity for the linearized conductivity tensors removes the remaining diffeomorphism. This last step does not require smallness of either weight.

</details>


### [176] [Area-preserving curvature flow for open curves with general contact angles on skew lines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05909)
*Fuya Hiroi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global existence, uniqueness, and exponential convergence to a circular arc for an area-preserving curvature flow of planar open curves ending on skew lines with general contact angles, treated as a free boundary problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work on moving boundary problems for higher-order geometric gradient flows assumed right-angle contact conditions to make boundary conditions natural. General contact angles require a genuinely free boundary formulation, which is the gap this paper addresses.

Method: Formulates the area-preserving curvature flow as a free boundary problem for planar open curves whose endpoints lie on skew lines at general contact angles; derives uniform a priori estimates; uses a constant-speed parametrization.

Result: Under suitable assumptions on the contact angles θ±, the angle θ between the skew lines, and the initial data γ0, proves global-in-time existence and uniqueness up to reparametrization, and exponential convergence to a circular arc with the same signed area.

Conclusion: The free boundary formulation with uniform a priori estimates yields well-posedness and long-time exponential stability of the flow, generalizing earlier right-angle boundary results to general contact angles.

Abstract: We study an area-preserving curvature flow for planar open curves whose end points lie on skew lines and meet the lines at general contact angles. In previous studies of moving boundary problems for higher-order geometric gradient flows, the right-angle condition has often been imposed to ensure that the evolving curves naturally satisfy the boundary conditions. However, under general contact-angle conditions, the evolution must be described by a free boundary problem. Under suitable assumptions on the contact angles $θ_\pm$, the angle $θ$ between the skew lines, and the initial data $γ_0$, we prove global-in-time existence and uniqueness up to reparametrization. We further prove that, after choosing a constant-speed parametrization at each time, the solution converges exponentially to a circular arc with the same signed area as the initial curve. Our analysis is based on a formulation of the evolution as a free boundary problem together with the derivation of uniform a priori estimates.

</details>


### [177] [Steady-State Phase Transition States and Large-Time Asymptotics for the Cauchy Problem of Compressible Navier-Stokes Equations with van der Waals Equation of State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05965)
*Yazhou Chen,Yi Peng,Xiaoding Shi,Xiaoping Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the Cauchy problem for the 1D isothermal compressible Navier-Stokes system with the van der Waals equation of state, proving well-posedness and asymptotic stability of steady-state two-phase phase-transition solutions at subcritical temperatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Finite mass perturbations break the uniqueness of the uniform steady state and generate infinitely many piecewise constant two-phase equilibria satisfying the Maxwell equal-area rule, motivating a selection principle to identify the physically relevant configuration.

Method: In Lagrangian coordinates, variational techniques construct piecewise smooth steady-state solutions for two-phase equilibrium under finite-mass perturbations. An artificial-viscosity regularization establishes existence and uniqueness (within each symmetry class) of the global minimizer; the vanishing-viscosity limit yields a physically admissible steady state with two phase interfaces.

Result: Uniform a priori bounds are derived for weak solutions near the two-phase equilibrium profile under small energy, piecewise-gradient, total-variation, and integral norms (allowing large pointwise jumps across phase boundaries). These global weak solutions converge to the steady-state two-phase equilibrium in L∞(R) as t→∞.

Conclusion: Artificial viscosity acts as a selection principle singling out the physically relevant two-interface steady state, which is asymptotically stable under small integral perturbations permitting large-amplitude discontinuities at phase interfaces.

Abstract: We study the Cauchy problem for the one-dimensional isothermal compressible Navier--Stokes system with the van der Waals equation of state, and analyze the well-posedness and asymptotic stability of steady-state phase-transition solutions at subcritical temperatures. Working in Lagrangian coordinates, we use variational techniques to construct piecewise smooth steady-state solutions corresponding to two-phase equilibrium states under finite-mass perturbations. Introducing artificial-viscosity regularization, we first establish existence of the global minimizer of the regularized variational problem, unique within each fixed symmetry class; passing to the vanishing artificial-viscosity limit then yields a physically admissible steady-state solution with two phase interfaces. For initial perturbations that are small with respect to energy, piecewise-gradient, total-variation, and integral norms but may exhibit large pointwise jumps across phase boundaries, we derive uniform a priori bounds for weak solutions near the two-phase equilibrium profile. We further prove that these global weak solutions converge to the steady-state two-phase equilibrium in $L^\infty(\mathbb{R})$ as $t\to\infty$, thereby characterizing their large-time asymptotic behavior. Physically, finite mass perturbations break the uniqueness of the uniform steady state and generate infinitely many piecewise constant two-phase equilibria satisfying the Maxwell equal-area rule. Artificial viscosity acts as a selection principle that singles out the physically relevant two-interface steady-state configuration, and this two-phase equilibrium is asymptotically stable under small integral perturbations that allow large-amplitude discontinuities at phase interfaces.

</details>


### [178] [Comparison of neural and spline representations for physics-informed learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06294)
*R{é}gis Duvigneau*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This work rigorously compares neural networks and B-Spline representations within a physics-informed learning framework across four PDE problems with closed-form solutions, analyzing accuracy, efficiency, convergence rates, sampling, loss minimization, and precision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Physics-informed machine learning is emerging as a paradigm for solving PDEs (parametric modeling and inverse design), but neural network training faces difficulties: slow convergence, low accuracy, and sensitivity to hyperparameters. A better understanding of the underlying mechanisms is needed.

Method: A rigorous comparison of neural networks versus B-Splines representations within the same physics-informed learning framework, using four benchmark problems with closed-form solutions for numerical study.

Result: The study establishes a rigorous analysis of accuracy and efficiency of both representations in terms of asymptotic convergence rates, sampling requirements, loss function minimization, and required arithmetic precision.

Conclusion: The comparison illuminates the mechanisms behind the training difficulties of physics-informed neural networks and clarifies trade-offs between NN and B-Spline representations.

Abstract: Physics-informed machine learning as recently emerged as a new paradigm for the resolution of partial differential equations, including functionalities for parametric modeling and inverse design, using neural networks to represent the solution fields. However, several difficulties have been reported concerning the training of such networks, resulting in slow convergence, low accurate solutions and sensitivity to the choice of hyper-parameters. In order to better understand the mechanisms underlying these difficulties, the present work proposes a rigourous comparison of neural networks and B-Splines representations within the same physics-informed learning framework. Four problems with closed-form solutions are numerically studied to establish a rigorous analysis of the accuracy and efficiency of the two representations, in terms of asymptotic convergence rates, sampling requirements, loss function minimization and arithmetic precision needed.

</details>


### [179] [Propagation of Energy Moments for the Plasma--Charge Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06134)
*Zhaopeng Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves propagation of energy moments for the 3D plasma-charge model (repulsive Vlasov-Poisson system with a moving point charge): for every K>2, an initially finite Kth energy moment stays finite on bounded time intervals, with minimal assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior results likely required extra conditions such as higher-order moment assumptions, smallness of plasma mass, or initial separation between plasma and the moving point charge. This work aims to remove such restrictive hypotheses.

Method: The proof uses a critical space-time density estimate combined with a direct control of the electric field along the trajectory of the moving charge.

Result: For every K>2, an initially finite Kth energy moment remains finite on every bounded time interval, without higher-order moment assumptions, smallness conditions on plasma mass, or initial separation from the point charge. The constructed global weak solution conserves total energy for almost every time.

Conclusion: The paper establishes robust propagation of energy moments for the plasma-charge model and constructs global weak solutions with almost-everywhere energy conservation under minimal assumptions.

Abstract: This paper proves propagation of energy moments for the three-dimensional plasma-charge model, namely, the repulsive Vlasov-Poisson system with a moving point charge.
