For every \(K>2\), an initially finite \(K\)th energy moment remains finite on every bounded time interval, without any higher-order moment assumption, smallness condition on the plasma mass, or initial separation from the point charge. The constructed global weak solution conserves total energy for almost every time. The proof relies on a critical space-time density estimate and a direct control of the electric field along the moving charge.

</details>


### [180] [Phase-Field Approximation of Vectorial Cohesive Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06206)
*Francesco Colasanto*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes Γ-convergence for anisotropic phase-field functionals modeling cohesive fracture in nonlinear, vectorial settings, showing the cell problem reduces to one-dimensional form under certain conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Anisotropic cohesive fracture in multidimensional, vector-valued, geometrically nonlinear settings requires a rigorous variational framework to link phase-field models to sharp-interface cohesive laws.

Method: Γ-convergence analysis of a class of anisotropic phase-field functionals; characterization of limiting energy (quasiconvexified bulk, Cantor/recession part, cohesive surface density via asymptotic cell problem); reduction of the vectorial cell problem to one-dimensional under operator-norm recession condition; extension of cohesive law reconstruction.

Result: The limiting energy decomposes into bulk, Cantor, and cohesive surface terms; the vectorial cell problem explicitly reduces to a 1D variational problem when the recession function is an operator-norm square compatible with anisotropy; phase-field potentials can be designed to recover prescribed anisotropic, jump-dependent traction-separation laws g(ζ,ν)=γ(ν)g_*(|ζ|).

Conclusion: The cohesive law reconstruction procedure is extended to the vectorial framework, enabling explicit design of phase-field potentials matching prescribed anisotropic, jump-dependent traction-separation laws.

Abstract: We study the $Γ$-convergence of a general class of anisotropic phase-field functionals modelling cohesive fracture in the multidimensional, vector-valued, and geometrically nonlinear setting. The limiting energy comprises a quasiconvexified bulk term, a Cantor part governed by its recession function, and a cohesive surface energy density defined via an asymptotic cell problem. After establishing the structural properties and equivalent formulations of the limiting surface density, we show that, whenever the recession function of the elastic energy is given by the square of an operator norm compatible with the phase-field anisotropy, the vectorial cell problem explicitly reduces to a one-dimensional variational problem. As a consequence, we extend the cohesive law reconstruction procedure of \cite{alessi2025phasefieldpart2} to the vectorial framework, explicitly designing phase-field potentials that recover prescribed classes of anisotropic, jump-dependent traction-separation laws of the form $g(ζ,ν)=γ(ν)g_{\scal}(|ζ|)$.

</details>


### [181] [Surface Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard System: Analysis and Structure-Preserving Discretization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06552)
*Maxim Olshanskii,Mansur Shakipov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a surface Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard system for tangential flow and phase separation on stationary hypersurfaces, proving global well-posedness of weak solutions and developing a structure-preserving unfitted finite element method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Membrane phase separation coupled with tangential viscous flow is important in biology, but the surface Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard system lacks a complete well-posedness theory and energy-stable numerical schemes.

Method: Equations posed on a closed stationary C^2 hypersurface in R^{d+1} (d=2,3). Existence/uniqueness via weak solutions with velocity orthogonal to Killing fields; Lipschitz dependence in energy norms and higher regularity for d=2. A fully discrete unfitted finite element scheme using penalty on approximate Killing fields with projections in transport/capillary terms.

Result: Global existence and uniqueness of weak solutions for H^1(Γ) initial phase fields, satisfying energy equality and mass conservation. Lipschitz continuous dependence in energy norms on finite intervals, even for data with different means; higher regularity in d=2. The scheme conserves total phase and satisfies an exact discrete energy identity. Experiments identify Killing fields and show phase separation on a torus and a liposome with experimental parameters.

Conclusion: The work provides a rigorous analytical foundation and a structure-preserving numerical method for the surface Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard system, validated by numerical experiments on biologically relevant geometries.

Abstract: We study the surface Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard system governing tangential flow and phase separation in viscous fluid membranes. The equations are posed on a closed, stationary $C^2$ hypersurface $Γ\subset\mathbb{R}^{d+1}$, $d=2,3$. For constant viscosity and mobility and a class of regular double-well potentials, we prove global existence and uniqueness of weak solutions for initial phase fields in $H^1(Γ)$, with the velocity chosen orthogonal to the Killing fields. These solutions satisfy an energy equality and conserve mass. We establish Lipschitz continuous dependence in energy norms on every finite time interval, including for initial data with different means, and derive higher regularity results for $d=2$ under additional smoothness assumptions on the surface, potential, and initial data. We also develop a fully discrete unfitted finite element method that conserves the total phase and satisfies an exact discrete energy identity. The method combines a penalty on approximate Killing fields with projections in the transport and capillary terms to control these modes while preserving the energy balance. Numerical experiments examine the identification of Killing fields and illustrate phase separation on a torus and a liposome with experimentally informed material parameters.

</details>


### [182] [EXISTENCE AND UPPER BOUNDS OF FUNDAMENTAL SOLUTIONS TO KINETIC EQUATIONS WITH ROUGH NON-LOCAL DIFFUSION](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06214)
*Pascal Auscher*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies fundamental solution operators for Kolmogorov equations with symmetric non-local diffusion and rough kernels, proving they are induced by measurable kernels with sharp upper bounds given by a kinetic profile.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding fundamental solution operators for Kolmogorov equations with non-local diffusion and rough kernels is a fundamental problem, particularly how transport and non-local diffusion interact to shape the solution's behavior.

Method: The proof combines P.-A. Meyer's small-jump and loss decomposition with Nash's ideas, using a purely analytic approach.

Result: The operators are shown to be induced by measurable kernels, and sharp upper bounds are established in terms of a kinetic profile arising from the interplay of transport and non-local diffusion.

Conclusion: The work provides sharp measurable-kernel representation and upper bounds for fundamental solutions of Kolmogorov equations with rough non-local diffusion, via a purely analytic method.

Abstract: We study the fundamental solution operators associated to Kolmogorov equations with symmetric non-local diffusion with rough kernels. We prove that these operators are induced by measurable kernels and prove sharp upper bounds in terms of a kinetic profile governed by the interplay of transport and non-local diffusion. Our proof combines the small-jump with loss decomposition of P.-A. Meyer with the ideas of Nash. The proof is purely analytic.

</details>


### [183] [Transparent Boundary Conditions for the Time-Fractional Heat Equation on Metric Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06631)
*Anis Bel Hadj Hassin,Matthias Ehrhardt*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper derives transparent (non-reflecting) boundary conditions for the time-fractional heat equation on a metric star graph, showing the vertex operator is an Abel-type convolution kernel of order β/2, and proves the diffusion-coefficient sum rule κ1=κ2+κ3 holds for all fractional orders β, verified numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The time-fractional heat equation models subdiffusive transport in disordered and porous branched media. Transparent boundary conditions are needed to avoid spurious reflections at network junctions, but their form for fractional (memory) transport on branched graph geometries is not established.

Method: The exterior branch problems are solved via Laplace transform, yielding the non-local vertex operator at the junction as an Abel-type convolution kernel of order β/2. The authors rigorously prove the sum rule and uniqueness for the coupled vertex problem, then validate with an implicit L1 finite-difference scheme on the coupled PDE system.

Result: The non-local vertex operator is an Abel-type convolution kernel of order β/2, generalizing the classical half-derivative kernel at β=1. The diffusion-coefficient sum rule κ1=κ2+κ3, which eliminates thermal backflow, holds unchanged for every fractional order β. Numerical results confirm the analysis, comparing the star-graph solution to a reference solution on an unbranched line with matched diffusivity.

Conclusion: Transparency of the junction depends only on the network's diffusivities, not on the memory of the transport law. The fractional-order-independent sum rule provides a robust design principle for transparent junctions in subdiffusive branched media.

Abstract: In this work, we study transparent boundary conditions for the time-fractional heat equation on a metric star graph. This equation is the standard continuum model for subdiffusive heat and mass transport in disordered and porous branched media. Using the Laplace transform to solve the exterior branch problems, we demonstrate that the non-local vertex operator observed by the incoming bond is an Abel-type convolution kernel of order $β/2$, which generalizes the classical half-derivative kernel recovered at $β=1$. Our main result is that the diffusion-coefficient sum rule $κ_1=κ_2+κ_3$, known to eliminate thermal backflow for the classical heat equation, remains unchanged for every fractional order $β$: the transparency of the junction depends only on the network's diffusivities and not on the memory of the transport law. We rigorously prove this and uniqueness for the coupled vertex problem. We confirm our results numerically at the level of the underlying coupled partial differential equation system with an implicit L1 finite-difference scheme. We compare the star-graph solution to a reference solution on an unbranched line with matching diffusivity.

</details>


### [184] [Energy dissipation and free boundary stability of the Stefan problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06267)
*Filippo Paiano,Bozhidar Velichkov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves stability and continuity of free boundaries for one-phase and two-phase weak solutions of the Stefan problem in any dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Control of free boundary displacement in the Stefan problem is difficult; the authors seek a flexible tool to analyze stability, continuity, and long-time behavior of weak solutions in both one-phase and two-phase settings.

Method: Two main ingredients: (1) an energy conservation principle for weak solutions in general domains (both one-phase and two-phase), yielding a modulus of continuity for free boundary displacement; (2) a new lower density bound for the melting region at free boundary points moving with maximal velocity.

Result: Stability and continuity of free boundaries are established in any dimension; the energy conservation principle provides a modulus of continuity and a flexible tool for long-time behavior analysis.

Conclusion: The approach yields qualitative and quantitative results on asymptotic behavior of weak solutions, with applications discussed.

Abstract: In this paper we prove the stability and the continuity of the free boundaries of two-phase and one-phase weak solutions to the Stefan problem in any dimension. Our proof has two main ingredients. The first one is an energy conservation principle for weak solutions that we derive for general domains in both the one-phase and the two-phase settings; this energy conservation principle provides the modulus of continuity for the displacement of the free boundary and is a flexible tool for analyzing long-time behavior of solutions. The second key ingredient for the control of the displacement of the free boundary is a new lower density bound for the melting region at free boundary points that move with maximal velocity. We also discuss some applications, showing a series of qualitative and quantitative results on the asymptotic behavior of weak solutions.

</details>


### [185] [Dispersion for the Schrödinger equation inside strictly convex domains: the general case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06305)
*Oana Ivanovici,Fabrice Planchon*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs a sharp local-in-time semiclassical parametrix for the Schrödinger equation on strictly convex domains/manifolds with Dirichlet boundary, proving dispersion estimates with a 1/4 exponent loss versus the boundaryless case, and deriving Strichartz and nonlinear applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Boundary effects in semiclassical Schrödinger propagation on convex domains are not fully understood; the aim is to obtain sharp dispersion estimates accounting for Dirichlet boundary reflections.

Method: Construct a sharp local-in-time semiclassical parametrix on smooth strictly convex domains (and Riemannian manifolds with strictly convex boundary), then derive dispersion estimates for the Green function.

Result: The fixed-time Green function decay shows a loss of 1/4 in the exponent of h/t relative to the boundaryless case, which is sharp. On 3D compact manifolds with convex boundary, spectrally localized Strichartz estimates give global well-posedness in the energy space for the defocusing cubic NLS, matching Burq-Gérard-Tzvetkov, and higher Sobolev norm growth bounds extend Planchon-Visciglia-Tzvetkov.

Conclusion: Dispersion on strictly convex domains is governed by a sharp parametrix with a quantified 1/4 loss, yielding Strichartz estimates and nonlinear Schrödinger results analogous to the boundaryless setting.

Abstract: We consider a general smooth bounded strictly convex domain $Ω\subset\mathbb{R}^d$ of dimension $d\geq2$ and describe dispersion for the semiclassical Schrödinger equation with Dirichlet boundary condition. Our results hold more generally on compact smooth Riemannian manifolds with smooth strictly convex boundary. More specifically, we construct a sharp local in (semiclassical) time parametrix and then proceed to obtain dispersion estimates: our fixed-time decay rate for the Green function exhibits a loss of $1/4$ in the exponent of $h/t$ with respect to the boundaryless case. The loss is sharp for dispersion, as shown earlier by the first author in the case of a model convex domain. On compact three-dimensional manifolds with smooth strictly convex boundary, the resulting spectrally localized Strichartz estimates yield global well-posedness in the energy space for the defocusing cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation, thus matching the corresponding result for boundaryless manifolds due to Burq-Gérard-Tzvetkov. Moreover, we extend bounds on the time growth of higher Sobolev norms from Planchon-Visciglia-Tzvetkov to our setting.

</details>


### [186] [Rigidity results for Euler flows in two dimensional exterior domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06309)
*Diego Alonso-Orán,Daniel Sánchez-Simón del Pino,Juan J. L. Velázquez*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For steady 2D incompressible Euler flow outside a compact obstacle with inflow at every boundary point, the flow is irrotational iff the velocity has no stagnation points, under suitable far-field direction and decay assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Steady exterior Euler flows are classically linked to potential (irrotational) flow, but rigorous conditions guaranteeing irrotationality in unbounded domains with non-trivial boundaries are subtle. The paper targets characterizing when vorticity must vanish in such exterior settings.

Method: Analytic study of steady solutions of the 2D incompressible Euler equations in the exterior of a compact domain. Assumes fluid enters the exterior domain through every point of the obstacle boundary, plus suitable assumptions on the direction and decay of velocity at infinity. Establishes an equivalence between irrotationality and absence of stagnation points.

Result: Under the stated boundary-inflow, direction and decay assumptions at infinity, it is proven that the flow is irrotational if and only if the velocity field has no stagnation points.

Conclusion: The irrotationality of such exterior steady Euler flows is equivalent to the absence of stagnation points, providing a sharp characterization of potential flow in this setting.

Abstract: We study steady solutions of the two dimensional incompressible Euler equations in the exterior of a compact domain. We assume that fluid enters the exterior domain through every point of the obstacle boundary. Under suitable assumptions on the direction and decay of the velocity at infinity, we prove that the flow is irrotational if and only if the velocity has no stagnation points.

</details>


### [187] [Hessian operators, null Lagrangians and the integral comparison principle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06313)
*Sławomir Dinew,Szymon Myga,Marcin Sroka*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper characterizes when Hessian operators satisfy an integral comparison principle, linking this to null Lagrangians and classifying the admissible operators as linear combinations of k-Hessians (exactly k-Hessians in the homogeneous case), with an analogous complex Hessian result.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The integral comparison principle is a fundamental tool in the theory of Hessian equations and pluripotential theory, yet the precise class of operators admitting it was not well understood. The authors aim to determine exactly which Hessian-type operators obey this principle and to reveal obstructions to developing a genuine pluripotential theory, for instance for the J-equation.

Method: The authors establish a connection between the integral comparison principle and the notion of a null Lagrangian from the calculus of variations. Under mild conditions, they perform a complete classification of the operators satisfying the principle.

Result: The only operators satisfying the integral comparison principle are linear combinations of k-Hessians; in the homogeneous case, they are precisely the k-Hessians themselves. An analogous classification is obtained for the complex Hessian operator, revealing substantial obstructions to a genuine pluripotential theory for the J-equation.

Conclusion: The integral comparison principle severely restricts the admissible Hessian operators, tightly tying them to k-Hessians via null Lagrangians. The complex analogue yields significant obstructions, notably for the development of a pluripotential theory for the J-equation.

Abstract: We investigate the conditions under which a Hessian operator satisfies the integral comparison principle. We establish a rather unexpected connection with the notion of a null Lagrangian from the calculus of variations. Under mild conditions we obtain a complete classification, showing that the only operators for which the integral comparison principle holds are linear combinations of $k$-Hessians and, in the homogeneous case, precisely the $k$-Hessians themselves. An analogous result is established for the complex Hessian operator, revealing in particular substantial obstructions to the development of a genuine pluripotential theory for the $\mathcal{J}$-equation.

</details>


### [188] [Solutions stable outside a compact set for anisotropic quasilinear equations with negative exponent or exponential nonlinearity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06326)
*Nhat Vy Huynh,Phuong Le*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies nonlinear elliptic equations driven by the Finsler p-Laplacian in R^N, proving nonexistence of stable-outside-a-compact-set (and thus finite Morse index) solutions for the negative-power equation when p ≤ N < p(p+3)/(p-1) above the critical q threshold, and for the exponential equation when p < N < p(p+3)/(p-1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sobolev-type and Hardy-type critical exponents for Δp u = u^{-q} and -Δp u = e^u are well understood in the isotropic case, but the corresponding theory with a Finsler norm H (the Finsler p-Laplacian) is not. The authors aim to extend stability and nonexistence results to this anisotropic setting and to fill gaps in stability arguments used in earlier isotropic/anisotropic proofs.

Method: Key tools are: (i) non-stability obstructions proved by comparison with explicit radial barriers having frozen right-hand sides, showing a positive exterior solution cannot stay above θ times the singular solution near infinity when N > qτ; (ii) integral estimates for operators div(B'(H(∇u))∇H(∇u)) with p-growth, established with fully justified truncation and absorption procedures; and (iii) adaptation/gap-filling of stability arguments.

Result: Nonexistence of solutions stable outside a compact set (hence no finite Morse index solutions) for Δp^H u = u^{-q} with p ≤ N < p(p+3)/(p-1) and q above the sharp critical threshold, and for -Δp^H u = e^u with p < N < p(p+3)/(p-1). In the borderline N=p case, solutions of -ΔN^H u = e^u stable outside a compact set are exactly the finite-mass solutions classified by Ciraolo and Li. Sharpness is shown when N>p, and for the negative-power equation when N=p>2.

Conclusion: The Finsler p-Laplacian admits the same critical/stability thresholds as the isotropic theory in the regimes studied, with a special classification in the borderline case N=p. The barrier-comparison obstructions are independent of stability and hold more generally, and the new integral estimates repair gaps in earlier isotropic and anisotropic proofs.

Abstract: We study the equations $Δ_p^H u=u^{-q}$, $u>0$, and $-Δ_p^H u=e^u$ in $\mathbb{R}^N$, where $Δ_p^H u=\operatorname{div}(H(\nabla u)^{p-1}\nabla H(\nabla u))$ is the Finsler $p$-Laplacian associated with a uniformly convex norm $H$, and $p\ge2$. We prove nonexistence of solutions stable outside a compact set for the negative-power equation when $p\le N<\frac{p(p+3)}{p-1}$ and $q$ lies above the sharp critical threshold, and for the exponential equation when $p<N<\frac{p(p+3)}{p-1}$. Consequently, neither equation admits solutions of finite Morse index in these ranges. In the borderline case $N=p$, we prove that the solutions of $-Δ_N^H u=e^u$ that are stable outside a compact set are precisely the finite-mass solutions classified by Ciraolo and Li. A main ingredient is independent of stability: if $N>qτ$, where $τ=\frac{p}{q+p-1}$, a positive exterior solution of $Δ_p^H u=u^{-q}$ cannot remain above $θ$ times the singular solution near infinity for any $θ>1$. An analogous obstruction holds for the exponential equation. Both obstructions are proved by comparison with explicit radial barriers having frozen right-hand sides. We also prove sharpness when $N>p$, and for the negative-power equation when $N=p>2$. Finally, for operators $\operatorname{div}(B'(H(\nabla u))\nabla H(\nabla u))$ with $p$-growth, we establish the integral estimates needed for the stability arguments with fully justified truncation and absorption procedures. These arguments also repair gaps in earlier proofs for the corresponding isotropic and anisotropic problems.

</details>


### [189] [Large deviations for mean field particle systems via analysis on spaces of measures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06333)
*Valentin Pesce*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes large deviation principles for mean field interacting particle systems in T^d with vanishing noise, using a PDE-based (Freidlin-Wentzell) approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive large deviation principles for mean field interacting particle systems with vanishing noise, treating it as a Freidlin-Wentzell type problem.

Method: A PDE approach on the space of probability measures is outlined self-containedly. It shows the transfer function converges to the unique viscosity solution of a limiting Hamilton-Jacobi equation on the space of probability measures as particle number goes to infinity.

Result: The transfer function of the system converges to the unique viscosity solution of the limiting Hamilton-Jacobi equation, and the rate function is explicitly derived by formulating the limiting PDE solution as the value function of an optimal control problem.

Conclusion: The PDE approach yields large deviation principles and an explicit rate function for mean field interacting particle systems in the vanishing noise limit.

Abstract: This paper introduces a robust strategy to establish large deviation principles for mean field interacting particle systems in T d with vanishing noise. Viewed as a Freidlin-Wentzell type problem, our approach relies on recent techniques for partial differential equations (in short PDE) on the space of probability measures [5]. We first outline the proof strategy for the PDE approach to large deviations in a self-contained manner. As the number of particles goes to infinity, we then show that the transfer function of the system converges to the unique viscosity solution of a limiting Hamilton-Jacobi equation on the space of probability measures. Finally, we explicitly derive the rate function by formulating the solution of this limiting PDE as the value function of an optimal control problem.

</details>


### [190] [Existence of positive Solutions to Hénon equation on integer Lattice graph and Half-Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06353)
*Huyuan Chen,Lixun Jia*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence of positive solutions for a discrete Hénon equation on the integer lattice (dimensions ≥3) and half-space (dimensions ≥2) with zero Dirichlet boundary conditions, for sufficiently large nonlinearity exponents, using weighted Banach spaces, Green kernel estimates, and Schauder's fixed point theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Hénon-type potential with positive exponent prevents the use of standard variational compactness arguments, so alternative methods are needed to establish existence of positive solutions.

Method: The authors combine weighted Banach spaces, two-sided Green kernel estimates, and the Schauder fixed point theorem to overcome the lack of compactness.

Result: Existence of positive solutions is established on the whole lattice in dimensions at least three, and on the half-space in dimensions at least two, for sufficiently large nonlinearity exponents.

Conclusion: Discrete Hénon equations admit positive solutions in the considered settings despite the absence of standard variational compactness.

Abstract: We study positive solutions of a discrete Hénon equation on the integer lattice graph and on the half-space, with zero Dirichlet boundary conditions in the half-space case. The nonlinearity is governed by a Hénon-type potential with positive exponent. For sufficiently large nonlinearity exponents, we prove the existence of positive solutions both on the whole lattice in dimensions at least three and on the half-space in dimensions at least two.
  Thanks to the Hénon potential, the standard variational compactness is unavailable to obtain the positive solution. Our proofs combine weighted Banach spaces, two-sided Green kernel estimates and the Schauder fixed point theorem.

</details>


### [191] [Logarithmic upper bounds and axial front selection for spatially periodic lattice Fisher--KPP equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06381)
*Yuanyang Hu,Zhenkun Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the position and shape of invasion fronts for spatially periodic Fisher-KPP equations on higher-dimensional lattices with localized initial data, determining logarithmic bounds, Bramson corrections, and critical pulsating fronts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding how invasion fronts propagate in spatially periodic Fisher-KPP equations on higher-dimensional lattices is important, particularly the role of periodic spectral geometry in determining front position and shape.

Method: Combines a periodic transverse heat kernel, a scalar lower comparison, critical-tail analysis, and a positive Dirichlet quotient comparison.

Result: Periodic spectral geometry yields a directional logarithmic upper bound. When the linear growth rate is independent of the propagation coordinate, a matching axial Bramson correction and the critical pulsating front are proved; the selected profile includes the local periodic cell with a bounded phase whose derivative tends to zero. For fully varying growth rates, a Dirichlet quotient comparison reduces the matching lower bound to two explicit estimates on the linear seed and nonlinear loss.

Conclusion: The framework characterizes front propagation times, level reaching times, and asymptotic time gaps, extending Bramson-type corrections to periodic lattice settings.

Abstract: We study the position and shape of invasion fronts for spatially periodic Fisher--KPP equations on higher-dimensional lattices, starting from localized initial data. Periodic spectral geometry determines a directional logarithmic upper bound. When the linear growth rate is independent of the propagation coordinate, we prove the matching axial Bramson correction and identify the critical pulsating front approached by the solution. The selected profile includes the local periodic cell and a bounded phase whose derivative tends to zero. This description also determines the times at which prescribed levels reach distant lattice sites and the asymptotic time gaps between levels or neighboring cells. The proof combines a periodic transverse heat kernel, a scalar lower comparison, and critical-tail analysis. For growth rates varying in every coordinate, a positive Dirichlet quotient comparison reduces the matching lower bound to two explicit estimates on the linear seed and its accumulated nonlinear loss.

</details>


### [192] [Rough Axisymmetric Euler Dynamics: Global Energy Compactness and Kinetic Passage Across Regularity Breakdown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06385)
*Gi-Chan Bae,Hongxu Chen,Chanwoo Kim*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Establishes global energy compactness for rough 3D axisymmetric Euler flow without swirl and uses it to build hydrodynamic limits of kinetic equations beyond finite-time Euler breakdown.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hydrodynamic limits of collisional kinetic equations typically rely on Euler regularity, which can break down in finite time; a compactness framework is needed to extend limits past such breakdown.

Method: Proves global energy compactness for regular approximate axisymmetric Euler flows via sharp radial dynamics controlling the absolute impulse, for relative vorticity in L^1∩L^p; applies this to Coulomb Landau and non-cutoff soft-potential Boltzmann equations.

Result: Compactness holds in C_tL_x^2 for p≥4/3 without sign/moment assumptions; below 4/3 under uniform impulse control. Impulse propagates for p≥5/3, controlled for 1<p<5/3 under one-sided L^{p#} integrability (p#=4p/(3p-1), sharp); one-sign finite-impulse extends to all p>1. Constructs strong solutions on T^ε→∞ with energy-conserving Euler limits.

Conclusion: Provides global energy-conserving Euler continuations past finite-time regularity breakdown in hydrodynamic limits, with a common strong trace at the singular time and sharp thresholds for compactness.

Abstract: We establish global energy compactness for rough three-dimensional axisymmetric Euler flow without swirl and use it to construct hydrodynamic limits of collisional kinetic equations beyond finite-time Euler regularity breakdown. For relative vorticity in $L^1\cap L^p$, regular approximate Euler flows are globally compact in $C_tL_x^2$ for $p\ge4/3$, without a sign condition or any spatial-moment assumption. Below $4/3$, the same conclusion holds under uniform control of the absolute impulse. We identify the sharp radial dynamics governing this confinement: the absolute impulse is propagated without a sign condition for $p\ge5/3$, while for $1<p<5/3$ it is controlled under one-sided $L^{p^\sharp}$ integrability, where $p^\sharp= {4p} / (3p-1)$, and this exponent is sharp. In particular, for one-sign finite-impulse vorticity the resulting global energy compactness holds throughout the full range $p>1$.
  For the Coulomb Landau equation and a class of non-cutoff soft-potential Boltzmann equations, we construct strong solutions on lifespans $T^\varepsilon\to\infty$ realizing prescribed rough finite-energy Euler initial data and obtain energy-conserving axisymmetric Euler limits on every fixed time interval. If the corresponding regular Euler evolution loses regularity in finite time, the hydrodynamic fields converge to the regular Euler solution up to the singular time and have a common strong trace there, while subsequences yield global energy-conserving Euler continuations past that time.

</details>


### [193] [Coercivity and regularity for nonlocal divergence-form operators with measurable kernels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06408)
*Anup Biswas*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies nonlocal divergence-form operators with symmetric kernels, negatively answering an open question about bounded discontinuous weak solutions, then proves coercivity and interior Hölder regularity under a stronger one-sided lower bound plus an L^p annuli condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Open Question 2.1 of FRRO24 asked whether certain kernel conditions imply regularity; the authors also address a coercivity conjecture from CS20 and IS20.

Method: Construct a counterexample kernel satisfying the FRRO24 conditions yielding a bounded discontinuous weak solution; then impose a one-sided lower bound and an L^p bound on K over annuli to derive a coercivity estimate and Hölder regularity.

Result: A kernel is constructed for which the problem admits a bounded discontinuous weak solution, negatively answering Open Question 2.1. Under the stronger one-sided lower bound and L^p annuli hypothesis (p>1), a coercivity estimate and interior Hölder regularity of weak solutions are established.

Conclusion: The coercivity conjecture of CS20 and IS20 holds under the additional L^p hypothesis for p>1, while the earlier weaker kernel conditions are insufficient for regularity.

Abstract: We consider nonlocal divergence-form operators of the type $$PV\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}(u(x)-u(y))K(x, y) dy=0\quad \text{in} \; B_2,$$ where the kernel $K$ is symmetric ($K(x, y) = K(y, x)$). We first show that there exists a kernel satisfying the conditions of \cite[Open Question 2.1]{FRRO24} for which this problem admits a bounded discontinuous weak solution, answering that question negatively. We then impose the stronger one-sided lower bound suggested in \cite{CS20,IS20}, together with an $L^p$ bound of $K$ over annuli, and prove a coercivity estimate and interior Hölder regularity of weak solutions. In particular, this establishes the coercivity conjecture of \cite{CS20,IS20} under the additional $L^p$ hypothesis for $p>1$.

</details>


### [194] [Regularity for $H$-systems of bounded distortion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06441)
*Sławomir Kolasiński*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Weak solutions to the n-harmonic H-system in W^{1,n}(U,R^{n+1}) are locally bounded when the distortion |Du|^n/|Ju| is bounded where |u| is large; if H is Hölder, u is locally Hölder.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish regularity (local boundedness and Hölder continuity) for weak solutions of the n-harmonic H-system, where standard methods are complicated by the nonlinear, dimension-matching structure and the possibly unbounded target behavior.

Method: Push forward |Du|^{n-2}Du ∘ Du* by u to obtain a linear functional on compactly supported continuous maps, controlled by the equation. Under bounded distortion, this functional satisfies a Michael-Simon-type inequality (De Philippis, Gennaioli, Pigati, Rindler) for measures obeying a first-order PDE, giving lower bounds on mass ratios of the push-forward of |Du|^n L^n.

Result: The solution u is locally bounded when its distortion |Du|^n/|Ju| is bounded on the set where |u| is large; if additionally H is Hölder continuous, u is locally Hölder continuous.

Conclusion: The mass-ratio lower bounds implied by the Michael-Simon-type inequality yield local boundedness, and Hölder regularity of H upgrades this to local Hölder continuity of u.

Abstract: We consider weak solutions $u \in W^{1,n}(U,\mathbf{R}^{n+1})$ of the $H$-system $- \operatorname{div} ( |\mathrm{D} u|^{n-2} \mathrm{D} u ) = (H \circ u) \, \mathbf{J} u$, where $n \ge 2$, $U$ is the open unit ball in $\mathbf{R}^n$, and $H$ is bounded. We show that $u$ is locally bounded if its distortion $|\mathrm{D} u|^n / |\mathbf{J} u|$ is bounded on the set where $|u|$ is large. If moreover $H$ is Hölder continuous, then $u$ is locally Hölder continuous. The proof pushes forward $|\mathrm{D} u|^{n-2} \mathrm{D} u \circ \mathrm{D} u^*$ by $u$ to a linear functional on continuous maps of $\mathbf{R}^{n+1}$ into $\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{R}^{n+1},\mathbf{R}^{n+1})$ with compact support, whose values on derivatives are controlled by the equation. When the distortion is bounded, this functional satisfies the hypotheses of an inequality of Michael--Simon type for measures satisfying a first-order partial differential equation, due to De Philippis, Gennaioli, Pigati, and Rindler. The inequality yields lower bounds for the mass ratios of the push-forward of $|\mathrm{D} u|^n \mathscr{L}^n$ by $u$, and these imply boundedness.

</details>


### [195] [Global dynamics for the 1d quartic Klein-Gordon equation with an internal mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06456)
*Gong Chen,Gael Y. Diebou,Adilbek Kairzhan,Fabio Pusateri*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes a 1D nonlinear Klein-Gordon equation with a potential, quadratic localized nonlinearity, and quartic non-localized nonlinearity, proving global dynamics of the internal mode amplitude via radiation damping and radiation scattering under the Fermi Golden Rule, with application to kink asymptotic stability in perturbed φ⁴ models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The cubic version of this problem is closely tied to open questions about global-in-space dispersive asymptotics near kink solutions in the φ⁴ model. The quartic, non-localized interaction is essentially sharp for the intended analysis, making the problem technically delicate and relevant to kink stability.

Method: Uses the distorted Fourier transform along with refined dispersive decay and smoothing estimates, assuming a single discrete eigenvalue (internal mode) below the continuous spectrum and the natural Fermi Golden Rule.

Result: Provides a global description of the internal mode amplitude dynamics through the radiation damping mechanism and proves scattering for the radiation. Also proves asymptotic stability of kinks for scalar field models that are perturbations of the φ⁴ model.

Conclusion: The analysis successfully handles the sharp quartic non-localized nonlinearity, yielding internal mode damping and radiation scattering, with direct applications to kink asymptotic stability in perturbed φ⁴-type scalar field models.

Abstract: We study a one dimensional nonlinear Klein-Gordon equation with a potential, a localized quadratic nonlinearity, and a non-localized quartic nonlinearity. We assume that the linearized operator has a single discrete eigenvalue below the continuous spectrum, corresponding to an ''internal mode''. This eigenvalue generates localized, time-periodic solutions for the linear equation. Assuming the natural Fermi Golden Rule, we give a global description of the dynamics of the amplitude of the internal mode through the radiation damping mechanism, and prove scattering for the radiation. Our approach is based on the distorted Fourier transform and a collection of refined dispersive decay and smoothing estimates.
  The corresponding cubic problem is closely related to the question of global-in-space dispersive asymptotics for perturbations of kink solutions in the classical $φ^4$ model, which remains a challenging open question. From the perspective of nonlinear estimates, a quartic (non-localized) interaction is essentially sharp for our analysis, and closing the estimates with such a low degree nonlinear term requires a careful and delicate treatment.
  As a direct application of our analysis, we prove a result on the asymptotic stability of kinks for classes of scalar field models that can be seen as perturbations of the $φ^4$ model.

</details>


### [196] [Optimal control of the Dyson equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06460)
*Charles Bertucci,Pierre-Louis Lions,Panagiotis E. Souganidis*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies an optimal control problem for the Dyson equation describing mean-field evolution of large random matrix spectra, treating regular and singular terminal data, and characterizes the value function via the HJB equation using viscosity solutions in the space of measures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Dyson equation governs the mean-field spectral evolution of large random matrices, and an optimal control framework for it is of interest, but no complete treatment existed covering both regular and singular terminal data.

Method: The authors construct admissible controls and prove existence of optimal ones, then perform a mathematical analysis of the associated Hamilton-Jacobi-Bellman equation via the theory of viscosity solutions in the space of measures.

Result: Existence of optimal controls is established for both regular and singular terminal data, and the value function is characterized as the unique viscosity solution of the infinite-dimensional HJB equation.

Conclusion: The optimal control problem for the Dyson equation is fully solved, with the value function uniquely identified as the viscosity solution of the associated infinite-dimensional HJB equation.

Abstract: We provide a complete study of the optimal control problem associated to the so-called Dyson equation, which describes the mean field evolution of the spectrum of large random matrices. We treat both the case of regular and singular terminal data. We construct admissible controls and prove the existence of optimal ones. We then proceed to give a thorough mathematical analysis of the associated Hamilton-Jacobi-Bellman equation by means of the theory of viscosity solutions in the space of measures. In particular, we characterize the value function of the optimal control problem as the unique viscosity solution to this infinite dimensional HJB equation.

</details>


### [197] [Regularity of soda-can domains for the $p$-parabolic equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06497)
*Benny Avelin,Mikko Parviainen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper classifies boundary regularity of the origin for the p-parabolic equation in soda-can domains Θ_{l,θ} for all p>1 and n≥2, showing the origin is regular at l=p iff p≥2n/(n+1), and answers negatively a well-known open question about whether a single barrier characterizes boundary regularity in the degenerate range p>2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Boundary regularity for the p-parabolic equation is well understood in the singular range p<2 but less so in the degenerate range p>2. A well-known open question asks whether the existence of a single traditional barrier suffices to characterize boundary regularity in the degenerate range, and soda-can domains provide a natural setting to probe this.

Method: The authors analyze the p-parabolic equation in soda-can domains Θ_{l,θ} = {(x,t) : 0 < -t < θ|x|^l < θ}, classifying the regularity of the origin across all p>1 and n≥2. They construct a traditional barrier at the origin and compare this with actual regularity/irregularity of the origin.

Result: At the endpoint l=p, the origin is regular if and only if p ≥ 2n/(n+1). A traditional barrier exists at the origin for every p>2 and l>0. However, when 2<p<n and l<p, the origin is nevertheless irregular.

Conclusion: The existence of a traditional barrier does not suffice to characterize boundary regularity in the degenerate range p>2, answering the well-known open question negatively.

Abstract: We classify the regularity of the origin for the $p$-parabolic equation in the soda-can domains $$Θ_{l,θ}=\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^n\times\mathbb{R}:0 < -t < θ|x|^l < θ\},\text{ where }l,θ>0.$$ The classification covers every $p>1$ in dimensions $n\ge2$. At the endpoint $l=p$, the origin is regular if and only if $p\ge2n/(n+1)$. We also prove that the origin admits a traditional barrier for every $p>2$ and $l>0$. When $2 < p < n$ and $l < p$, the origin is nevertheless irregular. This answers negatively the well-known open question whether one barrier suffices to characterize boundary regularity in the degenerate range $p>2$.

</details>


### [198] [Unconditional uniqueness for the cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation in $\dot{H}^{\frac12}(\Bbb R^3)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06501)
*Yongming Luo,Jiayu Zheng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper resolves unconditional uniqueness for the cubic NLS on R^3 at the critical Sobolev regularity s=1/2, which had remained open.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unconditional uniqueness for cubic NLS on R^3 was known for scaling-subcritical Sobolev spaces with 1/2<s<1, but the critical endpoint case s=1/2 was still open.

Method: Adapts Kato's bootstrap argument to a new auxiliary space, the critical Besov space Bdot^{-1}_{∞,∞}. To handle the lack of coefficient regularity at the endpoint, the authors perform a second Duhamel iteration of Born type and estimate the resulting double Duhamel integral as a whole.

Result: Improved estimates are obtained that let the bootstrap close in Bdot^{-1}_{∞,∞} using only the available regularity of the coefficients, establishing uniqueness at s=1/2.

Conclusion: Unconditional uniqueness for the cubic NLS on R^3 at the critical endpoint s=1/2 is proven, closing a previously open problem.

Abstract: While unconditional uniqueness for the cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation (NLS) on $\Bbb R^3$ is known in the scaling-subcritical Sobolev spaces $\dot H^s(\Bbb R^3)$ for $1/2<s<1$, the critical case $s=1/2$ has remained open. We resolve this problem by adapting Kato's bootstrap argument to a new choice of auxiliary space, namely the critical Besov space $\dot B^{-1}_{\infty,\infty}$. The main difficulty at this endpoint is that the natural solution class does not provide the coefficient regularity required to close the bootstrap in this space. To overcome this difficulty, we perform a second Duhamel iteration of Born type and estimate the resulting double Duhamel integral as a whole. This yields improved estimates that allow the bootstrap to close in $\dot B^{-1}_{\infty,\infty}$ using only the available regularity of the coefficients.

</details>


### [199] [Uniform oscillation properties of almost periodic functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06515)
*Alain Haraux*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A synthetic review unifying scattered results on oscillation length, covering periodic functions, almost periodic functions with zero mean, examples from continuum mechanics, and a related open problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Results on oscillation length were scattered across the literature and never presented in a unified form, so the paper aims to synthesize them.

Method: The paper investigates the oscillation length for periodic functions precisely, establishes a uniform oscillation length for scalar almost periodic functions with mean-value 0, and derives upper bounds for almost periodic functions arising in continuum mechanics.

Result: A uniform oscillation length exists for scalar almost periodic functions with zero mean; upper bounds are obtained for continuum mechanics functions, and a finite oscillation length with respect to time is shown in any open subset of some vibrating continuous media.

Conclusion: The work consolidates existing results on oscillation length and recalls a long-standing open problem on vibrations of a square membrane with fixed edge.

Abstract: A synthetic view is provided on scattered results which were never presented in this form in the literature. First we investigate precisely the oscillation length for periodic functions. Then, we establish the existence of a uniform oscillation lentgh for any scalar almost periodic function with mean-value 0. After that, we investigate upper bounds of the oscillation length for some almost periodic functions which appear in the study of continuum mechanics, and we show the existence of a finite oscillation length with respect to time in any open subset of some vibrating continuous media. The paper finally recalls a long standing open problem on the vibrations of a square membrane with fixed edge.

</details>


### [200] [Interior Regularity of Mixed Local-Nonlocal Parabolic Semilinear Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06537)
*Sergio Junquera,Leandro M. Del Pezzo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes existence, uniqueness, and regularity of classical solutions to a semilinear parabolic equation driven by a mixed local-nonlocal operator $\mathcal{L} = -(-\Delta)^s + \Delta$ with Dirichlet boundary conditions, showing solutions are Hölder continuous in space-time and $C^{2,\alpha}_{loc}$ in space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mixed local-nonlocal diffusion operators combine the classic Laplacian with the fractional Laplacian, an area of growing interest, but the well-posedness and regularity of classical solutions for the associated semilinear parabolic problem remain to be fully characterized.

Method: The authors prove existence, uniqueness, and regularity of classical solutions for the semilinear parabolic equation with operator $\mathcal{L} = -(-\Delta)^s + \Delta$ under Dirichlet boundary conditions, then analyze interior regularity of these solutions.

Result: The solutions are shown to be Hölder continuous in both space and time, and $C^{2,\alpha}_{loc}$ in space for all positive times.

Conclusion: Classical solutions to this mixed local-nonlocal semilinear parabolic problem exist uniquely and exhibit strong interior regularity, being space-time Hölder continuous and spatially $C^{2,\alpha}_{loc}$ for all $t>0$.

Abstract: In this paper we prove the existence, uniqueness and regularity of classical solutions \break of a semilinear parabolic equation with a mixed local and nonlocal diffusion operator \break $\mathcal{L} = -(-Δ)^s + Δ$ and Dirichlet boundary conditions. Here, $(-Δ)^s$ is the integral fractional laplacian and $Δ$ is the classic local laplacian. We then study the interior regularity of said solutions and conclude that they are Hölder continuous in both space and time, and they are $C^{2,α}_{loc}$ in space for all positive times.

</details>


### [201] [Finite-time blowup of the IPM equation from smooth initial data without forcing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06544)
*Fan Zheng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves finite-time gradient blow-up for the unforced incompressible porous media (IPM) equation on the 2D torus, removing the forcing required in prior IPM blow-up constructions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous IPM blow-up constructions (Alpöge–Buckmaster–Coiculescu; Córdoba–Martínez-Zoroa) relied on an external forcing term. This work aims to establish blow-up in the genuinely unforced IPM setting.

Method: A key identity links the logarithmic growth of a layer's gradient to the negative pressure curvature transverse to its transported phase. A nearly vertical parent layer rotates and amplifies a new phase, gaining exponentially in the reciprocal parent tilt. An asymmetric periodic profile bounds the pressure Hessian uniformly, preventing rapid gradient decay. Layers are seeded at time zero, with phase means chosen via a terminal displacement problem, and a finite-order expansion is corrected into an exact unforced solution. A scale induction then yields the result.

Result: Blow-up of two derivative components at the origin, with convergence to a terminal density in every C^η space for η < 1.

Conclusion: Finite-time gradient blow-up is established for the unforced 2D IPM equation, extending prior forced constructions to the unforced case.

Abstract: We prove finite-time gradient blow-up for the unforced incompressible porous media equation on the two-dimensional torus, removing the forcing used in the IPM blow-up constructions of Alpöge, Buckmaster, and Coiculescu \cite{lit:ABC} and Córdoba and Martínez-Zoroa \cite{lit:CMIPM}. The key is an identity that expresses the logarithmic growth of a layer's gradient as the negative pressure curvature transverse to its transported phase. A nearly vertical parent layer rotates and amplifies a new phase, with a gain exponential in the reciprocal parent tilt. An asymmetric periodic profile gives a uniform upper bound for the pressure Hessian, preventing rapid decay of the gradient magnitude. Each layer is seeded at time zero; its phase means are selected by a terminal displacement problem, and a finite order expansion is corrected to an exact unforced solution. The scale induction then shows blow-up of two derivative components at the origin and convergence to a terminal density in every $C^η$, $η<1$.

</details>


### [202] [Global $L^1$ Exponential Observer for the Quasilinear Diffusion Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06546)
*Mohamed Camil Belhadjoudja,Kirsten Morris*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A nonlinear boundary observer is designed for a 1D quasilinear diffusion equation with Neumann boundary conditions, achieving global exponential stability of the estimation error in the L1 norm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: State estimation for quasilinear diffusion equations is challenging due to nonlinear diffusivity and limited boundary measurements; the paper aims to design an observer using only the state at one spatial end as output.

Method: The observer is a copy of the system with a nonlinear (in the output) correction term applied at the boundary. A Lyapunov-based analysis is used to prove stability of the estimation error dynamics.

Result: Global exponential stability of the zero equilibrium of the estimation error dynamics is established in the L1 norm, for general nonlinear diffusivity lower-bounded by a positive constant (uniformly parabolic).

Conclusion: The proposed boundary nonlinear observer guarantees global exponential convergence for uniformly parabolic quasilinear diffusion equations with Neumann boundary conditions under single-end state measurement.

Abstract: A nonlinear observer is designed for a one-dimensional quasilinear diffusion equation with Neumann boundary conditions, using as an output the state at one end of the spatial domain. The observer consists of a copy of the system, with a nonlinear (in the output) correction term at the boundary. A Lyapunov-based analysis is performed to ensure global exponential stability of the zero equilibrium for the estimation error dynamics in the $L^1$ norm. The result holds for a general nonlinear diffusivity, under the assumption that it is lower-bounded by a positive constant, i.e., that the diffusion equation is uniformly parabolic.

</details>


### [203] [Low Mach number limit of the full Navier-Stokes equations with no-slip boundary conditions and large temperature variations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06567)
*Xiaoyu Gu,Song Jiang,Qiangchang Ju,Yaobin Ou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Rigorous justification of the low Mach number limit for the full compressible Navier-Stokes equations in a 3D smooth bounded domain with no-slip boundary and well-prepared data, establishing Mach-independent uniform strong solution estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the low Mach number limit of the full compressible Navier-Stokes equations under no-slip boundary conditions, where the vorticity boundary relation available in slip-boundary settings is unavailable.

Method: Constructs an energy functional in a Sobolev framework to obtain uniform estimates of strong solutions on a Mach-independent time interval, accounting for heat conduction and large temperature variations. Employs a divergence-free Stokes lifting of the boundary trace of the time derivative of modified velocity to define a test function vanishing on the boundary.

Result: Uniform estimates for strong solutions independent of the Mach number on a time interval independent of the Mach number.

Conclusion: The low Mach number limit is rigorously justified for the full compressible Navier-Stokes equations in a 3D smooth bounded domain with no-slip boundary and well-prepared initial data.

Abstract: In this paper, we rigorously justify the low Mach number limit of the full compressible Navier-Stokes equations in a three-dimensional smooth bounded domain, subject to the no-slip boundary condition for the velocity and well-prepared initial data. In the presence of heat conduction and large temperature variations, we establish the uniform estimates for strong solutions on a time interval independent of the Mach number by constructing an energy functional in a Sobolev framework. The no-slip boundary condition cannot provide the vorticity boundary relation used in the slip-boundary setting. A key ingredient is to employ a divergence-free Stokes lifting of the boundary trace of the time derivative of modified velocity to define a test function vanishing on the boundary.

</details>


### [204] [The $L^1$-realization of an elliptic operator with unbounded coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06659)
*Sallah Eddine Boutiah*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops an L^1-theory for elliptic operators with unbounded coefficients, extending prior semigroup generation results to the endpoint p=1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous semigroup generation results for these elliptic operators were established only for 1<p<∞; the endpoint case p=1 was left open.

Method: The authors study the L^1-realization of the operator, characterize the generator domain via weighted estimates, and compare it with the L^p-semigroups for 1<p<∞.

Result: The L^1-realization generates a positive analytic C_0-semigroup that is compact for every positive time; additionally consistency with the L^p-semigroups is proven.

Conclusion: The L^p-theory for elliptic operators with unbounded coefficients is completed by including the endpoint p=1.

Abstract: We establish an $L^1$-theory for a class of elliptic operators with unbounded coefficients, extending the semigroup generation results of Boutiah et al. (J. Differential Equations 264 (2018), 2184--2204) and Canale et al. (Ann. Sc. Norm. Super. Pisa Cl. Sci. (5) 16 (2016), 581--601) to the endpoint $p=1$. The $L^1$-realization generates a positive analytic $C_0$-semigroup that is compact for every positive time. We characterize the generator domain through weighted estimates and prove consistency with the corresponding $L^p$-semigroups for $1<p<\infty$.

</details>


### [205] [Finite-time blow-up for the real-valued defocusing energy-supercritical quintic NLW](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06678)
*Matthieu Cadiot,Alexandru D. Ionescu,Stan Palasek*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs a real-valued blow-up solution to the defocusing energy-supercritical quintic wave equation on R^{1+10}, via a discretely self-similar profile proven to exist with a computer-assisted Newton–Kantorovich argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The defocusing quintic wave equation on R^{1+10} is energy-supercritical, where blow-up phenomena are poorly understood. Blow-up examples for defocusing energy-supercritical NLW were largely unknown, motivating explicit real-valued constructions.

Method: Construct a discretely self-similar solution in a backward light cone of the form u(t,x)=(T-t)^{-1/2}W*(ω* log(1/(T-t)), |x|/(T-t)) with 2π-periodic, real-analytic profile. Existence of the profile is proved by a computer-assisted Newton-Kantorovich argument in a Banach algebra of Fourier-Chebyshev coefficients, using exact mode operators (hypergeometric, upper triangular in Chebyshev basis) and uniform high-frequency inverse bounds. Approximate profile found via non-rigorous numerical continuation from rotating self-similar profiles of the complex equation.

Result: A nonzero real-analytic profile W* exists; cutting off its data yields smooth, compactly supported radial data whose solution agrees with the self-similar solution in the backward light cone, is smooth on [0,T]×R^10 except at the vertex (T,0), and blows up at the self-similar rate. These appear to be the first real-valued blow-up solutions of defocusing energy-supercritical NLW.

Conclusion: The work establishes the first real-valued blow-up solutions for defocusing energy-supercritical nonlinear wave equations, with rigorous error control; all codes and approximate objects are publicly available on GitHub.

Abstract: We consider the defocusing quintic wave equation $\partial_t^2u-Δu+u^5=0$ for real-valued functions on $\mathbb{R}^{1+10}$, which is energy-supercritical. We construct a discretely self-similar solution in a backward light cone, of the form $$u(t,x)=(T-t)^{-1/2}W^*\big(ω^*\log\frac1{T-t},\frac{|x|}{T-t}\big),$$ where the nonzero profile $W^*$ is $2π$-periodic in its first variable and real-analytic up to and across the light cone. Cutting off its initial data gives smooth, compactly supported, radial data whose solution coincides with the discretely self-similar solution in the backward light cone, is smooth on $[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{10}$ except at the vertex $(T,0)$ of the cone, and blows up there at the self-similar rate. The solutions we construct here appear to be the first real-valued blow-up solutions of defocusing energy-supercritical NLW.
  Existence of the real profile is proved by a computer-assisted Newton--Kantorovich argument in a Banach algebra of Fourier--Chebyshev coefficients, with rigorous error control. We find the approximate profile by numerically continuing rotating self-similar profiles of the complex-valued equation. This step is not essential for the blow-up proof, so it is left as non-rigorous numerics. The main analytic ingredients are an exact description of the mode operators for the linear part, which are hypergeometric and upper triangular in the Chebyshev basis, and a bound for their inverses that is uniform at high frequencies. All computer-assisted proofs, including the requisite codes and the approximate objects, are accessible on GitHub at \cite{code}.

</details>


### [206] [Finite-time blowup for 2D unforced IPM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06712)
*Mimi Dai*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Constructs an odd smooth initial density for the unforced 2D incompressible porous media equation on T^2 that develops finite-time blowup, with certain derivatives blowing up while density and velocity stay bounded in L^2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Demonstrate finite-time blowup for the unforced IPM equation, extending the forced-IPM mechanism of Córdoba and Martínez-Zoroa to the unforced setting.

Method: Iterative angular amplification of localized oscillations with rapidly growing frequencies, starting from a stationary solution. Perturbations each constructed from time zero to set up the geometry for the next stage; a one-sided upper bound on the pressure Hessian (inspired by the OpenAI Euler construction) controls backward preparation and initial perturbations.

Result: An odd smooth initial density whose solution blows up in finite time: ∂_{x2}ρ(t,0) and ∂_{x1}u_1(t,0) tend to +∞ as the maximal smooth existence time is approached, while density and velocity remain bounded in L^2.

Conclusion: Finite-time blowup occurs for the unforced incompressible porous media equation, achieved by adapting a forced-IPM iteration scheme combined with a one-sided pressure Hessian bound for control.

Abstract: We construct an odd smooth initial density for the unforced incompressible porous media (IPM) equation on $\mathbb T^2$ whose solution develops a finite-time blowup. The components $\partial_{x_2}ρ(t,0)$ and $\partial_{x_1}u_1(t,0)$ tend to $+\infty$ as the maximal smooth existence time is approached, while the density and velocity remain bounded in $L^2$. Starting from a stationary solution, we iterate the angular amplification of localized oscillations with rapidly growing frequencies, adapting the mechanism of Córdoba and Martínez-Zoroa [7] for forced IPM. Each perturbation is constructed from time zero and creates the geometry configuration for the next iteration stage. A one-sided upper bound for the pressure Hessian is applied to control backward preparation and initial perturbations, following an idea from the Euler construction of OpenAI [14].

</details>


### [207] [Fractional Calderón problem with a potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06766)
*Ali Feizmohammadi,Katya Krupchyk,Gunther Uhlmann*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Local Cauchy data for a fractional Schrödinger equation on a closed Riemannian manifold uniquely determines the manifold, metric, and potential up to an isometry fixing the observation set.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Addresses the inverse problem of whether local boundary/observation data encodes the geometry and potential of a manifold for fractional Schrödinger equations, avoiding restrictive geometric assumptions.

Method: Uses functional calculus identities to recover the local action of the resolvent of the Laplace-Beltrami operator.

Result: Proves that knowledge of local Cauchy data determines the manifold, its metric, and a smooth complex-valued potential up to an isometry fixing the observation set, for any nonempty open observation subset.

Conclusion: The result establishes uniqueness/rigidity for fractional Schrödinger inverse problems on closed manifolds with minimal assumptions.

Abstract: We prove that the knowledge of local Cauchy data for a fractional Schrödinger equation on a smooth closed and connected Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two determines the manifold, its metric, and a smooth complex- valued potential, up to an isometry fixing the observation set. The observation set may be any nonempty open subset and no further geometric assumptions are required. The proof uses functional calculus identities to recover the local action of the resolvent of the Laplace-Beltrami operator.

</details>


### [208] [On the Dirichlet problem for the degenerate $2$-Hessian equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06853)
*Yasheng Lyu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global $C^{1,1}$ solvability for the Dirichlet problem of the degenerate 2-Hessian equation on strictly mean convex $C^{3,1}$ domains, resolving the $k=2$ case of a longstanding problem, and establishes global semiconvexity for general $k$-admissible solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The degenerate 2-Hessian equation's Dirichlet problem had a longstanding open solvability question (the $k=2$ case), and understanding global semiconvexity structure of admissible solutions was unclear.

Method: Establish a lower bound for $\sigma_{3}[D^{2}u]$ independent of $\inf_\Omega f$ to uncover a global semiconvexity structure; extend via analogous arguments to $3\leq k\leq n-1$ on the unit ball; construct a counterexample for sharpness.

Result: Global $C^{1,1}$ solvability holds for general $C^{3,1}$ boundary values and nonnegative $f\in C^{1,1}(\overline\Omega)$. Global semiconvexity with an $\inf f$-independent bound is obtained for $k$-admissible solutions on the unit ball. The $C^{3,1}$ boundary value assumption is shown sharp in the Hölder scale.

Conclusion: The $k=2$ case of the longstanding open problem is resolved, and the $C^{3,1}$ boundary regularity is optimal.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish global $C^{1,1}$ solvability of the Dirichlet problem for the degenerate $2$-Hessian equation on bounded strictly mean convex $C^{3,1}$ domains $Ω$, with general $C^{3,1}$ boundary values and nonnegative right-hand sides $f\in C^{1,1}(\overlineΩ)$, resolving the $k=2$ case of a longstanding open problem. We uncover a global semiconvexity structure for general $2$-admissible solutions by proving a lower bound for $σ_{3}[D^{2}u]$ independent of $\inf_Ωf$. For $3\leq k\leq n-1$, we also establish global semiconvexity for $k$-admissible solutions on the unit ball with $C^{3}$ boundary values and $f^{1/(k-1)}\in C^{1,1}(\overline{B}_{1})$, with a bound independent of $\inf_{B_{1}}f$. A counterexample shows that the $C^{3,1}$ boundary value assumption for global $C^{1,1}$ solvability is sharp in the Hölder scale.

</details>
