### [243] [GPU-Initiated Discrete Simulated Bifurcation: Low-Latency Requests and Streaming Dense Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06622)
*Yaocheng Chen*

Main category: cs.DC

TL;DR: A discrete simulated bifurcation (dSB) architecture built on NVIDIA DOCA GPUNetIO that keeps the receive-solve-reply path on-device, enabling low-latency concurrent serving of resident models and streaming of dense models exceeding GPU memory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: GPU-based optimization faces two communication bottlenecks: coordinating frequent requests and delivering dense models that exceed device memory. Existing socket-based servers incur CPU data-path overhead and latency under concurrent load.

Method: A persistent GPU service receives field updates, executes each solve within one thread block, and returns results, using exact integer coupling sums, GPU work queues, and batched transmission via DOCA GPUNetIO. For oversized models, a streaming solver retains dynamical state on GPU and reuses incoming coupling tiles across replicas.

Result: Under load from 400 to 800 thousand requests/s, median latency rises only 6%; at highest load, median and p99 are 189 and 218 μs vs 288 and 609 μs for a tuned CPU proxy. Streaming evaluates ten-million-variable dense binary matrices at ~307 Gb/s, consuming a 12.5-TB logical matrix through a 64-MiB packet buffer; ground-state recovery and reference agreement verify correctness.

Conclusion: The two modes together scale dSB to concurrent requests and dense models beyond GPU memory, overcoming both communication bottlenecks.

Abstract: GPU-based optimization faces two communication bottlenecks: coordinating frequent requests and delivering dense models that exceed device memory. We present a discrete simulated bifurcation (dSB) architecture that addresses both through NVIDIA DOCA GPUNetIO. For resident models, a persistent service receives field updates, executes each solve within one GPU thread block, and returns the result. Exact integer coupling sums, GPU work queues, and batched transmission keep the receive--solve--reply path on the device without a dedicated CPU data-path core. In comparisons with socket-based servers using the same solver, the largest latency gains occur under concurrent load. As the offered load increases from 400 to 800 thousand requests per second, median round-trip latency rises by only 6\%. At the highest tested load, median and 99th-percentile latencies are 189 and 218~$μ$s, compared with 288 and 609~$μ$s for the tuned persistent CPU proxy across repeated runs. For models larger than device memory, a streaming solver retains dynamical state on the GPU and reuses incoming coupling tiles across replicas. It evaluates ten-million-variable dense binary matrices at approximately 307~Gb/s, consuming a 12.5-TB logical matrix through a 64-MiB packet buffer. Ground-state recovery on planted instances and agreement with reference executions verify the computation. Together, the two modes scale dSB to concurrent requests and dense models beyond GPU memory.

</details>


<div id='astro-ph.IM'></div>

# astro-ph.IM [[Back]](#toc)

### [244] [Xenia: A Table-Driven Code for Astrophysical Multi-Fluid Hydrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04655)
*Lei Xing*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: Xenia is a table-driven, table-driven multi-fluid hydrodynamics code for escaping planetary atmospheres, using Python-declared species and physics compiled to symbolic tapes passed to shared C/Kokkos kernels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Coupled escape-atmosphere models need neutral and ion species with distinct velocities and thermal pressures coupled by photoionization, recombination, radiation, and collisions. Implementing such models is hindered when species definitions and stiff interactions are embedded in solver-specific kernels.

Method: Species, coupling channels, chemistry, heating/cooling, and radiation are declared in Python, compiled to symbolic tapes, and passed via a versioned interface to shared C/Kokkos kernels—changing a problem changes data, not kernel code. Electrons use a pressure-only quasi-neutral closure (density from ions, no mass/momentum advance). Stiff coupling is handled by operator splitting: radial radiation scan, hyperbolic transport, and cell-local implicit source solve, with symbolic tapes yielding an analytic finite-difference-free Jacobian for direct dense LU.

Result: The public v0.1 release is validated with acoustic convergence, dustybox drag relaxation, conservation and closure contracts, two-fluid cloud-shock coupling, curvilinear geometry bookkeeping, three escape chains, and 394 passing tests.

Conclusion: Xenia is released under GPLv2. The authors note limitations: conservation/positivity not matching mature production codes, radiation uses a radial Beer-Lambert prefix product rather than a general solver, escape chains are reproducibility checks not new science, and backend evidence is limited to functional deployment and equivalence fingerprints.

Abstract: Models of escaping planetary atmospheres require neutral and ion species with distinct velocities and thermal pressures, coupled by photoionization, recombination, radiation, and collisions. Implementing and extending such coupled models is often hindered when species definitions and stiff interactions are embedded in solver-specific kernels. We present Xenia, a table-driven code for astrophysical multi-fluid hydrodynamics designed to lower this barrier. Species, coupling channels, chemistry, heating and cooling, and radiation channels are declared in Python, compiled to symbolic tapes, and passed through a versioned interface to shared C/Kokkos kernels; changing a problem therefore changes data rather than kernel code. For ionized configurations, electrons provide a pressure-only quasi-neutral closure: electron density follows from ion populations, electron mass and momentum are not advanced, and only electron pressure is transported. Stiff coupling is treated by operator splitting into a radial radiation scan, hyperbolic transport, and a cell-local implicit source solve. The same symbolic tapes yield an analytic finite-difference-free Jacobian for a direct dense LU solve. The public v0.1 release is validated with acoustic convergence, dustybox drag relaxation, conservation and closure contracts, two-fluid cloud-shock coupling, curvilinear geometry bookkeeping, three escape chains, and 394 passing tests. Conservation and positivity are not claimed to match mature production codes; radiation uses a radial Beer-Lambert prefix product rather than a general solver; escape chains are reproducibility checks rather than new science predictions; and backend evidence is limited to functional deployment and equivalence fingerprints. Xenia is released under GPLv2 at https://github.com/YFG-tec/xenia.

</details>


<div id='math.PR'></div>

# math.PR [[Back]](#toc)

### [245] [A PDE approach to quantitative asymptotic stability of the optimal filter in the linear detectable setting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02850)
*Minh Van Hoang Nguyen,Sangmin Park*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper establishes quantitative asymptotic stability of the optimal nonlinear filter for linear-drift, additive-noise signal-observation models (non-Gaussian initial law), using the Zakai equation and a gauge transformation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Asymptotic stability of the optimal filter—whether a filter initialized with the wrong initial law converges to the correctly initialized one—is of fundamental practical and theoretical importance. The sharp condition (detectability) is known for the Gaussian/Kalman-Bucy case, but stability beyond Gaussian initial laws remained to be characterized.

Method: Studies the Zakai equation for the unnormalized filter. (1) Analyzes the asymptotic behavior of the deterministic part of the Zakai equation with general nonlinear drift, placing detectability in that context. (2) For linear drift, characterizes the Zakai solution via a gauge transformation separating finite-dimensional stochastic dynamics from infinite-dimensional deterministic dynamics. (3) Uses this representation to prove stability.

Result: Asymptotic exponential filter stability is established in the χ^p-divergence (w.r.t. a reference optimal filter), the total variation norm, and the 2-Wasserstein distance, under the detectability condition and non-Gaussian initial law.

Conclusion: Detectability yields asymptotic exponential filter stability for linear-drift models even without Gaussian initial laws, with the gauge-transformation representation providing quantitative convergence rates across multiple metrics.

Abstract: The filtering problem asks to estimate a signal process, the initial state of which is unknown, given its partial and noisy observations. From a probabilistic perspective, the best estimate at each time is the optimal filter, which is the conditional law of the signal given all observations up to the time. The problem of asymptotic stability of the optimal filter, which asks whether the filter initialized with the incorrect initial law converges to the correctly initialized filter, is of fundamental practical and theoretical importance.
  This paper studies the quantitative asymptotic stability of the optimal filter for signal-observation models with linear drift and additive Brownian noise. When the initial law is additionally assumed to be Gaussian, the optimal filter reduces to the celebrated Kalman-Bucy filter, for which the detectability condition on the signal and observation matrices is the sharp condition ensuring asymptotic stability. In the linear setting where the detectability condition holds but the initial law need not be Gaussian, we make the following contributions by studying the Zakai equation satisfied by the unnormalized filter. First, we study the asymptotic behavior of the deterministic part of the Zakai equation in the general case with nonlinear drift, and place the detectability condition in this context. Second, in the case of linear drift, we characterize the solution of the Zakai equation (hence the optimal filter) by a gauge transformation that separates the finite-dimensional stochastic dynamics from the infinite-dimensional deterministic dynamics. Third, we use this representation to establish asymptotic exponential filter stability in the $χ^p$-divergence (with respect to a reference optimal filter), the total variation norm, and the $2$-Wasserstein distance.

</details>


### [246] [A PDE approach to quantitative asymptotic stability of the optimal filter in the linear detectable setting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02850)
*Minh Van Hoang Nguyen,Sangmin Park*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper studies quantitative asymptotic stability of the optimal filter for signal-observation models with linear drift and additive Brownian noise, extending stability results beyond Gaussian initial laws.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The asymptotic stability of the optimal filter—whether a filter initialized with incorrect initial law converges to the correctly initialized one—is fundamental both practically and theoretically. While the Kalman-Bucy filter case (Gaussian initial law) is well understood via the detectability condition, stability remains less clear when the initial law is non-Gaussian.

Method: The authors analyze the Zakai equation for the unnormalized filter. They (1) study the asymptotic behavior of its deterministic part in the general nonlinear-drift case and place detectability in this context; (2) for linear drift, characterize the Zakai solution via a gauge transformation separating finite-dimensional stochastic dynamics from infinite-dimensional deterministic dynamics.

Result: Using this representation, they establish asymptotic exponential filter stability in the χ^p-divergence (relative to a reference optimal filter), the total variation norm, and the 2-Wasserstein distance.

Conclusion: Under the detectability condition, the optimal filter is exponentially stable asymptotically even when the initial law is non-Gaussian, with quantitative convergence guaranteed in several divergence and metric notions.

Abstract: The filtering problem asks to estimate a signal process, the initial state of which is unknown, given its partial and noisy observations. From a probabilistic perspective, the best estimate at each time is the optimal filter, which is the conditional law of the signal given all observations up to the time. The problem of asymptotic stability of the optimal filter, which asks whether the filter initialized with the incorrect initial law converges to the correctly initialized filter, is of fundamental practical and theoretical importance.
  This paper studies the quantitative asymptotic stability of the optimal filter for signal-observation models with linear drift and additive Brownian noise. When the initial law is additionally assumed to be Gaussian, the optimal filter reduces to the celebrated Kalman-Bucy filter, for which the detectability condition on the signal and observation matrices is the sharp condition ensuring asymptotic stability. In the linear setting where the detectability condition holds but the initial law need not be Gaussian, we make the following contributions by studying the Zakai equation satisfied by the unnormalized filter. First, we study the asymptotic behavior of the deterministic part of the Zakai equation in the general case with nonlinear drift, and place the detectability condition in this context. Second, in the case of linear drift, we characterize the solution of the Zakai equation (hence the optimal filter) by a gauge transformation that separates the finite-dimensional stochastic dynamics from the infinite-dimensional deterministic dynamics. Third, we use this representation to establish asymptotic exponential filter stability in the $χ^p$-divergence (with respect to a reference optimal filter), the total variation norm, and the $2$-Wasserstein distance.

</details>


### [247] [Stochastic heat equations with non-Lipschitz coefficients driven by symmetric $α$-stable Lévy white noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04895)
*Juan J. Jiménez*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper constructs weak solutions with càdlàg paths in negative Sobolev spaces for the stochastic heat equation driven by symmetric α-stable space-time Lévy noise on a bounded interval with zero Dirichlet boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To handle the stochastic heat equation with heavy-tailed (α-stable) Lévy noise, where solutions are not function-valued and classical frameworks fail, requiring a distribution-valued (negative Sobolev) setting.

Method: Consider the equation on a bounded interval with zero Dirichlet boundary conditions and symmetric α-stable space-time Lévy white noise (1<α<2), with continuous drift and noise coefficients of at most linear growth. For bounded deterministic initial data and any r>1/2, construct a weak solution with càdlàg paths in H_{-r}, and a predictable mild solution on the same stochastic basis with the same noise.

Result: Existence of a weak solution with càdlàg paths in H_{-r} for every r>1/2; existence of a predictable mild solution on the same stochastic basis and noise; at each fixed deterministic time, the mild solution is a density of the distribution-valued weak solution.

Conclusion: The stochastic heat equation with linear-growth coefficients driven by α-stable Lévy noise admits weak solutions in negative Sobolev spaces, and these are linked to a predictable mild solution that represents their densities at fixed times.

Abstract: We study the stochastic heat equation on a bounded interval with zero Dirichlet boundary conditions, driven by symmetric $α$-stable space--time Lévy white noise, where $1<α<2$. Both the drift and noise coefficients are continuous and have at most linear growth. For bounded deterministic initial data and every $r>1/2$, we construct a weak solution with càdlàg paths in the negative Sobolev space $\mathbb{H}_{-r}$. We also construct a predictable mild solution on the same stochastic basis and with the same noise. At each fixed deterministic time, this mild solution is a density of the distribution-valued weak solution.

</details>


### [248] [Stochastic PDE construction of some non-local or non-translation-invariant Anderson operators in the full subcritical regime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05391)
*Léonard Ferdinand,Samaël Mackowiak,Aurélien Minguella*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper constructs Anderson-type operators in the full subcritical regime via an SPDE (flow) approach, covering fractional Laplacian perturbations on the torus and full space, and harmonic oscillator perturbations on the full space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the construction of Anderson-type operators to the full subcritical regime, including non-translation-invariant operators, which requires new renormalisation techniques.

Method: A stochastic PDE argument based on Duch's flow approach. Optimal estimates on the difference between the solution and the deterministic heat flow are derived. Renormalisation with constants is handled by a general argument for the non-translation-invariant case. Tools: Hille-Yosida theorem (fractional Laplacian on torus, harmonic oscillator) and Klein-Landau theorem (fractional Laplacian on full space).

Result: Optimal estimates on the difference between the solution and the deterministic heat flow, yielding a Weyl law for the fractional case on the torus and for the harmonic oscillator case.

Conclusion: Anderson-type operators are constructed in the full subcritical regime, with Weyl laws established in the relevant cases and a general renormalisation argument developed for non-translation-invariant operators.

Abstract: We construct Anderson-type operators in the full subcritical regime via a SPDE argument. Our work encompasses perturbations by a Gaussian noise of the fractional Laplacian on the torus or on the full space, and of the harmonic oscillator on the full space. In every case, we are able to derive an optimal estimate on the difference between the solution and the deterministic heat flow, which allows us to get a Weyl law for the fractional case on the torus and for the harmonic oscillator case, and so in the full subcritical regime. For the non-translation-invariant operator, the main novelty is that we develop a general argument in order to prove how to renormalise the operator with some constants. The SPDE argument uses the flow approach introduced by Duch. To construct the operators, we use the Hille-Yosida theorem for the fractional Laplacian on the torus and the harmonic oscillator, and the Klein-Landau theorem for the fractional Laplacian in the full space.

</details>


### [249] [Geometric and stochastic framework for a regularized $p(x)$-Laplacian](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05609)
*Mustafa Avci*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper constructs an Itô diffusion whose generator reproduces the regularized p(x)-Laplacian on a prescribed profile, and establishes its analytic and probabilistic properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect the regularized p(x)-Laplacian (a nonlinear PDE operator with variable exponent) to an underlying stochastic diffusion process, enabling probabilistic analysis of profile reproduction.

Method: Constructs an Itô diffusion via a full divergence expansion, with covariance equal to twice the gradient derivative of the regularized flux and a logarithmic drift from spatial variation of p(x). Uses classical regularity assumptions, tangent spectra analysis, and ellipticity bounds on bounded gradient ranges.

Result: Derives exact tangent spectra of unregularized and regularized fluxes, characterizes behavior at zero gradient, establishes quantitative ellipticity bounds, proves boundedness and local Lipschitz continuity, strong existence and pathwise uniqueness up to exit, identifies the interior generator of the boundary-stopped process, and verifies the profile-reproduction identity.

Conclusion: The construction yields initial-point stability, localized martingale identities, and Dynkin's formula; the coefficient selection is canonical under flux-based prescriptions but not uniquely determined by profile reproduction alone.

Abstract: For a prescribed classical profile and a fixed positive regularization parameter, we construct an Itô diffusion whose interior generator reproduces the regularized $p(x)$-Laplacian when applied to that profile. We determine the exact tangent spectra of the unregularized and regularized fluxes, characterize their behavior at zero gradient, and establish quantitative ellipticity bounds on bounded gradient ranges. The full divergence expansion identifies a covariance equal to twice the gradient derivative of the regularized flux, evaluated at the profile gradient, and a logarithmic drift generated by spatial variation of the variable exponent $p(x)$. Under explicit classical regularity assumptions, we establish boundedness and local Lipschitz continuity of the coefficients and prove strong existence and pathwise uniqueness up to domain exit. We identify the interior generator of the boundary-stopped process and verify the profile-reproduction identity. Further consequences include initial-point stability for a fixed global extension, localized martingale identities, and Dynkin's formula at bounded stopping times. An explicit nonconstant exponent example illustrates the construction at a critical point, and the coefficient selection is shown to be canonical under the flux-based prescriptions rather than determined uniquely by profile reproduction alone.

</details>


### [250] [Large time behavior of Lévy processes and their nonlocal Schrödinger semigroups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05698)
*Mateusz Kwaśnicki,Phanuel Mariano,Hugo Panzo,Jing Wang*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper establishes the exact exponential decay rate of survival probabilities for symmetric Lévy processes on unbounded open sets, expressed via the bottom of the spectrum of the associated nonlocal Schrödinger operator, with quantitative upper bounds and a new probabilistic proof linking spectral and pointwise decay.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Survival probabilities and their asymptotics for Lévy processes on unbounded domains are important in probability and analysis, but a direct connection between the spectral properties of nonlocal Schrödinger operators and pointwise decay of survival probabilities was previously unknown for jump processes. This paper aims to fill that gap.

Method: The proof is probabilistic. It decomposes the Lévy process into a finite-range jump process (capturing small jumps) and an independent compound Poisson process (describing large jumps), then analyzes the Feynman-Kac semigroups to relate the survival probability decay to the bottom of the spectrum of the nonlocal Schrödinger operator.

Result: The main result proves the exact exponential asymptotic decay rate for the survival probability in terms of the bottom of the spectrum of the associated nonlocal Schrödinger operator, along with quantitative upper bounds that include an explicit polynomial correction.

Conclusion: The approach provides a direct link between spectral properties of nonlocal Schrödinger operators and pointwise decay of survival probabilities, a connection previously unknown for jump processes, thereby advancing the understanding of large-time asymptotics for symmetric Lévy processes on unbounded open sets.

Abstract: We study the large time asymptotics of the Feynman-Kac semigroups of the symmetric Lévy process on unbounded open sets. Our main result proves the exact exponential asymptotic decay rate for the survival probability given in terms of the bottom of the spectrum of the associated nonlocal Schrödinger operator. We also prove quantitative upper bounds with an explicit polynomial correction. The proof is probabilistic and is done by decomposing the Lévy process into a finite-range jump process collecting the small jumps and an independent compound Poisson process describing the large jumps. Our approach gives a direct link between the spectral properties of nonlocal Schrödinger operators and pointwise decay of survival probabilities, which was previously unknown for jump processes.

</details>


### [251] [Spectral gap for the stochastic primitive equations under degenerate Brownian forcing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05814)
*Quyuan Lin,Rongchang Liu,Kening Lu*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: Weighted Wasserstein spectral gap for 3D primitive equations under finite-rank additive noise; four forcing directions suffice (and are optimal), yielding unique ergodicity and exponential H1 mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard methods fail due to weak moment bounds and highly degenerate (finite-rank) noise. Need a new coupling criterion to handle this regime.

Method: Introduce a quantitative stable–compact asymptotic coupling criterion; apply a quantitative infinite-dimensional Hörmander analysis tracking degree and coefficient cost of Lie-bracket polynomials in Fourier frequency. Noise is phase-complete finite Fourier with quadratic saturation.

Result: Establishes weighted Wasserstein spectral gap on E = H1 ∩ L8 with ∂z u ∈ L4; four real forcing directions suffice and this is optimal in the phase-complete class.

Conclusion: System is uniquely ergodic and exponentially mixing in H1 topology.

Abstract: We prove a weighted Wasserstein spectral gap for the three-dimensional primitive equations on $\mathscr E=\{u\in H^1\cap L^8: \partial_zu\in L^4\}$ under finite-rank additive Brownian forcing that is phase-complete finite Fourier and satisfies a quantitative quadratic saturation condition. In particular, four real forcing directions suffice and this number is optimal within the phase-complete class. Consequently, the system is uniquely ergodic and exponentially mixing in the $H^1$ topology.
  The main difficulties stem from the lack of sufficiently strong moment bounds and the highly degenerate nature of the noise, placing the problem outside the direct reach of standard methods. We introduce a new quantitative stable--compact asymptotic coupling criterion that captures the delicate balance between the available control of the growth of initial perturbations and the accuracy--cost of compensating them by perturbing the noise. For the primitive equations, this leads to a quantitative infinite-dimensional Hörmander analysis requiring tracking the degree and coefficient cost of Lie-bracket polynomials with the Fourier frequency.

</details>


### [252] [A Class of Stochastic Partial Differential Equations with Jumps in Fluid Dynamics: Large and Moderate Deviation Asymptotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05837)
*Arnab Ganguly,Padmanabhan Sundar*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper establishes large and moderate deviation principles for nonlinear SPDEs driven by multiplicative Poisson random measures in a small-noise regime, using local monotonicity and the weak convergence approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large and moderate deviations for nonlinear SPDEs under jump noise are not well understood, especially for models arising in fluid dynamics and turbulence (e.g., Navier–Stokes, MHD, shell models). The goal is a general framework covering these important models.

Method: An abstract framework for locally monotone evolution equations over a Hilbert triple. Well-posedness of the associated controlled equations is established via the method of local monotonicity. Large and moderate deviation principles are then proved via the weak convergence approach.

Result: Large and moderate deviation principles are established for this broad class of nonlinear SPDEs driven by multiplicative Poisson random measures.

Conclusion: The abstract framework encompasses key models in fluid dynamics and turbulence, including 2D Navier–Stokes, MHD, the GOY shell model of turbulence, the 2D tidal equations, and Navier–Stokes equations with nonlinear viscosities.

Abstract: We establish large and moderate deviation principles for a broad class of nonlinear stochastic partial differential equations driven by multiplicative Poisson random measures in the small-noise regime. Our abstract framework is formulated for locally monotone evolution equations over a Hilbert triple and encompasses several important models arising in fluid dynamics and turbulence theory, including the two-dimensional Navier--Stokes equations, magnetohydrodynamics (MHD), the GOY shell model of turbulence, the two-dimensional tidal equations, and Navier--Stokes equations with nonlinear viscosities. A key analytical ingredient is the well-posedness of the associated controlled equations, established using the method of local monotonicity. The large and moderate deviation principles are then proved via the weak convergence approach.

</details>


### [253] [W-BSDEs with Common Noise: Well-posedness, Characterization, and Consequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06593)
*Mao Fabrice Djete,Mattia Martini*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper introduces W-BSDEs (backward SDEs on Wasserstein space) with common noise, reduces well-posedness to a characteristic equation, and derives applications to McKean-Vlasov control, mean field games, PDE representation, and mean-field limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent results on Wasserstein-space BSDEs are extended to the common noise setting, enabling probabilistic constructions of functionals on the space of probability measures and their intrinsic gradients, with applications beyond convexity or separability assumptions.

Method: The W-BSDE is formulated in Markovian form along admissible flows of conditional laws from McKean-Vlasov dynamics with common noise. Solutions are pairs (Y,Z), with Y a functional on probability measures and Z its intrinsic Wasserstein gradient. Well-posedness is reduced to a characteristic equation for a field p; when solvable, a W-BSDE solution is constructed and Z identified with p and the intrinsic gradient of Y.

Result: Existence for the W-BSDE; probabilistic construction of a Wasserstein-space functional and its intrinsic gradient; verification principles for McKean-Vlasov control and mean field games with common noise without convexity/separability; dynamic representation of the characteristic field via a McKean-Vlasov FBSDE; Y solves the associated semilinear PDE on probability measures in viscosity sense (classical under smoothness); W-BSDEs arise as intrinsic mean-field limits of finite-dimensional BSDEs on empirical measures, with convergence of value and rescaled martingale integrands.

Conclusion: The W-BSDE framework unifies existence, PDE representation, control/game verification, and mean-field limiting results in the common noise setting.

Abstract: We study a notion of backward stochastic differential equation on the Wasserstein space, called a W-BSDE. This work extends to the common noise setting recent results of the first author. The equation is formulated in Markovian form along admissible flows of conditional laws generated by McKean-Vlasov dynamics with common noise. A solution is a pair of fields $(Y,Z)$, where $Y$ is a functional on the space of probability measures and $Z$ is the corresponding intrinsic Wasserstein gradient.
  Our main result shows that the well-posedness of the W-BSDE can be reduced to a characteristic equation for a field $p$. Whenever this equation admits a suitable solution, we construct a W-BSDE solution and identify $Z$ with both the characteristic field $p$ and the intrinsic gradient of $Y$. This gives an existence result for the W-BSDE and, at the same time, a probabilistic construction of a functional on the Wasserstein space together with its intrinsic gradient.
  We then develop several consequences of the theory. We obtain verification principles for McKean-Vlasov control problems and for mean field games with common noise, without relying on convexity or separability assumptions. We also show that the characteristic field admits a dynamic representation through a McKean-Vlasov forward-backward SDE. The first component $Y$ solves the associated semilinear PDE on the space of probability measures in the viscosity sense, including the second-order term induced by common noise. Under additional smoothness, $Y$ is a classical solution. Finally, we prove that W-BSDEs arise as intrinsic mean-field limits of classical finite-dimensional BSDEs written on empirical measures, with convergence of both the value component and the rescaled martingale integrands.

</details>


<div id='math.SP'></div>

# math.SP [[Back]](#toc)

### [254] [Planar Dirichlet eigenvalue bounds from a trace identity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05636)
*Romain Speciel*

Main category: math.SP

TL;DR: Proves an asymptotically sharp lower bound for Dirichlet Laplacian eigenvalues on planar bounded open sets, achieving two-thirds of Pólya's conjectured bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Pólya's conjecture gives a natural lower bound for Dirichlet Laplacian eigenvalues in terms of area, but it remains open; the paper aims to make progress toward it.

Method: The proof uses a trace identity for the commutator between a certain multiplication operator and its adjoint.

Result: An asymptotically sharp lower bound is established, yielding (2/3)·4πk/A ≤ λ_k for every k, i.e., two thirds of Pólya's conjectured bound.

Conclusion: The work provides a rigorous lower bound approaching Pólya's conjecture up to a factor of 2/3 for planar Dirichlet eigenvalues.

Abstract: We prove an asymptotically sharp lower bound for the eigenvalues $λ_k$ of the Dirichlet Laplacian on bounded open sets in the plane with area $A>0$. The proof employs a trace identity for the commutator between a certain multiplication operator and its adjoint. As a corollary, we obtain $(2/3)\cdot 4πk/A\leq λ_k$ for every $k$, which is two thirds of the bound conjectured by Pólya.

</details>


<div id='math.LO'></div>

# math.LO [[Back]](#toc)

### [255] [Uniform Computability of Isotypic Block Diagonalization for Finite-Group Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02547)
*Konrad Burnik*

Main category: math.LO

TL;DR: The paper studies universal isotypic block-diagonalization in the Type-2 theory of effectivity (TTE), proving a uniform computability result for the universal block diagonalizer for commutants of finite group representations, and applies it to blockwise QR factorization in the isotypic basis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper is motivated by the need to understand computability (in the TTE sense) of isotypic block-diagonalization, particularly for commutants of finite group representations, and to exploit this for structured matrix factorizations such as QR in the isotypic basis.

Method: The authors work within the Type-2 theory of effectivity (TTE) and obtain a uniform computability result for the universal block diagonalizer for commutants of given finite group representations. For orthogonal representations, they compute ordinary QR factorizations blockwise in the isotypic basis, then transform back to the original coordinates.

Result: They achieve a uniform computability result for the universal block diagonalizer. For orthogonal representations, transforming back the blockwise QR yields a factorization A = QX where Q is orthogonal and X is generally not upper triangular. Additionally, with a Wedderburn-adapted orthogonal basis, the transformed-back factors can be computed within the commutant.

Conclusion: Isotypic block-diagonalization for commutants of finite group representations is uniformly computable in TTE, enabling blockwise QR computation in the isotypic basis; the resulting factorization A = QX has orthogonal Q but generally non-triangular X, and under a Wedderburn-adapted orthogonal basis the factors can be computed within the commutant.

Abstract: We study the universal isotypic block-diagonalization problem in the Type-2 theory of effectivity (TTE). We obtain a uniform computability result for the universal block diagonalizer for commutants of given finite group representations. As an application, when the given representation is orthogonal, we compute ordinary $QR$ factorizations blockwise in the isotypic basis. After transforming back to the original coordinates this yields a factorization $A=QX$, where $Q$ is orthogonal and $X$ is generally not upper triangular. Moreover, given a Wedderburn-adapted orthogonal basis, the transformed-back factors can be computed within the commutant.

</details>


### [256] [Uniform Computability of Isotypic Block Diagonalization for Finite-Group Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02547)
*Konrad Burnik*

Main category: math.LO

TL;DR: The paper studies universal isotypic block-diagonalization in Type-2 effectivity (TTE) and provides a uniform computability result for the universal block diagonalizer of commutants of finite group representations, with applications to blockwise QR factorization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish computability (in the sense of TTE) for the universal isotypic block-diagonalization of commutants of finite group representations, and to exploit this for structured matrix factorizations, such as blockwise QR in the isotypic basis.

Method: Use Type-2 theory of effectivity (TTE) to derive a uniform computability result for a universal block diagonalizer acting on commutants of given finite group representations; for orthogonal representations, compute ordinary QR factorizations blockwise in the isotypic basis, then transform back to original coordinates.

Result: A uniform computability result for the universal block diagonalizer. For orthogonal representations, this yields a factorization A = QX, where Q is orthogonal and X is generally not upper triangular. With a Wedderburn-adapted orthogonal basis, the transformed-back factors can be computed within the commutant.

Conclusion: Isotypic block-diagonalization is uniformly computable in TTE, and this enables structured QR-type factorizations respecting the commutant, giving factors A = QX computable within the commutant under a Wedderburn-adapted orthogonal basis.

Abstract: We study the universal isotypic block-diagonalization problem in the Type-2 theory of effectivity (TTE). We obtain a uniform computability result for the universal block diagonalizer for commutants of given finite group representations. As an application, when the given representation is orthogonal, we compute ordinary $QR$ factorizations blockwise in the isotypic basis. After transforming back to the original coordinates this yields a factorization $A=QX$, where $Q$ is orthogonal and $X$ is generally not upper triangular. Moreover, given a Wedderburn-adapted orthogonal basis, the transformed-back factors can be computed within the commutant.

</details>


<div id='math.ST'></div>

# math.ST [[Back]](#toc)

### [257] [Bayesian Operator Learning: Posterior Existence and Convergence of Point Estimates for Gaussian Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03666)
*Niklas Reinhardt,Jakob Zech*

Main category: math.ST

TL;DR: This paper develops a Bayesian framework for learning nonlinear operators between infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces, establishing posterior existence, convergence rates, and minimax optimality, with an application to the Darcy flow solution operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Learning operators between infinite-dimensional spaces (operator learning) is important for PDEs and scientific computing, but existing work largely lacks a rigorous Bayesian statistical treatment, including posterior well-definedness, convergence rates, and optimality guarantees.

Method: The authors formulate operator learning as recovering a map g_0 between separable Hilbert spaces from n noisy input-output pairs with Gaussian white noise. They place operator-valued priors on continuous operators, prove existence of the posterior as a regular conditional distribution, characterize its Radon-Nikodym derivative, and for Gaussian priors derive convergence rates for the posterior mean (a ridge-regularized kernel estimator). Minimax optimality is analyzed over hyperrectangles.

Result: The posterior exists as a regular conditional distribution with a characterized Radon-Nikodym derivative. For Gaussian priors, the posterior mean achieves algebraic convergence rates in n, matching a ridge-regularized kernel estimator, and is minimax optimal up to logarithmic factors when prior smoothness matches the ground truth. Explicit learning rates are derived for the Darcy flow solution operator.

Conclusion: The work provides a rigorous Bayesian foundation for operator learning, with well-defined posteriors, rate-optimal Gaussian-prior estimators, and concrete rates for a PDE solution operator, bridging Bayesian nonparametrics and operator learning.

Abstract: We develop a Bayesian framework for learning nonlinear operators between infinite-dimensional spaces. Given a map $g_0:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}$ between separable Hilbert spaces, we study the recovery of $g_0$ from $n\in\mathbb{N}$ noisy input-output pairs $(\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{Z})=(X_i,Z_i)_{i=1}^n$ with $Z_i= g_0 (X_i ) + E_i$. Here the $X_i\in\mathcal{X}$ are randomly drawn 'design' points in a compact subset of $\mathcal X$, and the $E_i$ are assumed to be i.i.d. draws from a Gaussian white noise process indexed by $\mathcal{Y}$. For any 'operator-valued' prior $\mathbb{P}_G$ supported on the space of continuous operators, we show existence of the posterior $\mathbb P_{G|(\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{Z})}$ as a regular conditional distribution, and provide a characterization of its Radon-Nikodym derivative. For Gaussian priors, we establish algebraic (in the sample size $n$) convergence rates for the posterior mean towards the ground truth; this corresponds to a ridge regularized kernel estimator. Moreover, we show that the posterior mean is minimax optimal (up to logarithmic factors) over hyperrectangles when the smoothness of the prior matches that of the ground truth. To illustrate the applicability of our analysis, we derive explicit learning rates for the Darcy flow solution operator.

</details>


### [258] [Bayesian Operator Learning: Posterior Existence and Convergence of Point Estimates for Gaussian Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03666)
*Niklas Reinhardt,Jakob Zech*

Main category: math.ST

TL;DR: A Bayesian framework for learning nonlinear operators between infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces, with posterior characterization and convergence rates for Gaussian priors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Learning operators between infinite-dimensional spaces from noisy input-output pairs is central to scientific computing and operator learning, but a rigorous Bayesian statistical theory for such problems is lacking.

Method: The authors define an operator-valued prior on continuous operators, prove existence of the posterior as a regular conditional distribution, and characterize its Radon-Nikodym derivative. For Gaussian priors, they derive convergence rates for the posterior mean, relating it to a ridge-regularized kernel estimator and establishing minimax optimality.

Result: Existence of the posterior is shown for general operator-valued priors. For Gaussian priors, algebraic convergence rates in sample size n are established for the posterior mean toward the ground truth, and minimax optimality (up to logarithmic factors) is proven over hyperrectangles when prior smoothness matches the truth. Explicit rates are derived for the Darcy flow solution operator.

Conclusion: The work provides a rigorous Bayesian theory for nonlinear operator learning, with posterior guarantees and near-optimal rates, demonstrated on the Darcy flow operator.

Abstract: We develop a Bayesian framework for learning nonlinear operators between infinite-dimensional spaces. Given a map $g_0:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}$ between separable Hilbert spaces, we study the recovery of $g_0$ from $n\in\mathbb{N}$ noisy input-output pairs $(\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{Z})=(X_i,Z_i)_{i=1}^n$ with $Z_i= g_0 (X_i ) + E_i$. Here the $X_i\in\mathcal{X}$ are randomly drawn 'design' points in a compact subset of $\mathcal X$, and the $E_i$ are assumed to be i.i.d. draws from a Gaussian white noise process indexed by $\mathcal{Y}$. For any 'operator-valued' prior $\mathbb{P}_G$ supported on the space of continuous operators, we show existence of the posterior $\mathbb P_{G|(\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{Z})}$ as a regular conditional distribution, and provide a characterization of its Radon-Nikodym derivative. For Gaussian priors, we establish algebraic (in the sample size $n$) convergence rates for the posterior mean towards the ground truth; this corresponds to a ridge regularized kernel estimator. Moreover, we show that the posterior mean is minimax optimal (up to logarithmic factors) over hyperrectangles when the smoothness of the prior matches that of the ground truth. To illustrate the applicability of our analysis, we derive explicit learning rates for the Darcy flow solution operator.

</details>


### [259] [Sharp dimensional analysis of midpoint methods for Langevin sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06308)
*Fan Chen,Sinho Chewi,Jianfeng Lu,Matthew S. Zhang*

Main category: math.ST

TL;DR: The paper analyzes deterministic (Heun) and randomized midpoint discretizations of Langevin dynamics, showing improved gradient query complexity bounds via Malliavin calculus and proving deterministic methods can outperform randomized ones in high dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous upper bounds for Langevin discretizations relied on standard coupling techniques, leaving room to improve complexity guarantees and to clarify the relative power of deterministic versus randomized midpoint methods.

Method: Deterministic Heun and underdamped exponential midpoint discretizations are analyzed for π ∝ e^{-V} with αI ⪯ ∇²V ⪯ βI and κ = β/α. Proofs exploit stationarity cancellation and smoothing via Malliavin calculus; matching lower bounds and Gaussian-calculation lower bounds for randomized/Poisson midpoints are established.

Result: Deterministic Heun achieves Õ(κ^{4/3} d^{1/3} ε^{-2/3}) gradient queries; underdamped exponential midpoint achieves Õ(κ^{5/4} d^{1/4} ε^{-1/2}) for √α W₂ ≤ ε. At bounded κ, lower bounds match the d and ε powers of both deterministic methods. Randomized and Poisson midpoint methods (overdamped/underdamped) have lower bound d^{1/3} ε^{-1/3} despite benign initialization.

Conclusion: In high dimensions, deterministic discretizations can provably outperform their randomized counterparts for sampling from strongly log-concave targets, contradicting the usual intuition that randomization helps.

Abstract: We study deterministic and randomized midpoint discretizations of Langevin dynamics for a target $π\propto e^{-V}$, where $0 \prec αI\preceq\nabla^2V\preceqβI$ and $κ=β/α$. To achieve $\sqrtα\,W_2\leqslant\varepsilon$, we show that deterministic Heun uses at most $\widetilde O(κ^{4/3}d^{1/3}\varepsilon^{-2/3})$ gradient queries, and underdamped exponential midpoint uses $\widetilde O(κ^{5/4}d^{1/4}\varepsilon^{-1/2})$. The proofs exploit cancellation at stationarity and smoothing using techniques from Malliavin calculus, outperforming previous upper bounds based on standard couplings. At bounded condition number, a lower bound matches the $d$ and $\varepsilon$ powers of both deterministic methods. To contrast, for the randomized midpoint methods and Poisson midpoint with at least two grid points (both overdamped and underdamped variants), a simple Gaussian calculation yields a lower bound $d^{1/3}\varepsilon^{-1/3}$ to get an $\varepsilon$-close sample despite starting at a benign initialization. This shows surprisingly that in high dimensions, deterministic discretizations can outperform their random counterparts.

</details>


<div id='cs.DS'></div>

# cs.DS [[Back]](#toc)

### [260] [Near-Exact Computation of Independent Chip Model Placement Probabilities for Thousands of Players](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06028)
*Wataru Inariba*

Main category: cs.DS

TL;DR: DE-ICM is a deterministic O(Mn²) algorithm for computing Independent Chip Model placement probabilities and prize equities, achieving near double-precision accuracy and fast runtime even for large poker tournament fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Exact ICM computation is considered intractable for large fields because its definition considers all possible finishing orders. This motivates a faster, accurate deterministic method.

Method: DE-ICM evaluates a classical integral representation where, conditional on a player's exponential clock, the number of players ahead is Poisson-binomial. It uses double-exponential quadrature on a single grid with closed-form truncation error bounds and field-size-dependent step size, plus a numerically stable deconvolution recovering leave-one-out coefficients in O(n) time from a shared product.

Result: On exactly solvable instances up to 4,000 players, worst relative equity error is 4.0e-14 and worst absolute placement-probability error 1.1e-14. The full placement matrix for 1,000 players takes 0.2 seconds on one CPU core.

Conclusion: DE-ICM provides a practical deterministic solution to large-scale ICM computation, achieving near double-precision accuracy with a full placement matrix for 1,000 players in 0.2 seconds on a single CPU core.

Abstract: The Independent Chip Model (ICM) converts the chip stacks of the players remaining in a poker tournament into finishing-place probabilities and prize equities. It is the standard model in tournament solvers, but its definition considers all possible finishing orders, and exact computation has been regarded as intractable for large fields. The same mathematics appears in other fields; for instance, the ICM is a Plackett-Luce ranking model with stacks as weights. We present DE-ICM, a deterministic algorithm that computes the placement probabilities of all $n$ players for all paid places in $O(M n^{2})$ time, where the number $M$ of quadrature nodes is fixed for the admissible inputs and the target accuracy. Its numerical scheme allows the target to be set close to the accuracy of double-precision arithmetic. The algorithm evaluates a classical integral representation in which, conditional on a player's exponential clock, the number of players ahead is Poisson-binomial. A double-exponential quadrature on a single grid evaluates every integral; its truncation errors have closed-form bounds, and the step size, which controls the discretization error, follows from the field size. A numerically stable deconvolution then recovers each player's leave-one-out coefficients in $O(n)$ time from one shared product. On exactly solvable instances with up to 4,000 players, the worst observed relative error of an equity is $4.0 \times 10^{-14}$ and the worst absolute error of a placement probability $1.1 \times 10^{-14}$. The full placement matrix for 1,000 players takes 0.2 seconds on one CPU core.

</details>


<div id='cs.CE'></div>

# cs.CE [[Back]](#toc)

### [261] [A Generalized Source Integral Equation for Homogeneous Penetrable Scatterers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02223)
*Boris Diner,Yaniv Brick*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: This paper extends the generalized source integral equation (GSIE) approach to improve low-rank compressibility of moment matrices for convex homogeneous penetrable scatterers, formulated for TM scattering and validated with superior compressibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional surface integral equation (SIE) formulations for penetrable scatterers yield moment matrices that are not readily low-rank compressible, limiting the efficiency of hierarchical matrix methods. The paper aims to enhance compressibility of these moment matrices.

Method: An indirect SIE formulation is proposed where the scattered field is represented by a single distribution of generalized sources that radiate only weakly into the scatterer, while interior fields use a separate distribution of conventional sources. The generalized kernel reduces the effective dimensionality of wave interactions between surface subdomains, making exterior integral operator moment matrix blocks exhibit enhanced rank deficiency. Formulated for the transverse magnetic (TM) scattering problem.

Result: For lossy scatterers, the low-rank (LR) compressibility of hierarchical matrix structures is secured for the full system. For scatterers in a much slower background medium, significant savings with very delayed asymptotic scaling of costs are achieved. Superior compressibility is demonstrated and validated on representative examples.

Conclusion: The extended GSIE enhances moment-matrix compressibility for essentially-convex homogeneous penetrable scatterers, enabling efficient low-rank hierarchical matrix representations, particularly for lossy scatterers and scatterers in slow background media.

Abstract: The generalized source integral equation (GSIE)approach is extended to enhance the compressibility of moment matrices for essentially-convex homogeneous penetrable scatterers. In the proposed indirect surface integral equation (SIE) formulation, the scattered field representations use a single distribution of generalized sources that radiate only weakly into the scatterer. A separate distribution of conventional sources is used for representing the interior fields. The wave interactions between surface subdomains via the generalized kernel are of a reduced effective dimensionality. The corresponding exterior integral operator moment matrix blocks exhibit enhanced rank deficiency. For lossy scatterers, this secures the low-rank (LR) compressibility of hierarchical matrix structures for the full system. For scatterers in a much slower background medium, significant savings with very delayed asymptotic scaling of the costs can be achieved. The GSIE is formulated for the transverse magnetic (TM) scattering problem. It is validated for representative examples and its superior compressibility is demonstrated.

</details>


### [262] [Feature tracking in physics-informed neural networks via joint optimization of nonlinear deformation manifolds: application to shocks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02230)
*Akshay Thakur,Matthew Zahr*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: FT-PINN augments a solution network with a learned diffeomorphic deformation map so collocation points track shocks of arbitrary geometry, resolving shocks accurately with a limited budget where vanilla PINNs fail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: PINNs converge to inaccurate solutions for conservation laws with shocks because uniformly distributed collocation points undersample localized features, letting the residual be dominated by already well-resolved regions.

Method: The solution network is defined on a fixed reference domain and composed with a diffeomorphic deformation map from a parameterized nonlinear manifold. Deformation and solution parameters are trained jointly by minimizing the pulled-back conservation-law residual, concentrating collocation points along features without prior location knowledge. Boundary preservation is enforced exactly via tangential projection of displacement, and folding is discouraged by a one-sided Jacobian determinant penalty. The framework is agnostic to parameterization choice.

Result: On four test problems (space-time viscous Burgers with merging shocks, decelerating Burgers shock, space-time Euler shock tube, steady 2D Euler regular shock reflection), FT-PINN resolves shocks at correct locations under a limited collocation budget. A vanilla PINN with the same architecture, budget, and training either misplaces shocks or fails to form them.

Conclusion: FT-PINN enables accurate shock resolution for conservation laws with arbitrary shock geometry by adaptively tracking features through a learned deformation map, outperforming vanilla PINNs under equal budgets.

Abstract: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) often converge to inaccurate solutions for conservation laws with shocks, because uniformly distributed collocation points undersample localized features and let the residual be dominated by regions that are already well resolved. We propose a feature-tracking PINN (FT-PINN) in which the solution network is defined on a fixed reference domain and composed with a diffeomorphic deformation map from a parameterized nonlinear manifold. The deformation and solution-network parameters are trained jointly by minimizing the pulled-back conservation-law residual. This lets collocation points concentrate along features of essentially arbitrary geometry, including curved, oblique, and merging shocks, without prior knowledge of their locations. The framework is agnostic to the choice of parameterization. Boundary preservation is enforced exactly through a tangential projection of the displacement, and folding is discouraged by a one-sided penalty on the Jacobian determinant. On four test problems (space-time viscous Burgers with merging shocks, a decelerating Burgers shock, the space-time Euler shock tube, and steady 2D Euler regular shock reflection), FT-PINN resolves shocks at their correct locations with a limited collocation budget. A vanilla PINN with the same architecture, budget, and training either misplaces the shocks or fails to form them.

</details>


### [263] [A Bifurcation-Based Domain Decomposition Method with Neural Operators for Blood Flow Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02238)
*Yuzhou Zhao,Han Zhang,J. Matias Di Martino,Jean-Michel Morel,Guillermo Sapiro*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: A framework decomposes a vascular network into bifurcation units and trains an operator network to map unit parameters to local solutions, then uses operator-network-driven Schwarz waveform relaxation to correct inter-unit discontinuities, achieving fast and accurate hemodynamic simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-quality, computationally efficient flow measurements across complex vascular networks are essential for clinical applications but remain challenging to obtain.

Method: The vascular network is decomposed into bifurcation units. An operator network maps unit-specific parameters to local solution fields of each bifurcation unit. Windkessel-model outlet parameters are lumped and inlet boundary conditions from parent-unit solutions are incorporated to rapidly approximate flow and pressure fields. Operator-network-driven Schwarz waveform relaxation is then applied across bifurcation units to correct discontinuities and improve accuracy.

Result: On 7-segment and 55-segment arterial tree models, the method achieves 13x-17x wall-clock speedups over conventional 1D numerical simulation, with 1% relative L2 errors in pressure and velocity. Pulse wave velocity biomarkers agree with the reference within 1-2%, and the same trained operator generalizes to different tree-like 1D vascular network topologies.

Conclusion: Coupling bifurcation-unit operator networks with Schwarz waveform relaxation yields fast, accurate, and generalizable hemodynamic simulations, suitable for clinical use across varying vascular network topologies.

Abstract: Fast and accurate simulation of hemodynamic behavior within vascular networks is essential for numerous clinical applications. However, obtaining high-quality and computationally efficient flow measurements across complex vascular networks remains challenging. To address this, we first decompose the vascular network into a set of bifurcation units and then develop an operator network capable of mapping unit-specific parameters to the local solution fields of each bifurcation unit. By lumping the Windkessel-model outlet parameters and incorporating inlet boundary conditions from the solution of parent units, the flow and pressure fields can be rapidly approximated. Subsequently, operator-network-driven Schwarz waveform relaxation is applied across bifurcation units to correct discontinuities and improve numerical accuracy. On 7-segment and 55-segment arterial tree models, the proposed method achieves $13\times$ to $17\times$ wall-clock speedups over conventional 1D numerical simulation, with relative $L^2$ errors of 1% in both pressure and velocity. The resulting pulse wave velocity biomarkers agree with the conventional reference to within 1--2%, and the same trained operator generalizes to different tree-like 1D vascular network topologies.

</details>


### [264] [A space-time finite element formulation for geometrically exact shear-deformable beams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03285)
*Ivo Steinbrecher,Alexander Humer*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: A space-time finite element formulation for transient analysis of geometrically exact shear-deformable beams, using a mixed approach with velocity fields and handling curved beams, kinks, and moving material domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical time-stepping schemes treat space and time separately, limiting the ability to handle non-material boundaries and axially moving continua without modifying the underlying beam formulation.

Method: A unified space-time finite element framework with a mixed approach introducing translational and angular velocities as independent fields. Rotations are parametrized via nodal rotation (pseudo-)vectors with objective interpolation of the triad field. Time-dependent material domains are represented directly through the space-time surface geometry.

Result: Numerical examples show convergence to classical time-stepping solutions, objectivity of the formulation, and the influence of space-time stabilization. The sliding spaghetti problem demonstrates applicability to axially moving continua.

Conclusion: The proposed space-time formulation supports generally curved beams with kinks and rigid joints, and enables treatment of non-material boundaries and moving continua without altering the beam formulation.

Abstract: We present a space-time finite element formulation for the transient analysis of geometrically exact shear-deformable beams. The formulation treats space and time within a unified finite element framework and employs a mixed approach in which translational and angular velocities are introduced as independent fields. The rotation field is parametrized using nodal rotation (pseudo-)vectors and a suitable objective interpolation of the triad field is employed. The resulting formulation supports generally curved beam structures, including geometric kinks and rigid joints. Furthermore, time-dependent material domains can be represented directly through the geometry of the space-time surface, enabling the treatment of non-material boundaries without modifying the underlying beam formulation. Numerical examples demonstrate convergence to solutions obtained with classical time-stepping schemes, objectivity of the formulation, and the influence of the space-time stabilization. Finally, the sliding spaghetti problem illustrates the applicability of the proposed approach to axially moving continua.

</details>


### [265] [A Generalized Source Integral Equation for Homogeneous Penetrable Scatterers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02223)
*Boris Diner,Yaniv Brick*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: This paper extends the generalized source integral equation (GSIE) approach to improve the compressibility of moment matrices for essentially-convex homogeneous penetrable scatterers, using an indirect surface integral equation formulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hierarchical matrix methods for solving scattering problems face high computational costs due to dense moment matrices. Improving rank deficiency and low-rank compressibility of these matrices, especially for penetrable scatterers, can significantly reduce computational costs.

Method: An indirect surface integral equation (SIE) formulation where scattered field representations use a single distribution of generalized sources that radiate only weakly into the scatterer, while a separate distribution of conventional sources represents interior fields. The GSIE is formulated for the transverse magnetic (TM) scattering problem.

Result: The generalized kernel reduces the effective dimensionality of wave interactions between surface subdomains, and exterior integral operator moment matrix blocks exhibit enhanced rank deficiency. For lossy scatterers, this secures low-rank compressibility of hierarchical matrix structures for the full system; for scatterers in a much slower background medium, significant savings with delayed asymptotic scaling are achieved. Validated on representative examples showing superior compressibility.

Conclusion: The extended GSIE enhances moment matrix compressibility for essentially-convex homogeneous penetrable scatterers, validated for TM scattering with superior compressibility demonstrated.

Abstract: The generalized source integral equation (GSIE)approach is extended to enhance the compressibility of moment matrices for essentially-convex homogeneous penetrable scatterers. In the proposed indirect surface integral equation (SIE) formulation, the scattered field representations use a single distribution of generalized sources that radiate only weakly into the scatterer. A separate distribution of conventional sources is used for representing the interior fields. The wave interactions between surface subdomains via the generalized kernel are of a reduced effective dimensionality. The corresponding exterior integral operator moment matrix blocks exhibit enhanced rank deficiency. For lossy scatterers, this secures the low-rank (LR) compressibility of hierarchical matrix structures for the full system. For scatterers in a much slower background medium, significant savings with very delayed asymptotic scaling of the costs can be achieved. The GSIE is formulated for the transverse magnetic (TM) scattering problem. It is validated for representative examples and its superior compressibility is demonstrated.

</details>


### [266] [Feature tracking in physics-informed neural networks via joint optimization of nonlinear deformation manifolds: application to shocks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02230)
*Akshay Thakur,Matthew Zahr*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: FT-PINN uses a learned diffeomorphic deformation map to concentrate collocation points along shocks, resolving conservation laws accurately within a limited budget.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vanilla PINNs converge to inaccurate solutions for conservation laws with shocks because uniformly distributed collocation points undersample localized features, letting the residual be dominated by already well-resolved regions.

Method: The solution network is defined on a fixed reference domain and composed with a diffeomorphic deformation map from a parameterized nonlinear manifold. Deformation and solution parameters are trained jointly by minimizing the pulled-back conservation-law residual. Boundary preservation is enforced exactly via tangential projection of displacement, and folding is discouraged by a one-sided Jacobian determinant penalty. The framework is agnostic to parameterization.

Result: On four test problems (space-time viscous Burgers with merging shocks, decelerating Burgers shock, space-time Euler shock tube, steady 2D Euler regular shock reflection), FT-PINN resolves shocks at correct locations with a limited collocation budget, whereas a vanilla PINN with the same architecture, budget, and training either misplaces or fails to form shocks.

Conclusion: Feature-tracking via adaptive deformation enables PINNs to concentrate collocation points along shocks of arbitrary geometry without prior location knowledge, achieving accurate conservation-law solutions under limited budgets.

Abstract: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) often converge to inaccurate solutions for conservation laws with shocks, because uniformly distributed collocation points undersample localized features and let the residual be dominated by regions that are already well resolved. We propose a feature-tracking PINN (FT-PINN) in which the solution network is defined on a fixed reference domain and composed with a diffeomorphic deformation map from a parameterized nonlinear manifold. The deformation and solution-network parameters are trained jointly by minimizing the pulled-back conservation-law residual. This lets collocation points concentrate along features of essentially arbitrary geometry, including curved, oblique, and merging shocks, without prior knowledge of their locations. The framework is agnostic to the choice of parameterization. Boundary preservation is enforced exactly through a tangential projection of the displacement, and folding is discouraged by a one-sided penalty on the Jacobian determinant. On four test problems (space-time viscous Burgers with merging shocks, a decelerating Burgers shock, the space-time Euler shock tube, and steady 2D Euler regular shock reflection), FT-PINN resolves shocks at their correct locations with a limited collocation budget. A vanilla PINN with the same architecture, budget, and training either misplaces the shocks or fails to form them.

</details>


### [267] [A Bifurcation-Based Domain Decomposition Method with Neural Operators for Blood Flow Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02238)
*Yuzhou Zhao,Han Zhang,J. Matias Di Martino,Jean-Michel Morel,Guillermo Sapiro*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: This paper proposes a neural operator framework that decomposes vascular networks into bifurcation units, learns a mapping from unit-specific parameters to local solution fields, and applies operator-network-driven Schwarz waveform relaxation to rapidly and accurately simulate hemodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fast and accurate simulation of hemodynamic behavior in vascular networks is crucial for clinical applications, but obtaining high-quality, computationally efficient flow measurements across complex networks remains challenging. Conventional 1D numerical simulation is accurate but too slow.

Method: The vascular network is decomposed into bifurcation units. An operator network maps unit-specific parameters to local solution fields of each unit. Windkessel-model outlet parameters are lumped and inlet boundary conditions from parent-unit solutions are incorporated to rapidly approximate flow and pressure fields. Then operator-network-driven Schwarz waveform relaxation is applied across bifurcation units to correct discontinuities and improve numerical accuracy.

Result: On 7-segment and 55-segment arterial tree models, the method achieves 13x to 17x wall-clock speedups over conventional 1D numerical simulation, with relative L2 errors of 1% in both pressure and velocity. Pulse wave velocity biomarkers agree with the conventional reference within 1-2%, and the same trained operator generalizes to different tree-like 1D vascular network topologies.

Conclusion: The proposed operator-network framework, combined with Schwarz waveform relaxation, enables fast and accurate hemodynamic simulation, preserves key biomarkers, and generalizes across vascular network topologies, making it promising for clinical applications.

Abstract: Fast and accurate simulation of hemodynamic behavior within vascular networks is essential for numerous clinical applications. However, obtaining high-quality and computationally efficient flow measurements across complex vascular networks remains challenging. To address this, we first decompose the vascular network into a set of bifurcation units and then develop an operator network capable of mapping unit-specific parameters to the local solution fields of each bifurcation unit. By lumping the Windkessel-model outlet parameters and incorporating inlet boundary conditions from the solution of parent units, the flow and pressure fields can be rapidly approximated. Subsequently, operator-network-driven Schwarz waveform relaxation is applied across bifurcation units to correct discontinuities and improve numerical accuracy. On 7-segment and 55-segment arterial tree models, the proposed method achieves $13\times$ to $17\times$ wall-clock speedups over conventional 1D numerical simulation, with relative $L^2$ errors of 1% in both pressure and velocity. The resulting pulse wave velocity biomarkers agree with the conventional reference to within 1--2%, and the same trained operator generalizes to different tree-like 1D vascular network topologies.

</details>


### [268] [A space-time finite element formulation for geometrically exact shear-deformable beams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03285)
*Ivo Steinbrecher,Alexander Humer*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: A space-time finite element formulation for transient analysis of geometrically exact shear-deformable beams, using a mixed approach with velocity fields as independent variables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical time-stepping schemes treat space and time separately, complicating handling of moving/non-material boundaries and axially moving continua. A unified framework is needed for geometrically exact beams with kinks, rigid joints, and time-dependent material domains.

Method: Space-time finite element formulation treating space and time in a unified framework. Mixed approach with translational and angular velocities as independent fields. Rotation parametrized via nodal rotation (pseudo-)vectors with objective interpolation of the triad field. Time-dependent material domains represented directly through the space-time surface geometry.

Result: Numerical examples show convergence to solutions from classical time-stepping schemes, objectivity of the formulation, and the influence of space-time stabilization. The sliding spaghetti problem demonstrates applicability to axially moving continua.

Conclusion: The proposed space-time formulation supports curved beams with kinks and rigid joints, enables non-material boundary treatment without modifying the beam formulation, and is applicable to axially moving continua.

Abstract: We present a space-time finite element formulation for the transient analysis of geometrically exact shear-deformable beams. The formulation treats space and time within a unified finite element framework and employs a mixed approach in which translational and angular velocities are introduced as independent fields. The rotation field is parametrized using nodal rotation (pseudo-)vectors and a suitable objective interpolation of the triad field is employed. The resulting formulation supports generally curved beam structures, including geometric kinks and rigid joints. Furthermore, time-dependent material domains can be represented directly through the geometry of the space-time surface, enabling the treatment of non-material boundaries without modifying the underlying beam formulation. Numerical examples demonstrate convergence to solutions obtained with classical time-stepping schemes, objectivity of the formulation, and the influence of the space-time stabilization. Finally, the sliding spaghetti problem illustrates the applicability of the proposed approach to axially moving continua.

</details>


### [269] [Temperature-Dependent Multiphysics Modeling of Additive Friction Stir Deposition Using Multi-Task Coupled Physics-Informed Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04082)
*Dhrubajyoti Gupta,Nikhil Gotawala,Raghav Gnanasambandam,Rohit Kannan,Hang Z. Yu,Jian Yu,Zhenyu James Kong*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: This work develops MCoPINN, a steady-state multi-task coupled physics-informed neural network that predicts 3D velocity and temperature fields in additive friction stir deposition (AFSD) while reconstructing temperature-dependent thermophysical properties from sparse data, achieving accuracy comparable to FVM at much lower computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: AFSD involves strongly coupled thermal and material-flow fields. High-fidelity finite-volume methods (FVMs) resolve these accurately but are computationally expensive, limiting repeated evaluation across process conditions. Additionally, treating thermophysical properties (thermal conductivity, density, specific heat) as constants introduces substantial error due to their strong temperature dependence.

Method: A steady-state multi-task coupled physics-informed neural network (MCoPINN) is developed to predict 3D velocity and temperature fields and simultaneously reconstruct temperature-dependent thermophysical properties from sparse material data. A theoretical analysis decomposes the prediction error into property reconstruction and neural field solver contributions. A controlled 1D nonlinear heat-conduction problem validates this decomposition and property reconstruction under sparse data, before applying the framework to AFSD.

Result: MCoPINN reproduces FVM benchmark thermal and material-flow fields while improving thermal prediction over a constant-property CoPINN, and matches experimental thermocouple measurements. Computationally, FVM required ~52 hours per operating condition versus ~8.5 hours of training for MCoPINN.

Conclusion: MCoPINN can account for temperature-dependent thermophysical properties in full-field AFSD prediction while requiring significantly less computation than the FVM benchmark, offering an efficient alternative for coupled-field process modeling.

Abstract: Additive friction stir deposition (AFSD) involves strongly coupled thermal and material-flow fields generated by frictional heating, severe plastic deformation, and tool-imposed boundary conditions. High-fidelity finite-volume methods (FVMs) can resolve these coupled fields accurately, but their computational cost limits repeated evaluation across process conditions. A separate modeling challenge arises from the strong temperature dependence of thermophysical properties. Treating thermal conductivity, density, and specific heat as constants can introduce substantial error in the predicted thermo-mechanical response. This work develops a steady-state multi-task coupled physics-informed neural network (MCoPINN) that predicts the three-dimensional velocity and temperature fields while reconstructing temperature-dependent thermophysical properties from sparse material data. A theoretical analysis formally decomposes the MCoPINN prediction error into contributions from property reconstruction and the neural field solver. A controlled one-dimensional nonlinear heat-conduction problem is first used to demonstrate this error decomposition and evaluate property reconstruction under sparse data. The framework is then applied to AFSD and evaluated against an FVM benchmark and experimental thermocouple measurements. MCoPINN reproduces the benchmark thermal and material-flow fields while improving the thermal prediction relative to the constant-property CoPINN. The benchmark FVM required approximately 52 hours per operating condition, whereas MCoPINN required about 8.5 hours of training.
