Sharpness is shown via a lower estimate for special sequences, reducing the problem to boundedness of a discrete supremum operator between weighted Lebesgue sequence spaces on nonincreasing sequences.

Result: A sharp (two-sided) estimate for the nonincreasing rearrangement of $M_\gamma x$ is obtained, leading to a reduction of the original problem to a previously studied operator boundedness question.

Conclusion: The complete characterization of the boundedness of $M_\gamma$ between $\lambda_p(v)$ and $\lambda_q(w)$ is obtained by combining the sharp rearrangement estimate with the authors' earlier results on the discrete supremum operator.

Abstract: In this paper, we give the complete characterization of the boundedness of the discrete fractional maximal operator $M_γ$, defined on $\mathbb{Z}^n$, $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $γ\in [0,n)$, between the classical Lorentz sequence spaces $λ_p(v)$ and $λ_q(w)$, where $0<p,q<\infty$, $\{v(m)\}$ and $\{w(m)\}$ are non-negative sequences defined on $\mathbb{N}$. We first obtain a sharp upper estimate for the nonincreasing rearrangement of the discrete fractional maximal operator $M_γx$, with a supremal operator involving a discrete Hardy operator and the nonincreasing rearrangement of the sequence $\{x(k)\}$, and we show that this estimate is sharp by showing a lower estimate for special sequences. Using this result, we can reduce the characterization of the boundedness of the discrete fractional maximal operator $ M_γ$ between the discrete weighted Lorentz sequence spaces to the boundedness of the discrete supremum operator between weighted Lebesgue sequence spaces, restricted to nonincreasing sequences. This problem was investigated in the authors' recent paper, which enables us to obtain the final characterization.

</details>


### [299] [Spectral Barron spaces of periodic functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06169)
*Mourad Choulli,Shuai Lu,Hiroshi Takase*

Main category: math.FA

TL;DR: This work extends spectral Barron spaces to periodic functions on tori and locally compact Abelian groups via weighted sequence spaces, and studies PDE well-posedness and two inverse problems for evolution equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional spectral Barron spaces are defined on the whole space or on bounded domains with boundary smoothness assumptions, leaving periodic settings and more general group structures largely unaddressed.

Method: The authors define spectral Barron spaces for periodic functions using Fourier series expansions that are isometrically isomorphic to weighted sequence spaces built on the l1 sequence space, extending the framework to high-dimensional tori and locally compact Abelian groups.

Result: Well-posedness of PDEs is established in these new spaces, and two specific inverse problems for evolution equations are investigated, yielding conditional stability within the spectral Barron space framework on the torus.

Conclusion: The study broadens spectral Barron space theory to periodic and group-theoretic settings, providing new tools for PDE analysis and inverse problem stability.

Abstract: Spectral Barron spaces are Fourier-Lebesgue-type spaces that arise in the approximation of functions by neural networks. Conventional spectral Barron spaces are mostly considered on the whole space or on a bounded domain with certain smoothness assumptions on the boundary. In this work, we investigate spectral Barron spaces for periodic functions, thereby extending the study of spectral Barron spaces to high-dimensional tori and, more generally, to locally compact Abelian groups. These extensions are carried out by considering Fourier series expansions that are isometrically isomorphic to weighted sequence spaces built upon the $\ell^1$ sequence space. We discuss the well-posedness of PDEs in these newly introduced spaces. Two specific inverse problems for evolution equations are investigated, yielding conditional stability within the framework of spectral Barron spaces on the torus.

</details>


<div id='quant-ph'></div>

# quant-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [300] [Accelerated search for global extrema in structured quantum signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04024)
*Sachin S. Bharadwaj,Katepalli R. Sreenivasan*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum algorithm that searches amplitude-encoded signals for global extrema via a nonlinear amplitude transformation, achieving polylogarithmic query complexity for structured signals and demonstrating it on turbulence data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extreme values govern consequential and anomalous behaviors of physical systems but are rare and costly to identify in large datasets. Quantum computation could reduce this cost, yet quantum amplitudes cannot be read, copied, compared, or marked by conventional search oracles, so standard approaches fail.

Method: An algorithm for searching amplitude-encoded quantum signals that exploits underlying structure through a nonlinear amplitude transformation, identifying and certifying a global extremum while exponentially reducing the effective search space for structured signals. It provides a measurable criterion for exploitable structure and efficient classical verification.

Result: Polylogarithmic query complexity in data size N with global completeness and classical verification. On line cuts of velocity gradients from a 1024^3 turbulence simulation (over one billion grid points), it consistently isolates the extreme tail, reducing median search space to O(log N), with clustering further accelerating the search.

Conclusion: The results establish a direct connection between physical structure and quantum advantage in extreme-value search, showing that structured signals enable exponential reduction in search space.

Abstract: Extreme values of a physical system are rare by definition, yet they can govern some of its most consequential and anomalous behavior. Their sparsity makes them particularly costly to identify in large data sets. Quantum computation offers a potential way to reduce this cost. The challenge is that quantum amplitudes cannot simply be read, copied, compared, or marked using conventional search oracles. Here we introduce an algorithm for searching amplitude-encoded quantum signals that exploits their underlying structure. Through a nonlinear amplitude transformation, the algorithm identifies and certifies a global extremum while exponentially reducing the effective search space for structured signals. We establish a polylogarithmic query complexity in the data size $N$, together with global completeness, efficient classical verification, and a measurable criterion for quantifying exploitable structure. We demonstrate the framework using computational turbulence data. Applied to line cuts of velocity gradients from a $1024^3$ simulation containing more than one billion grid points, the algorithm consistently isolates the extreme tail and reduces the median search space to $O(\log N)$. The clustered structure characteristic of turbulence further accelerates the search. These results establish a direct connection between physical structure and quantum advantage in extreme-value search. For accessibility, the main text emphasizes the central ideas and results, while the theoretical derivations are provided in the Appendices.

</details>


### [301] [Quantum data loading from the learned shared structure of real signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06076)
*Pablo Herrero Gómez,Antonio Jimeno Morenilla,David Muñoz-Hernández,Higinio Mora Mora*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: A quantum-native loader learns reusable low-dimensional dataset structure so each signal is prepared with one fixed circuit set by a few numbers, matching the strongest structured loader at equal gate cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Preparing quantum states from classical data can cost more than the computation they serve, and most loaders tailor a circuit to each input, wasting reusable structure.

Method: The loader learns a low-dimensional description of a dataset and uses one fixed circuit parameterized by a few numbers to prepare every signal; parameters can be inferred from a random subset.

Result: Across seven views of five public datasets it meets the strongest structured loader's targets at equal gate cost with several times fewer numbers per signal; in a preregistered blind replication the subset for within-10%-of-full-accuracy stayed constant across a 16x signal increase, unlike the baseline. However, it declines cases it cannot represent, covering fewer cases and no electrocardiogram.

Conclusion: Learning reusable dataset structure yields efficient quantum state preparation, but at the cost of reduced coverage for unrepresentable signals.

Abstract: Preparing quantum states from classical data can cost more than the computation they serve; most loaders tailor a circuit to each input. Here we show that the signals of a real dataset share structure that can be learned once and reused. Our quantum-native loader learns a low-dimensional description of a dataset and prepares every signal with one fixed circuit set by a few numbers. Across seven views of five public datasets it meets the targets of the strongest structured loader at equal gate cost with several times fewer numbers per signal. These numbers can be inferred from a random subset: in a preregistered blind replication the subset needed to come within ten per cent of full-signal accuracy stayed constant within a prespecified margin as signals grew sixteenfold, whereas the structured loader needed ever more. It declines what it cannot represent, covering fewer cases than that baseline and no electrocardiogram.

</details>


### [302] [Ground state energy estimation and preparation by the truncated power method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05627)
*Savero Lukianto Chandra,Daochen Wang,Carolyn Zhang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a new classically tractable regime for the Guided Local Hamiltonian Problem (GLHP): when the true ground state has polynomially bounded ℓ1-norm, the classical Truncated Power Method dequantizes quantum algorithms up to a factor of ‖ψ‖₁².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: GLHP is BQP-complete for small error but classically tractable for large error. The authors seek an orthogonal regime where the problem becomes classically tractable, providing conditions under which quantum advantage disappears.

Method: They analyze the Truncated Power Method (TPM), a classical algorithm originally introduced by Kirby et al. in a quantum context, and bound its runtime relative to quantum algorithms for GLHP in terms of all relevant parameters, with the only extra cost being a factor of ‖ψ‖₁².

Result: TPM has a runtime polynomially comparable to quantum algorithms for GLHP in all relevant parameters, except for an extra factor of ‖ψ‖₁². Hence, when ‖ψ‖₁² is polynomially bounded, TPM dequantizes quantum algorithms for GLHP.

Conclusion: If the true ground state ψ satisfies polynomially bounded ℓ1-norm, GLHP is classically tractable via TPM, establishing a new regime where quantum algorithms can be dequantized.

Abstract: The Guided Local Hamiltonian Problem (GLHP) is the problem of ground state energy estimation and preparation given a guiding state having good overlap with the true ground state. The problem is known to be BQP-complete for small error but is classically tractable for larger error. In this work, we identify an orthogonal regime in which the problem is classically tractable, namely when the true ground state $ψ$ has polynomially bounded $\ell_1$-norm. In particular, we show that the Truncated Power Method (TPM), a classical algorithm introduced by Kirby et al. in the quantum context, has a runtime that is polynomially comparable to quantum algorithms for GLHP in all relevant parameters, except for an extra factor of $\|ψ\|_1^2$. In other words, if $\|ψ\|_1^2$ is polynomially bounded, then TPM dequantizes quantum algorithms for GLHP.

</details>


<div id='math.DG'></div>

# math.DG [[Back]](#toc)

### [303] [The Calderón problem for harmonic spinors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03116)
*Carlos Valero*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The conformal class of a compact spin manifold with boundary is uniquely determined by the boundary conjugation map of the Dirac operator (MIT boundary conditions), assuming the metric is locally conformally real-analytic. This also yields uniqueness of real-analytic unitary connections via the twisted BC map.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Boundary value problems for Dirac operators (e.g. via MIT boundary conditions) encode geometry, and one wants to know how much of the interior geometry is determined by boundary data of harmonic spinors. The paper addresses whether the boundary conjugation map determines the conformal class and connection data.

Method: Studies the boundary conjugation (BC) map for the Dirac operator with MIT boundary conditions on compact spin manifolds with boundary. Assumes metrics are locally conformally real-analytic (LCRA). Proves that equivalence of BC maps on an open subset of the boundary implies conformal equivalence; extends to twisted Dirac operators to recover real-analytic unitary connections up to local gauge.

Result: If two LCRA metrics have equivalent BC maps over an open subset of the boundary, the manifolds are conformally equivalent. The LCRA class includes conformally Einstein manifolds and all smooth surfaces. Moreover, in dimensions greater than 2, a real-analytic unitary connection on an LCRA manifold is determined up to local gauge equivalence by the BC map of its twisted Dirac operator.

Conclusion: Boundary data of harmonic spinors—specifically the BC map—uniquely determines conformal class (and, in higher dimensions, real-analytic unitary connections up to gauge), under a local conformal real-analyticity assumption that covers important geometric classes.

Abstract: We show that the conformal class of a compact spin manifold with boundary is uniquely determined by its boundary values of harmonic spinors if the metric is locally conformal to a real-analytic metric in some coordinates. More precisely, we consider the boundary conjugation (BC) map for the Dirac operator corresponding to MIT boundary conditions, and prove that if two locally conformally real-analytic (LCRA) metrics have equivalent BC maps over an open subset of the boundary, then the two manifolds are conformally equivalent. The class of LCRA manifolds includes conformally Einstein manifolds, as well as all smooth surfaces. We also show that a real-analytic unitary connection on an LCRA manifold is uniquely determined up to local gauge equivalence by the BC map of its twisted Dirac operator in dimensions greater than 2.

</details>


### [304] [The Calderón problem for harmonic spinors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03116)
*Carlos Valero*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The conformal class of a compact spin manifold with boundary is uniquely determined by boundary values of harmonic spinors (via the boundary conjugation map for the Dirac operator with MIT boundary conditions), provided the metric is locally conformally real-analytic.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Boundary data of harmonic spinors (encoding bulk geometry) is conjectured to determine the interior conformal class, analogous to boundary rigidity problems. The paper aims to prove such a boundary determination result.

Method: Study the boundary conjugation (BC) map for the Dirac operator under MIT boundary conditions. Assume metrics belong to the locally conformally real-analytic (LCRA) class (includes conformally Einstein manifolds and all smooth surfaces). Show equivalence of BC maps on an open boundary subset implies conformal equivalence. Extend to twisted Dirac operators with real-analytic unitary connections.

Result: If two LCRA metrics have equivalent BC maps over an open subset of the boundary, the two manifolds are conformally equivalent. Additionally, in dimensions >2, a real-analytic unitary connection on an LCRA manifold is uniquely determined up to local gauge equivalence by the BC map of its twisted Dirac operator.

Conclusion: Boundary values of harmonic spinors uniquely determine the conformal class for LCRA spin manifolds with boundary, and similarly determine real-analytic unitary connections (up to local gauge), establishing a spinorial boundary determination principle.

Abstract: We show that the conformal class of a compact spin manifold with boundary is uniquely determined by its boundary values of harmonic spinors if the metric is locally conformal to a real-analytic metric in some coordinates. More precisely, we consider the boundary conjugation (BC) map for the Dirac operator corresponding to MIT boundary conditions, and prove that if two locally conformally real-analytic (LCRA) metrics have equivalent BC maps over an open subset of the boundary, then the two manifolds are conformally equivalent. The class of LCRA manifolds includes conformally Einstein manifolds, as well as all smooth surfaces. We also show that a real-analytic unitary connection on an LCRA manifold is uniquely determined up to local gauge equivalence by the BC map of its twisted Dirac operator in dimensions greater than 2.

</details>


### [305] [Boundary behaviour of stable Allen-Cahn limit-interfaces in Riemannian manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04833)
*Martin Man-chun Li,Davide Parise,Lorenzo Sarnataro*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper studies boundary behavior of limit-interface varifolds from stable/finite Morse index Allen-Cahn solutions with Neumann boundary conditions, proving curvature varifold structure and ruling out classical tangent cones at the boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Part of a series developing min-max theory for free boundary minimal hypersurfaces via the Allen-Chahn equation; extends previous work on general critical points to stable/finite Morse index solutions, aiming toward optimal boundary regularity.

Method: Analyze boundary behavior of limit-interface integral varifolds arising from stable or finite Morse index solutions to the Allen-Cahn equation with Neumann boundary condition.

Result: The limit-interface is a curvature varifold with boundary (Mantegazza sense) satisfying a generalized stability inequality; classical tangent cones at the boundary are ruled out; the first variation is singular with respect to its weight measure; confirms the conjecture that Malchiodi-Ni-Wei boundary concentration examples have unbounded Morse index.

Conclusion: These results are key ingredients for optimal boundary regularity in Allen-Cahn free boundary min-max theory, to be shown in forthcoming work.

Abstract: This is the second paper in our series of works to develop the min-max theory for free boundary minimal hypersurfaces in Riemannian manifolds with boundary via the Allen-Cahn equation. Building on our previous work for general critical points, we study the boundary behaviour of limit-interface integral varifolds arising from stable (or finite Morse index) solutions to the Allen-Cahn equation with Neumann boundary condition. More precisely, we prove that the limit-interface is a curvature varifold with boundary in the sense of Mantegazza satisfying a generalized stability inequality. Furthermore, we rule out classical tangent cones at the boundary and show that the first variation of the limit-interface varifold is singular with respect to its weight measure. In particular, our result confirms a folklore conjecture that the boundary concentration examples of Malchiodi, Ni and Wei have unbounded Morse index. As will be evident in our forthcoming work, the results obtained in this paper are crucial ingredients towards the optimal boundary regularity in the Allen-Cahn free boundary min-max theory.

</details>


### [306] [Spectral Gap Estimates on Nearly Einstein Manifolds with Integral Curvature Deviation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04846)
*Shunzi Guo*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: Establishes a quantitative stability result: compact manifolds that are nearly Einstein (small traceless Ricci plus scalar curvature deviation in L^p) satisfy a sharp lower bound on the first nonzero Neumann/Laplace eigenvalue approaching the Bakry--Qian one-dimensional model bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical Bakry--Qian theorem gives the optimal lower bound for the first nonzero eigenvalue in terms of dimension, curvature, and diameter, but only for exactly Einstein (constant Ricci) manifolds. This paper asks how robust that bound is under small perturbations, i.e. whether near-Einstein manifolds still satisfy an almost-optimal eigenvalue lower bound.

Method: Defines an Einstein deviation Θ combining traceless Ricci and scalar curvature error from a reference R_E, measured via κ_Ein(p) in L^p (p>n/2). Uses the Bochner formula with decomposition Ric=(n-1)K_E g+θg+Ric° and an auxiliary function J satisfying ΔJ-τ|∇J|²/J-2JΘ=-σJ with Neumann boundary conditions, combining Moser iteration and a maximum principle.

Result: For every α∈(0,1) there is ε_0>0 (depending on n,p,α,diameter bound D,R_E) such that diam(M)≤D and κ_Ein(p)<ε_0 imply λ_1(M)≥α λ_1(n,K_E,D), where λ_1(n,K,D) is the Bakry--Qian model eigenvalue (with λ_1=nK when K>0 and D≥π/√K).

Conclusion: The Bakry--Qian eigenvalue estimate is quantitatively stable under small Einstein deviation: as κ_Ein(p)→0 the classical theorem is recovered, and near-Einstein manifolds inherit an almost-sharp spectral gap lower bound.

Abstract: Let $(M^n,g)$ be a compact Riemannian manifold of dimension $n\ge 2$, possibly with nonempty convex $C^2$ boundary. We study the first nonzero eigenvalue $λ_1(M)$ of the Laplace--Beltrami operator, subject to the Neumann boundary condition when $\partial M\neq\emptyset$. Given a reference scalar curvature $R_E>0$, set $K_E:=R_E/(n(n-1))$, and define the traceless Ricci tensor $\mathring{\Ric}:=\Ric-\frac{R}{n}g$ and the Einstein deviation \[ Θ:=|\mathring{\Ric}|+\frac{|R-R_E|}{n},\qquad κ_{\mathrm{Ein}}(p):=\left(\frac{1}{\vol(M)}\int_MΘ^p\,dV\right)^{1/p}, \quad p>n/2. \] We say that $M$ is $\varepsilon$-nearly Einstein if $κ_{\mathrm{Ein}}(p)<\varepsilon$. The main result states that for every $α\in(0,1)$ there exists $\varepsilon_0=\varepsilon_0(n,p,α,D,R_E)>0$ such that if $\diam(M)\le D$ and $κ_{\mathrm{Ein}}(p)<\varepsilon_0$, then \[ λ_1(M)\ge α\,λ_1(n,K_E,D), \] where $λ_1(n,K,D)$ is the first nonzero eigenvalue of the Bakry--Qian one-dimensional model problem with curvature parameter $K$, with the convention $λ_1(n,K,D)=nK$ when $K>0$ and $D\geπ/\sqrt{K}$. The proof combines the Bochner formula for the decomposition $\Ric=(n-1)K_Eg+θg+\mathring{\Ric}$ with an auxiliary function $J$ satisfying \[ ΔJ-τ\frac{|\nabla J|^2}{J}-2JΘ=-σJ \] and Neumann boundary conditions, together with Moser iteration and a maximum principle. As $κ_{\mathrm{Ein}}(p)\to0$, the estimate recovers the classical Bakry--Qian theorem.

</details>


### [307] [The pseudo-Riemannian spectral zeta function density on scattering manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05528)
*Ashkan Sadat Kyaee*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper studies meromorphic continuation of spectral zeta densities for complex powers of Laplace-type operators on pseudo-Riemannian scattering spaces of mixed signature and arbitrary dimension, relating the poles to geometric invariants like scalar curvature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extends the local Feynman complex power analysis of Dang and Wrochna beyond the even-dimensional Lorentzian setting to mixed-signature pseudo-Riemannian spaces of arbitrary dimension.

Method: Combines uniform Feynman resolvent estimates with a direct analysis of the Hadamard parametrix in pseudo-Riemannian signature; uses a geometric no-return argument instead of causal convexity.

Result: Diagonal trace densities of (P ± iε)^{-α} admit meromorphic continuation, with poles related to local geometric invariants, in particular the pseudo-Riemannian scalar curvature.

Conclusion: The local spectral zeta analysis for complex powers generalizes to mixed-signature pseudo-Riemannian scattering spaces in arbitrary dimension, linking poles to curvature invariants.

Abstract: We study spectral zeta densities associated with complex powers of principally scalar Laplace-type operators on pseudo-Riemannian scattering spaces of mixed signature and arbitrary dimension. The construction extends, beyond the even-dimensional Lorentzian case, the local analysis of Feynman complex powers developed by Dang and Wrochna. Namely, for $\varepsilon > 0$, we consider complex powers $(P\pm i\varepsilon)^{-α}$ and prove that the diagonal trace densities admit meromorphic continuation. We relate their poles to local geometric invariants, in particular to the pseudo-Riemannian scalar curvature. Our proof combines uniform Feynman resolvent estimates with a direct analysis of the Hadamard parametrix in pseudo-Riemannian signature. Beyond the Lorentzian case, the use of causal convexity is replaced by a geometric no-return argument.

</details>


### [308] [On Polynomial Complex Hessian Equations I: Non-Linear Non-Spectral Elliptic Hessian Equations on Riemannian Manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06529)
*Rémi Delloque*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper studies a generalized class of fully non-linear elliptic Hessian equations on compact Riemannian manifolds, weakening the standard symmetry assumption of Caffarelli–Nirenberg–Spruck, deriving a priori estimates, and solving the equations via a continuity method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical framework of Caffarelli, Nirenberg and Spruck assumes the equation is a symmetric function of the eigenvalues of the Hessian. This paper aims to relax that assumption to a weaker one, thereby broadening the class of solvable Hessian equations.

Method: The authors establish a priori estimates for solutions under suitable assumptions on the equation, then apply a continuity method to obtain existence of solutions.

Result: A priori estimates are derived for solutions of the generalized fully non-linear elliptic Hessian equations under appropriate hypotheses, enabling the continuity method to be applied successfully.

Conclusion: The symmetry assumption of Caffarelli–Nirenberg–Spruck can be weakened while still solving the Hessian equations, extending existence theory to a more general class of equations on compact Riemannian manifolds.

Abstract: We study a general class of fully non-linear elliptic Hessian equations on compact Riemannian manifolds. In particular, we replace the standard assumption that the equation is a symmetric function of the eigenvalues of the Hessian made by Caffarelli, Nirenberg and Spruck by a weaker one. After deriving a priori estimates for solutions under suitable assumptions on the equation, we solve it with a continuity method.

</details>


### [309] [Rigidity of conformal metrics with constant sixth-order $Q$-curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06573)
*Daowen Lin,Wangzhe Wu*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: On closed connected Einstein manifolds with positive scalar curvature in dimension n≥6, any smooth conformal metric with constant sixth-order Q-curvature must itself be Einstein.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper studies rigidity of conformal metrics with constant higher-order Q-curvature, extending Obata-type results to the sixth-order Q-curvature setting. It seeks to show that the Einstein condition is preserved under conformal changes when the sixth-order Q-curvature is constant.

Method: An Obata-type identity is used as the main tool. The proof applies this identity to a smooth metric conformal to the given Einstein metric g_0, exploiting the constancy of the sixth-order Q-curvature to derive that the conformal metric must be Einstein.

Result: For (M^n, g_0) a closed connected Einstein manifold with positive scalar curvature and n≥6, every smooth metric conformal to g_0 with constant sixth-order Q-curvature is proven to be Einstein.

Conclusion: The result provides a rigidity theorem for conformal metrics with constant sixth-order Q-curvature, showing that on such Einstein manifolds the only conformal metrics with constant sixth-order Q-curvature are Einstein.

Abstract: Let $(M^n,g_0)$ be a closed connected Einstein manifold with positive scalar curvature, where $n\ge6$. Using an Obata-type identity, we prove that every smooth metric conformal to $g_0$ with constant sixth-order $Q$-curvature is Einstein.

</details>


<div id='astro-ph.SR'></div>

# astro-ph.SR [[Back]](#toc)

### [310] [Mesoscale Turbulence in Type Ia Supernova Deflagrations II. Evidence for Fuel Preheat Due to Flame Thermal Expansion from Lagrangian Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02677)
*Ryan Learn,Andrey Zhiglo,Tomasz Plewa*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

TL;DR: Multidimensional simulations with Lagrangian tracers reveal a two-step mechanism by which Rayleigh-Taylor unstable deflagration fronts in Type Ia supernovae precondition adjacent fuel, shortening ignition times via flame-driven expansion and acoustic pressure rebalancing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent work (Brooker+25) showed systematic shortening of fuel ignition times near Rayleigh-Taylor unstable deflagration fronts, but the physical origin of this preconditioning was unexplained. This paper aims to identify the thermodynamic mechanism behind it.

Method: Multidimensional simulations plus Lagrangian tracer-particle analysis of the thermodynamic evolution of fuel near the flame front, examining flame-driven expansion and ignition-time variability in partially degenerate carbon-oxygen plasma across low- and high-density models.

Result: The most strongly preconditioned fuel parcels (a minority of the fuel layer) undergo pressure-constrained expansion with modest density drops and temperature increases from ~2x10^8 K to ~7x10^8 K (13-63% above unperturbed fuel temperature). Despite moderate perturbations, the strong temperature sensitivity of carbon ignition substantially shortens ignition times.

Conclusion: A two-step mechanism is identified: flame thermal expansion pushes fuel out of hydrostatic equilibrium, then weak acoustic waves restore pressure balance, transferring internal energy from degenerate to thermal components and raising temperature. Unsteady thermonuclear flames can thus precondition fuel over distances larger than the laminar flame thickness. Whether this leads to secondary ignition or deflagration-to-detonation transition remains untested, as nuclear burning is disabled in the analyzed fuel.

Abstract: Thermonuclear flame fronts in Type Ia supernovae develop hydrodynamic instabilities that generate turbulent mesoscale flow and substantial local thermodynamic variability. Recent work by \citet{Brooker+25} demonstrated systematic shortening of fuel ignition times near Rayleigh--Taylor unstable deflagration fronts, but the physical origin remained unclear. We investigate the thermodynamic evolution of fuel near the flame front using multidimensional simulations and Lagrangian tracer-particle analysis, focusing on flame-driven expansion and ignition-time variability in partially degenerate carbon--oxygen plasma. Tracer trajectories show that the most strongly preconditioned fuel parcels near the flame front, which form a minority of the fuel layer, undergo approximately pressure-constrained expansion with modest density reductions and temperature increases of $\approx 2\times10^{8}$~K to $\approx7\times10^{8}$~K ($\approx13$ to $63$ per cent above the unperturbed fuel temperature) between the low- and high-density models. Despite moderate thermodynamic perturbations, the strong temperature sensitivity of carbon ignition produces substantial shortening of ignition times. The results indicate a two-step mechanism: flame thermal expansion perturbs the fuel away from hydrostatic equilibrium, while restoration of pressure balance by weak acoustic waves redistributes internal energy from degenerate to thermal components. This raises the fuel temperature and produces localized thermodynamic preconditioning. These results suggest that unsteady thermonuclear flames can shorten ignition times ahead of the flame, influencing fuel over distances larger than the laminar flame thickness. Whether this preconditioning leads to secondary ignition or contributes to deflagration-to-detonation transition remains untested here, as nuclear burning is disabled in the analyzed fuel.

</details>


### [311] [Mesoscale Turbulence in Type Ia Supernova Deflagrations II. Evidence for Fuel Preheat Due to Flame Thermal Expansion from Lagrangian Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02677)
*Ryan Learn,Andrey Zhiglo,Tomasz Plewa*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

TL;DR: Simulations with Lagrangian tracers reveal that Rayleigh-Taylor-unstable thermonuclear flame fronts precondition fuel via a two-step mechanism (thermal expansion + acoustic pressure rebalancing), raising fuel temperature and shortening ignition times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work (Brooker+25) found systematic shortening of fuel ignition times near Rayleigh-Taylor unstable deflagration fronts in Type Ia supernovae, but the physical origin of this preconditioning was unclear. This study aims to explain the underlying thermodynamic mechanism.

Method: Multidimensional simulations combined with Lagrangian tracer-particle analysis of the thermodynamic evolution of partially degenerate carbon-oxygen plasma near the flame front, spanning low- and high-density models.

Result: The most strongly preconditioned fuel parcels (a minority of the fuel layer) undergo approximately pressure-constrained expansion with modest density reductions and temperature increases from ~2e8 K to ~7e8 K (13-63% above unperturbed fuel temperature). The strong temperature sensitivity of carbon ignition yields substantial ignition-time shortening despite moderate perturbations.

Conclusion: A two-step mechanism is identified: flame thermal expansion perturbs fuel from hydrostatic equilibrium, then pressure rebalancing by weak acoustic waves shifts internal energy from degenerate to thermal components, causing localized preconditioning over distances larger than the laminar flame thickness. Whether this leads to secondary ignition or deflagration-to-detonation transition remains untested, as nuclear burning is disabled in the analyzed fuel.

Abstract: Thermonuclear flame fronts in Type Ia supernovae develop hydrodynamic instabilities that generate turbulent mesoscale flow and substantial local thermodynamic variability. Recent work by \citet{Brooker+25} demonstrated systematic shortening of fuel ignition times near Rayleigh--Taylor unstable deflagration fronts, but the physical origin remained unclear. We investigate the thermodynamic evolution of fuel near the flame front using multidimensional simulations and Lagrangian tracer-particle analysis, focusing on flame-driven expansion and ignition-time variability in partially degenerate carbon--oxygen plasma. Tracer trajectories show that the most strongly preconditioned fuel parcels near the flame front, which form a minority of the fuel layer, undergo approximately pressure-constrained expansion with modest density reductions and temperature increases of $\approx 2\times10^{8}$~K to $\approx7\times10^{8}$~K ($\approx13$ to $63$ per cent above the unperturbed fuel temperature) between the low- and high-density models. Despite moderate thermodynamic perturbations, the strong temperature sensitivity of carbon ignition produces substantial shortening of ignition times. The results indicate a two-step mechanism: flame thermal expansion perturbs the fuel away from hydrostatic equilibrium, while restoration of pressure balance by weak acoustic waves redistributes internal energy from degenerate to thermal components. This raises the fuel temperature and produces localized thermodynamic preconditioning. These results suggest that unsteady thermonuclear flames can shorten ignition times ahead of the flame, influencing fuel over distances larger than the laminar flame thickness. Whether this preconditioning leads to secondary ignition or contributes to deflagration-to-detonation transition remains untested here, as nuclear burning is disabled in the analyzed fuel.

</details>


### [312] [Measured energy exchange in coronal hole solar wind from its solar origins to the heliosphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04669)
*Yeimy J. Rivera,Alin R. Paraschiv,Samuel T. Badman,Alberto M. Vasquez,Federico A. Nuevo,Ryan J. French,Momchil Molnar,Roberto Susino,Zihao Yang,Marco Romoli,Richard A. Frazin,Michael L. Stevens,Stuart D. Bale,Deborah Baker,Jasper Halekas,Thomas A. Schad,Christopher J. Owen,Philippe Louarn,Jenna Samra,Giuliana de Toma,Daniela A. Lacatus,Giulio Del Zanna,Chad A. Madsen,Enrico Landi,Manolis K. Georgoulis,Amir Caspi,Éric Buchlin,Nour E. Raouafi,Luca Franci,Miho Janvier,Cooper Downs,Roberto Livi,Phyllis Whittlesey,Ali Rahmati,Davin Larson,Nikos Sioulas*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

TL;DR: Multi-perspective observations around the April 8, 2024 Total Solar Eclipse trace a solar wind stream from its low-coronal birthplace to in situ measurements by Parker Solar Probe, computing a complete energy budget across the Alfvén surface.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect the solar atmosphere with heliospheric in situ measurements and construct a comprehensive energy budget of a solar wind stream from its coronal origin to beyond the Alfvén surface, testing whether coronal and in situ energy budgets are consistent.

Method: Coordinated ground- and space-based remote observations (multi-wavelength, near-contemporaneous) are combined with in situ measurements. Plasma parameters and an energy budget are derived at the low-coronal birthplace (1.05 Rsun) and independently computed at and beyond the Alfvén surface from in situ data of the same stream.

Result: Total wave energy flux dominates the low-coronal budget (11 W m-2 Alfvén, 7-23 W m-2 compressive MHD modes), with enthalpy (13 W m-2) and gravitational work (2.5 W m-2) making up the rest. The coronal budget matches in situ measurements only if compressive energy flux is included and attributed to the fast magnetosonic mode. Nearly all thermal and wave energy converts to ion heating, acceleration, and gravitational potential energy within the Alfvén surface. The stream behaves as a thermal-pressure driven wind with added MHD fluctuation energy; the Alfvénic component follows a near-dissipation-free curve below the Alfvén surface but deviates more strongly above it.

Conclusion: The coronal and in situ energy budgets of the solar wind stream are consistent only when compressive (fast magnetosonic) energy flux is included, and the stream is well-described as a thermal-pressure driven wind with MHD fluctuation energy deposition. Alfvénic fluctuations evolve nearly dissipation-free below the Alfvén surface, deviating above this critical surface.

Abstract: Coordinated ground- and space-based remote observations are used to provide a comprehensive, multi-perspective view of the solar atmosphere that we connect with in situ measurements in the heliosphere centered around the April 8 2024 Total Solar Eclipse observed across North America. These near-contemporaneous, multi-wavelength datasets are used to directly derive the plasma parameters and compute an energy budget of a solar wind stream at its low-coronal birthplace (at an altitude of 1.05Rsun). The energy budget is also independently computed at and beyond the Alfvén surface from the in situ observations of the same stream. The total wave energy flux dominates the low-coronal budget (11 W m-2 Alfvén, 7 to 23 W m-2 in compressive MHD modes), while enthalpy (13 W m-2) and work done against gravity from 1.0Rsun (2.5 W m-2) make up the remainder. The total coronal energy budget is consistent with the total measured in situ by Parker Solar Probe if and only if compressive energy flux is included and attributed to the fast magnetosonic mode. Nearly all this thermal and wave energy is converted to ion heating, acceleration and gravitational potential energy within the Alfvén surface. The stream evolution is well-described as a thermal-pressure driven wind with additional energy deposited by MHD fluctuations. The observed evolution of the Alfvénic component of these fluctuations follows a near-dissipation-free curve below the Alfvén surface while deviating from it more strongly above this critical surface.

</details>


### [313] [Fast and Differentiable MHD Surrogate for Solar Wind using Neural Operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05657)
*Prateek Mayank,Enrico Camporeale,Thomas E. Berger,Zhenguang Huang,Gabor Toth*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

TL;DR: SNOW is a fast, differentiable neural operator surrogate that emulates global MHD solar wind solutions from 20 to 240 Rs, achieving full 3D output in under one second on a single GPU.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Global MHD simulations give the full plasma state of the heliosphere but are computationally expensive, limiting applications that need repeated evaluations. A fast surrogate is needed.

Method: SNOW combines a rotational ballistic approximation, a spherical harmonic neural operator, and causal radial context to autoregressively propagate all eight MHD variables through consecutive shell-to-shell transitions. It is trained and evaluated on AWSoM simulations spanning a solar cycle.

Result: SNOW remains stable across the full rollout, reproduces large-scale plasma and magnetic structures (fast/slow wind streams, magnetic polarity sectors, vector orientations, fieldline connectivity, bulk-flow structures), and keeps magnetic divergence and induction diagnostics comparable to reference MHD. Full 3D solution generated in under one second on a single GPU.

Conclusion: Fast neural emulation of global MHD solar wind is feasible while retaining physically meaningful plasma and magnetic field structure.

Abstract: Global magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) simulations provide the complete plasma state of the heliosphere, but their computational cost limits applications requiring repeated model evaluations. We introduce SNOW (Solar Neural Operator for Wind), a fast and differentiable surrogate for global MHD solar wind. SNOW combines a rotational ballistic approximation, a spherical harmonic neural operator, and causal radial context to autoregressively propagate all eight MHD variables from 20 to 240 Rs through consecutive shell to shell transitions. Trained and evaluated using AWSoM simulations spanning a solar cycle, SNOW remains stable across the complete rollout. The model successfully reproduces the large scale plasma and magnetic field structures, including fast and slow wind streams, magnetic polarity sectors, vector orientations, fieldline connectivity, and bulk-flow structures. Additionally, the magnetic divergence and induction diagnostics remain comparable to the reference MHD solutions. The complete three-dimensional solution is generated in less than one second on a single GPU. These results demonstrate the feasibility of fast neural emulation of global MHD solar wind solutions while retaining physically meaningful plasma and magnetic field structure.

</details>


### [314] [Coherent radio bursts, polarity reversal, and quiescent emission from the M4V dwarf WW PsA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05955)
*Jackson Mitchell-Bolton,Laura Driessen,Tara Murphy,Joshua Pritchard,Barnali Das,Richard Dodson,Martin Meyer,Jonghwan Rhee,Yuanming Wang*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

TL;DR: Discovery of coherent, ~100% circularly polarised radio bursts from M4V dwarf WW PsA in ASKAP DINGO data, favouring the electron cyclotron maser mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Coherent radio emission from active M dwarfs appears as stochastic solar-like bursts or beamed, rotationally modulated emission (e.g., auroral pulses). Detailed monitoring is needed to probe the plasmas and magnetic fields driving this emission.

Method: Searched for polarised radio emission from stars within 85 pc using 2.2×10^4 h of stellar dynamic spectra from the ASKAP DINGO survey, plus a VASTER pipeline detection.

Result: Found three long-duration (≳30 min), ~100% circularly polarised bursts from WW PsA in 283.3 h, plus a fourth burst in a separate epoch. One burst shows a mid-burst polarity reversal. An oblique rotator model gives a high obliquity β = 74(+6/-22) deg.

Conclusion: The mid-burst polarity reversal favours the electron cyclotron maser mechanism over plasma emission; the bursts are tentatively attributed to rotationally modulated ECM pulses of variable intensity.

Abstract: Coherent radio emission from magnetically active M dwarfs is largely observed in two varieties: stochastic solar-like bursts and beamed, rotationally modulated emission including auroral pulses. Detailed monitoring of these stars is critical for probing the plasmas and magnetic fields driving the emission. We conducted a search for polarised radio emission from stars within 85 pc, covering $2.2\times10^4$ h of stellar dynamic spectra from the ASKAP Deep Investigation of Neutral Gas Origins (DINGO) survey. We report the discovery of coherent radio bursts from the M4V dwarf WW PsA. In addition to quiescent emission, we detect three long-duration ($\gtrsim$30 min) bursts, each ~100% circularly polarised, in 283.3 h of data. We also report a fourth burst in an epoch not covered by our search, detected using the VASTER pipeline. One of the bursts features a polarity reversal midway through, based on which we favour the electron cyclotron maser (ECM) mechanism over plasma emission. We tentatively propose the bursts are due to rotationally modulated ECM pulses of variable intensity. We infer the required physical properties of WW PsA, including a high obliquity $β= 74^{+6}_{-22}$ deg under an oblique rotator model.

</details>


### [315] [AI Agents as a Research Team: A Human-Supervised Case Study in Stellar Spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05424)
*Marwan Gebran,Ian Bentley*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

TL;DR: A qualitative case study of a human-supervised team of specialized AI agents (Grok Bot) that supported an astronomy project, applying the CondGen label-to-spectrum network and reporting an MAE of 0.003681 on 21,657 synthetic test spectra, with work assembled in under a week under human supervision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To illustrate how a human-supervised team of specialized AI agents can be delegated scientific research tasks—orchestration, implementation, data curation, critique, literature search, figure and manuscript work—within a real astronomy research project.

Method: Qualitative case study of the Bot Spectroscopist team (Grok Bot powered by xAI Grok). Work was divided among specialized agents while humans decided scientific scope, model changes, and manuscript inclusion. The application is CondGen, a deterministic label-to-spectrum network extended to five elemental abundances, built on a pre-existing spectral database, notebooks, and research program.

Result: The baseline assessment achieved an MAE of 0.003681 in continuum-normalized flux on 21,657 synthetic test spectra. Binned diagnostics show reconstruction error varies with effective temperature and abundance. A non-monotonic cool-star magnesium response was flagged, requiring a matched SYNSPEC abundance sweep to check physical fidelity. Analysis, diagnostics, figures, and initial drafting were assembled in less than a week.

Conclusion: The study illustrates task delegation, artifact exchange, critique, and correction under human supervision. No comparison with a human team from the same resources was performed, so the elapsed-time account does not establish a productivity or labor saving.

Abstract: We present a qualitative case study of a human-supervised team of specialized AI agents supporting an astronomy research project. The Bot Spectroscopist team, described in the project record as operating through Grok Bot powered by xAI Grok, divided work among orchestration, numerical implementation, data curation, spectroscopy critique, literature search, figure preparation, manuscript editing, and review. Human decisions determined scientific scope, model changes, and manuscript inclusion. The scientific application is CondGen, a deterministic label-to-spectrum network extending our previous work to five individual elemental abundances. The reported baseline assessment achieved a mean absolute error (MAE) of 0.003681 in continuum-normalized flux on 21,657 synthetic test spectra. Existing binned diagnostics show that reconstruction error varies with effective temperature and abundance. The bots also flagged a non-monotonic cool-star magnesium response for further investigation; a matched SYNSPEC abundance sweep is needed to assess its physical fidelity. Building on a pre-existing spectral database, notebooks, and research program, the authors estimate that the reported analysis, diagnostics, figure preparation, and initial manuscript drafting were assembled in less than a week through human prompting and exchanges among agents. No comparison with a human team starting from the same resources was performed, so this elapsed-time account does not establish a productivity or labor saving. The study illustrates task delegation, artifact exchange, critique, and correction under human supervision.

</details>

### [316] [Effect of transition layer on wakefield in plasma waveguide](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06396)
*Peter Markov,Kostyantyn Galaydych,Gennadiy Sotnikov*

Main category: physics.acc-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies plasma wakefield acceleration (PWFA) for positrons and electrons in a layered plasma waveguide (LPW) with a transition layer (TL), showing that the TL thickness affects acceleration and proposing parameter corrections to restore energy gain and stable transport.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Layered plasma waveguides with transition layers between regions of different plasma density are relevant to PWFA design, but the impact of the transition layer on acceleration conditions for positrons and electrons is not well understood.

Method: 2.5D particle-in-cell simulations of a PWFA in a layered plasma waveguide, modeled as a high-density near-wall tubular plasma surrounding a low-density on-axis column, separated by a transition layer where density varies smoothly.

Result: Using PWFA parameters optimized for a layered waveguide without a transition layer alters acceleration conditions when the TL is present, with the effect depending on TL thickness. When the TL thickness exceeds the skin-layer depth in the tubular plasma, the positron acceleration rate decreases substantially.

Conclusion: Methods are proposed to correct the plasma waveguide parameters, compensating for the reduced energy gain of accelerated bunches and ensuring stable beam transport.

Abstract: Using 2.5D particle-in-cell simulations, we investigate the parameters of a plasma wakefield accelerator (PWFA) for positrons and electrons in a layered plasma waveguide (LPW) in the presence of a transition layer (TL) between two layers of uniform plasma with different densities. The layered plasma is modeled as a combination of a near-wall tubular plasma of higher density and an on-axis plasma column of substantially lower density, separated by a TL in which the plasma density varies smoothly from the lower to the higher value according to a specified law. It is shown that using the PWFA parameters determined for a layered plasma waveguide without the transition layer changes the acceleration conditions when the TL is present, and that this change depends on the TL thickness. It is demonstrated that when the TL thickness exceeds the skin-layer depth in the tubular plasma, the positron acceleration rate decreases substantially. The methods are proposed for correcting the parameters of the plasma waveguide under consideration that compensate for the decrease in the energy gain of the accelerated bunches and ensure their stable transport.

</details>
